# Caustica engine notes

Per-feature design notes, moved out of CLAUDE.md on 2026-10-31 so sessions and subagents don't load them every time. Read the section for the area you are changing before you change it (grep the `## ` headings). Add each new feature's section here, not to CLAUDE.md.

## Status (2026-10-29)

**The scene expansion is finished.** It was paused on 2026-10-26 and completed on 2026-10-29. It added:

- 24 presets and 11 shapes.
- Up to 256 objects (4096 since instancing) and 32 extra area lights.
- An object and light editor, viewport picking, procedural patterns and a material library.

The old `WORK_IN_PROGRESS.md` checkpoint was removed once this was done; this section replaces it.

**Done on 2026-10-29:**

- The self-test is extended to all presets and live edits (180 frames).
- `rr_stability.py` uses an unlit scene; it was rerun and passes.
- Randomize covers presets 0–22 and keeps studio layouts, recoloring only diffuse and glass objects.
- The preset camera carries over only between Cornell presets.
- The preset combo syncs from `scene_name`, so Load, `--settings` and Randomize all update it.
- The wheel and distance slider now reach 200, matching `validate()`.
- Editor slider ranges match `validate()`.
- The reflection guide ray runs only when RR is active.
- `direct` is zeroed alongside `radiance` on NaN samples.
- New `--set` flag; `--help` lists the presets.
- Chess (20) and Neon (21) cameras reframed. All 24 presets were checked at 1440×900.
- README brought up to date.

**Rendering at 1440×900** (DLAA RR, 4 spp, debug exe): 3.7–6.6 ms GPU per frame. The slowest are mirror corridor (17) at 6.6 ms, neon (21) at 5.3 ms and emissive spheres (8) at 5.1 ms.

**Git.** The project is a git repo (root `cornell/`), with a private GitHub remote. Commit only when the user asks.

**Disk use.** `target/` is about 3.5 GB and `vendor/` about 0.3 GB. The root `.gitignore` already lists `native/target`, `native/vendor` and `native/captures`.

## Real lenses and glare (track 16, 2026-11-02)

- **Lens systems** (`src/lens.rs`, `camera_ray` in pathtrace.hlsl): `Settings::lens_system` (0 = the 5c thin lens; 1–4 `lens::LENSES`) applies to the perspective model only, and `Settings::real_lens()` makes `perspective()` false, so RR, the temporal upscalers, picking, the gizmo and BDPT light tracing are off. Prescriptions are pbrt / Kolb 1995 tables (radius, thickness, n_d, diameter; mm). `INFO` measures each lens: focal length from a paraxial ray from infinity (49.97, 21.78, 9.98, 50.66 mm) and the wide-open f-number from the entrance pupil (bisection on the height of an axial bundle: f/2.00, 2.75, 4.00, 2.81). The unit tests check these against the names.
- **`lens::pack`** (rebuilt by `Gpu::prepare_lens` when lens, f-number, focus, fov, aspect or dispersion change): stops down by scaling the stop radius by N₀/N; focuses with a paraxial ray from the axial object point (`focus_distance` × `lens_scale` × 1000 mm in front of the front vertex) and puts the film where it crosses the axis (clamped to 4 f); film half height = (f + focus extension) · tan(fov/2), or f · 1.6 · fov/2 for the fisheye (equidistant, so fov 100° shows its whole 156° circle). Exit-pupil bounds for 32 radial film bands (a 40² grid on the rear vertex plane, 4 film points per band, grown one cell; symmetric in y), and the centre's effective pupil E[pass · cos⁴θ] · area, which normalizes the exposure to 1 at the frame centre for every lens and f-number.
- **Shader:** film point = −ndc × film half size (the lens inverts the image), a uniform point in its band's bounds rotated to the film point's angle, `trace_lens` through every surface (pbrt's sphere-root choice; the stop is `inside_aperture`, so the blade count shapes the bokeh), then lens space (mm, −z forward) to world at `real_lens.z` units per mm with the eye at the front vertex. `camera_weight` = cos⁴θ · band area / centre pupil; vignetted samples are invalid (zero). The guide ray goes through the band's centre (falls back to the pinhole ray when that is blocked). BDPT and photon gathering multiply their camera throughput by `camera_weight` too.
- **Dispersion:** per-surface indices at 610 / 550 / 465 nm from a Cauchy fit through n_d and an Abbe number guessed from n_d (60 at 1.52 down to 28 at 1.80); `dispersion` scales the spread. With it on, each sample traces one random channel with weight 3. Off by default: the guessed glasses undo the designs' achromatic pairs and give rainbow fringes (purple fringing on the wide angle's light at 1).
- **Look** (`captures/lens/`, 640×400): framing and centre exposure match the pinhole; the Double Gauss at fov 60 (past its 44° field) shows mechanical and cos⁴ vignetting; the fisheye's barrel distortion; the singlet's spherical-aberration glow; fairy-lights at f/2 vs f/8 at scale 0.05 (bokeh and brightness).
- **Glare** (`Settings::glare`, `glare_size`, `glare_threshold`; display only, out of `transport_key`): `glare_pass` in post.hlsl, one dispatch of 512 groups (a group is a row or column, 256 threads, Stockham radix-2 FFT in groupshared memory for 3 channels) per mode, constants in slots 13-22 (the exposure meter has 23 and the pre-exposure 24-26: `CB_SLOTS` 27). When blades, rotation or size change (`glare_kernel`): the aperture (4×4 supersampled, `glare_settings.z` = 256 − 232 · size texels across) into region 0, row and column FFTs, then the PSF per channel |F(k/s)|²/s² with s = λ/550 nm, normalized by Parseval to unit sum, into region 1, and its FFT. Every frame: the highlights (luminance above the threshold, 4×4 box per cell of the 384-px image in the 512² grid; the padding keeps the circular convolution from wrapping), forward rows, columns forward × kernel × inverse, inverse rows with the 1/N². `lens_and_film` adds glare × strength (bilinear) before the vignette. A small bright light with 6 blades gives a 6-spike starburst with rainbow spikes; extended or dim sources give a coloured bloom.
- **Self-test:** 289 (Double Gauss at f/4, 6 blades, dispersion, glare) and 290 (fisheye) check that the image is finite and lit with RR off; 291 resets. `SCENES_FROM` is 292.

## BDPT and photon mapping (track 15 part 2, 2026-11-01)

- **Where:** `shaders/integrators.hlsl` (includes pathtrace.hlsl, compiled with `-D TEXTURE_MAPS`), `Settings::integrator` (0 path, 1 BDPT, 2 photon mapping), `Gpu::integrate` (one integrator over one region) and `Gpu::prepare_aux`. Both new integrators write the guides through `write_guides` (moved out of `trace`), use white noise and a box pixel filter, treat glass as solid in shadow rays (`glass_blocks`, so caustics come from real paths), and write a running mean into the accumulation like `trace` (`finish_frame`; the direct-light AOV holds the beauty). Not supported: volumes and fog, portals, nested media (a single glass, entered by the side of the hit), `mis_mode`, the LPE / path-record variants, more than 24 bounces in BDPT (`BDPT_BOUNCES`) or 32 in a photon walk (`PHOTON_WALK`).
- **aux** (root parameter 15, u3 space 2, a raw buffer): photon mapping's counter, 2^20 list heads and 32-byte photons (position, incoming direction, power, next) at 0, capacity photons × min(bounces, `PHOTON_BOUNCES`) (10; `photons` is at most 2^20, about 340 MB); BDPT's splats (rgb floats per render pixel) at `splat_offset`, zeroed by a copy when allocated and by `bdpt_resolve` after every frame.
- **BDPT** (PBRT-v3's formulation): a camera subpath and a light subpath of up to bounces + 2 / + 1 vertices in local arrays (`BVertex`: position, normal, throughput, area pdfs both ways, material and texture values), then every strategy (s, t) with s + t − 2 ≤ bounces, weighted by the balance heuristic over all of them (`mis_weight`, with the four endpoint densities passed in). Light subpaths start on table triangles and spheres (uniform area, cosine emission, both sides of a two-sided panel), chosen as next-event estimation chooses them, so s = 1 and the subpath share one origin density. The HDRI, the sky and the suns start no subpath: they keep the path tracer's two strategies (hits and NEE) with its MIS. Light tracing (t = 1) splats with float compare-exchange adds; it needs a pinhole perspective or tilt-shift camera without RR's jitter (`integrator.w` bit 0), otherwise the camera counts as a delta vertex. The pinhole importance uses the image-plane area times the region's share of the image (`photon.y`), which keeps camera and light strategies' sample counts consistent in battle mode. Dielectrics are delta vertices (η² only on camera subpaths); emission outside the light table (procedural emitters) is found by hits alone.
- **Visibility between subpaths** (`connected`): both ends move off their surfaces toward each other. With a shadow ray stopping a fixed 0.6 mm short of the far point (as NEE's do), grazing connections hit the far point's own surface (it lies a hair below a hit computed through an instance transform): the lit door of veach-ajar reached the wall at 35% of its light, the image 4% dark. A non-finite strategy term is dropped on its own (`add_term`), not the whole sample.
- **Photon mapping** (Knaus and Zwicker 2011, probabilistic progressive photon mapping): each frame `photon_clear` empties the grid, `photon_trace` emits `photons` × the region's share (sky and HDRI from a disk of the scene's bounding sphere, suns likewise, table emitters as BDPT's light subpaths), Russian roulette from the third bounce, deposits from the second hit on at gather surfaces (all but near-mirror metals, roughness < 0.2), into cells one radius wide (hashed, linked lists). `photon_gather` follows camera paths through delta surfaces and near mirrors to the first gather surface, takes direct light by NEE plus one BSDF ray (balance heuristic) and indirect light from the photons in the 27 neighbouring cells. The radius starts at `photon_radius` × the bounding radius (rebuild_scene; the floor is left out) and shrinks by (i + 2/3)/(i + 1) in r² per frame (`photon_frames`, `photon_scale`, reset with the accumulation), so frames are independent estimates and the running mean converges; the bias fades as samples grow.
- **Battle mode** (`Settings::battle`, kept across scene loads): quadrants path tracing, path + ReSTIR DI, BDPT and photon mapping, each dispatched over its region (`region` constant: trace, the ReSTIR passes, the new entries all offset and bound-check by it; ReSTIR's neighbours and history stay inside it) with its own constants (slots 9–12 × CB_STRIDE) and its own sample count (`Gpu::battle`). Timestamps 5–12 bracket each quadrant; the slowest one takes `spp` samples per frame and the others as many as fit in its time (at most 64, fractions carried). The photon quadrant takes one gather per frame and spends its share on photons instead (×1–4 its quarter of `photons`): gathers of one frame share a photon map, and counting them as samples ran its count to the cap 64 times sooner (found in review). RR, the native denoiser and the upscalers are off; `Gpu::samples` is the smallest quadrant's count. The overlay draws the split and each quadrant's samples and GPU seconds; with a reference loaded, the convergence graph plots each quadrant's RMSE×1000 against its GPU time.
- **Checked** (box filter for all, glass casting shadows, the same bounces; image mean against a path-traced reference): presets 0 and 3 BDPT 1.0003 / 1.0007 (per-pixel RMSE×1000 1.0 / 1.9 at 4096 spp), photon mapping 1.0003 / 1.0002; desert-monolith (sun) 1.0000 both; hdri-noon-spheres 0.998 / 0.997; cornell-caustic 1.003 both; veach-ajar BDPT 1.005 (the path tracer's own MIS and NEE-only renders differ by up to 1.6% in its dark half), photon mapping 1.03 at 16k spp (radius bias, falling). fairy-lights: both +1% over the path tracer, which is the light-sampling gap in roadmap "Known issues". Preset 3 needed 10 bounces (it sets 10): with BDPT capped at 8 its glass quadrant was 1.1% dark.
- **Equal samples on cornell-caustic** (160×90, RMSE×1000 against 65k spp): path 23.6, BDPT 6.8, photon mapping 5.1, at 1.2 / 8.2 / 3.5 ms per 16 spp: at equal time BDPT has about 1.8× and photon mapping about 7× less variance than path tracing there. On veach-ajar at equal time (about 220 ms): path 4.5, BDPT 5.9, photon mapping 6.1.
- **Cost** (release, 1440×900, preset 3, 4 spp, GPU ms per frame, RR / native): path tracing 9.6 / 10.3; BDPT 97 / 126 (the light-tracing splats run only without RR's jitter); photon mapping 6.6 / 6.0 (2^18 photons per frame); battle mode 48. With the integrator at path tracing nothing changed: presets 0 and 3, RR and native, two interleaved runs each against the ReSTIR snapshot, all within ±0.6 ms of noise. The scene bounds for sun and sky photons are measured only when photon mapping runs after a rebuild: measuring them in every `rebuild_scene` (all object vertices) made each timeline-export frame 10 ms slower and broke the self-test's export.
- **Native denoiser:** the new integrators keep the guide ray's albedo (coverage 1) and an empty specular AOV, so the native denoiser may fringe at edges and ghost on mirrors and glass with them; `trace` accumulates the samples' own albedo. RR is unaffected. Views 7 and 10 (error, variance) assume one sample count, so they are wrong across battle quadrants.
- **Self-test:** frames 285–287 (preset 3: BDPT, photon mapping, battle) check that the image is finite and lit and that every quadrant rendered.

## ReSTIR DI (track 15 part 1, 2026-11-01)

- **What it does:** with `Settings::restir` (Render → Advanced; off by default, on in `fairy-lights`), the light sample at a path's first hit comes from per-pixel reservoirs (Bitterli et al. 2020) instead of one draw from the power CDF. It covers the whole emitter table (triangles, sphere lights, suns); the HDRI keeps its own strategy, chosen with the same probability as before, and its MIS. Later bounces and volume scatters are unchanged.
- **Samples:** an emitter index plus two 16-bit numbers that name the same point from every surface: uniform area on a triangle or on the whole sphere (not its visible cap), a direction in a sun's cone. Shifting a sample between pixels or frames is the identity, with Jacobian 1. Source pdf = power selection × 1/area (sun: 1/(2π·om)).
- **Target:** unshadowed luminance of the table's flat emission × G × cos × a cheap BSDF (Lambert floored at 0.05/π, plus one isotropic GGX lobe), from per-surface terms read once (`make_domain`). A triangle point seen from behind gets 2% weight, not 0: on a closed emitter it is hidden by the object itself, which an unshadowed target cannot see. Measured on `fairy-lights`, direct light at 1 spp: flat-region RMSE 49 → 24 with the 2% weight. Keeping it above 0 keeps the result unbiased for two-sided panels and for a camera inside a luminous room.
- **Passes** (all in pathtrace.hlsl, compiled with the map code):
  - `restir_presample`: the frame's light tiles (Wyman and Panteleev 2021), 128 × 1024 samples drawn from the source distribution through Walker's alias table (`lighting::alias_records`, packed after the emitters in the emitter buffer, eight pairs per record; plain NEE keeps its CDF, so it is unchanged). An entry is stored in a `Reservoir` record: light | kind << 30, uv, source pdf, the emission's luminance, point, normal. Every 8×8 group takes its candidates from one tile, which keeps the reads in cache. Candidates that share a tile are correlated, but RIS only needs each one's marginal pdf, so this stays unbiased. On fairy-lights (150k emitter triangles, RR 1 spp, 1440×900), ReSTIR's overhead was +19.3 ms with a CDF search per candidate, +14.3 ms with the alias table and +2.5 ms with tiles, at the same quality.
  - `restir_initial` (z = layer): the guide ray's hit, `restir_candidates` RIS candidates from a tile (default 8), then a temporal merge with last frame's final reservoir at the reprojected pixel (normal > 0.8, within 5% of the view distance; history M capped at `restir_history`, default 20).
  - `restir_spatial` (z = layer): up to `restir_neighbors` (default 4) random neighbours within 30 px (at 1080 lines), normal > 0.9 and the plane distance within 5% of the view distance. Pairwise MIS (3k + 1 target evaluations instead of (k + 1)²).
  - `trace`: at bounce 0, sample s < layers merges its layer's final reservoir with one fresh candidate at the actual hit (the native pixel filter, depth of field and portals move the hit off the reservoir's surface). Weight 1, so BSDF hits on table emitters from that vertex count 0 (`previous_restir`), except a sphere light seen from inside it (`restir_covers`), which the area samples cannot reach (found in review).
- **Unbiased:** every merge is generalized RIS (Lin et al. 2022) with balance or pairwise weights that sum to 1 over the merged surfaces. Domains are picked by geometry only. A reservoir that holds no sample (W = 0) still takes part with its M: skipping it conditioned on its random outcome and measured +1.1% with one candidate. Visibility enters only through the final shadow ray (no visibility reuse). Checked at 4096 spp against light sampling only (`mis_mode` 1, 1 bounce): image mean within 0.1% and RMSE×1000 below that of an independent NEE run, on fairy-lights, candle-shelf and night-skyline. With all bounces, ReSTIR matches NEE-only on presets 0, light-temperature-row and colonnade-night (0.9999 vs 1.0002, 0.9979 vs 0.9976, 0.9995 vs 0.9994).
- **NEE vs MIS gap (2026-11-01), closed:** light sampling only read 0.57% darker than MIS and BSDF only on night-skyline and −0.24% on light-temperature-row. The fixed 0.6 mm shadow stop caused most of it and was fixed the next day ("Grazing shadow rays"); the rest was night-skyline's noise and a shading-normal mismatch on light-temperature-row's pawns, fixed 2026-11-10 ("Light sampling and BSDF sampling agree (accuracy audit B7)").
- **Layers:** samples within a frame would all share one reservoir, so each of the frame's first `layers` samples has its own chain: `min(spp, 4)`, capped so the buffer stays ≤ 1 GiB (4K: 2 layers). Later samples use plain light sampling. Buffer: root UAV u2 space 2 (root parameter 14), 2 × layers × render pixels × 32 B (final reservoirs, then `restir_initial`'s), allocated while `restir` is on. The light tiles follow (4 MiB). A new buffer, a full reset (`reset(true)`) or any material or emitter rebuild (keep-exposure mode skips the reset) drops the history; camera moves keep it.
- **Constants:** `restir` = flags (bit 0 on, bit 1 history valid, bits 8–15 layers), candidates, neighbours, history cap. The passes always use white noise (`white_noise`; `random()` is unchanged in `trace`).
- **Gain** (480×270, native raw, the end of a 1-spp `--orbit-test` against a 4096-spp reference, RMSE×1000 of l/(1+l) on flat pixels (93–98% of the image), direct light only): fairy-lights 62.3 → 24.0 (6.7× less variance), candle-shelf 42.4 → 12.8 (11×). With all bounces the gain is smaller, since indirect light dominates a white box: fairy-lights whole-image 97.5 → 88.8 before the back-face weight.
- **Cost** (1440×900, release, GPU ms, one run each, off → on): fairy-lights RR 1 spp 14.1 → 17.6, RR 4 spp 41.3 → 56.3, native 1 spp 11.6 → 14.2; candle-shelf 6.3 → 7.2, 12.7 → 18.9, 3.1 → 4.7; preset 0 4.0 → 4.4, 6.1 → 8.5, 1.3 → 1.8. That is roughly +0.5–3.5 ms per layer: the guide ray in `restir_initial`, 8 candidates, the spatial pass and the merge in `trace`. Off, nothing runs: the other shaders compile to the same code paths (`white_noise` is false, `previous_restir` stays false).
- **Self-test:** frames 283–284 (every light kind, then the sun alone) run with ReSTIR and 2 layers and check that the passes ran. `fairy-lights` runs it again in the scene sweep.
- **Not done:** visibility reuse (a shadow ray in the initial pass; biased unless the spatial MIS weights trace rays too); ReSTIR at later bounces (ReSTIR GI / PT); emissive texture maps and procedural emitters are still outside the table; the target ignores coat, sheen, anisotropy and transmission; the temporal history is dropped on any non-camera change, including every animation frame; the spatial radius is fixed, not adaptive; with a static camera and accumulation, temporal reuse correlates frames, so it converges more slowly than plain sampling (turn it off for offline renders, or set History to 0); no debug view of reservoir M or W.

## Light path expressions (track 14 part 2, 2026-11-01)

- **Language** (`lpe.rs`): OSL-style regular expressions over D G S T V (scattering) and L B (the light event), `.`, `R` = [DGS], sets, `[^ ]`, `( )`, `|`, `* + ?`; an optional leading C. Parsed to a Thompson NFA, subset-constructed, minimised (Moore) with states that can never accept dropped; at most 32 states (subset construction stops at 4096 subsets, so ".* D . . . L" fails fast); an expression whose language has no string ending in L or B is rejected. A leading C is stripped once, before any `|`. `Settings::lpe` (≤ 4, validated; kept across scene and preset loads) and `lpe_slot` (display only, excluded from the transport key). The table (t8, root parameter 13, an upload buffer): per slot, state and event, next | accept(next) << 8, 255 dead.
- **Events:** D, G (GGX, coat, sheen, grating orders; S instead below base roughness 0.1), S (a dielectric's reflection), T (refraction, any roughness), V (a volume scatter); L (emitters, suns, by NEE or by a hit), B (sky gradient, HDRI, physical sky: misses and HDRI NEE). Portals and nested-dielectric pass-throughs are no events.
- **Shader:** the `trace_lpe` variant (`-D TEXTURE_MAPS -D LPE`, picked while any slot is set; the other variants compile to the same code as before). Per path: the DFA states (8 bits per slot in one uint) and a label weight. A surface's light sample is split exactly into diffuse and the rest by `lpe_diffuse` (eval_bsdf's diffuse term after the sheen and coat dimming, times 1 − transmission). A continued path takes one label, drawn by a hash (not `random()`, so the beauty's samples are unchanged) with the luminance share of the diffuse part at the sampled direction, and the weight is multiplied by share / probability per channel: each slot is unbiased, a partition of all paths sums to the beauty in expectation. Sums per pixel in registers, then the running mean in `lpe_aov` (u1 space 2, root parameter 12, render pixels × slots float4, allocated while slots are set; a new buffer restarts the accumulation). Unclamped (the firefly clamp is ignored); non-finite contributions are dropped. Cost with 4 slots (preset 0, 1440×900, release): RR 5.33 → 6.90 ms, native 5.14 → 7.38 ms; off, the other variants are unchanged (5.31 / 5.15 before).
- **Display:** view 12 shows slot `lpe.y`, tone-mapped, and writes it to `linear_output` instead of the beauty, so `--capture x.exr` saves the AOV.
- **Checked:** the partition `C [LB]`, `C D .* [LB]`, `C [GS] .* [LB]`, `C [TV] .* [LB]` sums to the raw beauty (image mean to 1e-6; per-pixel RMSE×1000 of l/(1+l) 0.007–0.21 at 512–1024 spp) on presets 3 and 9, `scattering-materials` and `hdri-fire-sky-dragon`. `C D L` under MIS (labels drawn on BSDF hits) matches light sampling only (exact split) to 0.02% (preset 9) and 0.002% (3); `C [GS] L` within its noise (1.8% and 4.3%, both under 1σ: light sampling alone is very noisy on near-mirror lobes). Self-test 232–236 checks the partition sum (3%) at the self-test's sample count.
- **Not done:** object or material labels (OSL's `'name'` tags, "light off the red wall"); separate R / T for glossy transmission (rough glass is T); one AOV at a time on screen, no multi-layer EXR with every slot; AOVs are not denoised (RR / the native denoiser see only the beauty); 4 slots; light samples through portals don't exist, so portal paths are BSDF-found only; LPE costs the `trace_maps` code even in untextured scenes while on; exports clear the slots (the AOVs are not exported).

## Path visualizer (track 14 part 1, 2026-11-01)

- **Shader:** `trace` compiled a third time with `-D TEXTURE_MAPS -D PATH_RECORD` (`trace_paths` in build.rs and hotreload.rs). `recording` is a static const, and `record()` is empty without the define, so `trace` / `trace_maps` compile to the same code as before (only phi operand order differed in the disassembly). The recording variant runs on thread (0, 0) only, at render pixel `path_record.xy`, for `path_record.z` paths with the frame's sequence indices and RNG state: the first `sampling.y` are exactly the paths that frame accumulates, the rest the next indices. It writes no texture (the guide ray only sets `guide.material`).
- **Records** (`paths::Record`, 48 B: position, info = kind | bounce << 8 | path << 16, color, value, direction): the kinds and end reasons are listed above `trace`. Hit vertices are written after the next direction is chosen (kind = the lobe taken) with the throughput arriving and the luminance the hit added (emission, or the environment for an escape); light samples are written before their vertex (from, direction, distance, contribution, blocked); one end record per path (radiance after the firefly clamp, reason). Record 0 holds the count; capacity `PATH_CAPACITY` 16384.
- **GPU:** root parameter 11 is a root UAV (u0 space 2), bound in `bind()` for every dispatch. `Gpu::path_request` (output pixel, count; mapped to the render pixel as the light meter's) makes `render` dispatch one group before the frame's trace, copy the buffer to `path_readback`, and map it after Present into `path_result`.
- **App:** `UiState::paths` (`paths::PathView`). A left click while the window is open, or Alt+click, records instead of picking; P toggles the window; `--ui-paths X,Y[,I]` records (and selects path I) for UI captures. Lines are drawn with `View::segment` (clipped at the near plane), perspective camera only; escapes and sun / HDRI light samples are drawn `Settings::distance` long. Self-test frame 230 records 8 paths at the centre and checks their structure.
- **Not done:** LPEs (part 2); an animated photon along a path; NEE through portals and the false-hit pass-throughs of the shadow rays are not shown; the native filter's or RR's view of the pixel is not shown (the paths are raw samples); no export of the paths (OBJ / JSON); 64 paths at high bounce counts can fill the 16384 records (later paths are dropped, the window says so); the recording dispatch sits inside the frame's trace timestamp on the frame of a click.

## Procedural surfaces (track 13, 2026-11-01)

- **Objects:** `Object::sdf` (`Sdf`: none, exact sphere, exact torus, Mandelbulb, Menger level 5) renders the object as a procedural surface in the unit cube [−1, 1]³ of object space, under the usual transform. `kind()` returns a proxy mesh (sphere, torus, box) for CPU picking, framing and grounding; `geometry()` skips such objects (no GPU triangles, so the self-test counts still match); they are not in the emitter table (emission is found by BSDF hits only). The Scene tab's *Surface* combo picks it.
- **GPU:** one shared BLAS of one AABB (the unit cube), instanced per visible procedural object with `InstanceRecord.vertex_base` = the kind id; glass / hidden ones get FORCE_NON_OPAQUE like meshes. Every RayQuery loop (`intersect`, `occluded`, `surface_distance`) handles `CANDIDATE_PROCEDURAL_PRIMITIVE`: `sdf_hit` on the object ray (unnormalized, so t is the world t) and `CommitProceduralPrimitiveHit`; the glass-shadow and hidden-from-camera rules match triangles. A committed procedural hit takes its normal from the SDF gradient (tetrahedral differences), to world by the inverse transpose.
- **Intersection:** the sphere is analytic; the rest are sphere traced (128 steps, hit at 2e-4 object units; round shapes start at their bounding sphere; starting inside, for refraction, marches to the exit). The Mandelbulb uses Quilez's trigonometry-free power 8 (6 iterations; 3× cheaper than the polar form), the Menger sponge Quilez's cross subtraction.
- **Cost** (1440×900, native, `sdf-gallery` objects alone, GPU ms): mesh versions 5.6, exact sphere 4.9, exact torus 5.0, Mandelbulb 12.3, Menger 7.8; the gallery 19.5 (65 before the bounding sphere, the cheaper bulb and the step cap). Non-procedural scenes render the same as before (≤1e-6).
- **Scene:** `sdf-gallery`.

## Volumes (track 12, 2026-11-01)

- **Media.** Homogeneous only. An object's medium rides the nested-dielectric stack: a transmissive material with `density` > 0 (extinction per unit), `scatter_color` (single-scattering albedo) and `phase` (Henyey-Greenstein g). The colour still absorbs by Beer–Lambert as before. An IOR of 1 with roughness 0 makes an invisible boundary (smoke in a box). Global fog (`Settings::fog`) fills a box from the floor (y = −1) to `height`, ±`extent` on x and z, and exists only where the stack is empty (not inside glass).
- **Transport** (`pathtrace.hlsl`, after the false-hit loop): free-flight sampling on the grey extinction, so the pdf cancels the transmittance; a scatter before the surface multiplies by the albedo, does NEE through `sample_light` (shared with surfaces) weighted by the phase pdf with MIS, then samples HG. `previous_pdf` = phase pdf, so emission hits after a scatter are MIS-weighted like after a surface.
- **Shadow rays** (`medium_transmittance`): through a scattering object's medium up to the nearest surface (`surface_distance`, a minimal closest-hit query; refraction there ignored, as glass shadows do), then the fog past that point. Surface NEE applies it only when fog is on or the path is in a scattering medium. Clear glass keeps its old behaviour.
- **Packing:** `PackedMaterial` grew to 176 B (`medium` = albedo rgb + density, zeroed unless transmissive; `phase.x` = g). `Constants` gained `fog` (density, g, height, extent) and `fog_color`.
- **Cost:** a per-path `density` (the stack top's, updated with `absorption`) gates the volume code, so scenes without media pay ~1–3% GPU time (preset 0: 4.29 → ~4.35 ms; glass preset 3 within noise). Before that gate glass scenes paid 19%.
- **Checked:** non-volume renders match the previous build to 5e-7; Cornell box in fog, 32k spp: MIS vs NEE-only differ by 0.06% ± 0.13%.
- **Bounce budget:** a scatter uses one of the path's bounces, so dense media darken at the default 6 bounces; raise `bounces` in such scenes (the volume scenes use 8–32).
- **Absorption after false hits** (fixed with this track): the false-hit loop changed the medium stack without refreshing `absorption`, so leaving a higher-priority glass wall into water kept the glass's absorption; it now refreshes it (nested-dielectric scenes change slightly).
- **Denoisers:** RR handles haze well (cornell-fog, stage-beams). The native denoiser leaves the fog speckled: its demodulation and stops are surface-based.
- **Scenes:** `cornell-fog`, `stage-beams` (multiple scattering into the beams shows as sparse speckles at low spp: real variance), `scattering-materials` (milk, jade, smoke, wax, syrup), `underwater`.

## Upscalers (2026-10-29)

- **Setting:** `Settings.upscaler` (`Upscaler`: dlss default, fsr, xess, bicubic, bilinear). Used only when RR is off and `rw < width`.
  - It is in `transport_key`, so a change resets the render.
  - `main.rs` also calls `Gpu::reconfigure_upscalers()` when `rr`, `rr_preset`, `upscaler` or `dlss_preset` changes. This replaces `reconfigure_rr`.
- **`Gpu::prepare_upscaler`** creates the selected temporal upscaler lazily, at render size × output size.
  - A failure goes into `sr_failed`, and the display falls back to bicubic. `upscale_status` explains this in the UI.
  - `release_upscalers()` runs on resize and reconfigure.
- **Per-frame mode:** with a temporal upscaler active, `render()` calls `reset(false)` every drawn frame, so each frame is fresh jittered samples, as with RR. The native SVGF history (`camera_moved` → reproject) plus the upscaler do the accumulation. `run_upscaler` feeds color (ping when denoising, else accumulation), device depth (25) and motion (6), and writes texture 14.
- **Measured alternatives** (`tools/upscale_quality.py`, Classic, 1280×720, 50%):
  - Feeding the progressive accumulation (jittered or unjittered) measured worse: the upscalers assume each frame sits at its own jitter.
  - Still-camera quality now comes from a longer denoiser history: cap 256 where motion is 0, 24 otherwise (`post.hlsl` integrate).
- **Flags** in `scene_info.w`:
  - Bit 0: jittered primary rays. Both RR and temporal SR set it; trace reads it, no longer `modes.w`.
  - Bit 1: the display reads texture 14 (RR or SR output).
  - Bits 2–3: spatial filter (1 bilinear, 2 bicubic). Catmull-Rom, clamped to the 2×2 footprint against HDR ringing.
- **Jitter and motion conventions:**
  - All three take the same jitter sign as RR: the negated ray offset. Verified by probing all four sign combinations; the current one is best for each.
  - Motion is prev − curr in render pixels, scale 1. XeSS velocity scale is 1.
- **Results** (RMSE ×1000, light rim excluded; still / orbit):

| | Still | Orbit |
|---|---|---|
| Native 100% | 1.05 | 4.06 |
| RR | 1.90 | 2.60 |
| XeSS | 2.27 | 4.70 |
| FSR | 2.28 | 4.25 |
| DLSS SR | 2.62 | 3.82 |
| Bicubic | 4.15 | 5.91 |
| Bilinear | 4.70 | 6.01 |

  - With the rim included, DLSS SR and XeSS score about 9.5 on the still camera, because of how they blend the 12× HDR light edge.
- **Self-test:** frames 172–176 switch through every upscaler at scale 0.5 and assert that the available temporal ones start; frame 177 turns RR back on.
- **DLSS SR presets** (roadmap item 1, 2026-10-30): `Settings.dlss_preset` ∈ `scene::DLSS_PRESETS` (0 default, 10 J, 11 K, 12 L, 13 M), checked by `validate()` and kept across `preset()`.
  - `caustica_ngx_create_sr` sets all six `NVSDK_NGX_Parameter_DLSS_Hint_Render_Preset_*` hints on `sr_params`. A change reconfigures the upscalers (`main.rs` trigger list).
  - The UI combo shows only while DLSS SR is the active choice; `upscale_status` names the preset.
  - Self-test frames 180–184 cycle the presets and assert each starts; 185 restores default + RR.
  - Checked: at 50% (Performance), M renders identical to Default (NVIDIA picks M there), while J/K/L differ, so the hint reaches NGX.
  - SDK 310.9.16 has no RR presets beyond D/E/F.
- **egui scissor:** `draw_gui` skips meshes whose clipped scissor is empty. Off-screen windows after a self-test resize produced D3D12 warnings that failed the self-test, on `main` too.
- **Sliders:** all go through `crate::slider` (`SliderClamping::Edits`; since 2026-10-30 a `Slider` wrapper with double-click reset, see "Owner requests (item 6)"). egui's default clamps the stored value whenever a slider is drawn, which silently rewrote valid loaded values (found by the UI-plan agent). Self-test frames 178–179 guard this.

## Native HDR (roadmap item 2, 2026-10-30)

- **GUI colours with rgb above alpha** (fixed 2026-11-02): `Color32::from_white_alpha` and similar give premultiplied colours whose sRGB channels exceed alpha. gui.hlsl un-premultiplies (`c.rgb / c.a`) before linearising for HDR; that ratio (about 5 for white at alpha 12) linearised to about 50× white, so the box-select fill drew solid white. It now saturates the ratio first (scRGB and HDR10).
- **SDR GUI colours** (fixed 2026-11-06): `render::constants` set `eye.w` to 1, and only HDR overwrote it, so since native HDR `gui.hlsl` linearised egui's sRGB colours in SDR too, with nothing to re-encode them on the UNORM target. The panel (24, 26, 30) drew as (2, 3, 3), text grey 140 as 66. `eye.w` is now 0 outside HDR, as the shader's comment always said. An HDR display hides a regression here, so `sdr_constants_leave_the_gui_in_srgb` guards it; check SDR UI colours with `--capture-ui --set hdr_mode=1`.
- **Legibility, after that fix (UI review, 2026-11-06):** egui's weak text is about 2.2:1 on the panel, so `note()`, help text and tags use the plain label grey (about 5.3:1) and `field_hint` gives text-field placeholders the same, since egui draws them weak. `.weak()` stays only for the secondary frame number on the timeline. `theme()` fills text fields with grey 38 and outlines resting widgets in grey 70; both also apply to progress bars, the timeline track (its key marks and ruler numbers moved to the label grey to stay readable) and buttons. The Export note turns WARN when the name template has neither `{time}` nor `{spp}` (`video::name_repeats`), because a second save then overwrites the first; it names the folder from `dirs::output_dir`.
- **Settings** (display-only, not in `transport_key`, kept across `preset()`): `hdr_mode` 0 Auto (follow Windows), 1 SDR, 2 forced scRGB; `hdr_peak` and `hdr_white` in nits, 0 = from Windows.
- **Detection:** `App` holds a `display::Monitor`, re-queried on `Moved`/`ScaleFactorChanged` and when `current()` turns false (polled every 30 frames). This covers HDR toggles, calibration reruns and the SDR brightness slider. A change prints `DISPLAY DisplayInfo {…}`.
- **This machine:** Dell S3422DWG, Windows HDR on, 530 nits peak and full frame, SDR white 480 nits (slider at max). With white that close to the peak, the roll-off starts around 110 nits.
- **Swapchain:** `Gpu::set_hdr` flips `hdr_active` and calls `resize`, which uses `swap_format()` (RGBA16F or RGBA8) and sets G10/P709 or G22/P709.
  - **Color-space support depends on the current buffer format**, so check after `ResizeBuffers` (`color_space_ok`). Checking on the 8-bit swapchain always says no; if the check fails, it falls back to SDR.
- **Transform** (`post.hlsl` `hdr_map`), by tone map:
  - **Linear (0):** scene × exposure × paper white → BT.2390 EETF on the max channel in PQ space (knee = 1.5·PQ(peak) − 0.5) → min(peak) → /80. It keeps hue; the curve reaches the full peak only at 10,000 nits.
  - **Other tone maps (owner feedback: they had no effect in HDR):** t = `tone_curve` (the SDR curve, display light) ^ (2.2 / gamma). Below t = 0.5 the output is exactly t × paper white, the SDR brightness; above, u = (t − 0.5)/0.5 expands as 0.5 + 0.5·u/(1 − u + u/R), R = (r − 0.5)/0.5, r = peak/white: slope-continuous, reaching the peak at t = 1.
  - A first try, r·T(x/r), pushed midtones into the ACES toe (half as bright), and t/(1 − t + t/r) with no knee lifted ACES midtones by 27%.
  - Debug views and the A/B line use `sdr_in_hdr` (gamma-decoded × white).
  - `display::hdr_map` mirrors all of it (with `tone_curve`); unit test `hdr_tone_maps_keep_the_sdr_midtones_and_fill_the_headroom`.
  - `output_image` (10) stays SDR-tonemapped, so PNG, the sequence and high-res captures are unchanged.
  - The constants gain `hdr` = [on, white, peak, 0]; `eye.w` = white/80 tells `gui.hlsl` to linearize egui's premultiplied sRGB and scale it to paper white. There are two GUI PSOs (RGBA8 / RGBA16F).
- **Tests:** unit test `hdr_transform_keeps_midtones_and_respects_the_peak` (gray 0.4 at 200 nits → 1.0; clamp at peak/80; monotonic; hue kept) and `png_crc_matches_the_spec`.
  - Self-test 186–189: forced HDR with 1000/200; at 187 it reads back 18 and 26 and compares every pixel with `display::hdr_map` (worst relative error ~1e-3, the RGBA16F precision); 188 SDR; 189 Auto.
  - On an HDR monitor, Auto means the whole self-test runs in HDR.
  - The PQ PNG was decoded and matches linear EXR × white in BT.2020 to 0.1 nit.
- **HDR10 swapchain (2026-11-02):** `Settings::hdr10` (display-only) picks R10G10B10A2 with `G2084_NONE_P2020` instead of scRGB whenever HDR is on. `display` writes PQ code values in BT.2020 into texture 26 (then R10G10B10A2, so the copy to the backbuffer still works; `hdr.w` = 1), and `gui.hlsl` encodes egui as PQ when `eye.w` < 0 (a third GUI PSO). Readbacks of 26 and of the backbuffer decode back to scRGB (`display::hdr10_to_scrgb`), so HDR PNG, JPEG XR, the HDR10 video and UI captures work unchanged. If the swapchain refuses the colour space, `Gpu::hdr10_refused` keeps HDR on scRGB for the session. Self-test 226–229 (forced HDR10 at 1000 / 200 nits, compared with `display::hdr_map` to 2% of the brightest channel, then HDR PNG and JPEG XR). Through 10 bits the HDR PNG matched the scRGB one to 2e-7 mean.
  - egui alpha-blends on PQ code values in HDR10, so anti-aliased edges differ slightly from scRGB.
- **JPEG XR (2026-11-02):** `ImageFormat::Jxr`, `display::write_jxr`: what the HDR screen shows (texture 26) as scRGB `64bppRGBAHalf`, lossless, through WIC's `GUID_ContainerFormatWmp` encoder, the format Game Bar and the Snipping Tool use. Decoded with WPF's WMPhoto decoder: 1000-nit peak read back as 12.5.

- Layout: `TopBottomPanel` top bar and status bar, `SidePanel::right` (370 pt default, 320–560). Panel fill is 250/255 alpha over the render, which still fills the window (lens shift is phase 3).

- **Render tab (reworked after owner feedback: the first version's "AI / Built-in / Off" split and "Upscaler: not needed at 100%" read wrong):** one Reconstruction section in the old menu's terms. "DLSS Ray Reconstruction" checkbox (disabled with `rr_status` when `Gpu::rr_available()` is false), RR model, Quality mode, Upscaler (disabled while RR is on), DLSS preset, "Native denoiser (D)", Render scale (advanced or off-list).
  - Quality mode entries come from `quality_modes`: with RR, DLAA (100%); without, Native (100%), plus DLAA / FSR Native AA / XeSS Native AA (100%) for a temporal upscaler, then 67 / 58 / 50 / 33%.
  - **`Settings.native_aa`** (default false): at 100% `prepare_upscaler` runs the temporal upscaler as anti-aliasing. Off by default so `--no-rr` at 100% stays the progressive path that unbiased references rely on. It is forced off for high-res export (DLAA restarts every frame) and is in the reconfigure trigger list. Self-test 196 checks that DLAA starts.
- Exposure is shown in EV (log2), written back only on change.
- egui's default fonts lack `→` and `●`: the glyph unit test caught both. The status dot is painted.
- CLI for captures: `--ui-tab`, `--ui-advanced`, `--ui-select`.
- Self-test: 178 opens the Render tab for the slider-clamp guard; 190–195 draw the object, light, main light, camera, Look and Export views with advanced rows, then delete the added light through `delete_selected`.

## Analytic lights (track 7, 2026-10-30)

- **Kinds** (`scene::LightKind` on `AreaLight::kind`, default `rect`): `disk` (ellipse inscribed in `size`), `sphere` (diameter `size[0]`), `spot` (a disk plus `spread`, the full cone angle, and `blend`, the fading share of it), `sun` (`angle`, angular diameter; position only places its handle). The main light stays a rectangle. `AreaLight::direction()` is the rotated −Y; `radiance()` turns intensity into radiance: a rectangle's intensity is its radiance, a disk / spot / sphere's is divided by its projected area (π·a·b/4), so it shines as a 1 × 1 panel at any size, and a sun's by sin² of its angular radius (a white surface facing it reads the intensity). `cone()` gives the spot's outer and inner cosines (inner kept ≥ outer + 1e-5 for smoothstep).
- **Geometry** (`geometry::static_geometry`): a disk / spot is a 64-triangle fan (`DISK_SEGMENTS`, wound like the rectangle so cross(e1, e2) is the facing normal), a sphere the unit sphere mesh (64 × 32, vertices on the sphere, so the mesh is inscribed) scaled by the radius, a sun nothing. `geometry_key` includes the kind.
- **Materials** (`lighting::materials`): emission = `radiance()`; a sphere is two-sided (`optical.w` 0); a spot sets flag bit 5 (32) in `lobes.w` with the cosines in `secondary.xy`.
- **Emitter table** (`lighting::analytic`, `emitter_table(vertices, materials, analytic)`): suns first, then spheres, then triangles. Analytic entries have `position_area.w` −2 (sun: unit direction towards it in xyz, 1 − cos of its angular radius in `edge1_cdf.x`, computed as 2·sin²(α/2) for precision) or −1 (sphere: centre, radius). Lights of zero power get no entry (and a sphere's triangles then none either); `lighting::sun_count(&emitters)` counts the leading suns. Every triangle of a sphere's mesh maps (`emitter_lookup`) to the sphere's entry, so BSDF hits on the mesh get the sphere's pdf. Powers: sphere 4πr²·lum, spot triangles area·lum·½(sin²outer + sin²inner) (∫cos over the cone / π), suns lum·π·sin²α × a nominal 50-unit area. `Gpu::sun_count` goes to the constants as `lights.x` (the old unused `light_position_size`).
- **Shader:** `cone_sample` (uniform in the cone's solid angle, pdf 1/(2π·om); it draws 1 − cos and takes the sine from it, since a float cosine near 1 has too few steps for a 0.05° sun), `sphere_om` (1 − cos of the half angle a sphere covers, written s²/(1 + √(1 − s²)) so small far spheres don't cancel; 0 from inside), `spot()` (smoothstep on the cosine to the light's normal, on hits and in NEE). NEE on a sphere aims a shadow ray at the true sphere's near surface along the sampled direction; the inscribed mesh lies beyond it, so it never self-shadows. Misses test the first `lights.x` emitters (suns: inside when the direction's sin² to the sun, from a cross product, is below om·(2 − om)) with MIS against `(1 − p_env)·prob/(2π·om)`; hidden suns only from bounce 1. `emitter_pdf` handles spheres.
- **UI:** *Kind* combo, Diameter / Angle / Size by kind, Spread and Blend for spots, rotation in the main grid for spots and suns. *+ Light* is a menu of the kinds (`editor::new_light`: a bulb inside the Cornell volume, a spot above the stage, a sun from 35° / −30° at intensity 1). Gizmo outlines (`gizmo::light_outlines`) are polygons: rectangles, 32-point ellipses, a view-facing circle for spheres, a small disk for suns, plus a stroke along the direction for spots and suns; `polygon_hit` picks any of them. Scaling a sphere scales its diameter on every handle.
- **Checked:** MIS vs NEE-only vs BSDF-only means (480×300, raw native, open floor, sky 0): sphere 1.0000 / 1.0000, disk 0.9999 / 0.9998, spot 1.0000 / 1.0006 (4096 spp), sun 1.0000 / 0.9986 at 20° (block differences +0.15% ± 0.14%, noise); a 3° sun's BSDF-only image is 0.5% low at 4096 spp and a 1° sun's 5% low at 2048 but 0.14% at 8192: the heavy tail of hitting a small source. RR renders of a bulb and a spot in the Cornell box and a sun on the open floor look right.
- **Self-test 283–284:** one light of each kind (outlines on, the spot's inspector open; the per-frame count check covers their triangles and emitter entries), then asserts one sun in the table and keeps only the sun with the room open. Scene files start at 285 (449 frames).
- Not done: see roadmap track 7.

## UI leftovers of item 3 (2026-11-02)

- **Capabilities:** `Gpu::capabilities()` returns a `Backend { available, reason }` for RR, DLSS SR, FSR and XeSS. Startup reasons (`init_reasons`) come from the adapter name (not NVIDIA), the NGX init code, the NGX caps bits, and the bridges' return codes (1 = DLL missing next to the exe, 2 = missing export, XeSS 0x1xx = init result); `rr_failed` / `sr_failed` add "failed while rendering". The RR checkbox, the Upscaler combo and the Quality bundles show the reason on hover. Self-test 231: each backend is available exactly when its reason is empty.
- **Modified dots:** `crate::slider` compares the value with `reset_value` (the same field in the scene as loaded, or the `UiState` default) and draws an accent dot in an 8 px slot it always reserves, so rows don't shift; clicking the dot does what a double-click does. Tolerance 1e-6 relative.
- **File pickers:** LUT and reference EXR get *Browse…*; settings files go through `settings_dialog` (`Actions::save` / `load` now carry the path, the quick buttons pass ./settings.json; loading pushes an undo checkpoint).

## UI phases 3–5 (2026-10-30)

- **Preferences** (`prefs.rs`): `%APPDATA%\Caustica\prefs.json` beside the stats: `favorites` and `recent` (≤ 12) scene files by `prefs::key` (path relative to the scenes folder, forward slashes), `zoom` (egui zoom, Ctrl +/−, applied at startup), `advanced`, `hint_seen`. Loaded and written only in visible runs (`UiState::prefs_live`, set where the stats load); `save_prefs` writes on each change; `sync_prefs` (every frame in `App::ui`) follows zoom and advanced and draws the first-run hint (bottom centre, *Got it*).
- **Scenes:** the Scenes window has All / Favorites / Recent lists (`scene_list`; Recent sorts by recency) and a *Favorite* checkbox; loading a file (`load_scene_file`, `open_scene`) pushes it to the recent list. Menu and keys: *Open scene…* (Ctrl+O, rfd; a file in the list loads through `load_scene_file`, others through `scenes::load_into`), *Save scene* (Ctrl+S: overwrites the loaded file when it is a non-generated user scene, else asks), *Save scene as…* (Ctrl+Shift+S, defaults to `scenes/user/<slug>.json`; the file name becomes the title). `scenes::save_to` writes any path (keeping an overwritten file's tags); `save_user` uses it. After a save the list refreshes, `reset_baseline` follows and missing thumbnails render. `--preset` takes a slug of the preset name (`scenes::slug`, e.g. `mirror-corridor`) as well as a number. The settings.json save / load entries stay in the menu.
- **Quality bundles** (`ui::Bundle`, top bar): Labels (`Bundle::label`): Realtime 67%, Best 100% / Good 67% / Fast 50% / Fastest 33% (RR), Native denoiser 100%, Native denoiser 67% (67% with the first available of DLSS SR, XeSS, FSR, else bicubic), Accurate, Reference (no denoiser). `Bundle::of(&Settings)` derives the current one (else "Custom quality"); `apply` writes `denoiser`, `scale`, `native_aa` and the upscaler, and also Realtime's sampling ("Realtime preset (B2)") and Accurate's quality fields, which leaving Accurate clears ("Accurate quality bundle"). It reads Realtime's marker before Accurate's set or clear changes `bounces` and `restir`, so Realtime's specular budget never carries into Accurate or past it (`realtime_then_accurate_drops_the_specular_budget`). RR rows are disabled with `rr_status` when `rr_available()` is false. Unit test `quality_bundles_round_trip`.
- **Per-pass GPU time:** 5 timestamps (start, after trace, after reconstruction, after display, after UI; `TIMESTAMPS`), resolved after the UI draw. `Gpu::pass_ms` = trace, reconstruct (native filter, RR or upscaler), display, UI; `gpu_ms` is still start → display. The status bar shows them on hover, inline with advanced settings, and an estimate of the time to the sample cap (samples per second smoothed over ~1 s; `UiState::sample_rate`).
- **High-resolution still** is a one-frame `video::Job` (`Options::still`: the frame goes to `path` itself, PNG or linear EXR with the new *Linear EXR* row), up to 8192 on the long side (size 1–6× the window), 16–65536 spp; the progress window says "Rendering image". `UiState::video_pending` starts either kind of export. Self-test 172 asserts frame 170's still finished.
- **Toasts with Show in folder:** `UiState::toast_file(text, path)` (PNG, EXR, HDR PNG, exports, scene saves; 8 s) adds a button that runs `explorer /select,<path>` (`ui::show_in_folder`; folders open directly).
- **A/B split:** while comparing, a 12-point strip over the split line is draggable (`overlay`, ResizeHorizontal cursor, grip dot); `split` is display-only.
- **Top bar** (ui-batch-A): Hide panel and ? are laid out first in a right-to-left row; the rest wraps in what is left, in labelled clusters (Render on, Scene, Image, Playback, Tools) via `chrome::cluster(ui, name, width)`, which starts a new row when the group's estimated width does not fit, so a group never splits. The widths are estimates (changeable in the calls). Not visually checked on Windows. **Status bar** order, first to clip last: state / spp / ETA, resolution, HDR, pipeline (amber fallback), GPU ms, object counts. **Overlays** (transform header, toasts, first-run hint) use small anchor offsets plus `constrain_to(ctx.available_rect())` so they sit inside the panels; Hide panel still hides the status bar and timeline with the side panel (changeable). **First-run hint** has a second line: scenes, Look tab, F12, Tab.
- **Self-test 281–282:** a scene saved with `scenes::save_to` to the temp folder is opened (title and exposure checked, first in the recent list), the Recent and Favorites lists, a file toast, the A/B handle and the Native and Best bundles are drawn. Scene files started at 283 then (285 since track 7).
- Not done: the preset registry refactor (slugs only); thumbnails of material balls and lights; lens shift to centre the subject in the free viewport; GPU silhouette outlines from the id buffer; a capabilities struct with per-backend reasons (the bundles use `rr_status` and `upscaler_available`); file name templates for exports; the "what the denoiser is doing" line.

## Viewport interaction (UI phase 2, 2026-10-30)

- **Commands** (`ui::Command`, `App::run_command`): Duplicate (`UiState::duplicate_selected`: objects and area lights, offset copy named `… copy`, portal unpaired, selected), Hide (`toggle_hidden_selected`: camera visibility), ShowAll, Delete, Frame (`frame_selection`: `focus_object` for an object, else `frame_objects`), FrameScene, ResetCamera (`reset_camera`: yaw / pitch / distance / target / fov from `reset_baseline`, the scene as loaded), Move / Rotate / Scale (modal transforms). Each edit checkpoints for undo.
- **Keys** (`window_event`): Ctrl+D, Shift+D (duplicate, then a modal move), H, Alt+H (`App::alt`), Home, Page Up / Down (`step_scene`, shared with ◀ ▶), F1 (`shortcuts_window`, three groups side by side from `SHORTCUTS`), F frames the selection. The Ctrl / Shift D arms come before Alt+D (denoiser; plain D now only toasts it). Esc closes the menu before it deselects.
- **Mouse:** middle drag pans. Double-click: a left click within 400 ms and 6 px of the last (`last_click`) sets `frame_pick`, and the pick result frames that object. A short right click (< 3 px of drag, not over egui) picks under the pointer and sets `UiState::context_menu` (logical position); `App::context_menu` draws it as a foreground `Area` until an item, a press outside or Esc. A right click on empty space keeps the selection. Outliner rows open the same entries through egui's `context_menu`, queued in `UiState::pending` and run by `context_menu` in the same frame.
- **Outlines:** `geometry::object_corners` (the mesh's local AABB, cached by mesh pointer, through the object transform) → `gizmo::draw_box_outline` (convex hull of the projected corners; nothing when a corner is behind the camera). The selection is drawn in the accent colour, the hovered object in faint white. Hover comes from the GPU probe: while the pointer is over the viewport (no drag, transform or menu, perspective camera) `g.probe` is set every frame and `ui.hovered` = probe id − 6 when that is an object (ids are material + 1; objects are materials 5…).
- **Tests:** unit test `the_hull_drops_inner_points`; self-test 279–280 runs duplicate (count and selection), hide, show all, delete, Home (yaw back to the baseline) and Page Down + Page Up (same scene name), drawing the menu, hover outline and sheet. Scene files started at 281 then (283 since phases 3–5).
- Shift+D moves only when a copy was made (the main light has none); Ctrl / Shift+D never reach the denoiser key (Alt+D). The menu closes on a press outside its area (`contains_pointer`, so its own buttons still click).
- Not done: Alt-drag duplicate (Shift+D covers it); an outline disappears while any box corner is behind the camera (close-up walls); hover and outlines for lights (they have their own outlines) and for non-perspective cameras; outlines are boxes, not the silhouette (GPU outline from the id buffer is phase 5); the probe readback runs every hovered frame (one texel).

## Gizmos (roadmap item 3b, 2026-10-30)

- **State** in `UiState`: `tool` (Select / Move / Rotate / Scale, default Move), `local` (Global / Local), `hover` (handle under the pointer, from `overlay`), `transform` (running), `header`, and `undo` / `redo` stacks of `Settings` (≤ 100). `checkpoint` is called before deletes, adds, duplicates and at the end of a transform (with the original); `checkpoint_scene` before anything that replaces the scene. See "Undo and the last session".
- **Input** (`main.rs` `window_event`):
  - Left press on a hovered handle starts a handle transform (no orbit); release finishes it.
  - G / R / S start a modal transform when an object or light is selected and the pointer is over the viewport. R therefore only restarts when nothing is selected.
  - During a modal transform: left click confirms, right click cancels; keys go to `App::transform_key` (X / Y / Z, Shift for planes, digits, `.`, `-` toggles sign, Backspace, Enter, Esc).
  - Pointer deltas accumulate into `Transform::mouse`, ×0.1 with Shift. Ctrl snaps (0.1 units, 15°, 0.1×).
  - A short click picks objects and light quads (`gizmo::pick`); empty space deselects.
  - Ctrl+Z, and Ctrl+Y or Ctrl+Shift+Z, undo and redo.
- **Math:** an axis move uses the axis's on-screen direction (so the pivot follows the pointer, checked to 3 px); plane and free moves intersect pointer rays with the plane through the pivot; rotation is the screen angle around the projected pivot, with the sign flipped for axes facing away; scale is the ratio of pointer distances from the pivot. Rotations compose as quaternions and go back to YXZ Euler (objects: yaw = `rotation`, `tilt` = pitch / roll; area lights: `rotation` = [pitch, yaw, roll]). Per-axis scale uses local axes: X `scale_xz[0]`, Y `stretch`, Z `scale_xz[1]`, free = `size`. Main light: X scales width keeping depth, Z changes `light_aspect`; no rotation.
- **Drag cost** (release, 1440×900, `--drag-test`, frame ms): Classic 4.6 → 6.6, bunny 4.7 → 20, chess 4.9 → 22.
  - `geometry()` rebuilt every object (about 11 ms for 600k vertices), so it now caches each object's mesh per slot (`OBJECT_CACHE`, keyed by the `Object` and its material id). That saved about 4 ms.
  - The rest was new upload, BLAS and scratch allocations, plus the whole-scene BLAS build. Instancing (core track 4) fixed it: drags now rebuild only the TLAS (see "Instancing").
- Capture CLI: `--ui-tool`. Self-test 198–202: each tool drawn on object 0 and the main light, then a keyed G X 0.5 Enter (asserts +0.5 and that the transform ended), then undo (asserts the move is gone).
- Not done: see the list under roadmap item 3b. Object snapping, pivots, multi-select, Alt-drag and vector entry came on 2026-11-02 ("Multi-select, pivots and snapping"); cheap drags came with instancing.

## Multi-select, pivots and snapping (roadmap 3b leftovers, 2026-11-02)

- **Selection:** `UiState::selection` stays the active item; `also` holds the rest. `targets()` = active (if an object, area light or the main light) then `also`. `select` (plain click), `toggle` (Shift+click: add and make active, or remove), `select_many` (box, Ctrl+A). `tidy_selection` runs each frame: it drops `also` when the active item is not transformable or when the object / light counts changed since `also` was set (`also_counts`; undo or a load moved the indices). Commands that change counts themselves (duplicate, delete) update `also_counts`.
- **Input:** a left click records `pick_toggle` = Shift; the pick result toggles instead of selecting, and a Shift+click on empty space keeps the selection. A right click on part of the selection keeps it all (its menu acts on the group). Box select: B arms the next left drag, and the Select tool always drags a box (not while physics runs, where a drag grabs); `box_start` (points) is drawn in the overlay, the camera does not orbit meanwhile, a drag under 3 px is an ordinary click; `App::box_select` takes objects (visible), area lights (on) and the main light whose origin projects inside. Ctrl+A selects visible objects and lit area lights; Alt+A and Esc select nothing.
- **Transforms:** `gizmo::Transform` holds `targets` (active first; its axes orient the gizmo), `pivot` and `cursor`. `group_frame` places the gizmo at the median (also for individual origins), the active origin or the cursor. Move translates every target; rotate and scale change each target and, unless the pivot is *Individual origins*, swing / stretch its origin about the pivot (scale only along the constrained axes). `restore` puts every target back. The main light alone still has no rotate gizmo; in a group it only orbits the pivot.
- **3D cursor:** Shift+right-click reads the probe at the click (id and view depth, as picking does) and `View::surface_point` turns it into a world point; a miss leaves the cursor. Drawn as a red and white ring while it is the pivot.
- **Snapping** (`src/snap.rs`): with Ctrl and a *Snap* target other than Increment, a move casts the pointer ray on the CPU against every visible object except the moved ones and the room (wall and floor triangles, material ids 0–3). Surface: the group's lowest corner along the hit normal (object boxes from `object_corners`, light origins) rests on the hit point. Vertex / Edge: the pivot goes to the nearest corner of the hit triangle, or the nearest point on its edges. Axis and plane constraints keep only their part of the snapped move. A miss falls back to the unsnapped move.
- **Typed vector:** in a free or plane move, digits go to axis `field`; Tab stores it in `vector` and moves on, skipping a plane's excluded axis. The header shows `X 1, Y 2|, Z 0`.
- **Alt-drag:** a left press on a gizmo handle with Alt runs `duplicate_selected(s, false)` (copies in place, selected) before the handle transform starts.
- **Tests:** unit tests `a_group_turns_around_its_median_or_in_place` (90° about the median swings ±0.5 onto the z axis; individual origins stay put; 2× scale doubles the gap; restore), `a_free_move_takes_a_typed_vector`, `snapping_finds_the_floor_corners_and_edges`, `the_moved_object_is_skipped`. Self-test 233 (typed group move of two objects, in-place duplicate of both, undo) and 234 (select all, delete, undo).

## Scene files (roadmap item 4, 2026-10-30)

- `scenes/builtin/` holds 42 files plus `pack.json`, **generated by `tools/build_scenes.py`**: edit scenes there, never the JSON.
  - Helpers mirror the presets: `obj`, `float_at` (non-grounded, y is the mesh center), `light` with `aim()` (pitch/yaw so the downward quad faces a target), `kelvin()`, `studio()` and `dark_stage()`.
  - `check()` mirrors `validate()` ranges and fails with a list. `obj()` moves excess `stretch` / `scale_xz` into `size` (same world size) and wraps rotation to ±180.
  - Files are sparse (omitted fields take `Settings::default()`). Objects are always written in full because `Object::default()` is the tall block (stretch 1.7, rotation 18).
- `tools/scene_sheet.py` renders every scene (480×300, 48 frames, RR) into `captures/scenes/sheet.png`. All 42 were checked by eye and retuned.
- **Engine fix found by the scenes:** a light that is off (main `light_on`, area `enabled`) used to keep its unlit quad in the BLAS: black in reflections, and a shadow caster. `geometry()` now skips those quads, `geometry_key` includes `light_on` / `enabled`, and gizmo outlines and picking skip them too.
- Scene title = `scene_name`. The top-bar picker shows it, and the Scene tab shows the file's description. ◀ ▶ step through files while a file scene is loaded.
- Scenes window (`UiState::show_scenes`): search, tag chips, list grouped by pack, details, Load, "Save to scenes/user".
- CLI: `--scene`, `--ui-scenes` (opens the window for captures).
- Self-test frames 204+: load each listed scene file and assert the title; the per-frame GPU count checks cover each scene's geometry.
- **Thumbnails and licences (2026-10-30, owner request):**
  - `gpu.rs` supports any egui texture. `gui_textures` maps `TextureId` → texture, heap slot (32..512, `free_slots`) and a CPU copy for partial updates. `update_gui_textures` replaces `update_atlas`; `delta.free` is applied after `draw_gui`; `draw_gui` binds root table 4 per mesh texture. The heap grew from 64 to 512 descriptors.
  - `scenes::Entry` has `hash` (FNV-1a of the file), `credits` and `license` (from `pack.json`: `license`, `commercial_use`, `attribution`, `source`). `Entry::thumbnail()` = `scenes/thumbnails/<pack dir>--<stem>-<hash>.png` (gitignored).
  - `scenes::render_thumbnails(entries)` runs this exe headless per scene on a thread (320×200, 32 frames, `CREATE_NO_WINDOW`, written as `.partial.png` then renamed, stale hashes removed) and reports each result on a channel. The Scenes window starts it for missing thumbnails on first open, never when `hidden`. `update_thumbnails` loads at most 4 PNGs per frame into egui textures. `--make-thumbnails` does the same from the CLI and exits.
  - Cards are painted at a fixed 160×100 (egui's `ImageButton` padding made the grid uneven). The selected scene shows a 256×160 preview, licence badge, tags, attribution and credits.
  - **Rule (owner, 2026-11-10): a scene change re-renders or removes its thumbnail.** The name carries the FNV-1a of the scene file, so an edited scene has no thumbnail under its new name; `scenes::sync_thumbnails` (run by `request_thumbnails` and `--make-thumbnails`) first removes every scene thumbnail in the cache that no listed scene names (older hashes of an edited scene, deleted scenes; `remove_stale`), then the missing ones are rendered, and a finished render removes the other hashes of its scene (`remove_older`). Never show or ship a thumbnail whose hash differs from its scene's. **Render version (2026-11-10):** the hash also covers `scenes::RENDER_VERSION` (FNV-1a over its 4 little-endian bytes, then the file). Bump it in the same change whenever the engine alters a scene's image without the file changing (exact sun/moon astronomy, the 4 mm glass lift, shader fixes); every older thumbnail then has a stale name, `remove_stale` deletes it and the missing ones re-render, and an older bundled set is never found. Bumped to 2 (the astronomy, glass lift, shader fixes and the catalogue stars of night physical-sky scenes: one bump covers them all), then to 3 for E8 (the built-ins became auto exposure scenes and the night sky map is stored scaled up). Changeable default: a manual bump, not derived from a shader hash. Test `the_render_version_is_part_of_the_thumbnail_hash`. Tests: `a_changed_scene_has_a_different_thumbnail_name`, `stale_cached_thumbnails_are_removed`, `a_rendered_thumbnail_replaces_the_older_ones_of_its_scene`. The hash covers the scene file only, not the meshes or textures it names; changing those means touching the file or *Re-render thumbnails*.
  - **Bundled thumbnails (2026-11-10, owner request):** releases ship `thumbnails\` beside the exe (installer, zip, AppImage) so a fresh install doesn't render for minutes. `caustica --make-thumbnails --thumbnails-out DIR` renders the shipped scenes (not `user`) into DIR (`dirs::set_cache_override`) and the material presets' balls into `DIR/materials` (`balls::render_presets`); `tools/make_installer.ps1` runs it with the staged exe, `tools/release.ps1` copies the result to the VM and `tools/appimage.sh` packs it (or renders there if absent, or warns). `dirs::bundled_thumbnails()` is that folder (read-only). `sync_thumbnails` copies a bundled scene thumbnail of the same file name into the cache (`seed_from`; the name has the hash, so a changed or new scene finds none and renders its own); balls fall back to reading `bundled/materials/v1-<hash of the material JSON>.png` (`balls::load`). A bundled file whose hash no longer matches is simply never looked up. Tests: `a_bundled_thumbnail_is_used_when_the_hash_matches`, `a_bundled_thumbnail_is_ignored_when_the_scene_changed`, `bundled_balls_are_found_by_material_hash`. Bump `balls::VERSION` when the ball stage changes. The ball stage and the thumbnails are rendered by the platform that builds the installer (D3D12); the AppImage reuses them. Unverified: the release scripts end to end (Windows, Inno Setup, the VM).
  - Self-test 203 loads a generated texture as entry 0's thumbnail and opens the window with it selected, so the scene-file frames draw the textured UI under the debug layer.
- Relative asset paths: done with mesh import and HDRIs (paths relative to `scenes/`, see `mesh::resolve`).

## ffmpeg (2026-10-30, owner allowed pulling the binary)

- `tools/setup.ps1` `Get-Ffmpeg '9.0.2' '<sha256>'` downloads gyan.dev's essentials build from its GitHub release mirror (`GyanD/codexffmpeg`). The SHA-256 is pinned (it matched gyan.dev's published checksum on 2026-10-30). Only `ffmpeg.exe` and `LICENSE` are kept, in `vendor/ffmpeg` (gitignored with `vendor/`). A `VERSION-<ver>` marker skips repeat downloads.
- build.rs copies it next to the exe when present (optional; builds work without it). `video::ffmpeg()` prefers that copy, then PATH. The snapshot script copies it too.
- Linux port notes are listed in docs/roadmap.md under "Vulkan backend".

## Generators (core track 1, 2026-10-30)

- `generators.rs`: `generate(family, seed) -> Settings` for the 17 `FAMILIES` (the last four use procedural meshes, see "Mesh import"). RNG is xorshift64* seeded by seed and a hash of the family.
  - Helpers reuse `presets::{object, light, studio, material}` (now `pub(crate)`). `at()` places a mesh centre at a world y. `sun()` is a far quad aimed at the origin (warm 350, day 450, sky 0.2 / 0.55; brighter washed out). `frame()` is `frame_objects` × 0.78.
  - `finish()` names the scene "<family> · seed N", truncates to `MAX_OBJECTS` and runs `fit()` (excess stretch/scale into size, angles wrapped, as build_scenes.py does).
- New shape **Tetrahedron** (last in `Shape::ALL`; the mesh cache is indexed by enum order): base at y = −1, apex at +1, base circumradius √2.
- Menger level 2 merges filled cells into runs along x (fits under 256). The Sierpinski child centre is c + corner·scale/2. The sphereflake uses Haines' 6 + 3 directions, rotated per parent.
- Scene files with `"generator": {"family", "seed"}` regenerate in `scenes::read` (the title overrides the name). `scenes::save_generated` writes such a file to scenes/user. `tools/build_scenes.py` also writes `scenes/generated` (6 curated seeds × 15 families plus 3 each for forest and crowd; `GENERATED` table).
- Tests: every family × 24 seeds validates and is non-empty; generation is deterministic; Menger fits. The self-test sweeps every scene file, now 164 (437 frames).
- `tools/scene_sheet.py --pack generated` renders the pack. All 90 were checked; day cities and warm suns were dimmed, framing tightened.

## Scenes buildable today (roadmap 5e, 2026-10-30)

- `Settings.mis_mode` (0 MIS, 1 NEE only, 2 BSDF only) → `scene_info.w` bits 4–5. In the trace, mode 1 gives BSDF-found emission weight 0 (unless it came after a delta bounce) and NEE divides by `pl` alone; mode 2 skips NEE. Checked on veach-mis: the classic three images.
- `ui::seed_of_the_day()` (days since epoch × golden ratio) feeds `randomize` from the top bar's *Today* button.
- New generator scenes in `tools/build_scenes.py` use `room_shell()` (walls / ceiling / front as thin boxes) and `obj(portal=…)`:
  - `kusama-infinity-room`: 250 objects, emission 1.6, exposure 0.8; it blew out at emission 9.
  - `flavin-fluorescent` and `turrell-ganzfeld`: rounded seams are 1.6-scale cylinders in every corner and along the edges.
  - `infinite-corridor`: the end portals are paired, the near one rotated 180°.
  - `bigger-on-the-inside`: the box door is paired with the hall doorway at x = 30, which **must be rotated 180°** or the view leads back out of the hall.

## Mesh import (core track 2, 2026-10-30)

- Threads (2026-11-10): `mesh::load` holds the cache lock only for lookup and insert (`load_cached`), never across the parse; a duplicate parse by racing threads is accepted and the first insert wins. UI imports parse on a worker thread (`UiState::import_mesh`, landed by `land_mesh_imports`). See `docs/ui-review.md`, ui-batch-E.
- `mesh.rs`: `Mesh = Vec<(Vec3, Vec3)>` (position, normal per vertex, 3 per triangle), at most 20M triangles and 2 GB files. A cache maps path → `Result<Arc<Imported>, String>` (errors are cached too). `resolve` makes relative paths relative to `scenes::folder()`; `store` is the inverse. `load`, `of(&Object)`, `import_object(path)` (size 2, material from the file).
  - OBJ via tobj (triangulate, single index; MTL diffuse). glTF via `gltf::Gltf::open` + `import_buffers`, following node transforms; material from PBR plus KHR emissive_strength / transmission / ior (each needs a Cargo feature). Smooth normals when missing; normalised into −1..1.
- `Object.mesh` (≤ 1024 chars) replaces `shape` when set. `geometry.rs` uses `mesh::of(o)` in `object_frame` / `object_geometry`; a missing file falls back to the shape. The inspector shows a Mesh row (file name, "missing", *Use a shape*); the Scene tab has *+ Mesh…* (rfd file dialog).
- **Opaque split** (`Gpu::rebuild_scene`): triangles whose material needs the any-hit loop (`lobes.w` bit 1 hidden, or transmission > 0) go first as geometry 0; the rest is geometry 1, flagged OPAQUE. `opaque_offset` (count of the first range) goes in `environment.w`; `intersect` adds it when `CommittedGeometryIndex() != 0`. `geometry_key` includes transmission > 0 and light visibility for that reason. Bit-identical renders on presets 0, 14 and 17 (RMSE 0.000).
- The vertex buffer is a DEFAULT-heap buffer created COMMON (creating it COPY_DEST warned), filled by a copy from a staging upload, then NON_PIXEL_SHADER_RESOURCE.
- **GPU picking:** a click stores `pick_point`; that frame's probe also copies u19 (object id) to `probe_albedo + 768`, so `Probe` has `id`. `gizmo::pick_from_probe` maps id − 1 (4 main light, 5..5+N objects, then area lights) and still tests light quads on the CPU, nearer than the GPU hit (lights hidden from the camera are not in u19). `gizmo::pick` (CPU, every triangle) stays for tests.
- Self-test 257–259: GPU pick of object 0's projected centre; 258 imports an OBJ pyramid from the temp folder (asserts 6 triangles) plus an object with a missing mesh file.
- Unit tests: OBJ quad, glTF triangle with an embedded base64 buffer, an OBJ with two materials split into parts.
- **Parts:** a file is read as one part per material (glTF material index, OBJ `material_id`), all normalised by the file's box. `Object.mesh` = `file#n` picks part n; plain `file` is every part merged (`Imported::whole`, built lazily). `mesh::ground(o)` grounds a part by the whole file, so the parts of one file stay together. Empty parts are kept so indices never shift (terrain bands can be empty). `import_objects` (used by *+ Mesh…*) makes one object per non-empty part, named `<stem> · <material>`.
- **glTF texture averages** still set the material values (base colour, metallic B, roughness G, emissive); since core track 5 the maps themselves are sampled and scaled by value / average (see "Textures").
- `--mesh-info files…` prints each file's parts as JSON `Object`s plus triangle counts and exits. `tools/mesh_parts.json` is that output for the test models; `tools/build_scenes.py` reads it for the `scenes/meshes` pack (8 scenes: gold dragon in the Cornell box, jade Buddha, scan gallery, Spot and Suzanne, the ASWF chess set, glass dragon, toy car, duck and lantern).
- **Test models:** `tools/fetch_assets.ps1` (was fetch_models.ps1) downloads 11 models into `scenes/models/` (gitignored), pinned to a commit and SHA-256: 6 OBJ scans from alecjacobson/common-3d-test-models (xyzrgb dragon, happy Buddha, armadillo, Lucy, Spot, Suzanne) and 5 GLBs from KhronosGroup/glTF-Sample-Assets (DragonAttenuation, Duck, Lantern, ABeautifulGame, ToyCar). Lucy is Z-up (tilt −90). Without them the mesh scenes fall back to boxes. The snapshot script copies them with the scenes (about 95 MB).
- **Procedural meshes** (`procedural.rs`): `mesh: "gen:<kind>/<seed>[#part]"` builds in `mesh::read` and caches like a file (`resolve` leaves `gen:` paths alone). Kinds: `mandelbulb` (surface nets over the distance estimator, 240³ release / 80³ debug, sampled on all cores; power 5–9 and a phase twist per seed), `terrain` (384² fBm heightfield, ridged half the time, island falloff; parts sand / grass / rock / snow by height and slope, rock below normal.y 0.62), `menger` (voxels with exposed faces only; level 4 when seed % 3 == 0 in release, else 3), `tree` (recursive branching: 6-sided frusta; leaves are single-sided pointed ovals of 8 triangles folded along the midrib (`leaf`), since the shader turns normals toward the ray; parts bark / leaves / leaves (tint), about a third of the leaves in the third part so generators give it a second colour). The generator families `mandelbulb`, `terrain` (plus a sea box: stretch 0.065 puts it at height 0), `deep-menger` and `tree` (1–7 trees, summer or autumn) use them.
- **Perf of the opaque split** (2026-10-30, 1440×900, 300 frames, median of 3 alternating runs vs the core1 snapshot): Classic −7% RR / −8% native, bunny −7 / −8%, mirror corridor −3 / −3%, chess −7 / −4%, neon −3 / −4%.

## Video export (item 6, 2026-10-30)

- **Output size apart from the window:** `Gpu::width/height` is the output size, `swap_size` the swapchain's. `resize` = `resize_swap` (buffers, RTVs, `ui_frame`) + `resize_output` (upscalers released, all UAV textures). While they differ, `render` clears the backbuffer (grey) instead of copying texture 10/26 into it, then draws egui; `draw_gui`'s viewport and scissors use `swap_size`, and its vertex positions are scaled by output / swap size because `gui.hlsl` divides by `dimensions.zw` (the output size) from the trace constants. A UI capture (`ui_frame`) uses `swap_size`. `set_hdr` resizes to `swap_size`.
- **`video.rs`:** `Options` (size, fps, frames, spp, `seconds_per_frame` limit, `Format` H.264 / H.265 / HDR10 HEVC / ProRes / PNG / EXR sequence, `quality` = CRF or ProRes profile, orbit `degrees` / `pitch` / `distance` / `Easing`, path). Video sizes round down to even. `yaw(i)`: linear = i/frames·angle (360° loops), ease in-out = smoothstep over i/(frames−1). `ffmpeg_args` (unit-tested): raw RGBA (rgb48le PQ for HDR10) on stdin, `scale=out_color_matrix=bt709|bt2020nc:out_range=tv` with matching colour tags, libx264 slow / libx265 medium (`hvc1` tag) / prores_ks yuv422p10le; HDR10 adds x265 `hdr10=1:…:max-cll=<peak>,<peak/2>`. `ffmpeg_available` is cached per run.
- **`Job`:** `start` (falls back to PNG without ffmpeg; HDR10 needs `hdr_active`) snapshots the settings with progressive full-resolution choices (scale 1, no native AA, no vsync / rotate / compare, debug view 0), the orbit's pitch and distance, `resize_output`s to the export size (back to the window's if that or the writer fails) and spawns the **writer thread** (`sync_channel(2)`: ffmpeg's stdin, PNG via `save_buffer`, EXR via `Rgb32FImage`; scRGB → PQ BT.2020 with `display::scrgb_to_pq` on that thread). ffmpeg's stderr is drained by its own thread. `after_render` ends a frame at `spp` or the time limit, reads 10 (plus 26 for HDR10, 18 for EXR), keeps a ≤ 360-px preview, queues the frame with `try_send` (a full queue keeps it in `pending`, and `waiting()` makes `redraw` draw only the UI until the encoder catches up), moves the yaw and `reset(true)`s. `finish(cancel)` joins the writer, which returns the frames actually written (cancel kills ffmpeg and deletes the video; sequence frames stay) and resizes the output back to `swap_size`. `video::render` is the blocking loop (`--turntable`, self-test 253).
- **App:** `App::export` holds the job. In `redraw`, while it runs: no auto orbit, phone remote, gamepad, HDRI or LUT reload, HDR switch, render-scale resize or dirty-key checks (edits wait); probe, zebras and scopes off; render passes of the job's settings until 30 ms have gone (each presents the same egui primitives; the textures delta only on the first), then `after_render`. egui draws only `export_window` (preview, progress bar counting the current frame's samples or time, frame / spp, elapsed and remaining, Cancel). `window_event` drops all input except close, resize (`resize_swap` only), move, modifiers and cursor moves; egui still gets it. Close cancels the export.
