- **Dialog** (`video_ui.rs`, Export tab → *Export video…*, which takes pitch and distance from the camera): format (HDR10 disabled without HDR output, videos without ffmpeg), CRF or ProRes profile, file with *Browse…* (rfd), size presets (window, 720p … 8K, square, vertical) plus width × height, fps 24–60 (keeps the length), length in seconds ↔ frames, samples per frame, time limit per frame, orbit angle / elevation / distance / easing, *Use the current camera*.
- **ffmpeg details:** the scale filter is followed by `setparams` with the same colour tags, because filtered frames carry their own properties, which win over `-color_primaries` / `-color_trc` (without it the SDR files read `bt709/unknown/unknown`). Checked with `--turntable-format h264|h265|hdr10|prores`: H.264 High and HEVC Main tagged bt709, HDR10 HEVC Main 10 bt2020nc / bt2020 / smpte2084, ProRes HQ yuv422p10le; a decoded H.264 frame matches the PNG of the same frame to 1.8 / 255 on average (a BT.601 matrix would shift hues).
- **CLI:** `--turntable-format`, `--ui-video` (opens the dialog for captures).
- **Tests:** `video::tests` (orbit, even sizes, ffmpeg arguments). Self-test 273–278: the dialog starts a 3-frame 201×121 EXR export (276 checks the frames' size and that the output is back to the window's), then a 640×360 PNG export at 4096 spp is running at 277 and cancelled; 278 checks it ended. Scene files started at 279 then (281 since UI phase 2).

## Asset browser (item 6, 2026-10-30)

- **`assets.rs`** (no egui): catalogs of Poly Haven (`api.polyhaven.com/assets?t=hdris|textures|models`, about 2400 assets, sorted by download count), ambientCG (`api/v2/full_json?type=Material`, 250 per page, about 2000, with the JPG resolutions each offers) and the Khronos glTF samples (`model-index.json`, models with a `glTF-Binary` variant, about 120). `fetch_catalog` caches each as `scenes/thumbnails/assets/catalog-<source>.json`; `cached_catalog` reads it back (offline use). Thumbnails go to the same folder as `<source>-<kind>-<id>.img` (decoded with `image::load_from_memory`; PNG / JPEG).
- **HTTP:** `ureq` 2 with `native-tls` (Windows schannel, no OpenSSL or ring build), a shared agent with a user agent and timeouts. Downloads stream into `<file>.partial` with SHA-256 and progress (`Progress`: done, total from Content-Length, cancel), then rename.
- **What a download fetches** (`plan`, `use_path`): Poly Haven HDRI → `hdri/<id>_<res>.hdr`; texture → `textures/<id>/` (diff, nor_gl, arm or Rough); model → `models/<id>/<id>_<res>.gltf` plus every `include` (bin, textures/…) at its relative path; ambientCG → the `<id>_<RES>-JPG.zip`, of which only Color / NormalGL / Roughness / Metalness JPGs are extracted (by file name, flattened) into `textures/<id>/` and the zip deleted; Khronos → `models/<file>.glb`. Paths from catalogs pass `safe_relative` (no `..`, absolute, drive or odd characters). `installed` = the `use_path` exists, so files from `tools/fetch_assets.ps1` count too.
- **Pinning:** every downloaded file's SHA-256, URL and credit go into `scenes/assets.json` (gitignored, local); a later download of the same path with another hash fails and deletes the file.
- **`browser.rs`**: the Assets window (top bar *Assets*, `--ui-assets`). Source tabs, kind tabs for Poly Haven, search over names, ids and tags, pages of 48 cards (thumbnails fetched on 4 threads only for drawn cards, at most 6 decoded per frame; a green dot marks downloaded at the chosen resolution), a detail panel with credit, tags, page link, resolution choice (default 4k for HDRIs, 2k otherwise), *Download* with a progress bar and *Cancel*, then *Use as HDRI*, *Apply to the selected object / floor / walls* (texture set via `texture::apply_averages`) or *Add to the scene* (`mesh::import_objects`, undoable). *Other sources* lists Stanford, common-3d-test-models, McGuire, Bitterli, ORCA, Smithsonian, Sketchfab with licence and link.
- **CLI:** `--fetch-asset polyhaven/hdri/meadow_2@2k khronos/model/Duck …` (prints `ASSET <path>`), `--asset-catalog poly ambient khronos` (refreshes the caches).
- **Tests:** catalog parsing for all three sources, path safety and `from_spec`, zip extraction from a zip written in the test. Self-test 272 opens the window offline (`Browser::offline`, `set_catalog` with two Khronos entries, the first selected) and leaves it open while the scene files load. Checked by hand: one download from each source (Poly Haven HDRI, texture and the Barrel_01 glTF with its textures, ambientCG Fabric086, Khronos Avocado) rendered together.

## Textures (core track 5, 2026-10-30)

- **Mesh maps** (`mesh.rs`): parts keep UVs (`Part::uv`, parallel to the mesh; glTF TEXCOORD_0, OBJ `vt` with v flipped) and `Maps { base, normal, metal_rough, cutoff }` of `Arc<Map>`. `Map` is RGBA8 plus a box-filtered mip chain down to 1×1 and per-channel averages (sRGB decoded for colour maps); maps over 8192 are halved. glTF images are decoded once per (image, colour space) on all cores (`decode_all`), 8/16-bit layouts (16-bit keeps the high byte); OBJ reads `map_Kd` and `norm`/`bump` with the `image` crate (jpeg feature added). A normal map must average near (0.5, 0.5, >0.6) (`looks_like_normals`), so grey OBJ height maps are ignored. glTF alpha MASK uses its cutoff, BLEND is treated as MASK at 0.5; the cutoff is divided by the base factor's alpha. `mesh::maps(o)` returns a part's maps when `o.material.textures` (a whole multi-part file has none); `has_maps` ignores the flag.
- **Values scale the maps:** the importer keeps colour = factor × average (as before), and the GPU multiplies each map by value / average (`base_scale`, `surface_scale`). So an imported mesh looks exactly as authored, a slider edit tints or shifts the map, and *Texture maps* off (`Material::textures`, default true, also in `geometry_key` for opacity) shows the averages, which is what older builds rendered. Old scene files therefore get their textures without changes.
- **Texture sets** (`texture.rs`): `Material::texture` (a path, ≤ 1024 chars) and `texture_scale` (repeats per unit, 0.01–100, default 0.5). The path is an image or any map of a set; the set is every image with the same prefix (`prefix`: the name up to the first map word), recognised by name: Color / BaseColor / Albedo / diff (base), NormalGL / nor_gl (normal), NormalDX / nor_dx (green flipped), Roughness, Metalness, and Poly Haven's ARM (G roughness, B metal, used as is). Roughness and metalness are packed into one map (G, B). Cached per path, failures too. *Load…* in the material editor sets colour, roughness and metal to the averages (`apply_averages`). A set wins over a mesh's own maps; walls (records 0–2) and floor (3) take sets too, and then use their own material colour instead of left / right / base (`lighting::materials`).
- **GPU** (`Gpu::attach_maps`, called from `update_materials`): each map is uploaded once (all mips through one staging buffer, RGBA8, an `_SRGB` view for colour maps) into a free slot and keyed by its `Arc` address (the Arc is held); maps no object uses any more are released after `wait_idle`. `map_count()` for the self-test.
- **Shader:** `intersect` computes UVs and a per-triangle tangent from the UV derivatives (glTF's stored MikkTSpace tangents are not read) only for materials with flag 16, then samples the maps there (`sample_maps`): fewer registers are live than in the bounce loop. `Hit` carries `albedo`, `rough_metal` and `textured`; `apply_maps` copies them into the material after the emission is taken (emission stays flat). The bitangent is −dP/dv (glTF v runs down the image); checked on Khronos NormalTangentMirrorTest (all 15 spheres match the geometry column). Texture sets are box-mapped along the normal's dominant axis: static geometry in world units, objects in their own axes at world size (`CommittedObjectRayOrigin` scaled by the transform's column lengths). Masked materials (flag 8) are FORCE_NON_OPAQUE instances and `alpha_hole` tests the base alpha at LOD 0 in both any-hit loops (checked on Khronos AlphaBlendModeTest). Ray offsets use the interpolated normal `ng`, shading the normal-mapped `n`.
- **Mip level from ray cones:** `cone_width` / `cone_spread` start at one pixel (orthographic: a pixel's width, no spread), grow along each segment, and each rough bounce adds 0.5·α (specular) or 0.5 (diffuse) to the spread. The footprint at a hit is the width / |cos| (capped at 20×) × texture units per world unit (UV area over world area per triangle, or the box-mapping scale); level = log2(footprint × texels).
- **Two path-tracer variants** (build.rs and hotreload.rs list them): `trace` without map code and `trace_maps` with `-D TEXTURE_MAPS`; `Gpu::textured` (any material with flag 16) picks one per frame. The map code alone cost untextured scenes about 7% even when never run (registers), so they now run the variant without it.
- **Perf** (1440×900, 300 frames, median of alternating runs vs the item-6 snapshot): untextured scenes unchanged (classic −0.1%, gold dragon +0.3%); the chess set with its 33 maps +16% (it rendered flat averages before; +33% before mips and ray cones); the textured showroom +12%.
- **Assets:** `tools/fetch_assets.ps1` adds 6 ambientCG sets (2K JPG zips pinned by SHA-256, only Color / NormalGL / Roughness / Metalness extracted: Bricks097, Fabric062, Marble016, Metal032, Tiles074, WoodFloor041) and 2 Poly Haven sets (laminate_floor_02, rock_wall_08, with ARM maps) into `scenes/textures/<id>/` (gitignored, hard-linked by the snapshot script, now recursively). `tools/build_scenes.py` `textured(folder, scale)` reads the averages with PIL; `scenes/materials` has 4 scenes (showroom, brick Cornell box, rock garden, duck on wood).
- **Tests:** unit tests for a textured glTF quad written by `mesh::write_test_gltf` (UVs, maps, averages, cutoff, flag), mips and halving, map names and prefixes, a folder set with a DirectX normal map. Self-test 269–271: the quad uploads 2 maps; switching *Texture maps* off releases them. (272 is the Assets window.)

## Animation and timeline (track 8, 2026-10-31)

- **Data** (`anim.rs`, `Settings::animation`, skipped in files while default): `Animation { length, fps, shutter, tracks }`, `Track { path, keys }`, `Key { time, value: f64, interp }`, `Interp` Smooth (Fritsch–Carlson monotone cubic: never overshoots the keys, so keyed values stay inside `validate()`; zero slope at the ends) / Linear / Ease (smoothstep) / Hold, per segment from its key. A track's `path` is a JSON pointer without the leading slash into the serialized `Settings` ("objects/2/position/0"). Only floats animate (`is_f64` in the JSON: u32 / i32 / enums / strings / bools never), minus `NOT_ANIMATED` (scale, HDR levels, filter width, split, rotate speed, animation). `Animation::apply(s, t)` serializes, writes each track's value, deserializes and validates (on failure `s` comes back unchanged; missing paths are skipped). `removed(list, index)` drops and renumbers tracks when `delete_selected` removes an object or light. `validate()` bounds length 0.1–3600 s, fps 1–240, shutter 0–1, sorted finite keys. `animation` is out of `transport_key` (its effect arrives through the fields).
- **Playhead** (`anim::Timeline` in `App::timeline`, not saved): `advance` while playing (loops or stops), `update` applies the animation only when the time or the keys changed since the last call, so an edit at a still playhead stays until it moves (as in Blender) and `rest` (the settings as last applied) tells what was edited. Run in `redraw` after egui, skipped during an export.
- **Keying:** `key_selection` (I) keys, under the selection's `prefixes` (object / light subtree, or the camera's or main light's top-level fields from `entity`), every float that already has a track or differs from `rest`, and on the entity's first key its transform list; `key_changed` (Shift+I) keys every float that differs from `rest`; Alt+I `remove_keys`. Each is an undo checkpoint and resets `rest` / `applied` to the keyed state.
- **UI** (`timeline_ui.rs`): `timeline_panel` (bottom panel above the status bar while `UiState::show_timeline`, which a scene gaining keys turns on (`gained_keys`, whatever replaced the settings) and the top-bar *Timeline* toggles; closed on a keyed scene it reads *Timeline •*, and playback and K work without it): |◀, previous key, Play (K), next key, ▶|, time, frame, Loop, Key, Delete key, interpolation of the selection's keys at the playhead, then length, fps, shutter (as an angle) and Clear; `track_strip` paints seconds, every key faint, the selection's keys in the accent colour and the playhead, and scrubs (snapped to frames unless Shift). Shift+Left / Right step a frame. `about_to_wait` keeps polling while playing.
- **Export** (`video::Options::timeline`, dialog *Motion → Timeline*, enabled when there are keys): `Job::start` takes fps and frames from the animation (`Animation::frames`), keeps the camera (no orbit overrides), stores `base` (the settings before animation) and the GPU scene's `geometry` / `materials` keys; `animate(g, t)` applies the keys and rebuilds the BLAS / TLAS or materials when they changed. Frame i is t = i / fps. **Motion blur** (shutter > 0): `before_render` (called before every pass, also in `video::render`) sets `g.keep_exposure` (no reset on scene changes, no RR) and moves to t_i + (u − 0.5)·shutter / fps with u stepped by the golden ratio per pass; `after_render` turns keep-exposure off before the read-back. `finish` turns it off and rebuilds the user's scene when the job changed it (`scene_changed`). RR is therefore off for motion-blurred exports; orbit exports are unchanged.
- **Showcase:** `scenes/builtin/pool-break-1984.json` (`pool_break()` in build_scenes.py, with a `track()` helper): the cue strikes, the cue ball runs into the rack, three balls scatter; 1 s at 24 fps, 180° shutter.
- **Tests:** `anim::tests` (interpolations and no overshoot, apply skips ints / missing paths / invalid results, keying and index fixups). Self-test 146–149: object 0 keyed at 0 s and 1 s through `key_selection`, 10 tracks and the midpoint at 0.5 s; a two-frame 160×100 EXR timeline export with a 180° shutter; 149 checks both frames and clears the animation (the per-frame GPU count check covers the restored scene).

## Physical sky (track 9, 2026-10-31)

- **Settings** (`sky::PhysicalSky`, skipped in files while default; all transport fields; every float keyable): `on`, `time` (solar hours), `day` (1–366), `latitude`, `north` (sky rotation, degrees; 0 = north at −z, east +x), `turbidity` (1–10; aerosol density × (turbidity − 1), 2 = Hillaire's clear day), `intensity` (the sun above the atmosphere lights a white surface to this, as a sun light of that intensity), `sun_size` (0.53°), `ground` (albedo below the horizon), `visible`. While on it replaces the HDRI and the uniform `sky` light.
- **Sun position:** Cooper declination, hour angle 15°·(time − 12), local frame (east, up, −north). `sun_direction()` rotates by −north about +y to match the shader's φ = atan2(x, −z) − rotation (unit test).
- **Atmosphere** (km, Hillaire 2020 constants; observer 50 m up): Rayleigh (8 km), Mie (1.2 km, g 0.8, Cornette-Shanks), ozone tent (25 ± 15 km). Tables: transmittance 64 altitude rows (squared spacing) × 256 cosines (s·|s| spacing, dense at the horizon), earth shadow below each altitude's horizon; Hillaire's Ψ_ms 32 × 32 (64 Fibonacci directions, ground albedo 0.3). Sky radiance: 40 quadratic steps (12 for rays into the ground), single scattering (Rayleigh + Mie phase) plus Ψ_ms × σs. `Atmosphere::bare` (no tables) serves `sun()`, which `lighting::materials` calls every frame.
- **Bake** (`sky::bake`, 1024 × 512 release / 512 × 256 debug; tables and texels on all cores through `sky::parallel`; about 63 ms release, 640 ms debug, printed as `SKY_BAKE_MS` by the map test): upper half first, its irradiance plus the sun's lights the ground term of the lower half; a faint night floor keeps the CDF build from failing. Units: sun irradiance π·intensity outside the atmosphere. Goes through `environment::build` and `Gpu::set_environment`; the constants use strength 1 and rotation = north (`gpu.rs`), so North never re-bakes. `PhysicalSky::map_key` lists the fields the map depends on.
- **Environment slot:** `sky::environment_key(s)` (map key or HDRI path) replaces the old `hdri_loaded` compare in `redraw`; `sky::upload_environment` bakes, loads or clears. `video::Job` keeps its own key: `animate` re-bakes when a keyed sky moves (every motion-blur pass), `finish` restores the user's environment when it changed (best-effort, after the reset).
- **Sun light:** `lighting::materials` appends one record right after the area lights while the sky is on (`lighting::sun_material` = 5 + objects + lights, before portal records): colour = transmittance-weighted radiance / its max, emission = that max (0 below the horizon or at intensity 0), `secondary.xyz` = the sun direction (unused by the shader; it makes North change `material_key`). `lighting::analytic` puts the sky's sun first when it is up, so it counts in `lights.x`. Radiance = T·intensity / sin²(radius), as `AreaLight::radiance` for suns.
- **UI:** Stage section, *Physical sky* (`ui::physical_sky_ui`): time as HH:MM, date as "21 Jun", latitude, north, turbidity, sun intensity and size, ground, seen by camera, *Key a day* (linear `physical_sky/time` keys 06:00 → 20:00 over the timeline's length; no undo checkpoint). HDRI strength / rotation rows hide while the sky is on.
- **Scenes:** `sunlit-room-day-cycle` (room with an east window, keyed 06:30–19:30 over 12 s) and `sky-garden-dusk`, from `tools/build_scenes.py` (`sky()`, `sunlit_room()`; `check()` covers the sky ranges).
- **Tests:** `sky::tests` (sun path at 45° N in June and December, north turns sun and map together, reddening and setting, a finite sun record that follows north at intensity 0, a blue sky brighter towards the sun with a plausible sky irradiance, a night bake). Self-test 126–128: sky on at noon (map loaded, one extra sun), night (map loaded, no sky sun), off (unloaded).

## Diffraction grating (track 11 part 4, 2026-11-01)

- **Model** (`pathtrace.hlsl`, after `spec_probability`): grooves along the brush direction T (`aniso_frame`, so `anisotropy_rotation` and *Spun* place them: circles for a CD); order o of wavelength λ leaves where the tangential part of l is the mirror direction's moved by o·λ/spacing along B. Each order is the base GGX lobe (isotropic: an anisotropic grating keeps its anisotropy in the mirror part only) translated on the projected disk: f_o(l) = f(l0), with l0 = l moved back (`grating_move`), because f·cos·dω is f times projected area, which the move keeps. Orders ±1 take 0.35 of the strength each, ±2 0.15; an order whose mirror direction would leave past the horizon (evanescent; depends on v and λ only, `order_shares`) gives its share back to the mirror reflection, so a Blu-ray reflects plainly except at grazing views.
- **Sampling:** in the specular branch a grating material first picks an order by those shares, then moves an isotropic GGX sample by it; pdf_o(l) = pdf(l0)·cos l / cos l0, so an order sample weighs what a mirror sample does. `eval_bsdf` mixes the mirror's and the orders' pdfs, so MIS stays exact. Before this, orders reached by next-event estimation only: in a furnace (no lights) the grating spheres were dark and full of fireflies.
- **Wavelength:** one per grating hit (`grating_wavelength`, from a hash of pixel, sequence index and bounce, not the sampler's dimensions, so other materials keep their sequences), shared by that hit's NEE and BSDF sample. Importance-sampled from three Gaussian channel curves of equal area (R 600 ± 40, G 545 ± 35, B 455 ± 25 nm; a flat spectrum is white), so the RGB weight is 3·N_c / ΣN, at most 3. One wavelength per path multiplied the same weight in at every grating bounce: biased (E[w²] > 1) and fireflies of 1e7 between grating spheres. A float golden-ratio sequence (`sequence_index · 0.618`) had no fraction left at those magnitudes: every pixel drew 380 nm.
- **Data:** `Material::{grating (0–1), grating_spacing (µm, 0.3–10, default 1.6)}`, packed into `optical.w` as −(strength in thousandths × 16384 + spacing in nm) (`lighting::grating_code`): lights keep 0 / 1 there for two- / one-sided emission and the shader treats negative as two-sided, so the record does not grow. UI: *Diffraction grating*, *Groove spacing*; *Brush direction* and *Spun* show for gratings too. Not in glTF (no extension).
- **Variant:** grating code compiles only into `trace_maps` (`has_grating` = `texture_maps` && optical.w < 0), which `Gpu` now picks when any material has maps or a grating (`textured`). In `trace` it cost scenes without a grating 2–15% even when never run: two inlined `eval_bsdf` copies with four order lobes each. Tried and dropped: a rolled order loop (worse), one `eval_bsdf` copy shared by NEE and the BSDF sample in a two-pass loop (worse), per-hit precompute kept in the material copy (kept 8 more values live, no gain), a slimmer order body (helped part).
- **Checks** (debug, raw native): white furnace, 12 white-metal grating spheres (spacing 1.6 / 0.74 / 0.32 µm, roughness 0.05–0.5, spun and straight): 0.87–1.04 per channel, 9 pixels above 2 (max 11.7); residual colour at the rims: a rough order lobe partly past the horizon loses that part (red, shifted farthest, most), and the compensation tuned for the mirror lobe over-boosts an order moved inward (one blue patch 1.16). `optical-discs`, light-sampling-only vs MIS: 9×9-blurred RMSE×1000 1.86, off-disc pixels agree (mean −1e-5); on the discs light-only is 3.1% darker, the same scene without gratings 5.1% (the known offset on sharp metal, roadmap Known issues).
- **Scene:** `optical-discs` (`optical_discs()` and the `grating()` helper in build_scenes.py): a CD, a gold DVD and a Blu-ray lying on a dark stage and a CD standing up, under small lamps and a soft fill, so each shows rainbow streaks from the lamps and fans from the fill.
- **Cost** (release, 1440×900, 300 frames, two alternating runs against a release build of c4be8f1, GPU ms, RR / native): veach-mis 4.56 → 4.50 / 4.25 → 4.41, tea-set 4.84 → 4.77 / 5.65 → 5.65, layered-materials 4.49 → 4.52 / 4.87 → 4.91, glass-stack 6.29 → 6.22 / 6.91 → 7.08, brushed-metal 6.22 → 6.18 / 7.60 → 8.03 (within noise but brushed-metal native, about ±4% between runs); texture-brick-box (the maps variant, now with the grating code) 5.42 → 5.75 / 5.23 → 5.59; `optical-discs` 5.71 / 7.10 (4.54 / 5.37 with the gratings removed).

## Thin film (track 11 part 3, 2026-11-01)

- **Model** (glTF's KHR_materials_iridescence; `pathtrace.hlsl` `film_fresnel`, `spec_fresnel`): Belcour and Barla (2017), the Airy sum of a film between the outside and the base kept to two orders, with the spectral integral in closed form against Gaussian fits of the CIE matching functions (the Khronos sample viewer's constants), then XYZ → linear Rec. 709. No spectral rendering. IORs are relative to the outside: the film's, and the base's per channel from f0 ((1 + √f0) / (1 − √f0), so a metal's colour stands in for its complex IOR). Below 30 nm the film's IOR fades to the outside's (smoothstep), leaving the plain base Fresnel. It is evaluated once per hit at the view cosine, as glTF's model does (`prepare_film`, kept in the hit's copy of the material as `base_scale.rgb`), not per half vector: inlined at every Fresnel site it cost scenes without a film 3–9% in registers. The result replaces the base specular's Schlick term, lerped by the strength: the specular, the diffuse's 1 − F and the guides' specular albedo (RR and native AA) all see it. The coat and sheen are unchanged.
- **Dielectrics:** in the transmission branch the film's reflectance (outside = the medium the ray comes from, base = the one it enters, from the nested stack) replaces the exact Fresnel by the strength (not at total internal reflection): reflection is chosen by its mean over RGB and the throughput weighted by F / mean or (1 − F) / (1 − mean), so a transmissive sphere of IOR 1 with a 1.33 film is a soap bubble. The film adds no refraction (parallel walls).
- **Data:** `Material::{iridescence (0–1), iridescence_thickness (nm, 0–2000, default 400), iridescence_ior (1–2.5, default 1.3)}`, skipped in files while default. Packed into the free `surface_scale.zw` (no wider record): z = strength, w = thickness (whole nm) + 2048 × IOR in hundredths (`lighting::film_code`), exact as an integer float; `Gpu::attach_maps` now writes only `surface_scale.xy`. UI: *Thin film*, *Film thickness*, *Film IOR* in the advanced material rows. glTF: factor, IOR and `iridescenceThicknessMaximum` (the thickness texture is not read, so the maximum is used).
- **Energy compensation** with a film scales by the film's reflectance at normal incidence instead of f0 (`fc`).
- **Checks** (debug, raw native, `captures/film/`): white furnace, film over white metal at roughness 0 / 0.4 / 0.8 and thicknesses 200–700 nm: 0.98–1.00 of the background, none above it beyond the usual sphere-floor contact pixels; the soap bubble and film-coated glass vanish (1.00). A white diffuse base under a 450 nm film reads 0.915 / 0.959 / 0.924 (plain white diffuse 0.958): the diffuse is weighted by 1 − F(vh) while a rough GGX lobe returns less than F, which the base's 4% hides and a strong film shows (0.89 with f0 in the compensation, 0.90 with the per-half-vector film). `thin-film`, light-sampling-only vs MIS (320×200, 4096 spp): RMSE×1000 9.2 (the bubbles' delta paths), 9×9-blurred 1.25, mean −0.0003.
- **Scene:** `thin-film` (`thin_film()` and the `film()` helper in build_scenes.py): three soap bubbles (380 / 520 / 700 nm), anodized titanium at 60–260 nm oxide (IOR 2.4: blue, silver, bronze-magenta, cyan, gold), an oil slick (1.47, 420 nm) on a dark puddle and a beetle-green metal pawn.
- **Cost:** release, 1440×900, 300 frames, two alternating runs against a release build of `main` without the change, GPU ms, RR / native: veach-mis 4.06 → 4.24 / 3.90 → 4.07, tea-set 4.45 → 4.42 / 5.04 → 5.24, layered-materials 3.99 → 4.06 / 4.32 → 4.50, glass-stack 5.17 → 5.38 / 5.84 → 6.17 (about 1–5%, glass-stack 4–6%; runs differ by about 3%). `thin-film` itself 4.77 RR / 5.12 native. The first version (the film per half vector, inlined at each Fresnel site) cost 3–9%.

## Energy compensation and anisotropy (track 11 part 2, 2026-10-31)

- **Energy compensation** (Turquin 2019, the one-table form of Kulla and Conty 2017; `eval_bsdf`): GGX with separable Smith G1·G1 keeps only E(nv, roughness) of the light (F = 1); a white metal at roughness 1 kept 0.31 of a furnace background. The base specular is multiplied by 1 + f0·(1/E − 1) (per channel; a white metal gains 1/E, a dielectric's 4% specular almost nothing and its diffuse keeps the rest). The coat lobe is compensated in full (1/E), and the layers below it are dimmed by `coat_albedo(nv, roughness)`, the compensated coat's real albedo with f0 0.04 (= the old coat_fresnel(nv) when smooth): F(nv) nears 1 at grazing, where a rough coat's facets mostly see far smaller F(vh), which left dark rims. Non-reciprocal (view-side factor), as Filament's; sampling is unchanged, so MIS stays consistent.
- **Tables** (`tools/fit_layers.py` → `shaders/layers.hlsli`, constant arrays, about 35 KB of source): `GGX_ALBEDO` and `COAT_ALBEDO` 32 × 32 (roughness × nv, bilinear), `ANISO_ALBEDO` 12 × 4 × 8 × 6 (nv × view azimuth from the brushing direction folded to 0–90° × roughness × strength, quadrilinear, 16 taps, read only by anisotropic materials). Each entry is importance-sampled on a stratified grid; checked against brute-force hemisphere integration to 5 digits. No single effective roughness fits the anisotropic albedo to the isotropic table: it varies by 0.53–0.66 with the view azimuth alone, and the geometric mean, RMS and other means left compensated albedos between 0.54 and 1.11.
- **Anisotropy** (glTF's KHR_materials_anisotropy model): `Material::{anisotropy (0–1), anisotropy_rotation (degrees, −180..180), anisotropy_spun}` → `layers.w`, `sheen.w` (radians), flag bit 11. The base GGX takes alpha along the brushing direction T and lerp(alpha, 1, strength²) across it, so highlights streak across the brushing. T comes from `intersect` (`Hit::tangent`, filled only when `layers.w > 0`): the object's x axis (world x for the room and floor), or with *Spun* `cross(object y, p − object origin)`, circles around the object's y axis (radial highlights on a disc, a pan or a sphere's meridians); `aniso_frame` projects it into the tangent plane, falls back to `basis` where it is parallel to the normal (the poles), and turns it by the rotation. The coat and sheen stay isotropic. UI: *Anisotropy*, *Brush direction*, *Spun* in the advanced material rows. glTF: strength and rotation are read; glTF's direction is the rougher axis, so import adds 90°, and the rotation turns from the object's x axis, not the UV tangent.
- **Sampling:** anisotropic materials sample visible normals (Heitz 2018, `ggx_sample_aniso`; spec pdf G1(v)·D/(4 nv)); D·nh sampling, kept for isotropic materials (same random numbers as before), put fireflies on the steep facets of a strongly anisotropic metal (spun sphere alone in the furnace at 4096 spp: pixels above 1.05, 42 → 22; isotropic twin: 4).
- **Checks** (debug, raw native, 2048–8192 spp): white furnace, the metal spheres at roughness 0.5 / 0.75 / 1 read 0.90 / 0.61 / 0.31 of the background before and 0.99–1.00 after; the layered furnace's coated metal 0.90 → 0.99, its rough coats' rims gone, the brushed and spun white metal spheres 0.99 (0.60 with a geometric-mean roughness lookup); no sphere above 1 beyond noise; rare bright pixels remain where a metal sphere meets the floor (a 5.2 one also with the isotropic coated metal). `brushed-metal`, light sampling only vs MIS: RMSE×1000 0.97, 9×9-blurred 0.11, mean ratio 1.0000; BSDF only 1.0010 (the known BSDF-only offset).
- **Scenes:** `brushed-metal` (spun steel disc with a gold ring, spun sphere, copper pan with a brushed handle, brushed aluminium plate and sphere, a polished sphere for comparison); `layered-furnace` gained brushed and spun white metal spheres.
- **Cost** (GPU ms, 1440×900, debug exe vs the track 11 part 1 release snapshot, interleaved, two runs each, RR / native): veach-mis 4.52 → 4.51 / 4.23 → 4.39, tea-set 4.64 → 4.77 / 5.28 → 5.68, gem-display 5.01 → 5.30 / 5.64 → 5.99, glass-stack 5.72 → 6.09 / 6.11 → 6.52, cornell-caustic 5.74 → 6.08 / 5.86 → 6.19, layered-materials 4.35 → 4.43 / 4.67 → 4.89; brushed-metal 6.2 / 7.9. Part of it is longer paths: kept energy keeps Russian roulette from ending them. The first version cost 5–10% more on scenes that never take the anisotropic branch: eval_bsdf was inlined four times (three light-sampling strategies and the BSDF sample) and its anisotropic code weighed on all of them; next-event estimation now picks ld / le / pl / limit per strategy and evaluates once (renders match to float reordering, max abs diff 1e-6). Packing the tables as float4 (4608 floats take 1152 constant slots instead of 4608) and a rolled lookup loop made no measurable difference.
- **Changes old renders:** every rough metal and rough coat brightens (energy that was lost); dielectric bases barely move.

## Layered materials: clear coat and sheen (track 11, 2026-10-31)

- **Model** (glTF's KHR_materials_clearcoat and _sheen; `pathtrace.hlsl` `eval_bsdf`): base (Lambert + GGX as before) → sheen over it → coat over both. Sheen: Charlie distribution (Estevez and Kulla 2017, α = roughness², roughness clamped to 0.07) with their fitted Λ visibility, times `sheen.rgb`, divided by max(1, E(nv)) (the lobe's albedo passes 1 only at grazing views below roughness ~0.2); the base is dimmed by 1 − max(sheen)·E(nv). Coat: GGX with its own roughness, Schlick f0 0.04 (IOR 1.5), times `layers.x`; the base is dimmed by 1 − cc·F(nv). Both dim by the view direction only (glTF's form): dimming by both directions (reciprocal) lost 8–12% of a white base in the furnace. NEE and BSDF sampling evaluate the same function, so MIS stays consistent. Layers apply to the opaque part only (scaled by 1 − transmission, as the base).
- **Sampling:** the coat's own GGX lobe with probability `coat_probability` = 0.3·cc, otherwise the old spec / diffuse choice; the sheen rides on the diffuse lobe's cosine directions (no pdf term). The coat's random number is drawn only when cc > 0, so other materials keep their sample sequences (bit-identical). `ggx_sample` is the old inline GGX sampling, factored out.
- **Table:** `shaders/layers.hlsli` (generated by `tools/fit_layers.py`, 16 × 16, rows roughness 0..1, columns nv 0..1, bilinear `sheen_albedo`) holds the sheen's directional albedo as a constant array: no GPU resource. Neubelt's visibility was tried first and reaches an albedo of 5.7 at grazing.
- **Data:** `Material::{clearcoat, clearcoat_roughness (0.03), sheen, sheen_color (white), sheen_roughness (0.5)}`, all 0–1, skipped in files while default; `PackedMaterial::layers` = (coat, coat roughness, sheen roughness, −), `sheen` = colour × strength (both appended: 144 B per material). UI: *Clear coat* / *Coat roughness* and *Sheen* / *Sheen color* / *Sheen roughness* in the material's advanced rows. glTF: `mesh::layers` reads the factors from the raw extension JSON (gltf 1.4 has no typed access; Cargo enables its `extensions` feature); the sheen colour splits into strength (its largest channel) and a normalised colour; test `gltf_layers_import` (Khronos ToyCar, when downloaded).
- **Checks** (debug, `captures/layers/`): furnace spheres, centre / background: white diffuse 0.958 (the base's own 4% Fresnel loss), sheen at roughness 0 / 0.3 / 1 0.958 / 0.958 / 0.963, coat sharp / rough 0.960 / 0.951, rough metal 0.977 and with a coat 0.978; the rough coat's edge loses 7% (single-scatter GGX; Kulla–Conty is next in the track). Tea set with every object layered: light-sampling-only vs MIS 9×9-blurred RMSE×1000 1.05, mean −0.0001; BSDF-only was 1.6% brighter, and so was the unlayered tea set: the grazing shadow stop, fixed 2026-11-02 ("Grazing shadow rays"); on 2026-11-10 the three strategies agree to 0.01% ("Light sampling and BSDF sampling agree (accuracy audit B7)"). Cost on unlayered scenes (release, 1440×900, old → new, two runs each): veach-mis and cornell-caustic within noise; tea-set RR 4.58 → 4.76 and gem-display RR 4.86 → 4.99 (3–5%, both runs), though an earlier interleaved debug run showed no difference; likely the wider material record. `layered-materials` itself 4.3 ms RR / 4.6 native.
- **Scenes** (build_scenes.py, module-level `coat()` / `sheen()` helpers): `layered-materials` (`layered()`: red paint bare and coated, velvet cushion, peach, glazed teapot, dusty bronze, lacquered pawn, felt cone); `layer-sweeps` (rows of coat roughness, coat amount, sheen roughness, sheen amount, 0 to 1); `layered-furnace` (white base under coats of roughness 0–1, sheens 0.1–1, both, and a coated rough metal; the rough coats show their single-scatter loss as dark rims); `velvet-lounge` (`lounge()`); `perfume-vanity` (`vanity()`, also nested: bottles of glass 1, liquid 3 and sealed air 5).

## Nested dielectrics (track 10, 2026-10-31)

- **Rule** (Schmidt and Budge 2002, `pathtrace.hlsl` `medium_*`): each path keeps a stack of the transmissive materials it is inside: `uint64_t media`, four 16-bit entries from the bottom, `(material id + 1) | priority << 13`, 0 = empty; a full stack drops the push; ids above 8190 would alias (objects end at 4100). The current medium is the highest `Material::priority` (0–7, flag bits 8–10 of `lobes.w`, packed in `lighting::pack`), the latest entry on ties; empty = air (IOR 1). A surface with `transmission > 0` is a **false hit** when the ray enters it below the current medium's priority, or leaves a material that is in the stack but is not the current medium: the stack is updated, the ray continues from `p − ng·0.0003` in the same direction, no bounce is counted (`false_hits` ≤ 32 per path) and `previous_pdf` / `previous_delta` / `previous_position` stay, so MIS still refers to the last real vertex.
- **Cost:** the skip runs in a small loop around `intersect` at the top of the bounce loop (absorption and cone width per skipped segment there), not as a `continue` of the bounce loop. Measured on cornell-caustic at 1440×900 (debug exe, GPU time; HEAD 5.34 ms RR): a `uint4` stack plus a `continue` block cost 6.2 ms, the 64-bit word 5.8, the inner loop 5.4. Run-to-run noise is about ±0.3 ms, so measure each variant twice and against a same-session baseline. A true hit refracts with n1 / n2 taken from the stack (entering: the current medium → this one; leaving: this one → what the stack falls back to). Leaving a material that is not in the stack (the camera started inside it) keeps the old rule: into the current medium, or air.
- **Absorption** follows the current medium (`medium_absorption`, from the material's flat colour, so patterns and maps no longer tint it) and is recomputed only when a refraction changes the stack; false hits leave it, so a segment through a false surface keeps the medium it travels in.
- **Fresnel:** dielectrics now use the exact unpolarised Fresnel (`fresnel_dielectric`) instead of Schlick, which was poor between two dense media and reflected at grazing angles at n1 = n2. The TIR test is part of it (F = 1), and the reflect / refract choice always draws one random number (Schlick's path skipped it on TIR). Old glass scenes change slightly (see the roadmap entry). The guides' `specular_albedo` still uses Schlick.
- **Defaults:** every material has priority 0 (`skip_serializing_if` zero, so old scene files are unchanged); with equal priorities the latest entered medium wins, which makes touching or overlapping glass and water meet without an air gap. Ranking matters only for overlaps: in `glass-of-water` the water reaches into the glass wall and base (water 1 < glass 2), the ice pokes out of the water (3) and the air bubbles (IOR 1.0, 4) sit inside it.
- **Mesh:** `gen:glass/<seed>` (`procedural::glass`) is two lathed parts sharing one normalisation, *Glass* (chamfered foot, rounded rim, filleted inner floor; 0.045 wall, 0.2 base at height 2) and *Water* (side and floor 0.4 wall inside the glass, a meniscus rising 0.03 at the wall); seeds vary radius (0.55–0.65), taper (0–0.15) and fill (55–80%). `procedural::lathe` revolves an outline in (r, y) with the solid on its left, (dy, −dx) as the normal and smooth points averaging their edges; a band on the axis is a fan of single triangles. Test `glass_and_water_are_outward_and_nested` checks the volume by the divergence theorem (positive only with outward normals) and that the water stays below the rim.
- **UI:** *Priority* (0–7) in the material's advanced rows (`editor::material_ui`); `validate()` bounds it.
- **Priority as CSG** (scenes `layered-cocktail`, `paperweights`, `aquarium`, `perfume-vanity`; helpers `ranked`, `clear`, `cyl`, `block`, `ball` in build_scenes.py): a solid glass cylinder or box (1) with a liquid (2–4) and an air shape (IOR 1.0, 5–6) inside it reads as a filled vessel with no inner wall to model; a liquid or air shape that rises past the rim opens the glass's top (the rim is a false hit inside it), and one kept inside seals it. Overlap neighbouring liquids by about 0.01 rather than letting faces coincide. Anything that must poke out of the liquid into the air (ice) needs the top priority (7). Opaque objects (fish, gravel, coins) simply sit inside a medium. Pitfall: a flat-bottomed solid resting on the floor shows the floor under it fully lit (shadow rays pass glass) plus caustic fireflies, and its bottom is coplanar with the floor. It stays small under the cocktail glasses, the block paperweight and the bottles, but dominated a wide solid disc, so the millefiori paperweight is a low dome resting on a curve. Cost (debug exe, 1440×900, GPU ms, RR / native): layered-cocktail 9.4 / 11.3, aquarium 8.8 / 9.7, perfume-vanity 7.5 / 9.0, paperweights 6.8 / 7.9, against glass-of-water 10.6 / 12.6; layer-sweeps 4.8 / 5.7, velvet-lounge 6.2 / 7.6, layered-furnace 3.6 / 4.1.
- **Checks** (debug build, 400², raw native, 4096 spp, against a 16k-spp reference; `captures/nested/`): the water tank with an inner box of the same IOR and colour ranked above the water matches the plain tank to noise (RMSE×1000 7.76 vs 6.34; the plain render shares its first 4096 samples with the reference, which explains the 1.2×); an IOR-1.5 orange box ranked *below* the water vanishes (6.42), while the same box at equal priority shows (23.4).

## Third batch (2026-10-31): image formats, native AA, anisotropic filtering

- **Image formats** (`video.rs`): `ImageFormat` (Png, Jpeg, Exr, HdrPng; `file(stem)` adds `.png` / `.jpg` / `.exr` / `.hdr.png`), `read_image` (texture 10, 18 or 26 by format), `write_image` (one writer for everything) and `save_current`. *Save the current image* has one button per format (`Actions::save_image`, F12 = PNG, written as `cornell.<ext>`); the high-resolution still takes `Options::image` and `jpeg_quality` (`UiState::export_format`, `jpeg_quality`, shared by every JPEG save); image sequences map `Format::Png` / `Exr` through `Options::image_format()`. HDR PNG needs `hdr_active` (checked in `Job::start` and `read_image`). `--capture` still uses `Gpu::capture`. Self-test 141–145: JPEG and EXR quick saves, a JPEG high-res still (size checked by `App::expect_image`); 187–188 the HDR PNG save.
- **Native AA** (RR off; `modes.w == 0`): the trace writes u3 / u4 as the running mean of the samples' first-hit diffuse / specular albedo (read before the guide ray), and u3.a as coverage, the share of samples whose first hit is the guide's material (misses count when the guide missed). `post.hlsl` `store` (pass 6) lerps from `integrated` to the filtered value by coverage before restoring albedo. With RR on, u3 / u4 stay the guide's (RR inputs). `Settings::pixel_filter` (0 Box, 1 Gaussian σ = width/6 cut at 3σ via `erfinv`, 2 Blackman-Harris by 16-step bisection of its CDF; default 2) and `filter_width` (0.5–4 px, default 1.5) go to `lights.y` / `lights.z`; `filter_offset` replaces the uniform sub-pixel offset of the progressive path only (jittered frames keep `jitter.xy`). Box at width 1 is bit-identical to the old path. Both are in `keep_output` and kept across `preset()`. `tools/native_aa.py` measures it (see the roadmap entry for the numbers). Self-test 158–161 runs the Gaussian (width 3) and Box filters on the native path.
- **Anisotropic filtering:** sampler s1 is `D3D12_FILTER_ANISOTROPIC`, `MaxAnisotropy` 16. `intersect` keeps world dP/du, dP/dv (`T`, `B`; static box maps t/scale, object box maps the rotated axes / scale, mesh UVs from the UV derivatives) and `footprint()` turns the cone (width across the ray, width/|cos| along it, cos ≥ 0.05) into uv gradients through the inverse Gram matrix; `sample_map_lod` clamps the ratio to `Settings::anisotropy` (`lights.w`, 1–16, default 16) and calls `SampleGrad`. No T (degenerate UVs) falls back to an isotropic `uv_scale` footprint.
- **Build gotcha:** after the repo restructure, a target dir built before the move keeps build.rs's old `native\vendor` link path (no rerun trigger fires). `cargo clean -p caustica` in that target dir fixes the `nvsdk_ngx_d.lib` link error.

## Owner requests (item 6, 2026-10-30)

- **Slider double-click reset:** `crate::slider` returns `Slider`, a wrapper around `egui::Slider::from_get_set` (as `Slider::new` builds it, plus `.integer()` for integral types) that forwards `suffix`, `logarithmic`, `smallest_positive`, `fixed_decimals`, `custom_formatter` and `custom_parser`. Its `Widget::ui` checks `slider_double_clicked` (hovered or pressed + `pointer.button_double_clicked`: a slider senses drags, so egui never reports it as double-clicked) and then writes the reference value.
  - The reference comes from `reset_source(live, reference)` registrations (address range of a live struct → same offset in a reference of the same type; `reset_value` picks the innermost range). `App::ui` registers the settings against `reset_baseline` (a clone taken whenever `scene_name` changes: the scene as loaded, preset, generated or randomized), each object and light against the baseline's same index (or the type default), and `UiState` against `ui_defaults`, and clears them after the frame. The exposure row (a local EV value) resets by hand. Hover text "Double-click to reset" on every slider.
  - Unit test `double_clicking_a_slider_resets_it` drives a real egui `Context` with pointer events: a rail click moves the value, a double-click resets it.
- **Light outlines:** `Settings::light_outline` and `AreaLight::outline` (default false; display only: removed from `transport_key`, per light too). `gizmo::draw_lights` skips lights whose outline is off unless selected; `pick_from_probe` only tests drawn outlines (hidden ones are picked in the Scene tab). "Show outline" rows sit under each light's "On". Self-test 266–268 asserts they default off, then draws all of them.
- **HDRIs:** radiance is bilinear (`env_bilinear`, wrap in u, clamp in v); the sampling pdf stays per texel, and MIS still agrees (studio and noon checks rerun: means 0.4486 / 0.4486 / 0.4485 and 0.5969 / 0.5970 / 0.5945, as before). The map is RGBA16F (`f32_to_half`, unit test `half_floats_round_trip`), divided by `env_scale` = max(1, peak / 60000) so suns past 65504 survive; strength and `inv_norm` are multiplied back in the constants. `MAX_WIDTH` 8192.
  - 21 Poly Haven maps were rendered as backgrounds at 1440×900 and checked at 1:1; satara_night (sensor noise) and moonless_golf (motion blur) were dropped, 19 kept (4k; 8k for kloofendal, venice_sunset and the_sky_is_on_fire). The old 2k files are gone. Before/after on venice_sunset: the 2k nearest-texel skyline was visibly blocky, 8k bilinear is smooth.
  - `scenes/environments` gained night on the Bund, teapot in the hallway, snow day and gold dragon at dusk (9 scenes).

## Instancing (core track 4, 2026-10-30)

- **Layout:** `Gpu::rebuild_scene` builds (1) the static BLAS from `geometry::static_geometry` (floor, room, main light, area lights; world space, split into non-opaque / opaque ranges as before, `opaque_offset` in `environment.w`), only when its vertices change; (2) one BLAS per distinct mesh key (`geometry::object_source`: `shape:<Kind>` or the `mesh` string), object-space, in `meshes`; (3) a TLAS of the static instance (InstanceID 0) and one instance per visible object (`geometry::object_transform`, DXR 3×4 row-major).
- **Mesh buffer:** every mesh in use is packed into one object-space vertex buffer (t1). When the set of mesh keys changes it is rebuilt and only new keys get a BLAS (a built BLAS holds its own copy of the triangles, so old ones are reused with new `first` offsets). The static vertices live in their own buffer (t6), so light edits don't touch the meshes.
- **Instance table** (t7, `InstanceRecord` ↔ `Instance` in common.hlsli): `vertex_base`, `material` (5 + object index; u32::MAX for the static instance, whose vertices carry ids), `emitter_base` (start of this instance's range in `emitter_lookup`, u32::MAX for non-emissive objects), `object`. Built in `rebuild_scene`, emitter bases filled and uploaded in `update_materials`.
- **Emitters** stay world-space triangles: static ones, then each emissive object's `object_geometry` (in instance order). The self-test's count check (full soup vs GPU) still holds.
- **Shader:** `candidate_material(instance, prim)` in both `Proceed()` loops; `intersect` reads the instance, then static vertices (+ `opaque_offset` for geometry 1) or `vertices[vertex_base + prim·3]` with the normal taken to world space as `mul(n, (float3x3)CommittedWorldToObject3x4())` (inverse transpose).
- `geometry::lowest_y` caches grounding per (mesh pointer, rotation, scale) because instancing asks for every object's frame on every rebuild (a chess part scans the whole 1.6M-vertex file).
- `frame_objects` is exact up to 256 objects; past that it uses each mesh's box corners through the instance transform (a 3600-object forest took minutes in the debug unit tests). The generator test runs 3 seeds for forest / crowd; unit tests take about 60 s in debug.
- `MAX_OBJECTS` = 4096. New generator families `forest` (400–1800 trees from 6 shared tree meshes, two objects each) and `crowd` (up to 54² copies of one shape).
- **Measured** (1440×900, release, median of 3 alternating runs vs the core3 snapshot): drag frame time bunny 26 → 5.5 ms, chess preset 24 → 6.1 ms, ASWF chess set 141 → 7 ms. Plain render GPU time: Classic +7% RR / +5% native, bunny +1 / +5%, chess +6 / +2%, mirror corridor −3 / −3%, neon −2 / +1% (two-level traversal costs a little in tiny scenes).
- **Render check** vs the core3 build (raw native, 512 and 4096 spp): RMSE ×1000 below 0.2 on presets 0, 2, 8, 13, 20 and the chess set; glass scenes differ by noise (the glass dragon 7.4 → 2.7 from 512 to 4096 spp). The glass lab (14) keeps a difference where glass objects' bottom faces lie exactly on the floor: coplanar faces z-fight, and the instance transform moves them by float rounding, so the other face wins in places. Neither build is more correct.

## HDRI environments (core track 3, 2026-10-30)

- **Settings:** `hdri` (path, ≤ 1024, relative to `scenes/` like meshes), `hdri_strength` (0–1000), `hdri_rotation` (±360°), `hdri_visible` (camera rays see it; off, bounce-0 misses return `background`). All transport fields. Stage section of the Scene tab: Load… (rfd, .hdr / .exr), Clear, strength, rotation, "Visible to camera".
- `environment.rs`: `load` (image crate, `hdr` and `exr` features; non-finite → 0) → `build(w, h, rgb)`: halves maps wider than 4096, then per row a conditional CDF of luminance, and a marginal CDF over rows weighted by sin θ of the row centre, in the extra last row. `inv_norm` = w·h / (2π² Σ lum·sinθ_row). A black map is an error.
- **GPU:** `Gpu::set_environment` uploads both through `upload_texture` (staging per footprint row) into textures that live across resizes (`env_views` at heap slots 30, 31, re-created in `resize`). Constants gain `environment_map` = [strength, rotation rad, flags (bit 0 loaded, bit 1 visible), inv_norm]. `environment_loaded()` for tests. `main.rs` reloads when `Settings::hdri` differs from `hdri_loaded`; a failure clears it with an error toast.
- **Shader:** sampling and the pdf work per texel; radiance is bilinear since item 6 (`env_bilinear`), u = φ/2π + 0.5 with φ = atan2(x, −z) − rotation, v = θ/π. `sample_env` binary-searches the marginal then the row, reusing each remainder for the position inside the texel; pdf = lum·inv_norm·sinθ_row / sinθ. NEE picks the HDRI with `p_env` = 0.5 when emissive triangles exist (1 otherwise); triangle NEE pdfs and BSDF-hit weights carry (1 − p_env). Misses after a non-delta bounce get the MIS weight against `p_env·env_pdf`. The light-sampling modes (`mis_mode`) work as for triangles. Without an HDRI nothing changes (renders match the previous build to 2e-7).
- **Checked:** spheres under the noon sky (a sun a few texels wide) and the photo studio, 2048–4096 spp, MIS vs NEE-only vs BSDF-only: means agree to 0.02% on the studio; on the noon sky NEE-only matches MIS to 0.27% per 20×20 block while BSDF-only is the expected firefly field. Unit tests: the pdf integrates to 1; big maps halve; black maps fail.
- **Assets:** `tools/fetch_assets.ps1` also fetches Poly Haven HDRIs (CC0, SHA-256 pinned) into `scenes/hdri/` (gitignored): 19 at 4k / 8k since item 6 (the first five were 2k). `scenes/environments` (9 scenes, `ENV_SCENES` in build_scenes.py; they start with no lights, since the studio's hidden rim light still casts shadows).
- Self-test 260–265: a generated 64×32 .hdr with a sun (written with the image crate), asserted loaded; hidden from the camera; mis modes 1 and 2; cleared and asserted unloaded. Scene files start at 273 (texture frames 269–271 come before them).
- Not done: the sun is not split out as a separate light (its shape is the map's texels), no ground projection / shadow catcher, no MIS compensation. (Maps are RGBA16F since item 6.)

## Toys (roadmap 5d, 2026-10-30)

- **Keep-exposure mode (was light painting, the user toggle removed 2026-11-10):** `Gpu::keep_exposure`, set only by the motion-blur video export (`video::Job::before_render`, cleared in `after_render`/finish). It makes `rr_active` false and `prepare_upscaler` return None (status "Kept exposures accumulate at render resolution") and stops `update_materials` and `rebuild_scene` from resetting, so the passes of one frame, each at another moment of the shutter, sum into one image. The app's L key, the *Paint light* button, `App::painting`, the sample-cap lift, the `painting_keeps` transport-key rule and the CPU viewport's twin (`Live::set_painting`, the carried sums and `Request.paint` in `reference/progressive.rs`) went with it: they only served the toggle, and the CPU timeline export (`reference::exposure`, `Progressive::over`) never used them. Changeable: git history before this commit has the whole viewport path if it comes back.
- **Portals:**
  - `Object.portal` (index or −1; `validate` checks the pair). `lighting::materials` appends one record per portal holding the 3×4 rows of `portal_matrix(a, b)` = local(b) · diag(−1,1,−1) · local(a)⁻¹, where local = translate(pivot) · R · scale(0.35·size·(sx, stretch, sz)).
  - The object's own record gets flag bit 2 in `lobes.w`, a white colour, no emission and no pattern, and `secondary.w` = the matrix record's index.
  - Trace: on a portal hit, the map lands on the pair's far face, so a hit from inside (`!h.front`) just passes through. Up to 16 crossings per path, each with `bounce--`. `previous_delta = true` gives emission found after a crossing MIS weight 1.
  - The guide ray sees the portal surface itself, so RR guides are approximate there.
- **Walk mode** (`walk.rs`): moves the orbit camera's eye with distance 0.5, so looking turns in place (`place()` re-derives the target, clamped to ±20). Since item 6's second batch a real walk: `Walk::feet` is the state (re-derived when the camera moved by anything else, `placed`), gravity 4 and jump 1.25 units/s (≈ 0.2 high), eye 0.62 standing / 0.36 crouched (Cornell box ≈ 5.5 m), all × `scale` (mouse wheel, 0.2–5). The body is spheres of radius 0.15 from step height (0.12) to the head, pushed out of nearby triangles (`near` prefilters by box; Ericson's closest point); standing, pushes only slide sideways or down. `ground` casts a ray down from step height (from the previous feet while falling; one step below the feet while grounded, so stairs and slopes down are followed) and takes the highest walkable hit (normal.y ≥ 0.64, 50°). Fly mode (F) is the old free camera; it turns on by itself when nothing is below the camera on entry or after falling below y −20 (`message` → a toast). B toggles head bob (one dip per 0.42-unit stride). The camera is placed only when the eye moves by more than 1e-5, so standing still never restarts the accumulation. Triangles come from `scene::geometry`, rebuilt when `geom_key` changes. A dedicated `KeyboardInput` arm tracks held keys while walking, and only an allowlist reaches the normal shortcuts (`App::global_key`: F12, F1, Tab, Ctrl+Z / Y / O; not Ctrl+S, which is crouch and back). Losing window focus clears the held keys. Unit tests: the Cornell back wall stops a walker; landing, standing still, a 0.1 step climbed and a 0.5 block not; jump height, crouch and fly.
- **Turntable** (`video.rs`): since item 6 a `video::Job` (see "Video export"). `--turntable` runs `video::render` (blocking) at rendered frame 2 of a headless run (frames default to 4), H.264 CRF 16 at the window size, or PNG frames without ffmpeg.
- **Gamepad:** `gilrs` is polled in `remote_and_gamepad` (interactive runs only). Untested: no controller here.
- **Phone remote** (`remote.rs`): std `TcpListener` on 8642–8661 (`0.0.0.0` from the UI, `127.0.0.1` in tests). Every request needs `?t=<8-char token>`.
  - Endpoints: `GET /` (embedded page), `GET /state` (JSON of `KEYS`, refreshed every 0.5 s), `POST /set?k=key` with a JSON body, limited to `KEYS` plus `preset` (±1) and `restart`.
  - Edits apply through serde and `validate()`, like `--set`.
  - Turning it off flips an `AtomicBool`, after which the listener answers 410 (the port stays bound until exit).
  - QR code via the `qrcode` crate → egui texture.
- Self-test 243–256 (240–242 are free): portal records (244), walking moves the camera (252), a 3-frame turntable (253), a remote edit over HTTP applied (256). Scene files start at 260.

## Camera and look (roadmap 5c, 2026-10-30)

- **Constants grew past 512 B:** passes now sit at `CB_STRIDE` = 1024 (trace 0, filter 1–7, display 8; buffer 9 KB, compile-time assert). New blocks: `lens`, `camera_model`, `look`, `lift` (w = grain cell size), `grade_gamma` (w = LUT strength), `gain` (w = frame seed). Mirror any change in common.hlsli.
- **Trace:** `camera_ray(pixel, lens_sample, …)` handles every model. Guides use the pinhole ray (`lens_sample` false), so RR and the denoiser guides stay sharp.
  - Lens: `aperture_point()` gives a disk or an n-gon fan. The focal plane is at `focus_distance` along the view axis, or tilted about the camera right axis (model 4).
  - Cat-eye rejects lens points outside a circle shifted by the pixel's NDC (`valid=false` → zero throughput).
  - Ortho uses `camera_model.w` = distance·tan(fov/2) as the half height. Equirect is ±π by ±π/2. The little planet is a stereographic projection looking down.
  - Non-perspective guide depth is the hit distance.
- **`Settings::perspective()`** (models 0 and 4) gates RR (`rr_active`), the temporal upscalers (`prepare_upscaler`), click picking and the gizmo.
- **Display:**
  - `lens_and_film` adds halation and vignette on linear light before the tone map. Halation is 24 taps of `scene_at` (RR/SR output or the native image) above 1.0, tinted red-orange.
  - `film_look(t)` does lift/gamma/gain on the encoded value, then the LUT (manual trilinear over u29). It runs inside `tonemap` and in `hdr_map`'s tone-mapped branch (not the Linear HDR branch).
  - Grain is hashed per cell and frame, weighted to the midtones, after the tone map.
  - Tone maps 6 AgX and 7 PBR Neutral (`validate` allows < 8).
- **LUT:** u29 is a 3D RGBA32F texture (`texture_3d`) kept outside `textures`, so `resize` only re-creates its view (`lut_view`, heap slot 29). The root UAV range is `TEXTURES + 1`. `look::read_cube` parses .cube (3D, domain 0..1, size 2–128). `App` reloads it when `lut_path` changes.
- **Probe** is now `gpu::Probe { linear, albedo, depth, id }` (depth from u5, id from u19). Ctrl+left-click sets `focus_pending`; the next readback's depth becomes `focus_target`, eased with 1 − e^(−10·dt) per frame.
- Look fields (halation, grain, grain_size, vignette, lift, grade_gamma, gain, lut_path, lut_strength) are display-only, so they are excluded from `transport_key`.
- Self-test 216–239: aperture, blades and cat-eye; models 1–4 (218 asserts RR off); AgX, PBR Neutral; the film look plus a generated inverting LUT (224 asserts it loaded); Ctrl+click focus at the center (239 asserts it settled). Scene files start at 240.
- Visual check: `captures` at 640×400 of bokeh, ortho skyline, equirect Stonehenge, little-planet forest, tilt-shift skyline, AgX + halation/grain/vignette, PBR Neutral, all as intended.

## Film stocks and panorama metadata (roadmap 5c leftovers, 2026-11-02)

- **Film stock** (`ui::FILM_STOCKS`): UI presets onto `grain`, `grain_size` and `halation` (no new setting); the combo shows the stock whose three values match, else Custom. Velvia 50, Ektar 100, Portra 400, Vision3 500T, CineStill 800T (strong halation: no anti-halation layer), Portra 800 pushed, Delta 3200 (grain only; there is no black-and-white conversion).
- **GPano XMP:** `video::set_equirect` is set from `projection == 2` every frame. `write_image` (quick saves, high-res still, PNG / JPEG sequences) and `video::tag_capture` (`--capture`, script `capture`; not UI captures, which show more than the panorama) add Google's panorama XMP: an iTXt `XML:com.adobe.xmp` chunk after IHDR in PNG, an APP1 `http://ns.adobe.com/xap/1.0/` segment after SOI in JPEG, with the full-sphere size = image size. Unit test `equirect_images_carry_the_panorama_xmp` (decodes after tagging: chunk CRC and segment length); checked on a real 800×400 equirect render (Pillow reads the XMP; a perspective render gets none).

## Debug views and meters (roadmap 5b, 2026-10-30)

- `Constants.tools` (mirrored in common.hlsli): zebras, scopes, heat scale for bounces (min(bounces, 8)), and BVH steps per sample at the top of the log map (256).
- Trace counts `proceed_steps` in both `RayQuery` loops and the bounces used, written to u27. Views 8–11 are in `display`: 8 is `(bounces − 1) / (max − 1)`; 9 is `log2(1 + steps/spp) / log2(257)`; 10 is the variance of the accumulation (log); 11 is false colour in EV around 0.18 of the exposed image.
- Scopes: `bin_scopes` atomics in `display` on the final SDR value. Before display, `clear_scopes` copies a zero upload buffer into u28; after display, `queue_readbacks` copies u28 and the light-meter texels (u18 at the output pixel, u3 at the render pixel) into one readback buffer. `finish_readbacks` maps it after Present into `Gpu::scopes` / `probe_result`.
- App: `g.zebras` / `scopes_on` / `probe` are set from `UiState` before `render`. The convergence graph reads u18 with `read_floats` at sample counts growing by 1.25× and computes RMSE ×1000 of l/(1+l) on the CPU (a 20 MB readback each time, so a hitch). The graph clears when samples drop.
- `stats.rs`: loaded only when the window is visible; saved every 30 s and on close. Achievements are checked each frame in `redraw`.
- Self-test 204–207 cycle views 8–11. 208 turns on zebras, scopes and the meter (cursor at the center). 209 asserts the luma histogram counts exactly width×height and the meter texels are finite. 210 sets the current image as the reference and restarts. 215 asserts ≥ 2 finite graph points and turns the tools off. Scene files start at frame 220.
- Capture CLI: `--ui-scopes` (scopes + zebras).

## Tooling (roadmap 5a, 2026-10-30)

- `tools/perf_history.py` appends to `captures/perf-history.csv` (date, commit, `+` if src/shaders/bridge are dirty, preset, mode, means) and plots the PNG with matplotlib. First row, commit 3c4c8a4+ (GPU ms, RR / native): Classic 3.81 / 3.13, bunny 3.96 / 3.90, mirror corridor 5.98 / 5.71, neon 4.52 / 4.27.
- **Build snapshots:** `tools/snapshot_build.ps1 -Name N -Notes T` copies exe + DLLs + scene packs (not thumbnails) into `builds/<date>-N/` with NOTES.md (gitignored). `scenes/models` and `scenes/hdri` are hard-linked, not copied (about 800 MB of pinned downloads). The owner asked for one per major item, so they can compare builds later. The exe finds `scenes/` next to itself.

## Review findings: fixed and merged (2026-10-29)

Done on branch `review-findings` and fast-forwarded into `main` (commit c72f163). The pre-fix state is commit 1fc032d. All results were measured.

**Native denoiser** (see "Rendering behaviour" for the design). Scores are ×1000, measured with `tools/motion_error.py`.

| Case | Before | After |
|---|---|---|
| Orbit, 2 spp: error / blurred / light band | 20.65 / 16.94 / 36.9 | 9.63 / 4.40 / 7.0 |
| Still camera, 16 spp | 12.96 | 6.31 |
| Still camera, 1024 spp | 4.79 (raw 2.43) | 1.56 |

- The old filter was biased: its blurred output was fed back as history, so it was worse than raw above 64 spp.
- The white specks came from per-pixel variance estimates that were near zero by chance. The 3×3 variance prefilter fixed them, so no separate firefly clamp was added.
- The light "ghost" was the spatial and temporal blur crossing from the light to the ceiling. The object-id stop fixed it.

**Sobol** decorrelates per reset and is now 4× cheaper. See "Rendering behaviour".

**Other fixes:**
- Pan follows the screen axes, and the target is clamped to ±20.
- The Pause glyph is ⏸, and the resolution label reads "Render … · output …".
- The `tests::ui_strings_have_glyphs` unit test checks every non-ASCII character in UI strings against egui's fonts.
- The Delete key only works while the Scene objects window is open.
- The UI capture is copied before Present.
- Marble veins are thinner and softer.
- The CLI is order-independent.
- RENDER_OK prints means.

**Performance:** 1440×900, 4 spp, mean of 200 frames, before → after on this branch.

| Case | Before | After |
|---|---|---|
| Classic, raw | 2.87 | 1.64 |
| Classic, native denoiser | 7.48 | 3.10 |
| Bunny (13), native | 7.53 | 3.86 |
| Mirror corridor (17), native | 10.04 | 5.75 |
| RR | 3.95 | 3.88 |
| With UI drawn, wall clock | 11.8 | 4.2 |

The gains come from:
- First-hit shadow rays.
- The Sobol pair cache.
- The packed filter guide with demodulated filtering.
- One egui upload per frame.
- Skipping dirty-key serialization when settings are unchanged.

**Not a bug:** windows going off-screen after a resize only happened because the self-test resizes the swapchain without resizing the window.

**Still open (not started):**
- Faint low-frequency blotches at very low spp, which is typical of SVGF.

## Physics sandbox (track 17 part 1, 2026-11-02)

- **Gizmo transforms pause the simulation** (fixed 2026-11-02): `step_physics` returns while `ui.transform` runs. Before, the transform re-applied the pose only on pointer moves and gravity dropped the object in between (holding an arrow still let it fall); a direct grab was fine because its spring acts every step. When the transform ends the poses no longer match `written`, so the simulation restarts from them. Tests: `a_held_box_stays_up_when_the_pointer_stops` (the grab spring) and self-test 237–238 (an arrow move with physics on keeps the elevation).
- `src/physics.rs`, rapier3d 0.36 (glam-based; its glam is a different version, so poses cross as plain floats). `Settings::physics` (`enabled`, `gravity`, `speed`, `bounce`, `friction`) is omitted from scene files while it is at its defaults and is out of `transport_key`.
- One dynamic body per visible, non-portal object; the body origin is `object_frame`'s centre, so writing back is `grounded = false`, `position`/`elevation` = translation, `rotation`/`tilt` = the quaternion as YXZ Euler (the same order `scale_rotation` builds). Colliders: cuboid for built-in boxes, a ball for uniform spheres (and SDF spheres), else a convex hull of up to 4000 points of the grounding mesh (`geometry::hull_points`); tori, rings and knots are therefore filled in. Static: a floor slab (`floor_extent`, 1 unit thick) and, with `cornell_room`, the ceiling and three walls (the front is open).
- Fixed 1/120 s steps, at most 8 per frame (slower frames become slow motion), times `speed`. Only awake bodies that moved more than 0.5 mm or 0.05° are written, so a settling scene stops changing the geometry key and accumulates (without the threshold, rapier's sub-millimetre jitter restarted accumulation every frame and the image stayed at 4 spp). Bodies past ±39 or below −30 turn fixed.
- `Sim::matches` compares the objects with what the sim last wrote: any outside edit (gizmo, inspector, undo, a new scene) or a bounce / friction / stage change rebuilds the simulation (velocities are lost). A gravity change wakes every body. Enabling the simulation records an undo checkpoint, so Ctrl+Z restores the poses from before.
- **Press priority** (owner reports 2026-11-03): `App::physics_press` grabs the body under the pointer unless a gizmo handle is hovered. A hovered handle takes the press even when another body lies in front of it, since the gizmo is drawn over everything. The one exception is the gizmo's inner part over one of its own bodies: that press grabs the body, so a selected body can still be thrown. v0.3.2 grabbed on any inner-part press, which took gizmo clicks away when a body was in front; the press now checks what is under the pointer against the selection. Self-test 292–293: a press on a handle does not grab an unselected ball in front of it, a press on the ball grabs it, and the held ball rises toward the pointer.
- Tools: left-press on an object grabs it: a velocity drive sets the grabbed point's velocity to 20× its distance to the cursor ray (capped at 20 units/s), with spin damping, and the body is 21× heavier while held so it shoves others aside (a dominance group let pushed bodies sink into it); release throws it with the velocity it has, and a click without a drag still selects. E explodes under the pointer (radial impulse, up to 6 units/s within 1.2 units, a little upward). The Scene tab has a Physics section (sliders, Wake all, Explode at the floor centre, awake count).
- Generator family `physics` (Physics playground): dominoes, brick wall, block pyramid, Jenga tower, ball pit, all with the simulation on; `scenes/generated/physics-{1,2,4,7,13}.json`. Bodies start awake and settle in the first second (bodies spawned asleep never fall, so a ball pit would hang in the air).
- Not done: see roadmap track 17.
- Bodies and strings (2026-11-02, for the Newton's cradle): `Object::body` is dynamic (default), fixed (a fixed rapier body with its collider: frames, tables) or none (no collider). `Object::tether {object, from}` makes the object a string: no body; `update_strings` points its cylinder from `from` to the object's centre (axis local y, `stretch` = half length / (size·0.35)) after every step that moved something. The strings ending at a dynamic body become one joint to a world body: one string, a ball joint at `from`; two or more, a hinge on the line through the first two points (a cradle's V). Deleting an object fixes or clears tether and puzzle-target indices.
- The cradle needs gaps between the balls (2 cm on 34 cm balls): touching balls, or a 5 mm gap that a 2 m/s ball crosses within one 1/120 s step, are solved as one contact group, and two balls fly out instead of one (middle balls moved 0.32–0.38 units). More solver iterations, stiffer contacts and no warm-starting did not help; the gap alone gets the middle balls under 0.03. `newtons_cradle_passes_the_swing_through` checks it on the scene file.

## Optics bench (track 17 part 2, 2026-11-02)

- Shapes `convex_lens`, `concave_lens` (spherical faces of radius R = (1 + 0.25²)/(2·0.25) on a unit disc, axis local z, half thickness 0.3 / 0.05 at centre / rim, reversed for concave; standing on the rim) and `prism` (equilateral triangle in xy, apex up, extruded along z). Shading normals are outward, which is what `h.front` uses.
- Material `splitter` (flag bit 12, 4096): a thin partial mirror. A ray reflects with probability `reflectivity`, else passes straight on, both tinted by `color`; a delta event (no light sampling, no BSDF). Path tracing: before the emission step (pass-through doesn't count as a bounce; LPE sees the reflection as a specular reflection event). BDPT / photon mapping: `make_vertex` marks splitter vertices `V_DELTA` (never connected) and `sample_vertex` does the same choice.
- Spot lights in light subpaths (BDPT, photon mapping) now sample their cone uniformly in solid angle (`emit_dir`, `light_dir_pdf` agrees for MIS) instead of the cosine hemisphere: a 4° spot got about 0.1% of the photons before.
- Scene `optics-bench`: a 3° spot grazing the floor through a convex lens, a 50/50 splitter, a concave lens on the reflected arm and a prism on the transmitted one, with two white screens; photon mapping, since the light on the floor and screens after glass is a caustic that path tracing cannot find from a small light. Haze was tried first: a beam in fog seen through glass is S·D·S and stays black in every integrator (none of the bidirectional ones supports media).

## Laser puzzle (track 17 part 3, 2026-11-02)

- `src/laser.rs`, `Settings::puzzle` (`enabled`, `origin`, `direction`, `target` object index or −1, `par`, `color`; out of `transport_key`, omitted at defaults). Scene → *Laser puzzle* edits it and shows "Solved / Not solved · N moves · par P".
- The beam is traced on the CPU, not rendered: geometric optics on every visible object's world triangles (Möller–Trumbore, interpolated vertex normals). Mirrors (metallic > 0.5, roughness < 0.2) reflect; beam splitters fork it (front faces, power × reflectivity and × 1 − reflectivity; back faces pass); materials with transmission > 0.5 refract by Snell with total internal reflection (no Fresnel split); anything else stops it. At most 64 segments; branches below 1% power end. Retraced only when the objects or the puzzle change (a JSON key).
- Drawn as viewport lines over the image (with the gizmo layer, perspective cameras only); width and opacity follow √power. The roadmap's GPU "sensor material" was not needed: the CPU trace makes win detection exact, free of readback and independent of the integrator and sample count.
- Moves: each finished transform that changed the settings counts one (`finish_transform`); the count resets when `scene_name` changes. Solving toasts "Solved in N moves (par P)".
- Levels: `scenes/puzzles` pack, `laser-1..3` (one mirror turn; a periscope; a return past a beam splitter with a mirror to move). `laser::tests` checks each starts unsolved and that its intended solution solves it within par.

## Scripting

- 2026-11-02, track 19. `--script file.rhai` runs a [Rhai](https://rhai.rs) script (the `sync` feature, so the engine can live on another thread). The script thread owns the engine; every registered function sends a `Request` over a channel and blocks on its reply, and `App::tick` answers requests before each frame (`poll_script`). `frames(n)` parks its reply sender in `Script::waiting` and is answered after the n-th rendered frame, so the script and the renderer never touch the same state.
- Functions: `load(name)` (scene file by path, stem or title, else a preset slug), `set(key, json)` (a JSON pointer into `Settings`, e.g. `objects/0/rotation`; a bare word is taken as a string; the result goes through `validate()` and a rejected value leaves the settings alone), `frames(n)`, `reset()`, `capture(path)` (PNG or EXR, creates folders), `samples()`, `scenes()`. Errors become Rhai runtime errors with the line.
- A headless run needs `--frames` or `--script`; with a script it exits after the script ends and prints `SCRIPT_OK frames=N`, or fails with `SCRIPT_ERROR`. Windowed, the end shows a toast. Paths and scene lookup are relative to the working directory, as with `--scene`.
- Headless, no frame renders between two calls (`poll_script` waits up to 50 ms for the next request and renders only while a `frames(n)` is running), so a script's frame and sample counts are the same on every run. Windowed, the app keeps drawing between calls (the UI stays live) and `about_to_wait` keeps redrawing while a script runs. `capture` and `samples` after a `load`/`set`/`reset` with no frame since are errors (they would report the previous image). `--frames`, `--capture` and `--self-test` are rejected with `--script`; a panicked script thread counts as a failure.
- Example: `examples/scripts/batch.rhai`. Not done: see roadmap track 19.

## Audio-reactive lights

- 2026-11-02, track 19. Scene → Audio → "Lights follow the music". `audio::Audio::start` opens the default output device's mix as an input stream: cpal's WASAPI host sets `AUDCLNT_STREAMFLAGS_LOOPBACK` when an input stream is built on an output device. Only f32 shared-mode output is handled (the Windows default). The callback mixes to mono into a 2048-sample ring.
- Each frame `update` takes the window, applies a Hann window and an FFT, and takes the RMS bin magnitude in 20–250 Hz, 250–2000 Hz and 2–12 kHz. Each band is divided by its own peak, which halves every 4 s (automatic gain, so quiet and loud music both swing the lights), then smoothed per frame time (`smoothing` at 60 fps).
- WASAPI loopback delivers no packets during silence, so a window older than 200 ms counts as silence (otherwise the lights freeze at the last level).
- `audio::apply` scales the frame's copy of the settings in `redraw` (the same clone the renderer already used), never `App::settings`: saving, undo and the inspector keep the scene's values. Multiplier = `floor + gain × level`; main light = bass, area light i and emitter i = band i mod 3. The copy changes every frame, so accumulation restarts every frame (as when the timeline plays) and `update_materials` rebuilds the emitter table; use RR.
- Off in headless runs and video exports (deterministic output). cpal reports a change of the default output device as a stream error but keeps capturing the old device, and some drivers and audio tools send that notification as soon as a capture opens. So a stream error is not fatal: `Audio::recover` reopens the capture on the current default device (at most once a second, levels and gains kept). Only a reopen that fails shows an error and switches the option off.
- Tests: tones at 80 Hz, 900 Hz and 5 kHz land in their bands; `apply` scales by `floor + gain`. `loopback_capture_opens_and_delivers_samples` is `#[ignore]` (needs an output device and something playing).

## Text-to-scene

- 2026-11-02, track 19. Scene → "Describe a scene (AI)", on a background thread. It has three modes:
  - *New scene*: the result loads like a scene file, keeping the viewer's output choices, and is undoable.
  - *Edit this scene*: the current scene file goes in the prompt and the changed file replaces it, keeping the open file, so Ctrl+S saves it.
  - *Critique the render*: main.rs saves the current render as a PNG, scaled to fit 1568 px, and hands it to the UI through `critique_png`. The model answers with up to five notes on lighting, composition, colour and materials, then the improved scene. The notes show under the button, with *Apply the suggestions* and *Dismiss*.
- Command line: `--describe "text"` makes a new scene and `--describe-edit "text"` changes the scene loaded so far. Both run before rendering, so they combine with `--headless --frames --capture`. Status lines go to stderr; any notes are printed on a `DESCRIBE_NOTES` line.
- While a request runs, the status ("Waiting for the model", "Fixing the scene (try 2 of 3): …") shows with the elapsed seconds and a Cancel button. Cancel kills a running CLI; an HTTP request finishes in the background and its result is dropped. The model's text arrives whole, not streamed token by token.
- The settings are kept in prefs.json (`Prefs::ai`: provider, model, effort, endpoint, protocol), never with keys:
  - The backend, endpoint and key are set in Menu > Text-to-scene model.
  - Model and reasoning sit under the text box. The model is a text field with a *Pick* list (suggested names plus fetched ones) and, for HTTP backends, *Fetch* (`GET /models`; on the Anthropic protocol `/v1/models?limit=1000` with the version header).
  - *Reasoning* offers the levels the backend takes ("Default" sends none): `low`, `medium`, `high`, `xhigh`, `max` on Claude, and `minimal`, `low`, `medium`, `high` on OpenAI-style backends.
  - Changing the provider or endpoint clears the model, the level and the fetched list.
- Backends (`textscene::Provider`):
  - *Claude Code CLI* (the default):
    - Runs `claude -p --output-format json --no-session-persistence --system-prompt-file <file> [--model m] [--effort e]` with the prompt on stdin, with no console window.
    - Each call gets its own temp folder, removed afterwards, so no project's CLAUDE.md is read.
    - Tools are off (`--tools ""`), except that a critique allows Read for `render.png` in that folder.
    - It uses the CLI's own login, so a Claude subscription works without an API key. `--bare` is not used: it accepts only API keys.
