    - Each call is a fresh session, so a repair sends the whole exchange as one prompt (`cli_prompt`).
    - An npm install (`claude.cmd`) is started through `cmd /c` when `claude.exe` is not found. Runs are killed after 15 minutes.
    - Real runs: a reading nook (84 s), a chess board (2.5 min), the hourglass changed to night by a single candle (`--describe-edit`, 100 s), and a critique of a render with `--effort low` (24 s).
  - *ChatGPT via Codex CLI*: `codex exec --skip-git-repo-check --sandbox read-only --color never --output-last-message <file> [--model m] [-c model_reasoning_effort="e"] [--image render.png] -`, with the prompt on stdin. Codex has no system prompt option, so the instructions lead the prompt. It uses the Codex CLI's login (`codex login`: a ChatGPT account or an API key); the app never handles those credentials itself. **Not run here**: Codex is not installed on this machine, and the flags come from its documentation.
  - *opencode CLI*: `opencode run --format json [--model provider/model] [--file render.png]`, the prompt on stdin with the instructions leading it, from a private temp folder without `--auto` (no permission to edit or run anything). The reply is the `text` parts of the last message in the JSON event stream; an `error` event (for example a free model's usage limit) is the failure. *Fetch* runs `opencode models`, so the picker lists the models of every provider set up in opencode. Model variants go in the name (`#variant`); the reasoning picker offers only Default. Checked live with `claude-agent/haiku` (a valid scene in 13 s).
  - *Claude API*: `/v1/messages` over ureq (Rust has no official SDK):
    - Headers: `anthropic-version: 2026-10-21`, and refusal fallbacks (`fallbacks: "default"` with `anthropic-beta: server-side-fallback-2026-07-01`).
    - Body: model `claude-opus-5-5` unless set, `max_tokens` 32000, `output_config.effort` when chosen, and the system prompt with `cache_control: ephemeral`. The render goes as a base64 image block.
    - Key from the menu (session only) or `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`.
  - *OpenAI API*: `https://api.openai.com/v1/chat/completions` with `reasoning_effort` when chosen and the render as an `image_url` data URL. Key from the menu or `OPENAI_API_KEY`. A model is required (none is assumed): fetch or type one.
  - *Custom endpoint* (local models):
    - A base URL (Ollama `http://localhost:11434/v1` by default; LM Studio `:1234/v1`, llama.cpp `:8080/v1`).
    - A protocol: OpenAI chat completions, or Anthropic messages with plain fields (no caching or beta headers) and the key as both `x-api-key` and a bearer token. The key is optional.
    - A critique needs a vision model.
- All HTTP backends share one ureq agent builder (native-tls, 900 s timeout):
  - 429, 5xx and connection errors are retried 3 times with backoff; `retry-after` is honoured, capped at 60 s.
  - A read timeout is not retried. Other statuses are errors with the body.
  - Refusals (Anthropic `stop_reason: refusal`, OpenAI `message.refusal`) and cut-off replies (`max_tokens`, `finish_reason: length`) are errors.
- The system prompt is about 61 KB, roughly 16k tokens. A small local model needs a context window at least that large. It holds:
  - The schema: `include_str!("scene.rs")`, with the field docs, defaults and every `validate()` range.
  - The material library: every `presets::MATERIAL_NAMES` entry as `Material` JSON.
  - The generator families, with how a scene file names one with a seed.
  - Two small example files.
  - Structured outputs were not used: `Settings` has no JSON Schema (no schemars), and the validation ranges would not fit one anyway.
- The reply is parsed as follows:
  - The text between the first `{` and the last `}` goes through `scenes::parse` (wrapper, generator or bare settings) and `validate()`. The text around it, without code fences, becomes the notes.
  - On failure, the assistant's reply is sent back (Anthropic's content blocks unchanged, thinking included) with the error, up to two repairs.
- Tests (mocked transport):
  - A rejected scene is sent back and the fix accepted, and replies without JSON fail after the repairs.
  - An edit carries the current scene; a critique carries the PNG and returns the notes.
  - Cancel stops before the next request, and the CLI prompt carries the whole exchange.
  - Missing settings and effort levels are checked per provider; base64 is checked against the standard.
  - Ignored live tests: `live_critique_through_the_claude_cli` (`CRITIQUE_PNG`) and `live_fetch_models` (`FETCH_BASE`; checked against a mock serving both formats).

## Metaballs and track 18–19 leftovers (2026-11-02)

- *Metaballs* (`Sdf::Metaballs`, shader kind 5): a bottom pool, a top cap and four blobs on Lissajous paths joined by a polynomial smooth minimum. The field is built for an object 3× taller than wide (it works in q = (x, 3y, z) and returns d/3, a valid bound), so the `lava-lamp` wax object is a box of that aspect. The blob positions follow `Object::phase` (keyable like any float; validate keeps 0..10000). The phase travels in the high 24 bits of the procedural instance's `vertex_base` (thousandths), the kind in the low 8; `sdf`, `sdf_normal` and `sdf_hit` take that raw word. A phase change is a geometry-key change, so playback rebuilds the instance table (the BLASes stay cached).
- Scenes: `lava-lamp`, `nighthawks`, `audio-equalizer` (Scene → Audio on; object i and area light i follow band i mod 3, so the bars are ordered bass, mid, treble).
- Scripting: `get(key)` (the setting's JSON text). Text-to-scene: `Prefs::ai_profiles` and the *Backend* row in Describe a scene.

## Physical sky: night, clock time, altitude (track 9, 2026-11-02)

- `PhysicalSky` gains `clock` / `longitude` / `utc_offset` (`solar_time()`: clock + longitude/15 − zone + the equation of time), `altitude` (km; `Atmosphere::observer`), `stars` and `moon`. `map_key` uses the solar time, so the cache sees equal skies as equal.
- Night: after astronomical twilight (sun below −18°, `sun.y < −0.31`) the single- and multiple-scattering pass runs with the moon as its light at 2.5e-6 × the lit fraction of the sun (a hard switch; twilight is still much brighter than moonlight there). The moon trails the sun by its age (synodic month from the day of the year) on the sun's declination; its disc is at least a texel. Stars: a hash per texel (1 in 150 above the horizon), energy per star `7.5e-8 · u⁴ · stars · intensity`, tinted blue-white to orange, times the transmittance along the view. The constant night floor went from 2e-5 to 2e-7 × intensity (the old floor was brighter than a moonlit sky).
- Baking: `sky::bake_cached` keeps the last 12 maps by key. Windowed runs bake uncached skies on a worker thread (one at a time; a result for an outdated key is cached and dropped) and keep the previous map until it lands, then restart accumulation. Headless runs, the self-test and video exports bake in place, so their output stays deterministic.

## Physics and puzzle additions (track 17, 2026-11-02)

- Wind: a force `wind · gust · mass` set each step (`reset_forces` first), gust = 1 + gusts · (0.6 sin 1.3t + 0.4 sin(3.7t + 1)) on simulated time. Gravity is `down · gravity`.
- `same_bodies` replaces object equality in `Sim::matches`: material and name edits keep the simulation.
- Concave shapes: `decomposed()` runs V-HACD (resolution 48, at most 24 hulls) on the local, scaled mesh soup; cached by kind, mesh path and scale bits.
- Record: `record_physics` keys `position/0`, `position/1`, `elevation`, `rotation`, `tilt/0`, `tilt/1` of every dynamic body on each frame boundary of simulated time and stretches the animation's length.
- Sleep: activation thresholds 0.01 (normalized linear) and 0.08 rad/s, 1.5 s before sleeping.
- Laser: `beam_objects` turns the traced segments into unlit emissive cylinders (radius 0.015, emission 30 × power, at most 20 units each) in the frame's copy only, so picking, physics and the trace never see them.
- Scenes: `wrecking-ball` (one ball joint from the beam, mass 40×), `castle` (154 bodies; walls stop short of the corner towers so nothing starts interpenetrating).

## Grazing shadow rays (2026-11-02)

`nee_limit(len, cos)` ends NEE shadow rays 0.6 mm off the emitter's surface along its normal (`len − min(len/2, 0.0006 / max(|cos|, 0.02))`) instead of 0.6 mm along the ray. The fixed margin let grazing samples hit the emitter's own face: light sampling lost 0.57% on night-skyline and MIS 1.6% on the tea set (both now within noise).

## Data folders (installed, portable, development)

- `src/dirs.rs` decides once from the exe's folder: a `portable` file beside it → **portable** (everything beside the exe, prefs and stats too); a `target` folder among its ancestors → **development** (the repo's `scenes/` for user scenes, downloads and thumbnails, the working directory for images, videos and settings.json, prefs in %APPDATA%: the old behaviour); otherwise **installed**.
- Installed: prefs.json, stats.json, the `migrated` marker and `captures\` (saved images, high-res stills, the turntable default, settings.json, the reference EXR) in `%APPDATA%\Caustica`; `scenes\` there holds `user\`, `hdri\`, `models\`, `textures\` and `assets.json` (downloads); `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Caustica\thumbnails` holds scene thumbnails and `assets\` (catalogs, previews). The exe folder is only read.
- Lookups: `scenes::list` walks the pack folders of `dirs::scene_roots()` (user root first; a pack folder name present in both counts once, the user's). `mesh::resolve` (meshes, textures, HDRIs) takes the first root holding the file, else the user root; `mesh::store` and `prefs::key` strip whichever root holds a file, so stored paths and favourites stay relative.
- `dirs::migrate()` (start of `run`, installed only, once per user via the marker): copies `scenes\user`, `assets.json` with every file it lists (a zip's folder for extracted maps), `captures\` and `settings.json` from beside the exe; never overwrites. Copies, not moves (Program Files is read-only; the uninstaller removes the old files).
- Installer: per-user by default, Program Files through the privileges dialog (`PrivilegesRequiredOverridesAllowed=dialog`); uninstall deletes `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Caustica` and asks before deleting `%APPDATA%\Caustica`. `tools/snapshot_build.ps1` writes the `portable` marker (snapshots hard-link the repo's downloads).
- Not done: no UI to open the data folder or change it; the self-test's temp folders were already in `%TEMP%` and are unchanged.

## Media in BDPT and photon mapping (track 15/12, 2026-11-02)

- Both integrators now walk through homogeneous media like `trace`: fog (`Settings::fog`, its box) and object media (the nested-dielectric stack, `Material::medium`, priorities and false hits).
- `walk_step(o, d, camera, Walk, beta, …)` (integrators.hlsl) is the shared segment: free flight on the grey extinction of the medium the walk is in, false hits passed straight through (at most 32, absorption applied *before* the stack changes), Beer absorption on beta. It returns escape, a surface hit, or a scatter point with albedo, HG g and σt. `pathtrace.hlsl`'s false-hit loop had the same ordering bug (absorption after the stack update, so a ray leaving a medium was attenuated by the next one): fixed too.
- BDPT: a scatter is a vertex (`V_MEDIUM`, `BVertex::medium` = the stack's top entry). `vertex_f` and `sample_vertex` use the HG phase function there (pdf = value, so the MIS ratios are the phase pdfs); `to_area` drops the cosine for medium vertices; `area_pdf` converts from the previous vertex. Every connection (camera to light, light to camera, s/t joins) multiplies `segment_tr` (Beer over the segment in the medium both ends share, or the fog box's interval). `sample_vertex` takes the dielectric IORs from the walk's stack (`sv_media`), like `trace`.
- Caps: `BDPT_BOUNCES 24` (camera and light vertex arrays from it), photon walks and the gather at 32 (`PHOTON_WALK`); the old cap was 10, which cut paths through stacked glass and water.
- Photons: a scatter from the second interaction on deposits a *volume photon* (the sign bit of the stored power's red flags it; surface gathers skip them). Sky and sun photons meet the fog in their walk (the bounds sphere they start on encloses the fog box). The camera walk of the gather ends at its first scatter with `volume_direct` (NEE through the medium plus one phase-sampled ray, balance heuristic) and `gather_volume` (the photons within r: Σ phase · power / (photons · 4/3 π r³ · σt)). Emitters keep no photons (`keeps_photons`: a diffuse emitter gathered its own emission twice, the birthday cake's 87% red).
- The photon bounds sphere includes the fog box when fog is on (photons used to be emitted only towards the objects).
- `tools/integrator_sweep.py` (release, 240×135, 400 spp, mean colour against path tracing): 35 of 160 comparisons off by more than 5% before, 13 after. Media scenes before → after (BDPT / photon): underwater 45/47% → under 5%, stage-beams 37/38% → under 5%, aquarium 22/21%, scattering-materials 8%, snow-globe 13/8%, cornell-fog 9% → all under 5%; birthday-cake photon 87% and turrell 21% (emitters), mirror-room 26/18% and shape-material-matrix photon 27% (the IOR stack and caps) → under 5% too. Left over 5%: optics-bench 77% (BDPT and photon mapping agree: path tracing converges slowly on the L S+ D caustic of a hidden 3° spot), lava-lamp photon 27% (procedural SDF emitters emit no photons), kusama photon 13% (gather radius bias at mirror walls), sky-garden-dusk 11–12% and sunlit-room 10% (the physical sky's sun/sky split at low sun: path tracing itself differs between light-only and BSDF-only sampling there, see the roadmap), bigger-on-the-inside 8% (portals), cornell-caustic BDPT and veach-ajar photon at 5.0–5.1%.
- Limits: grey extinction only (chromatic media colour by albedo); connections stop at any surface, including invisible IOR-1 boundaries; the volume gather uses the surface radius sequence; deposits beyond the photon budget are dropped.

## Physics: incremental edits, joints, cloth, exports (track 17, 2026-11-02)

- `Sim::sync(settings, recording)` replaces the restart on every outside edit. It compares the objects with `written` (what the simulation last wrote): a new object gets a body, one deleted object loses its body (the rest shift down), an edited object's body is replaced (it starts at rest), and every other body keeps its velocity. It returns false (restart) when the stage changed (room, floor), an edit touches a joint (strings, links, or a body something hangs from), several objects were deleted at once, or what is keyframed changed. New bodies are woken (rapier inserts them asleep otherwise).
- Animation: an object keyframed on a transform field (`position`, `elevation`, `rotation`, `tilt`) is a kinematic position-based body. It follows the keys (`set_next_kinematic_position`) and pushes dynamic bodies, which no longer fight it. While recording, keyed objects stay dynamic (the keys are the recording's).
- Joints: `Object::links` (at most 8): ball joints to other bodies at a world point, converted to local anchors when the simulation starts; contacts between the jointed pair are off. Jointed bodies should have similar masses: a 160:1 chain weight made the solver explode (the generator's links use mass 20).
- Cloth: `Object::cloth` (resolution 4–48 per side, pin none / corners / edge, bending stiffness). An XPBD-style particle grid of our own (`ClothSim`: Verlet, 6 Gauss-Seidel passes over stretch, shear and bend constraints, floor and every collider by `QueryPipeline::project_point`, 2 cm thickness, contact friction). One-way: bodies don't feel the cloth. Each step publishes a mesh under a new key `cloth:<object>/<generation>` (the last two kept) into `CLOTH_MESHES`; `mesh::of` reads it, so the BLAS is rebuilt for the cloth only. A cloth sleeps after a second without motion and wakes on edits, blasts and moving bodies.
- Video: a timeline export with physics on runs its own `Sim` frame by frame in simulated time (`Sim::run`): the keys are evaluated, `take_poses` puts the simulated poses back, `sync` moves the kinematic bodies. Without keys the export takes the length and rate asked for. Motion blur is off with physics (the shutter passes would step the simulation back and forth).
- Record: starting a recording clears the recorded bodies' earlier transform keys; keys are linear; when it stops, `reduce_keys` drops every key that a straight line between its kept neighbours reproduces within 2 mm or 0.2°.
- `hull_points` adds the extreme vertex along 26 directions to its strided subsample, so a big mesh's hull keeps its tips.
- Playgrounds (the *physics* generator): `cards` (a house of cards, 3–4 storeys: lean 18°, 1 cm cards, friction 1; gaps of 2 mm, as wider gaps let the storeys drop), `chain`, `bridge` (planks jointed at both rope lines between fixed posts, a ball on it), `cloth` (a tablecloth over a fixed table and a pinned curtain in gusty wind), and the domino spiral now makes about three turns.
- Self-test frames 211–213 run a simulation (a ball falls, a cloth publishes a mesh), spawn a box mid-run and check the simulated time kept running (no restart).

## Shader Execution Reordering (track 20, 2026-11-03)

- **Runtime.** The OS D3D12 runtime stops at Shader Model 6.8. The D3D12 Agility SDK 1.620.1 (retail; `tools/setup.ps1` fetches it into `vendor/agility`, build.rs copies `D3D12Core.dll` and `d3d12SDKLayers.dll` to `D3D12\` beside the exe, CI and `tools/make_installer.ps1` package that folder) brings 6.9. The exe exports `D3D12SDKVersion` = 620 and `D3D12SDKPath` = `.\D3D12\` (`src/main.rs`, `/EXPORT` in build.rs). On the RTX 4070 Ti SUPER: shader model 6.9, raytracing tier 1.2, `ShaderExecutionReorderingActuallyReorders` true (startup prints a `D3D12:` line; `Gpu` `ser_support`).
- **Tracers as ray generation shaders.** `MaybeReorderThread` is raygen-only, so `pathtrace.hlsl`'s `trace` body is `trace_main`, called by the compute entry or, with `SER`, by `trace_rg` (`[shader("raygeneration")]`). Four more entries (`trace_ser`, `trace_maps_ser`, `trace_lean_ser`, `trace_maps_lean_ser`) compile it as `lib_6_9` libraries (`-exports trace_rg`; the file's other compute kernels are ignored there). `create_ser_pipeline` makes a ray tracing state object of the library, the compute root signature as its global one, a 4-byte payload and recursion 1 (the shader only uses inline RayQuery), plus a one-record shader table; `integrate` calls `DispatchRays` over the same threads as the compute dispatch (width and height = groups × 8). LPE runs use `trace_lpe_ser` (raygen too, since 2026-11-03). `CAUSTICA_NO_SER=1` uses the compute tracers (A/B runs), as does any device without SM 6.9 and tier 1.2.
- **Measured (C8, 3 interleaved runs, 1440×900 with RR, trace ms, compute → raygen):** Classic 2.18 → 1.87 (−14%), Sponza 13.73 → 11.92 (−13%), mirror corridor 3.60 → 3.32 (−8%), glass dragon 4.34 → 4.06 (−7%), stained-glass hall 2.55 → 2.20 (−14%); Classic's frame −6%. The same kernel, so the gain is in how the driver compiles and schedules a raygen shader at 6.9 (not traced further).
- **Reordering is a loss here.** With `dx::MaybeReorderThread` after each bounce's hit (hint: the material, 14 bits) the trace pass was 38–76% slower than compute; with 2 bits 58–65%; reordering once at bounce 1 still 35–105% slower. A reorder point spills the whole path state (throughput, media stack, MIS state, sampler state) and reloads it, and this kernel carries a lot of state; the coherence gained doesn't pay for it. So `SER_BITS` defaults to 0 (no call); `CAUSTICA_SER_BITS` / `CAUSTICA_SER_AT` at build time put it back for experiments. A smaller state at the reorder point (a wavefront split: hit, then shade per material) would be the way to try again.
- **Output.** Raygen and compute images aren't bit-identical (a different compile, so float rounding differs and single paths diverge), but converge to the same image: at 512 spp the means agree within 0.003% (mirror corridor) and 0.009% (glass dragon).
- **Validation layer (Agility).** Its layer flags every `CheckFeatureSupport` as superseded by `ID3D12Device16::CheckFeatureSupport1` (not bound by the `windows` crate) and every buffer created in a state other than COMMON; our default-heap buffers now start in COMMON (`buffer`), the upscaler SDKs' still don't, and `debug_messages` drops both notices.

## GPU efficiency (track 20a, 2026-11-02)

Owner rule: performance work never lowers quality (no fewer samples or bounces, no lower render scale, no performance mode). See roadmap 20a.

**Tools.** `RENDER_OK` now also prints `trace_ms_mean`, `reconstruct_ms_mean` (RR, or the native filter with `--no-rr`), `display_ms_mean` and `ui_ms_mean`, from the `Gpu::pass_ms` timestamps. `tools/perf_baseline.ps1` runs the sweep below into `captures/perf/baseline.csv` (the 2026-11-02 run is kept as `baseline-2026-11-02.csv`). `CAUSTICA_SHADER_DEBUG=1` at build time adds `-Zi -Qembed_debug` to DXC (same `-O3` code) so Nsight's shader profiler can map stalls to HLSL lines. Build it into its own target dir (`target/profile`).

**Baseline, 2026-11-02** (RTX 4070 Ti SUPER, 1440×900, 4 spp, each scene's own bounce count (Classic and bunny 6, neon 12, mirror corridor 24, Sponza 12, glass dragon 20), 300 frames, no VSync; ms):

| Scene | Mode | GPU | Frame | Trace | Reconstruct |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Classic (0) | RR | 5.23 | 5.77 | 2.85 | 2.29 |
| Classic (0) | native | 5.13 | 5.53 | 3.52 | 1.51 |
| Bunny (13) | RR | 5.53 | 6.04 | 3.18 | 2.26 |
| Mirror corridor (17) | RR | 7.81 | 8.40 | 5.45 | 2.27 |
| Mirror corridor (17) | native | 8.64 | 9.13 | 6.41 | 2.14 |
| Neon (21) | RR | 5.83 | 6.36 | 3.48 | 2.26 |
| Glass dragon | RR | 11.01 | 11.60 | 8.68 | 2.24 |
| Glass dragon | native | 12.77 | 13.26 | 10.68 | 2.00 |
| Stained-glass hall | RR | 7.96 | 8.53 | 5.62 | 2.25 |
| Glass stack | RR | 7.56 | 8.12 | 5.23 | 2.24 |
| Sponza | RR | 25.43 | 26.11 | 23.00 | 2.33 |
| Sponza | native | 30.74 | 31.36 | 28.05 | 2.60 |

Sweeps (native): Classic trace 1.03 / 1.55 / 3.52 ms at 1 / 2 / 6 bounces and 0.99 ms at 1 spp; mirror corridor 2.02 / 3.32 / 6.41 ms at 1 / 2 / 24 bounces and 1.85 ms at 1 spp.

**Reading:**
- **Trace scales linearly with samples** (Classic: about 0.84 ms per spp, a fixed part of about 0.15 ms for the guide ray). There is no large per-pixel overhead to remove. The cost is in the paths themselves.
- **Per-bounce cost grows with divergence:** Classic adds about 0.5 ms per bounce after the first. The mirror corridor's second bounce adds 1.3 ms, and its remaining 22 bounces only 3.1 ms together (Russian roulette and escaping paths end most of them). Glass scenes cost 2–3× Classic. These are the scenes where traversal returns to the shader for non-opaque candidates (glass, hidden lights) and where SER would help: fixes (a) and later track 20.
- **Sponza is the heaviest by far** (262k triangles, 69 textures, alpha-tested plants and curtains, an atrium lit mostly by bounce light from the sun through its open roof): 23 ms of trace. It is the main target for fixes (a), (b) and (e).
- **Fixed costs are a big share of light scenes:** DLSS RR is about 2.25 ms whatever the scene (40% of Classic). The native filter costs 1.5–2.3 ms, which is high for six passes at 1440×900 (fix (f)).
- **CPU/GPU bubble:** frame − GPU is 0.35–0.6 ms in every scene (5–10% on light scenes), and the fence wait is about the whole GPU time: the CPU records nothing while the GPU works (fix (c)).
- The low power draw fits these numbers: short frames with an idle gap each, RR's tensor-core work, and traversal that waits on memory. Nsight (occupancy, stall reasons) is still needed to split the trace time further.

**Nsight GPU Trace, 2026-11-03** (`tools/nsight_capture.ps1`: `ngfx.exe` GPU Trace, 3 frames after 6 s, clocks at base, windowed app, metrics auto-exported to `captures/perf/nsight/<scene>/BASE/GPUTRACE_FRAME.xls`; frame-level averages in %, since our passes have no markers yet):

| | Sponza | Mirror (17) | Classic (0) | Glass dragon |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| GPU busy (`gr__cycles_active`) | 91 | 74 | 50 | 67 |
| SM throughput | 14.5 | 18.7 | 15.9 | 13.2 |
| Warps active (compute) | 21 | 16 | 11 | 14 |
| SM active, warp slots empty | 66 | 48 | 31 | 46 |
| SM idle | 13 | 36 | 58 | 40 |
| Register file allocated | 80 | 56 | 37 | 49 |
| L1 / L2 hit | 71 / 98 | 77 / 90 | 83 / 81 | 75 / 90 |
| VRAM throughput | 6 | 12 | 12 | 9 |

- **Occupancy is register-limited everywhere.** Registers allocated / warps active is about 3.5 in every scene: about 150–160 registers per thread, which caps residency near 12–13 of 48 warps per SM. Sponza runs at that cap. The SMs are mostly waiting with too few warps to hide traversal and memory latency, while DRAM bandwidth stays at 12% or below. Fix (b) is first.
- **The windowed app idles the GPU much more than headless runs show.** GPU busy is 50% in Classic and 67–74% in the mirror corridor and the dragon. The headless bubble was 0.5 ms, but the windowed frame adds Present and egui on the CPU while the GPU waits. Fix (c) is second; confirm it with timestamps in a windowed run first.
- Pass markers (`BeginEvent`/`EndEvent` around trace, reconstruction, display, UI) would split these metrics per pass in `GPUTRACE_REGIMES.xls`.
- Pitfall: don't pass `--platform` to `ngfx.exe`. Qt takes it as its own platform-plugin option and fails with "Could not find the Qt platform plugin".
- Pitfall: the table above came from runs in which the shader profiler never ran. `--metric-set-name`, `--multi-pass-metrics` and `--real-time-shader-profiler` are per-architecture options and are ignored silently without `--architecture` (`REPRO_INFO.xls` then says "Warp State Sampling Disabled" and the metric set falls back to Async Compute Triage). Those traces are kept as `captures/perf/nsight/<scene>-asynctriage/`.

**Nsight GPU Trace with the shader profiler, 2026-11-03** (same script, now `--architecture Ada --metric-set-name "Top-Level Triage" --multi-pass-metrics --real-time-shader-profiler`; traces in `captures/perf/nsight/<scene>/`, read through the `ngfx-mcp` server). Frame averages in % of peak:

| | Sponza | Mirror (17) | Classic (0) | Glass dragon |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| RT core busy (`rtcore__cycles_executed`) | 31.0 | 18.6 | 14.6 | 14.5 |
| SM throughput | 14.6 | 22.5 | 27.9 | 18.1 |
| Warps active (compute) | 19.2 | 12.7 | 16.0 | 14.3 |
| Warp launch blocked: registers | 20.7 | 19.5 | 16.9 | 22.7 |
| Warp launch blocked: shared memory | 4.9 | 10.0 | 9.7 | 4.8 |
| Stall: no instruction (I-cache) | 5.8 | 2.2 | 2.1 | 6.1 |
| Stall: long scoreboard, L1/tex | 5.0 | 4.6 | 3.1 | 2.0 |
| Stall: long scoreboard, RT core | 4.5 | 2.5 | 1.7 | 3.1 |
| Stall: wait | 2.1 | 3.3 | 3.8 | 2.5 |

Top `trace` hotspots by HLSL line (% of the trace's shader samples; `trace` is 91% of all samples in Sponza and 58% in Classic, where DLSS RR takes most of the rest):

| | Sponza | Mirror | Classic | Dragon |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `intersect` `Proceed` loop (pathtrace.hlsl:192) | 22.4 | 14.6 | 14.0 | 23.1 |
| `occluded` `Proceed` loop (:264) | 10.9 | 11.1 | 7.6 | 10.1 |
| `cdf_search`, env map (:298) | 11.7 | – | – | – |
| `select_index`, emitter CDF (:590) | – | 14.6 | 0.7 | 1.0 |
| `random` / `hash` (:29, :20, :3) | 5.5 | 4.2 | 5.2 | 5.1 |
| `env_bilinear` (:319, :315) | 3.6 | – | – | – |
| `sample_maps` texture reads (:107–115) | 4.0 | – | – | – |

- **RT cores are not the limit.** They are busy 15–31% of the frame. Waiting on them is under 5% of issue stalls. Traversal lines (192, 264) are 22–34% of samples, but they wait for the RT core to return candidates and on memory, with too few warps to hide it.
- **Registers block warp launch 17–23% of the time** in every scene, and this confirms fix (b). The "no instruction" stalls (6% in Sponza and the dragon) are instruction-cache misses in the large kernel. Smaller variants help here too.
- **Binary searches are a new, cheap target.** The env-map CDF search costs 11.7% of Sponza's trace samples (two searches per env sample, about 20 dependent loads). The emitter CDF search in plain NEE costs 14.6% in the mirror corridor. Both are chains of dependent memory loads ("long scoreboard"). A guide table (Fishman's cutpoint method) gives exactly the same index from one load plus a short search. Sampling and Sobol stratification stay bit-identical, which an alias table would not (it is why plain NEE keeps its CDF, see `restir_presample`). This is fix (g) in roadmap 20a.
- `trace` declares no `groupshared`, yet shared-memory allocation blocks launch 5–10% of the time. Probably the driver's RayQuery state; not confirmed.
- The multi-pass runs report GPU busy 85–93% even in Classic, against 50% single-pass: multi-pass replays a frame back to back, which hides the CPU/GPU gap. Use the single-pass numbers above for the bubble (fix (c)).
- **Fix (g), cutpoint guide tables, done 2026-11-03.** Both CDFs keep a table of K entries (K = the largest power of two ≤ the entry count, so `u·K` and `k/K` are exact in f32): entry k is the search's result for u = k/K (`lighting::cutpoints`). `cdf_search` and `select_index` search only between entries k and k + 1, which always bracket the old answer because it never decreases as u grows (the predicate `u < cdf[i]` is monotone in i for u < 1, including the emitter table's forced final 1). Layouts: the env CDF texture doubles to width × 2(height + 1), with the guide rows after the CDF rows as f32 indices; the emitter guide follows the alias records in the emitter buffer, sixteen u32 per record (data-layouts.md). `environment::build` now rejects maps taller than wide: the marginal row never fit them, and the doubled texture would pass D3D12's 16384 rows. Renders are bit-identical where the build is deterministic (see roadmap 20a (g)); the first timings are within noise and need repeated runs.
- Fix (g) timing, 5 interleaved runs each (trace ms, before → after): Sponza 22.63 → 21.94 (−3.1%), mirror corridor 5.46 → 5.21 (−4.6%), Classic 2.85 → 2.98 (+4.3%). Classic's loss came from the two guide loads on its 2-entry table, so `select_index` now uses the guide from 64 emitters only (mirror corridor 25,602, neon 15,362; Classic 2, bunny 6). A smaller loss on Classic and the bunny then remained without any guide load (+1.8%, +4.9%): code size in the full kernel. LEAN (below) removed it.
- **Fix (b) step 1, LEAN variants, done 2026-11-03.** `trace_lean` / `trace_maps_lean` compile `pathtrace.hlsl` with `LEAN`: `fog_on()` / `restir_on()` are constant false, RayQuery skips procedurals (`RQ_BASE`), procedural-candidate code goes, and `lean_strip` zeroes the five feature gates (coat `layers.x`, anisotropy `layers.w`, sheen `sheen.rgb`, thin film `surface_scale.z`, extinction `medium.w`) in `material_at` and `medium_material`, so their branches fold. `Gpu::lean_scene` (set in `update_materials`, the only material upload path) holds when no visible SDF object exists and every material record has those gates ≤ 0; `integrate` uses LEAN when it holds and the frame's `fog[0]` and `restir[0] & 1` are off, the exact values the shader reads. Results, 4 interleaved runs each at 1440×900 with RR (trace ms, before → after): Sponza 23.61 → 14.30 (−39%), Classic 2.91 → 2.22 (−24%), bunny 3.23 → 2.12 (−35%), mirror corridor 5.68 → 3.72 (−35%), neon 3.68 → 2.48 (−33%), courtyard 4.19 → 2.88 (−31%); frame time −12% to −35%. Output: scenes with fog, ReSTIR or anisotropy keep the full kernel and are bit-identical; LEAN scenes differ only through float rounding (Sponza and Classic by ≤ 6e-7 per pixel; scenes with more lobe choices let a rounding change flip a random choice, so single paths diverge, but image means agree to ≤ 1.5e-5 relative after 16 frames). So the register cost of features a scene doesn't use was about a third of the trace pass. Next in (b): split the remaining kernel further (glass and the media stack, emissive meshes, portals) and re-profile Sponza's occupancy.
- **Re-profile with LEAN (2026-11-03, `captures/perf/nsight/<scene>/`, the full-kernel traces in `<scene>-full/`).** Full → LEAN: RT core busy Sponza 31.0 → 43.3, mirror corridor 18.6 → 23.9, Classic 14.6 → 16.2, glass dragon 14.5 → 20.4; compute warps active +12% to +25%; SM throughput +4 to +6 points. Warp launches blocked by register allocation barely moved (Sponza 20.9 → 19.7, dragon 23.1 → 21.1, Classic 17.8 → 13.7): occupancy is still register-limited, and the gain came from the dropped code, not from many more warps. Stall shares: "no instruction" fell (Sponza 28 → 23%, dragon 33 → 19%: a smaller kernel fits the instruction cache), and waiting on the RT cores rose (Sponza 22 → 28%, dragon 17 → 22%) to Sponza's largest stall. So traversal is now the larger share, which points at (a); the per-candidate shader work for glass and camera-hidden objects was the part of (a) still open.
- **Fix (a) step 1, instance masks, done 2026-11-03.** Ordinary objects were already FORCE_OPAQUE; glass and camera-hidden objects were FORCE_NON_OPAQUE, so every candidate triangle of theirs ran the shader (a material load and two tests) for every ray. Now the two skip rules are instance masks (`gpu.rs` `instance_mask`, pathtrace.hlsl `MASK_HIDDEN` / `MASK_CLEAR_GLASS`): camera rays leave out bit 2, shadow rays bit 4 when glass doesn't block, and an object under one rule goes FORCE_OPAQUE with just that bit. Glass below 0.5 transmission (which the shader committed every time anyway) is opaque with 0xff. Still non-opaque: alpha-tested objects, objects under both rules, the static BLAS's hidden-light range and SDFs. Renders bit-identical on 11 scenes (glass dragon, glass of water, glass stack, hourglass, stained-glass hall, the caustic Cornell boxes, Sponza, presets 0/13/17; the photon-mapped box differs by ≤ 5e-7 from accumulation order). Trace ms, 4 interleaved runs at 1440×900 with RR: glass dragon 5.20 → 4.62 (−11%), glass of water 9.38 → 7.94 (−15%), stained-glass hall 3.24 → 2.70 (−17%), hourglass 2.44 → 2.26 (−8%); Sponza and Classic unchanged (no glass).
- Classic's multi-pass trace ran out of frames at `--frames 900` (about 5 ms frames); the script now passes 20000, and ngfx ends the app when the trace is done.
- **Fix (a) rest, opacity micromaps and the static split, done 2026-11-03.**
  - *Opacity micromaps* (DXR 1.2, tier 1.2; `src/omm.rs`, `Gpu::omm_blas`). Each alpha-tested mesh with a base-colour map gets a 4-state OMM array: micro-triangles in bird-curve order, subdivision up to level 7 at about 16 texels per micro-triangle, 64 MiB cap. Classification is conservative: a min/max alpha pyramid over the texel range a micro-triangle touches (wrap addressing, one texel margin for bilinear, cutoff ± 1/512), so only micro-triangles that are opaque or transparent at every sample are marked so; the rest stay unknown and run the any-hit test as before. The image is bit-identical with micromaps on and off. The BLAS key includes the map and cutoff; such instances get no FORCE flag. Sponza trace 13.45 → 12.98 ms (−3.6%, 3 rounds, 1440×900 RR). `CAUSTICA_NO_OMM=1` turns them off.
  - *Static split.* The static geometry was one BLAS with a non-opaque range for hidden lights and glass, so every ray paid the any-hit path there. It is grouped by `instance_mask` now, one BLAS and instance per group with the same masks and FORCE flags as objects. Single runs at 960×600 native: abacus 1.55 → 1.39 ms, stained-glass hall 1.78 → 1.43, mirror corridor 2.18 → 2.05; bit-identical.
  - *Pass markers.* `Gpu::stamp` wraps trace, reconstruction, display and UI in `BeginEvent`/`EndEvent`, so PIX and Nsight split the frame.
  - *Tried and reverted: (f) groupshared tiles* for the à-trous steps 1–2. The filter got slower (preset 0 1.70 → 1.83 ms) and the result was no longer bit-identical; the taps are L1 hits already.

## Photon gather and sweep follow-up (track 15, 2026-11-03)

- **SDF emitters emit photons.** `sdf_photon` (integrators.hlsl) picks an emissive SDF object by power (records after the guide records, data-layouts "Emitter"), draws an isotropic line through its bounding sphere and collects the line's crossings of the surface (up to 16, `sdf_hit`), then leaves from one chosen uniformly. By Cauchy-Crofton the lines hit a convex or concave surface's area evenly per crossing, so each crossing carries power π·L·A_sphere/2 (with the line density 2πR²·π), divided by the pick probabilities. The share of photons from the environment and triangle lights is unchanged when there are no SDF emitters, so other scenes' random sequences stay the same.
- **Emitters keep photons, with a slab in the gather.** Emitters used to keep none, because the gather sphere took a bright neighbour's photons (birthday-cake's flames, 87% too red); that also dropped light emitters reflect (lava-lamp's wax blobs lighting each other, the rest of its −12%). The gather now also requires |(q − x)·n| < r/4: a sphere alone counts photons on nearby surfaces that face elsewhere, which the disk area πr² doesn't account for. The slab shrinks with r, so the estimate converges to the same answer. Birthday-cake masked +0.4%, lava-lamp −1.8%.
- **Dusk was the pixel filter.** The path tracer's mean at dusk changed 4× with sun size (0.13° → 0.53°) while BDPT and photon mapping didn't. The sun covers under a pixel, so its few pixels hold most of the image's mean, and the path tracer's Blackman-Harris filter (weights don't sum to exactly 1 over the grid) and the others' box weight them differently; with `pixel_filter=0` the swing moves but stays. Leaving out the brightest 0.5% of pixels, PT and photon mapping agree within 0.3% at both sizes. `tools/integrator_sweep.py` now compares means without them.
- **Open:** the slab didn't change kusama-infinity-room's +17% (roadmap Known issues).

## Upscaler texture LOD, textured cloth, triangle-mesh colliders (2026-11-03)

- **Texture detail under upscalers (owner report: DLSS, XeSS and FSR lose texture quality as the preset drops).** Mips are picked by ray cones (core track 5), and camera cones were one *render* pixel wide (`2·tan(fov/2)/dimensions.y`). At 50% scale every camera hit read a mip one level coarser than the output needs, and no upscaler brings that detail back. The vendors' guidance is a texture LOD bias of log2(render/output); with cones that is a camera cone one *output* pixel wide (`dimensions.w`), now used in `trace`, the tracers in integrators.hlsl and the guide rays. At native scale nothing changes (the native images are identical). Sponza 1280×720 at 50%, 120 frames, against native: RMSE RR 0.0146 → 0.0114 (−22%), DLSS SR 0.0218 → 0.0206, XeSS 0.0240 → 0.0228, FSR 0.0256 → 0.0246; Laplacian detail up 5–15%. Cones feed only texture LOD, so nothing else changes.
- **Textured cloth.** `Object::maps_from` names a mesh part (`file.gltf#n`) whose maps an object uses (with `material.textures`, scaled by the map averages as for a mesh's own maps); `Cloth::uv` is the UV rectangle over the particle grid (u0, v0 at the pinned edge's first corner, u1, v1 opposite), and `physics::cloth_uvs` gives the UVs in `grid_mesh`'s order (`mesh::uvs`). The editor shows the UV corners when `maps_from` is set. sponza-cloth's curtains use Sponza's curtain parts (UV 0–1), its banners the banner parts (v 0.38–0.99 as the model's own, u scaled for square texels).
- **Fixed concave meshes collide as triangles** (`fixed_trimesh`): a convex hull or decomposition fills an arcade's arches, and a fixed body needs no volume. The mesh is the part as drawn (`mesh::of`), not the whole file. Only fixed bodies; no existing scene had a fixed concave mesh.
- **Cloth against triangle meshes.** rapier's `QueryPipeline::project_point` culls colliders by box, then projects onto each shape with no distance bound; for a triangle mesh that is a nearest-triangle search over the whole mesh, per particle. With Sponza's 16 parts (each box the whole building) sponza-cloth's frame went from 109 to 407 ms. The cloth step now leaves triangle meshes out of the query (a filter predicate) and projects onto them with `project_point_with_max_dist`, which prunes the mesh's BVH (407 → about 190 ms).
- **Cloths step in parallel** (`std::thread::scope`, one query view per thread since the filter's predicate isn't `Sync`): they are independent (one-way collisions), so the result doesn't depend on the thread count. The app advances physics by wall-clock time, so a slow step makes longer frames that ask for more steps; sponza-cloth's 18 cloths cost 21.5 ms per simulated 1/60 s with collisions (8.0 without) on one thread, 2.8 (1.4) in parallel (`physics::tests::sponza_cloth_cost`, ignored, a timing probe). Headless sponza-cloth at 1280×720: frame 83 ms with collisions, 73 without (407 before both fixes).

## Spectral rendering (future goal, part 1, 2026-11-03)

- **Switch.** `Settings::spectral` (Render → *Spectral*, below the integrator; scene files may set it). The path tracer runs `trace_spectral` (`-D TEXTURE_MAPS -D SPECTRAL`), or `trace_spectral_lean` under the LEAN rule (track 20a), or their SER twins (`Gpu::ser` indices 5 and 6); photon mapping runs `photon_trace_spectral`. Every other variant is unchanged and bit-identical: the spectral helpers (`up`, `to_rgb`, `hero`, `ior_of`, `begin_wavelengths`) are identities or constants there. LPE runs and the path visualizer stay RGB (no spectral twin), as does BDPT.
- **Three wavelengths in the colour channels.** Hero wavelength sampling (Wilkie et al. 2014) with three wavelengths instead of four: they ride in the `float3` channels, so every per-channel operation in the tracer (BSDF, Fresnel, Beer's law, MIS, roulette) is per wavelength and the code is shared. Wavelengths come from Radziszewski's visible pdf ∝ sech²(0.0072(λ − 538)) cut to 380–780 nm, the three at `frac(u + i/3)` of one hashed number per sample (`begin_wavelengths`). The number is a hash of pixel and sample index, not a Sobol dimension, so the RGB variants' sampling is untouched.
- **Uplift and output.** Mallett and Yuksel's (2019) sRGB spectral primaries (`shaders/spectral_tables.hlsli`, written by `tools/spectral_tables.py` from colour-science 0.4.7): `up(rgb)` = a 3×3 matrix per sample (the basis at the three wavelengths). It is linear, so emission, HDRIs, the sky and absorption colours uplift like reflectances, and white stays flat. `to_rgb` integrates against XYZ→sRGB · CIE 1931 2° · D65, normalized so a flat spectrum gives (1, 1, 1); D65 is folded into these output weights, since each path picks up emission exactly once. Round trip: any reflectance in [0, 1]³ under D65 comes back within 1.1e-4. Colours enter through `up` at: material colour and sheen (after the guides read the RGB values), emission, light samples (`sample_light`, `restir_nee`), the background and suns, medium albedo and absorption, beam splitters.
- **Hero only where direction depends on λ.** Dispersion (`Material::dispersion`, the Abbe number in `phase.y`; Cauchy through n_d at 587.6 nm, `ior_of`), a grating's orders (the grating wavelength is the hero's; `grating_weight` = 1), and a dispersive real lens (`lens_ior` rebuilds lens.rs's Cauchy curve from the red and blue indices) call `hero()`: the other two wavelengths are dropped and the hero weighs 3 (pbrt-v4's `TerminateSecondary`). A thin film is exact per wavelength (`prepare_film_waves`: the Airy terms at each wavelength instead of the CMF Gaussian fit) and keeps all three. A grating material calls `hero()` on every hit, whichever lobe is then sampled (unbiased, more colour noise on grating surfaces). With hero only, the film's indices are the hero's too (`ior_of`).
- **Negative components are kept.** A single wavelength lies outside the sRGB gamut (500 nm has negative red). Clamping each sample's radiance at 0, as the RGB tracer does, added red and green: +35% red on Classic with forced hero-only paths, and a white beam through the prism turned magenta in photon mapping. The spectral tracer's running mean keeps them (only the per-frame `noisy` input of RR and the denoiser is clamped); `finish_frame` and the photon gather do the same, which changes nothing for the RGB integrators (their terms are never negative). The native denoiser clamps the mean where it reads it (`post.hlsl` `integrate`).
- **Spectral photons.** `photon_trace_spectral` gives each photon one wavelength at emission (hero only from the start) and deposits its power as linear sRGB (`deposit` calls `to_rgb`); the gather stays RGB, and since the uplift is linear the two sides agree. The volume-photon flag moved from red's sign bit (spectral powers can be negative) to the lowest mantissa bit of blue (so RGB photon powers lose one ulp of blue; photon mapping was never bit-identical between runs). The camera side of photon mapping is RGB, so a prism seen directly in a photon-mapped scene does not split colours; its caustics do.
- **Checks.** Classic at 640×400, 256 spp: means (0.2078, 0.1609, 0.1224) RGB, (0.2075, 0.1609, 0.1223) spectral, (0.2075, 0.1607, 0.1222) with every path hero-only. The gem display matches its RGB look without dispersion; with it, the gems throw coloured fire. Self-test frames 286–289 run spectral photon mapping on dispersive glass, battle mode and the spectral lens; `dark-side` (the scene sweep) is spectral photon mapping in fog.
- **Cost (release, 1440×900, 4 spp, no RR, trace ms, mean of 2 interleaved runs).** Spectral always on the full tracer: 1.9–2.1× RGB on presets 0, 13, 17 and 21 (RGB ran LEAN there). With the LEAN spectral variant: Classic 2.47 → 2.86 (+16%), mirror corridor 3.86 → 4.56 (+18%). The rest is the per-sample uplift matrix and wavelength tables (registers) and more colour noise per sample, not measured yet.
- **Scene.** `dark-side`: a spot beam rising 28° into a floating 60° prism (IOR 1.7, Abbe 12) at minimum deviation, in light fog; the fan lands on the floor from x = 2.15 (420 nm) to 3.38 (680 nm).

## CPU reference renderer (future goal, 2026-11-04)

`--reference SPP --capture out.exr` (`src/reference/`) renders the scene on the CPU, every core, with no GPU device at all, into a linear EXR to hold against the GPU's raw accumulation (`--raw --no-rr --scale 1 --capture gpu.exr`) with `tools/reference_compare.py gpu.exr cpu.exr` (channel means and their ratio, relative RMSE, the worst blocks of a grid with their noise, `--heat` for a per-pixel ratio map).

- **Same model, separate implementation.** It reads what the GPU reads: the world-space triangles (`geometry::geometry`), the packed material records (`lighting::materials`), the camera (`Settings::camera`, the same inverse view-projection ray), the pixel filters (box, Gaussian by erfinv, Blackman-Harris by bisection, copied from the shader), the floor patterns (`material_at`), the sky gradient over the background colour, and the shader's rules: the hit frame (the side and the ray offsets from the triangle's face normal, the shading normal interpolated as in object space and turned toward the ray), one-sided emitters, the spot falloff, lights hidden from the camera skipped by camera rays, shadow rays skipping lights that cast none and (unless *Glass casts shadows*) clear glass, the unlit flag, Russian roulette from bounce 3, the bounce limit. The BSDF is `eval_bsdf`'s isotropic core with the same energy compensation (the GGX albedo table is parsed from layers.hlsli at first use, so it can't drift), sampled as the shader samples it (cosine or GGX normals by `spec_probability`, so the pdf it divides by is the same mixture), and the dielectric branch is the shader's (exact Fresnel, the nested-medium stack with priorities and false hits (the highest priority on top, the later among equals, at most 4 deep, seeded with the medium the camera is in, `geometry::camera_medium`), eta² on refraction, the rough-glass perturbation, and the glass colour as Beer–Lambert transmittance per unit inside it, `medium_absorption`).
- **Its own estimator:** a binned-SAH BVH (16 bins, leaves of 4, plain halving past depth 40 so the 64-entry traversal stack always holds), its own light sampling (emissive triangles by area × emitted luminance, a uniform point, the solid-angle pdf), balance-heuristic MIS against the BSDF (or one strategy alone, `mis_mode`, weighted as the shader does), PCG32 random numbers seeded by splitmix64 of pixel and sample (no Sobol, no ReSTIR, no reservoirs). So agreement checks the GPU's sampling, sample sequences, acceleration structures and shader arithmetic against an independent path tracer of the same model; it does not check the model against physics (the rough-glass blend and the glass-shadow shortcut are the shader's approximations, copied on purpose).
- **Refused scenes** (`reference::unsupported`, listed in the error): none since battle mode (2026-11-10, below); BDPT and photon mapping render since (below). LPE AOVs render since 2026-11-10 (below). Everything else in the earlier list renders, each in its own bullet below, spectral mode included (its film, gratings, dispersion, measured spectra and blackbodies). Measured spectra and blackbodies render as their RGB colour outside spectral mode, as the GPU does.
- **ReSTIR DI (2026-11-10):** renders, by its estimator rather than its reservoirs (which change only noise). At the first hit, for the share of samples the GPU gives reservoir layers (`min(spp, 4)`, fewer past 1 GiB: `lights::restir_share`, drawn per sample), the environment keeps its own strategy and MIS, the light table is sampled alone with weight 1, so BSDF hits on what it covers (all but a sphere light from inside) and on suns count 0, and procedural emitters get no light sample and count in full from the BSDF. With a bounce to spare this equals MIS in expectation; at `bounces` 1 it does not (table light in full, procedural lights none), which is why it was refused (plain path tracing: preset:0 1.0122, z 71). Neither side is wrong: it is the GPU's bounce-limit convention, kept. Measured (320 × 240, GPU 1024 / CPU 512 spp, `--set restir=true --set bounces=1`): preset:0 0.9994 (z −2.94), light-temperature-row 0.9993 (z −2.63), veach-mis 1.0005 (z 0.02) with one block 4.49× from a GPU firefly that is there at default bounces too and gone at GPU 4096 / CPU 2048 (0.9907, z −0.64, worst block 1.0064). Default bounces with ReSTIR: preset:0 0.9995 (z −2.86). nighthawks at `bounces` 1 fails the oracle's block test with the GPU's reuse on (GPU 1024 / CPU 512: block (6, 7) 1.1741, z 18.9; 4096 / 2048: (5, 1) 0.8452, z −17.5), but it is the oracle's independence assumption, not a CPU bug: spatial plus temporal reuse spreads one lucky sample over many pixels and frames, so a dim block's error is far above std(d) / √n. The bad block wanders, and block (5, 1) converges, 0.756 → 0.845 → 0.921 at GPU 1024 / 4096 / 16384 (the CPU's is steady), which a bias would not. With either reuse alone it passes (neighbours 0: 1.0021, z 0.78, worst 1.0099; history 0: 1.0012, z 0.54, worst 1.0071), and with both off 1.0004 (z 0.19, worst 1.0051) at 1024 / 512 and at 4096 / 2048 worst block 0.9514 (z −3.32); the 4096 / 2048 image mean 0.9558 (z −0.97) is one CPU pixel at 22.2 (a light sampled alone, no MIS; without it 1.0001). Measure ReSTIR scenes with `--set restir_neighbors=0 --set restir_history=0`: the converged image is the same.
- **Measured** (320 × 240, CPU 512–1024 spp against GPU 512–1024 spp with `--raw --no-rr --scale 1 --set firefly=0`; the CPU takes 1.4 s per 256 spp of the Cornell box on this machine): the Cornell box, veach-mis, mirror-room, white-furnace, nighthawks, gem-display, aquarium and shape-material-matrix agree in their channel means to 0.2–0.8% (most within 0.1%), the 8 × 8 blocks within their noise except shape-material-matrix (two blocks at 0.93, about 3.5 standard errors, explained below). cornell-water-tank and glass-of-water differed by 3–6% (blocks to 1.3–1.6, under the water): the water's bottom face lies on the floor (grounded, coplanar), which each renderer resolves its own way; lifted 1 cm, the tank agrees to 0.04%. So coplanar dielectric contacts make a scene ambiguous, not a renderer wrong. The first run found a reference bug of its own (shadow rays stopping short by less than their origin's offset hit the light itself: up-facing surfaces got no direct light).
- **In the viewport (2026-11-04):** Look → Compare → *Render reference on CPU* runs it in the background (`reference::Live`, one thread pool of all cores but one) at the output size and shows its running mean left of the A/B split, through the same display pass as the GPU (exposure, tone map, look, HDR), so CPU and GPU sit side by side. `Progressive` keeps per-pixel f32 sums and adds passes; each sample is still seeded by pixel and sample index, so any split into passes gives the same bits as the CLI's one pass (test `passes_match_one_render`). Passes start at 1 spp and are sized to about half a second (at most 64 spp); after each, the mean is uploaded into the reference texture (13, `Gpu::upload_reference`). It renders up to *Max samples*. An edit that changes the transport key, a camera move or a resize starts it over (the split shows the GPU until the first pass); *Freeze reference* replaces it; a refused scene shows the list of missing features under the button. While it runs an idle viewport still redraws every 200 ms to show new passes. Self-test frames 169–172.
- **Time and frame state (2026-11-06):** the CLI renders `reference::prepare(s, t)`: `at_time` (the tracks and per-frame script at `t`, physics and cloth stepped at 1/60 s as a video export steps them, strings following their objects, then frozen: tracks and script cleared, physics off, and `rotate` turned into `yaw + rotate_speed · t` and cleared, without which the GPU's main loop kept turning a camera the CPU held still), then what the app adds to each frame's copy (the flashlight, the laser puzzle's rods). `--time T` hands the frozen scene to either renderer, so both see the same frame even with physics, which on the GPU otherwise runs on the wall clock; `tools/oracle.py --time T` passes it to both sides. The viewport's frame copy already holds all of it, so `Live` takes that copy. **What this does not measure:** both sides call the same `at_time`, so a `--time` row shows the CPU renders the frozen scene as the GPU does, not that `at_time` matches playback. Against `video.rs` it was compared by reading only: the same logic, except for steps of t / ceil(60 t) in place of the video's frame steps, and a video export holds `rotate` off.
- **The software view (2026-11-07):** `--cpu`, Menu → *Render on the CPU*, the GPU / CPU switch in the top bar and in the Render tab show the CPU's render in the whole viewport (`App::cpu_view`, not saved with a scene). It reuses the A/B path: only the frame's copy given to `Gpu::render` carries `compare` and a `split` of 2, which `cpu_view()` in post.hlsl reads as "everything is the reference", also in `linear_output` (so EXR saves and the light meter read the CPU's values) and in `scene_at` (so glare and halation see them); below 1 the shader's path is unchanged. Exposure, tone map, look, LUT, zebras, scopes and HDR are the normal display pass. The GPU traces one pass per restart (`draw = samples == 0`), for what clicks read (ids, depth) and the meter's albedo, then idles; the loop wakes every 33 ms while the CPU works. PNG, JPEG, EXR and HDR saves take the CPU's image, with its sample count; layers are refused; high-resolution stills and videos still render on the GPU, and the Export tab says so. Everything is still drawn by a GPU that can trace rays: the machine without one comes later.
- **`Live` as a worker (2026-11-07):** one thread and a slot for the newest request (`follow`), replacing a restart per settled key. A request that differs from the running scene only in the camera (`same_but_camera`: the fields `redraw`'s camera-move test ignores) keeps the BVH and light tables (`Progressive::move_camera`); `a_moved_camera_matches_a_fresh_render` holds it to the same bits. Each restart of the view starts with a preview, one sample in the middle of each block spread over the block, block side chosen so it takes about 50 ms (`preview_block`, 1 to 32). A preview is neither cancelled by the next request nor dropped as stale: while the camera moves each frame brings a request, and either rule alone means no image for the whole drag (the first version had both: one preview image an orbit). Compare keeps its contract: it refuses what `unsupported` lists, and has no image until its first pass. The view renders what it knows and lists the rest on the viewport (`Progressive::partial`, measuring never goes through it). Workers are two fewer than the cores and run at low priority (`platform::background_thread`). Space pauses it after the running pass. Measured at 1280 × 720, `--orbit-test --no-vsync`, preset 0, under `target/safe.sh` (24 cores, so 22 workers): frame 7.2 ms against 7.0 ms for the GPU view, 71 previews in 200 orbit frames (49 a second); rerun 2026-11-09: 7.07 ms against 6.94 ms, 44 previews (31 a second). A captured EXR of the view, 16 spp on preset 0 at 320 × 240, is bit-identical to `--reference 16` (2026-11-09), and self-test frame 305 holds the save buttons' EXR to the view's image.
- **Vulkan's stale reference (found 2026-11-07):** `resize_output` left `reference_valid` set while recreating texture 13, unlike D3D12, so Compare's split read an empty texture after a resize or an export until the next image. It clears it now. The GPU's own picture is untouched.
- **Accumulation (2026-11-06):** per-pixel sums are f64 (`glam::DVec3`), so small samples beside a large one aren't lost.
- **Module layout (2026-11-06):** `mod.rs` (refusals, `run`), `prepare.rs`, `progressive.rs` (`Progressive`, `Live`, the view's previews), `scene.rs` (`Scene::new`, `Surface`, ray kinds), `bsdf.rs`, `bvh.rs`, `lights.rs`, `camera.rs`, `media.rs`, `sampling.rs`, `integrators/path.rs`. The split was a pure move: EXR hashes of five scenes were identical before and after.
- **What the oracle found in the CPU renderer (2026-11-06):** two CPU bugs, both from reading the world-space `Vertex` differently from the GPU's instances, fixed. (1) The side and the ray offsets came from the interpolated normal. The GPU has used the face normal for this since it found the same bug. On smooth meshes rays started on the wrong side, which darkened the emissive rows of shape-material-matrix by 0.4% (z 19). (2) `geometry::object_geometry` writes unit world normals, and interpolating those isn't what the GPU does: it interpolates in object space and then transforms, and under non-uniform scale the two differ (orrery's rings at stretch 0.15, far side 20% too bright, z -8.7). `interpolation_normal` undoes the normalisation, so the CPU interpolates the same field. The GPU was right both times.
- **Grazing shadow rays and `mis_mode` (2026-11-06), both CPU bugs:** the shadow ray stopped a fixed 2 mm along the ray, and from an origin offset toward the emitter a grazing ray crosses the emitter's plane 0.0003 / |cos| before the sampled point, so the light shadowed itself: the bug the GPU fixed on 2026-11-02 ("Grazing shadow rays"). The stop is now 0.6 mm off the plane along the normal (`shadow_limit`). At 2048 spp, `--time 0.7`, before / after: penumbra-ladder 1.0020 (z 32.8) / 1.0001 (z 2.2), flavin-fluorescent 1.0057 (z 22.5) / 1.0000 (z 0.0), fairy-lights with `restir=false` 1.0002 (z 1.4) after, snow-globe's block 1.0911 (z 12.8) / 1.0259 (z 2.6), colonnade-night z 5.3 / 1.0001 (z 0.6). The GPU was right. `mis_mode` used to be ignored, not refused (mode 2 at 2 bounces 0.8819, z -190); now on preset 0, modes 1 and 2 give 1.0001 (z 1.4) and 1.0003 (z 0.7) at the default bounces, 1.0001 (z 1.4) and 0.9999 (z -0.1) at 2. `rotate=true --time 1` went from 0.9355 (z -5.8) to 1.0002 (z 1.7).
- **Sweep of every built-in scene that renders (2026-11-06, 320 x 240, 2048 spp each side, `--time 0.7 --rgb`):** 48 render as stored; blackbody-forge and measured-cornell also render with `spectral=false`. 44 of those 50 pass, and all but the ones below sit under |z| 3 or so in the mean. The six fails: cornell-water-tank 1.0411 (z 88), stained-glass-hall 1.0849 (z 37, block 1.4163), glass-stack 0.9955 (z -17, block 0.9451), glass-of-water 0.9973 (z -6), aquarium 1.0058 (z 6), layered-cocktail 1.0002 (z 0.5, block 0.9673, z -6). All six are coplanar contacts, below. Passes worth a second look: shape-material-matrix's block 0.9835 (z -4.4, the same contact) and fairy-lights, next item.
- **GPU ReSTIR, a small excess, not traced:** with stored `restir=true` fairy-lights is 1.0010 (z 5.7) at 2048 spp and 1.0005 (z 6.4) at 8192; with `restir=false` 1.0002 (z 1.4) and 1.0002 (z 2.7). It shrinks with samples, so it isn't shown to be a fixed bias. preset 0 with `restir=true` is unsteady: its worst block was (6, 1) 0.9940 (z -3.5) at 2048, (7, 2) 0.9934 (z -10.7) at 8192 and (6, 1) 0.9971 (z -4.1) at GPU 32768 / CPU 8192, against 0.9981 (z -2.9) with it off. Below the oracle's floor, so these pass; the CPU has no ReSTIR to be wrong about.
- **Coplanar dielectric contacts: closed 2026-11-10 by the dielectric gap (section "Grounded dielectric gap").** Where two faces meet at equal t and one is a dielectric, the two renderers order them differently (DXR against this BVH), and neither is right. Measured at 4096 spp, GPU/CPU: cornell-water-tank 1.0409 (z 105), glass-of-water 0.9971 (z -8), aquarium 1.0059 (z 8.5; rows 79-80 alone, at 1.40, where the air box and the water share side faces in their 1 cm overlap; 1.0021 without them), shape-material-matrix blocks 0.98 under the grounded glass. Separated by 1 cm (the air box narrowed 1%), the same scenes give 1.0002 (z 0.9), 0.9998 (z -0.7), 1.0002 (z 0.3); the matrix with its glass lifted gives 1.0003 (z 4.8, 8192 spp) with every block at or under z 4.4. Three more from the sweep: stained-glass-hall (8.5%, the largest) with panes shifted 6 mm per column 0.9999 (z -1.1); glass-stack with its glass objects lifted 1 cm cumulatively 1.0004 (z 1.6); layered-cocktail needs both its contacts gone, its same-radius neighbouring liquids narrowed 1% and the glasses lifted 1 cm 1.0000 (z -0.1, worst block z -3.2), against narrowing alone with a block still at 1.0422 (z 5.2). These are the shape-material-matrix "two blocks at 0.93": the bias is in the geometry, not in either renderer.
- **Measured after these fixes (2026-11-06, 320 x 240, 4096 spp each side unless noted):** preset 0 1.0001 (z 0.9), veach-mis 1.0005 (z 0.7), mirror-room 0.9998 (z -0.5), white-furnace 1.0001 (z 1.2), nighthawks 1.0006 (z 1.0), gem-display 1.0129 (z 1.8, noise), shape-material-matrix 1.0000 (z 0.6), orrery --time 1.5 at 8192 spp 1.0003 (z 3.1, every block within noise), bowling-lane at t = 0 1.0005 (z 1.5). At 2048 spp with `--time 1.5`: pool-break-1984 1.0001 (z 0.4), torii-corridor 1.0000 (z 0.2), newtons-cradle 1.0002 (z 1.2), castle 1.0000 (z 0.0), festival-banners with the sky and sheen stripped (cloth) 1.0000 (z -1.6), swinging-lantern with sheen stripped 1.0004 (z 2.0). laser-1 with `beam` on (rods) 1.0002 (z 2.3), flashlight-crypt with an unreadable IES so the cone stands in 1.0003 (z 2.2); the flashlight's real beam waits for IES. Still open and small: veach-mis's image-border blocks 1.007 (z 8), bowling-lane's block (4, 7) 1.0064 (z 9), the matrix's lifted mean above.
- **Camera projections (2026-11-09):** `Scene::camera_ray` dispatches on the projection as `camera_ray` does, from the view rows (right, up, -forward): orthographic, equirectangular, little planet; perspective and tilt-shift without an aperture keep the pinhole ray, so existing renders are unchanged. Depth of field and lens systems render too (see Depth of field). Oracle at 320 x 240, 1024 / 512 spp: panorama-gallery (equirectangular) 1.0000 (z -0.2), preset 0 orthographic 1.0002 (z 0.9), mirror-room orthographic 0.9995 (z -0.3), mirror-room little planet 1.0007 (z 0.7). little-planet and isometric-workshop render too (sky and sun lights are supported). Test: `camera_rays_follow_each_projection`, `henyey_greenstein_is_normalised_and_sampled`.
- **Firefly clamp (2026-11-09):** after four samples each sample is scaled to at most `firefly` times the luminance of the pixel's mean so far (floor 0.05), as the GPU does with the mean at its frame's start; the two are the same estimator at one sample a frame, which `tools/oracle.py --firefly X` renders (it also passes X to the CPU; use equal spp, the bias depends on the count). At 320 x 240, 256 spp each side, firefly=2: veach-mis 0.9900 (z -0.8), preset 0 1.0004 (z 0.7); the clamp removed 15% and 8% of the CPU mean there, so it is exercised. Test: `firefly_clamp_scales_only_outliers_after_four_samples`.
- **Clear coat and sheen (2026-11-09):** `eval_bsdf` layers a Charlie sheen (Estevez and Kulla's fitted visibility, capped at albedo 1) over the base and a clear coat (IOR 1.5, compensated by 1/E) over both, each dimming what lies below by its view-direction albedo from layers.hlsli's COAT_ALBEDO and SHEEN_ALBEDO; the coat takes 0.3 × strength of the BSDF samples, the sheen rides on the cosine samples. Oracle at 320 x 240, 1024 / 512 spp: layered-materials 1.0002 (z 0.7), velvet-lounge 1.0003 (z 2.0), layer-sweeps 1.0003 (z 1.6), preset 0 0.9999 (z -0.4). Test: `coat_and_sheen_layers_conserve_energy`.
- **Environment, shadow catcher, physical sky (2026-11-09):** `reference/environment.rs` keeps its own f64 row and column CDFs from the map's texels (the HDRI from `environment::load`, the sky from `sky::bake_cached`, with stars and moon), samples the split sun's disc by its share, and weighs misses with MIS against `p_env` (half the light samples with emitters, all without); radiance is bilinear and the pdf the nearest texel's, as on the GPU. A light sample's pdf is the texel it was drawn from: recomputed from the direction, cos θ in f32 near the poles put it a row off, beside a star 1000× brighter, and a starry sky came out 18% too bright. Ground projection and the shadow catcher follow `camera_chain`. The physical sky's sun above the horizon renders as an analytic sun light (see Sun lights). Measured (320 x 240, GPU 1024 / CPU 512 spp): hdri-shadow-catcher (split sun, ground 1.7, catcher) 0.9994 (z -0.7), hdri-noon-spheres 0.9998 (z -0.03), desert-monolith under a night sky (time 23.5, stars 1000, intensity 1000) 0.9986 (z -0.4), at twilight without stars 1.0000 (z 0.3); preset 0 0.9999 (z -0.4) and veach-mis 1.0013 (z 0.7) unchanged.
- **Fog box (2026-11-09):** grey uniform fog outside every object, by free flight with Henyey-Greenstein light sampling and the fog's transmittance on every shadow ray (`media.rs`). `Fog::equiangular` only changes the GPU's sampling, so the CPU ignores it. Oracle at 320x240, 1024 GPU / 256 CPU spp: cornell-fog 1.0000 (z 0.03), weather-project 1.0006 (z 0.74), disco-floor 1.0280 (z 0.53). Test: `henyey_greenstein_is_normalised_and_sampled`.
- **Sphere lights (2026-11-09):** one table entry per sphere (power 4πr² × luminance, as `emitter_table`), its inscribed mesh mapped to that entry (`tri_light`) so a BSDF hit is weighed, not counted twice. NEE draws a direction uniformly in the cone the true sphere covers (`sphere_om`, `cone_sample`, pdf 1/(2π om)) with the shadow ray stopping 0.6 mm before the true surface; from inside (om 0) there is no sample and a hit counts in full. Oracle at 320 x 240, GPU 1024 / CPU 512 spp: bulb-workshop (5 spheres) 1.0005 (z 0.8, worst block z 2.6), preset 0 0.9999 (z -0.4). Tests: `a_sphere_lights_strategies_agree`, `a_sphere_light_is_one_entry`, `a_camera_inside_a_sphere_light_stays_finite`.
- **Sun lights and the physical sky's sun (2026-11-09):** one table entry per sun (`lighting::analytic`, so the sky's sun above the horizon comes with it), weighted by irradiance over the scene's projected area (luminance × π sin² × `Analytic::area`, as `emitter_table`), with the emission and the hidden flag (2) read from its material record. NEE draws uniformly in its disc (pdf 1/(2π om)), shadow ray to 1e4; a ray that leaves the scene adds every sun whose disc holds it (sin² from a cross product, which resolves a 0.05° sun), MIS-weighted like a triangle hit, hidden suns from bounce 1 on (`Scene::suns`). Test: `a_sun_lights_as_defined`. Oracle at 320 x 240, GPU 1024 / CPU 512 spp, all pass: sun-greenhouse 0.9818 (z -0.46; its sun-lit glass keeps the image noisy, worst block 4.1 at z 2.2), sky-garden-dusk 1.0052 (z 0.86), desert-monolith 0.9999 (z -2.72), sunlit-room-day-cycle 1.0011 (z 0.11), preset 0 0.9999 (z -0.41).
- **IES profiles and the flashlight (2026-11-09):** `bsdf::spot` reads a light's profile (flag 16384) back from the 20 words `lighting::materials` packs (`ies::pack`: three bytes each in layers, sheen, base_scale, surface_scale, medium.xyz, phase.x), linear in angle over 0–90°, zero at or behind 90°, and it replaces a spot's cone; files and `beam:` specs both arrive through `ies::profile`, and the CLI's `prepare` adds the camera flashlight as the app adds it to each frame. Selection stays power-only, as on the GPU. Test: `ies_profile_reads_the_packed_samples`. Oracle at 320 x 240, GPU 1024 / CPU 512 spp, all pass: ies-wall 0.9995 (z -0.91), bunny-museum 0.9774 (z -1.05, noisy), flashlight-crypt 1.0010 (z 1.94, worst block z -2.41).
- **Depth of field (2026-11-10):** perspective and tilt-shift take the thin lens in `camera::camera_ray`: each lens point (`aperture_point`: area-uniform disk, or an n-gon as one fan triangle then a folded uniform point, rotated by the blade rotation) aims at where the pinhole ray meets the focal plane, perpendicular to the axis or tilted about the right axis; the cat-eye returns None for a point outside the unit circle shifted by ndc·(aspect,1)·cat_eye·0.7, which counts as a black sample, as on the GPU. The pinhole draws no random numbers, so scenes without an aperture are bit-identical. No area normalisation: blades keep the brightness. Measured (320 x 240, GPU 1024 / CPU 512 spp): preset 0 aperture 0.1 focus 3 1.0005 (z 1.16); plus 6 blades, 20°, cat-eye 0.5 0.9998 (z -0.39); tilt-shift 10° 1.0007 (z 1.56); abacus 1.0005 (z 1.33). The reported 14% drop of abacus's GPU mean with its aperture is the cat-eye, not lost energy: with `cat_eye=0` the mean is 0.1681 against 0.1681 at aperture 0, and 0.1440 with 0.5 (a uniform average over the frame keeps 80% of lens points at 0.5); the barrel's vignetting is the intended look.
- **Real lens systems and their dispersion (2026-11-10):** with `Settings::real_lens` the camera ray comes from `lens::pack`'s buffer (`camera::RealLens`, built in `set_camera`): a film point (inverted), a uniform point in its band's exit-pupil bounds turned to the point's angle, traced surface by surface out of the front element (sphere caps, rim and stop tests, `refract` with the film-side index over the object-side one), the stop polygonal through `inside_stop` with the aperture's blades and rotation. Weight cos⁴ at the film × bounds area × `pack`'s centre normalisation seeds the path throughput (`radiance` now takes it); a blocked sample counts as black. With dispersion one channel is drawn per sample at weight 3. Spectral lens tracing (the hero wavelength, `lens_ior` c = 3) waits for the spectral group. Tests `lens_systems_weigh_one_at_the_centre` (all five within 3% of 1 at the centre; per channel with dispersion; red and blue leave apart off-axis), `the_bladed_stop_holds_the_aperture_samples`. Oracle preset 0, f/4, focus 4.7, 320 x 240, GPU 1024 / CPU 512 spp (fisheye 2048): Double Gauss 1.0001 (z 0.17), wide angle 1.0006 (z 0.79), fisheye 1.0010 (z 2.17), singlet 1.0011 (z 1.50), telephoto 1.0007 (z 1.25); Double Gauss with 6 blades 1.0004 (z 0.50); singlet with dispersion 1 at its defaults 1.0017 (z 1.37). GPU and CPU agree; no GPU change.
- **Portals (2026-11-10):** in `radiance`, after the surface and before shading, a front face of a flag-4 record maps the ray through the pair's affine map (the three rows of the record `secondary.w` points at, `path::portal_map`) and nudges it 1 mm along the new direction; a back face passes straight; neither is a bounce, a front crossing sets `previous_delta` (light sampling never sees through a portal), and after 16 crossings a portal shades as its white record, as `trace` does. The refusal scans now skip the map records (`material_records`): their matrix rows read as flags and gratings, so a portal with a negative z translation was refused as a diffraction grating too. Test: `a_portal_maps_its_centre_to_its_pairs_and_turns_the_ray_around`. Oracle at 320 x 240, GPU 1024 / CPU 512 spp: bigger-on-the-inside 0.9999 (z -0.54, worst block 1.0047, z 2.80), infinite-corridor (the 16-crossing cap) 0.9999 (z -0.19, worst block 1.0144, z 3.07). No GPU change.
- **Beam splitters (2026-11-10):** in `radiance`, after the portals, a flag-4096 record is a thin partial mirror while the path's crossing count (shared with portals) is under 64: a front face tints the throughput by the patterned colour and reflects about the shading normal with probability `reflectivity` (`lobes.y`), else passes straight; a back face passes untinted, so a closed box splits once, not twice. Either way the ray is nudged 0.5 mm along its direction and `previous_delta` is set; only a reflection is a bounce, and the sheet's own emission is never seen. Shadow rays still see the sheet as opaque (`lobes.z < 0.5`), as the GPU's do, so with light sampling the last bounce loses the light beyond a clear sheet; the test therefore uses BSDF sampling. Test: `a_beam_splitter_mixes_a_clear_sheet_and_a_mirror` (white at reflectivity 0 matches no sheet within 3%, 0.5 is the mean of 0 and 1). Oracle at 320 x 240, GPU 1024 / CPU 512 spp, `--time 0`: laser-3 1.0000 (z 0.14, worst block 1.0014, z 2.40). optics-bench (fog off) fails with or without the splitter flag (0.36, z -0.97, worst block 0.953, z -5.0, the same with `splitter: false`): the narrow spot through lenses is a caustic path tracing doesn't converge, not the splitter. No GPU change.
- **Motion blur on the CPU (2026-11-10; the viewport half was removed the same day, see "Keep-exposure mode"):** `--reference SPP --reference-time T` renders a timeline export's frame at T (`reference::exposure`): with the shutter open and keys, 32 moments at the middles of equal slices of the shutter centred on T (the GPU takes one per pass at golden-ratio steps), each a `prepare`d scene with its own BVH, and every sample draws one (`Progressive::over`; a still draws nothing, so its bits are unchanged); auto-rotation off as in the export; a simulated scene is refused. `tools/oracle.py --blur-frame N` compares it with the GPU's EXR export of frame N. Test `an_open_shutter_blurs_the_frame_over_its_moments`. Oracle at 320 x 240, GPU 1024 / CPU 512 spp: pool-break-1984 frame 2 (shutter 0.5, the break) `--blur-frame 2` 1.0000 (z -0.02, worst block 0.9608, z -2.63); preset 0 still 0.9999 (z -0.41), unchanged. No GPU image change.
- **Texture sets, box-mapped base colour (2026-11-09):** `reference/textures.rs` binds maps through `render::attach_maps` itself (slots, flag 16, value / average) and box-maps sets on the walls, the floor (world axes) and shapes (object axes at world size, the object normal being the transpose of the CPU's world normal, exact by `interpolation_normal`), after emission as on the GPU, so an emitter keeps its unmapped colour. Sampling is bilinear at level 0 with wrapping, sRGB decoded first, not the GPU's ray cones: the reference is what a converged image tends to, and supersampling level 0 over the pixel filter is that; the cones' trilinear, anisotropic prefilter is the GPU's noise trade, vendor-specific in its footprints. Still refused: maps on mesh UVs (the mesh's own, `texture_uv`), normal, metallic-roughness and emissive maps, alpha cutouts. Oracle (sets reduced to their colour map, 320 x 240, GPU 1024 / CPU 512 spp): texture-brick-box 0.9997 (z -0.8), texture-showroom 1.0023 (z 6.4, worst block 1.0078), texture-rock-garden 1.0026 (z 12.9); the same scenes untextured 1.0001 (z 0.5) and 1.0001 (z 0.7). The excess is the GPU's prefilter: rock-garden at 960 x 720 (two mip levels finer) is 1.0010 (z 8.9). Tests: `bilinear_level_0_is_exact_at_texel_centres_wraps_and_mixes_in_linear`, `box_mapping_lays_each_face_out_as_the_shader`, `colour_and_roughness_sets_bind_as_the_gpus`.
- **Mesh UVs, metallic-roughness and normal maps (2026-11-10):** `Scene::new` copies each mapped object's mesh UVs into its vertices (`plan::mesh_uvs`, as the GPU's instances carry them; `geometry` leaves them zero), and `Textures::map_frame` derives uv and tangent as `intersect` does: box mapping with its dP/du and sign on the walls, floor and shapes, else the triangle's UV frame (`uv_frame`, world edges over the UV determinant, which are the object's derivatives under its affine map; the bitangent sign in object space; none for degenerate UVs) with |w| repeats for `texture_uv`. Roughness (G) and metal (B) replace the record's where their scale is nonzero; the normal map (`normal_map`, unrenormalized texel, tangent made perpendicular, z kept above 1e-3) bends the shading normal after it faces the ray, so offsets and the side stay the face normal's. glTF tangents are read on neither side. Still refused: emissive maps and alpha cutouts. Oracle (320 x 240, GPU 1024 / CPU 512 spp): texture-brick-box 1.0098 (z 27), texture-showroom 1.0086 (z 21), texture-duck-on-wood 0.9931 (z -8.9) fail, and the cause is the GPU's prefilter, not the model: brick-box with only colour + roughness maps 1.0003 (z 1.0); with only colour + normal maps 1.0091 (z 25), direct light alone 1.0057, but with the texels magnified (texture_scale 0.04, so the GPU reads mip 0) 1.0003 (z 1.0) direct and 1.0016 (z 6.0) with bounces, the rest being the cone that a diffuse bounce widens by 0.5 rad and so flattens the normals indirect rays see; brick-box at 960 x 720 1.0078. duck-on-wood with only the duck's own map (mesh UVs) 1.0010 (z 2.3), with the sets' texels magnified 0.9986 (z -1.9); preset 0 0.9999 (z -0.4). Mip-averaged normals and roughness are a nonlinear prefilter of the shading, unlike colour, so a converged GPU image of a normal-mapped scene differs from the reference by about 1% at this size. Tests: `a_triangles_uv_frame_is_its_edges_per_uv_unit`, `a_flat_texel_keeps_the_normal_and_green_tilts_along_the_signed_bitangent`, `colour_and_roughness_sets_bind_as_the_gpus`, `a_meshs_cutout_renders` (UVs reach the triangles).
- **Emissive maps (2026-11-10):** flag 64 (`attach_maps`: secondary = slot + 1 and the mean emitted colour over its peak, medium.xyz = 1 / the map's mean) renders. `Scene::emission_colour` gives `emitted` its colour: the level-0 bilinear texel at the triangle's mesh UV (unscaled, read even under box mapping, as the GPU's emitter UV records), times secondary.yzw and medium.xyz, else the material's own; BSDF hits read it at the hit's barycentrics, light samples at the sampled ones (no emitter record needed). The light table weighs triangles by `lighting::emitted` (the mean colour), uniform over each, as the GPU's, so MIS is unchanged; `mesh_uvs` also fills UVs for flag 64. Oracle (320 x 240, GPU 4096 / CPU 2048 spp): a quad with a flat emissive map 0.9999 (z -1.9); with an 8 x 8 checker of hard black edges, magnified, 0.9993 (z -9.1), the emitter's share 0.9987 (z -8.1) both seen directly and reflected, which the flat map rules out of the model: the hardware sampler's fixed-point bilinear weights. Lantern.glb (emission 4 in cornell-bunny) 1.0055 (z 13), with emission 0 1.0034 (z 32): the lantern's normal and roughness maps, the known prefilter difference, which its own bulb light lights at close range (emitter pixels seen directly 1.0009). Test: `an_emissive_map_emits_its_texel_over_its_mean`.
