- **Alpha cutouts and stochastic blend (2026-11-10):** flag 8 (`attach_maps`: a base map and a nonzero cutoff in maps.w) renders in camera, bounce and shadow rays. `Bvh::intersect` hands `accept` each candidate in range as (material, triangle, barycentrics), as an any-hit shader sees it, and `Scene::accept` drops holes before the kind's rule, so a hole takes no bounce and no false hit. `Scene::alpha_hole` reads the base map's level-0 bilinear alpha at the raw vertex UVs (`mesh_uvs` fills them for flag 8 too; texture sets never have a cutoff, so box mapping never reaches it): MASK (c >= 0, already over the factor's alpha) is a hole where alpha < c; BLEND (c = −factor alpha) is opaque with probability alpha × −c, from a hash of the ray (origin and direction bits), the triangle and b.x rather than a sampler draw, so one ray decides the same way for a candidate and scenes without blending keep their random streams. Oracle (cornell box with a textured glTF quad, 320 x 240, GPU 4096 / CPU 2048 spp): a uniform-grey 2 x 2 map with alphas 1, 0.5, 0.78, 0 under MASK 0.6 1.0001 (z 1.4), under BLEND × 0.8 1.0001 (z 1.5). With differently coloured texels MASK is 1.0032 (z 39) and the opaque quad 1.0009 (z 9.8), direct light alone 1.0000 (z -0.4): the GPU's cones read mip 1 on indirect hits, whose colour averages in the transparent texel's white, a colour prefilter (ray-cone item), not the cutout. Sponza without its sun (physical_sky off, main light on, sky 0.4) 1.0092 (z 7.4), the normal-map prefilter difference above. Tests: `alpha_cutouts_and_stochastic_blend` (each ray type through the hole and on the solid texel; BLEND blocks 80% of 20000 rays within 4 sigma, stable per ray), `a_meshs_cutout_renders`.
- **Anisotropic GGX, including spun (2026-11-10):** layers.w > 0 renders. `Scene::tangent` gives the hit's brushing direction from the raw record as `intersect` does (world X on the walls, the object's x axis, or for spun brushing, flag 2048, the circle y × (p − origin) about its y axis, from `Textures::object_axes`); `aniso_frame` projects it onto the (mapped) shading normal and turns it by sheen.w. `eval_bsdf` takes the tangent: alpha along T, lerp(alpha, 1, w²) across, the anisotropic D and Smith G1, the 12 × 4 × 8 × 6 `ANISO_ALBEDO` table (view azimuth from T) for the compensation, and the visible-normal pdf G1(v)·D / 4nv; the base lobe samples `ggx_sample_aniso` (Heitz 2018). The coat stays isotropic; an isotropic material takes the old path unchanged. Oracle (320 x 240, GPU 1024 / CPU 512 spp): brushed-metal 0.9998 (z -0.31, worst block 0.9932), layered-furnace 1.0002 (z 0.79), preset 0 0.9999 (z -0.41). GPU and CPU agree; nothing in the GPU changed. Tests: `anisotropy_reduces_to_isotropic`, `anisotropic_white_metal_furnace` (f·cos/pdf averages 1 within 3% at four views).
- **Thin film in RGB (2026-11-10):** surface_scale.z > 0 renders outside spectral mode. `bsdf::film_fresnel` is the two-order Airy sum against the Gaussian CMF fit (`film_sensitivity`, Khronos constants), the film fading into the outside below 30 nm; `prepare_film` evaluates it once per hit at the view cosine into the hit copy's base_scale.rgb after the maps, with the IORs on both sides of a dielectric from the media stack (`Scene::interface`, shared with the transmission branch) or (1, opaque base from f0). `spec_fresnel` blends the base Fresnel toward it by strength in `eval_bsdf`, and the compensation uses its normal-incidence value; the dielectric branch chooses reflection by the mean of the blended reflectance and weights per channel. Gratings and the spectral film stay refused (spectral mode is). Oracle (320 x 240, GPU 1024 / CPU 512 spp): thin-film 1.0001 (z 0.37, worst block 0.9975), channels 1.0000 / 1.0001 / 1.0001; gem-display 1.0127 (z 0.94) and aquarium 1.0058 (z 3.43) still pass. GPU and CPU agree; nothing in the GPU changed. Test: `thin_film_decodes_and_stays_in_range`.
- **Diffraction gratings in RGB (2026-11-10):** optical.w < 0 renders outside spectral mode (spectral stays refused as a whole). `bsdf::grating` decodes strength and spacing (an IES record is not read as one) and `Scene::tangent` now also gives a grating its groove direction. Each grating hit draws its wavelength once from a forked stream (`Rng::fork`, keyed on the path's state and the bounce, so the main stream and every non-grating scene stay as they were): one of the three Gaussian channel curves, Box-Muller, clamped 300–900 nm, RGB weight 3·N_c/ΣN. Light sampling and the BSDF sample at the hit share it, so MIS stays exact. `eval_bsdf` takes it (0 = none): each order is the isotropic GGX lobe moved across the grooves (`grating_move`), the mirror keeps 1 − Σ shares, pdf_o = pdf(l0)·cos l / cos l0; sampling picks an order by `order_shares` and ends the path when the moved sample falls past the horizon, as the GPU. GPU and CPU agree on the model; no GPU bug found. Unit test: a white 4 µm grating keeps 1 ± 3% per channel in a furnace with its pdf integrating to 1, a Blu-ray's 320 nm grooves at a normal view have no orders. Oracle `optical-discs --rgb` (GPU 2048 / CPU 1024 spp, 320×240): GPU/CPU 1.0024, z 0.44, worst block 1.156 at z 2.77 (ok); CPU/GPU per channel 0.997 / 0.998 / 0.994.
- **Chromatic fog (2026-11-09):** `Fog::extinction` is read through `pack_tint` as on the GPU (8 bits per channel relative to the largest), giving a per-channel σ (the GPU and the CPU agree on the model); free flight picks a channel uniformly and weights by T·σ / mean(T·σ) at a scatter, T / mean(T) past the segment (`media::flight`, Kutz et al. 2017), and shadow rays take the per-channel transmittance. Grey fog keeps its single-random-number sampling (cornell-fog and weather-project unchanged: 1.0000 z 0.03, 1.0006 z 0.74). Oracle at 320x240, 1024 GPU / 256 CPU spp, cornell-fog with `extinction [0.4, 0.7, 1]`: 1.0005 (z 0.40, worst block 1.0135 z 2.80); per channel R 1.0009 (z 0.77), G 1.0003 (z 0.26), B 1.0005 (z 0.37). Test: `chromatic_flight_is_unbiased_per_channel`.
- **Heterogeneous fog (2026-11-09):** `Fog::noise` evaluates the same field as the GPU (lowbias32 lattice hash, smoothstep value noise, 4 octaves of fBm at ×2.03 with the same offsets, 1 + noise·(2 fBm − 1) clamped at 0; `media::fog_density`). Free flight is spectral delta tracking against the majorant max σ·(1 + noise) (`delta_tracking`), shadow rays ratio tracking (`ratio_tracking`, 0 below 1e-4), both capped at 1024 steps as on the GPU; uniform fog keeps its exact path. GPU and CPU agree on the model. Oracle at 320x240, 1024 GPU / 512 CPU spp: stage-beams (noise 0.75) 1.0055 (z 0.38, worst block 1.3388 z 1.89); cornell-fog with noise 2, size 0.3: 1.0000 (z −0.03, worst 1.0132 z 3.43), and with `extinction [0.4, 0.7, 1]`: 0.9986 (z −1.26, worst 1.0215 z 3.14); plain cornell-fog unchanged 0.9996 (z −0.44). Test: `heterogeneous_fog_tracking_matches_the_integrated_density` (both estimators against exp(−∫σk) in 4096 steps).
- **Media in objects (2026-11-09):** a transmissive material with `density` > 0 fills itself with a medium of extinction density × its packed tint (`media::extinction`, grey for blackbodies and the measured light as in `extinction_of`), scatter albedo `scatter_color` and HG `phase`, on top of its colour's Beer-Lambert absorption. The innermost dielectric of the stack decides (`object_medium`); fog never applies with a non-empty stack. Free flight runs on the segment after the false-hit loop only, those before it being absorbed alone, as `trace` does (not `walk_step`). Shadow rays (`shadow_transmittance`, the GPU's `medium_transmittance`): inside a scattering object, its extinction and absorption up to the nearest surface, refraction ignored, then the fog past it; inside clear glass 1. The camera's own medium comes from `camera_media` as before. GPU and CPU agree on the model. Oracle at 320x240, 1024 GPU / 512 CPU spp: scattering-materials 0.9999 (z −0.08, worst block 1.0000 z −2.14); sunset-tank with --rgb now 0.9922 (z -0.08, worst block 1.0179 at 4096 / 2048 spp); aquarium 1.0058 (z 3.43), gem-display 1.0127 (z 0.94), cornell-fog 0.9996 (z −0.44) unchanged in model. Tests: `an_objects_extinction_reads_its_tint`, `shadows_through_an_objects_medium`, `a_scattering_object_renders`.
- **Procedural objects (2026-11-10):** `reference/sdf.rs` keeps every visible SDF object (material 5 + index, `Sdf::id`, `object_transform`) beside the BVH; `Scene::intersect` tests them after the triangles with the same accept rules (camera, shadow and glass), carrying the ray into object space unnormalized so t stays the world t. The exact sphere, cylinder and cone are solved in closed form (cube test first, then the quadric and the caps), with analytic normals taken to world by the inverse transpose; the side is the normal's, and ng is the normal. Other kinds are sphere-traced with a tetrahedral gradient (fields below). Procedural objects take no maps (as on the GPU) and their emission is found by hits only (MIS weight 1; no SDF light sampling yet). Oracle at 320 x 240, 1024 / 512 spp, sdf-gallery with only the exact shapes: 1.0003 (z 1.76), but the block under the grounded glass cone fails (1.026, z 9.3): its base cap is coplanar with the floor, and which of the two a ray from inside meets first is a float tie (the CPU tests triangles first and keeps them on a tie; the GPU's order is the traversal's), so the GPU leaks more paths out through the base. Lifted 0.01 off the floor: 0.9997 (z -1.47), worst block z -2.72. Tests: `an_exact_sphere_under_a_transform_is_an_ellipsoid`, `the_exact_cylinder_and_cone_agree_with_their_fields`, `procedural_objects_are_hit_with_the_triangles_rules`.
- **Procedural fields (2026-11-10):** `sdf::field` adds the torus (tube radius `detail`, 0.28 by default), the Mandelbulb (power `detail`, 8 by default, scaled 1.15; power 8 the polynomial form, others the spherical one, as the GPU chooses), the Menger sponge (`detail` levels clamped to 1..6) and the lava-lamp metaballs (the phase, smooth minimum width 0.5), each written from its model in f32. The two Mandelbulb forms are not the same bulb: the polynomial one takes its azimuth from z (Quilez's atan(x, z)) and the spherical one from x, so power 8 and 8.001 are mirror images across x = z (the GPU's comment says the same bulb; a cosmetic discontinuity, left as is). Oracle at 320 x 240, 1024 / 512 spp, with every field: sdf-gallery 0.9996 (z -1.39, worst block 0.9916, z -4.67, inside the ok bound), lava-lamp 1.0010 (z 1.20), preset 0 0.9999 (z -0.41). Test: `procedural_fields_match_their_shapes`.
- **Procedural emitters, light-sampled (2026-11-10):** `lights::SdfLight` keeps each emissive SDF object (max channel × strength > 0) with the sphere bounding its unit cube and a power CDF (emission × r²), the GPU's `sdf_emitter_records` weights. `sample_light` gives them the non-sky samples alone, or half beside emissive triangles (`Scene::sdf_share`; triangles take 1 − share, and the sky takes half once either exists); none with BSDF sampling only. A sample is uniform in the cone the bound covers (the whole sphere from inside it) and counts when a shadow ray's nearest hit that way is the object, with `emitted` (one-sided, spot) and `shadow_limit` against the hit; a bounce that hits one weighs by `sdf_light_pdf`, MIS mode 1 drops it. Oracle at 320×240, 1024/512 spp: sdf-gallery with the torus at emission 8, GPU/CPU 0.9997 (z −0.85); same with mis_mode=1, 0.9973 (z −0.68); lava-lamp 1.0032 (z 3.85, under 4) and plain sdf-gallery 0.9996 (z −1.39) unchanged in mean. Unit test `sdf_emitter_light_sampling`: NEE and hits only agree within 4σ and NEE has lower variance.
- **Nested dielectrics and the camera medium (2026-11-10):** checked against the shader and complete: the stack (priority, later among equals, at most 4), false hits up to 32, the side and offsets from the face normal, eta² and Beer-Lambert, and the camera's medium (`geometry::camera_medium`, the same function the GPU calls) seeding the stack, so a camera inside a scattering object starts its free flight in it (`object_medium` reads the stack's top on every segment, the shader's seeding at path start). Oracle at 320x240, 1024 GPU / 512 CPU spp: underwater (camera in water with density 0.12; physical sky at time 19.2, the sun below the horizon) 0.9988 (z -0.69, worst block 1.0183 z 2.62); gem-display 1.0127 (z 0.94), aquarium 1.0058 (z 3.43; its coplanar contacts, above). Test: `the_camera_starts_in_the_medium_around_it`.
- **glass-of-water and layered-cocktail oracle fails (2026-11-10): coplanar surfaces, not the stack.** glass-of-water fails (1024/512 spp: 0.9972, z -3.47, block 0.9527) with every dielectric but the glass removed too, and only in its base: the grounded glass's foot lies in the floor's plane, the hit at equal t is the BVH's tie (the GPU mostly exits the glass and leaks the sky under the floor, the CPU hits the floor). Lifted 0.01 (glass and water) it passes with the full stack (4096/2048: 0.9999, z -0.30, block 1.0064 z 3.55). layered-cocktail's block (2,2) (4096/2048: 0.9673, z -6.89) needs both Headspace and Blue curaçao: they share scale_xz, so their side walls coincide where they overlap and the tie decides whether the second is entered. Headspace at scale_xz 1.075 removes it (cut-down scene 0.9612 z -8.03 to 1.0003 z 1.76). Neither renderer is wrong; the tie is undefined, so the scenes would need the contacts separated. Still open: the full cocktail's block (5,4) at the tumbler's whisky (GPU about 5% brighter, z 5.6-6.0; from a bright band GPU 0.65 vs CPU 0.48 at most, medians equal), not removed by shrinking Tumbler headspace or lifting Whisky.
- **Denoiser for the CPU view (2026-11-10):** `reference/denoise.rs` is the spatial half of the native filter (post.hlsl `integrate` and the five à-trous passes with the same stops: object id, normal^64, plane distance over the pixel footprint, luminance by the 3x3-blurred variance, coverage restoring edge pixels, demodulation by max(albedo, 0.05)); the temporal half has no job, the view restarting on a camera move. Its inputs come only when asked (`Progressive::keep_guides`): a pixel-centre guide ray, and per sample the first hit's albedo (diffuse + Fresnel specular), whether it hit the guide's object, and the squared luminance; they draw no random numbers, so the sums keep their bits (`the_denoisers_inputs_leave_the_sums_alone`). Off by default: Render tab "Denoise the CPU view" or `--cpu-denoise` (the view, or `--reference`'s output); the view's EXR save writes the plain mean (`Live::raw`), the oracle never passes it, and oracle stays preset 0 0.9999 (z -0.41), veach-mis 1.0013 (z 0.72). Preset 0, 320x240, RMSE of luminance clipped at 1 against a 2048 spp CPU render: CPU 64 spp raw 0.0175, denoised 0.0078; GPU native at 64 spp 0.0075 (raw 0.0145). Unclipped RMSE rises for both filters (the emitter's HDR edge). It filters every publish (no every-Nth throttle yet). Test: `filter_smooths_noise_and_keeps_object_edges`.
- **Lanes merged (2026-11-10):** the lights-and-camera, materials, media and app lanes meet in a few places, each settled as the GPU does it. The light table's pick (`Scene::pick`) takes the procedural emitters' share as well as the sky's, so suns, sphere lights and triangles all give way to SDF emitters by `sdf_share`, which counts every table entry (the GPU's `scene_info.x`). A procedural hit has no triangle: `Scene::intersect` hands `accept` no triangle (no alpha cutout), `apply_maps` and `emission_colour` leave it unmapped (the GPU's procedural hit skips `sample_maps`), and its tangent for anisotropy and gratings is the normal crossed with Y (X near the poles), as `intersect` gives it. The denoiser's guide ray and first-hit albedo go through `Scene::intersect` (procedural objects included) and take the thin lens or lens system with a fixed draw per pixel. `--reference` renders `exposure`'s moments with `--reference-time` and writes EXR, PNG or JPEG, denoised with `--cpu-denoise`; `--reference-lenient` renders the first moment. Two motion blurs remain: `reference::exposure` (32 moments, each sample draws one) for `--reference-time`, and `video::render_cpu`'s for a CPU timeline export (golden-ratio moments sharing the samples, as the GPU's passes); both average the shutter, but they could share one. Oracle after the merge (320 x 240, GPU 1024 / CPU 512 spp), every number as its lane measured it: the default list all ok (preset 0 0.9999, z -0.41); brushed-metal 0.9998 (z -0.31), thin-film 1.0001 (z 0.37), layered-furnace 1.0002 (z 0.79), lava-lamp 1.0032 (z 3.85), scattering-materials 0.9999 (z -0.08), stage-beams 1.0055 (z 0.38), cornell-fog 0.9996 (z -0.44) and chromatic 1.0003 (z 0.35), sdf-gallery 0.9996 (z -1.39), bulb-workshop 1.0005 (z 0.80), desert-monolith 0.9999 (z -2.72), bigger-on-the-inside 0.9999 (z -0.54), ies-wall 0.9995 (z -0.91), preset 0 at aperture 0.1 1.0005 (z 1.16), pool-break-1984 `--blur-frame 2` 1.0000 (z -0.02), optical-discs `--rgb` at 2048 / 1024 1.0024 (z 0.44); texture-brick-box still 1.0098 (z 27), the GPU's prefilter (see its bullet).
- **Spectral mode (2026-11-10):** `Settings::spectral` renders (`reference/spectral.rs`, `Waves`), off when LPEs force the path tracer to RGB as on the GPU. Three wavelengths per sample, stratified over the sech² importance from one number drawn from a forked stream (`Scene::waves`, so RGB draws keep their bits), ride in the Vec3 channels. Colours become spectra where they enter (`up`, the Mallett-Yuksel basis parsed from spectral_tables.hlsli): sky, HDRI, background, catcher, suns, sphere and triangle lights (`up_emitted`: a measured or blackbody spectrum scaled by the emitted/colour ratio), hit emission, material colour and sheen after the maps (`up_material`: a measured reflectance 1–3 only; an emitter's own spectrum is not an albedo), hit emission (`up_emission`), beam splitters, Beer-Lambert, medium albedo and extinction tints. The sample ends in `to_rgb` (Σ out(λ)·v/(3p(λ))) with no clamp. Hero termination (`hero`: channel 0 ×3, the rest dropped, once) at dispersive dielectrics (phase.y = Abbe number, Cauchy through n_d at 587.6 nm, `Waves::ior` for every medium in `interface`), gratings (hero wavelength, weight 1, before light sampling) and a dispersive lens system (`Channel::Nm`, on the Cauchy curve through `pack`'s three indices); after it, media sample by σ.x. The thin film is exact per wavelength over the uplifted f0. The wavelengths come from the CPU's own stream, not the GPU's Weyl sequence. **GPU bug fixed (2026-11-10):** both tracers used an emitter's own spectrum (a blackbody or the measured light, emission over D65, ~270× phase.w in a 1000 K body's far red) as its albedo too, so light bouncing off hot bars gained energy: blackbody-forge spectral gave 1e9–1e10 fireflies on both sides (GPU/CPU 0.0415, means 9e2× the RGB render). `up_material` (HLSL and CPU) now uplifts the RGB colour for emitters and `up_emission` keeps the own spectrum for what they emit; non-emitters render as before. Oracle after it (320 x 240): blackbody-forge spectral 1.0040 (z 6.50) at 1024/512 spp and 1.0036 (z 11.06, worst block 1.0084) at 4096/2048: fireflies gone, but a steady 0.36% GPU-over-CPU gap remains, open (the RGB render matches, 0.9998); diamond-fire 0.9516 (z -0.57), its means within 1.6% once both are clipped at 1000 (caustic fireflies on both sides); preset 0 0.9999 (z -0.41). Oracle `--set spectral=true` (320 x 240, GPU 1024 / CPU 512 spp): preset 0 0.9998 (z -0.95), measured-cornell 0.9997 (z -1.30), diamond-fire 0.9516 (z -0.57, noisy caustics), gem-display 1.0146 (z 1.06), white-furnace 1.0000 (z -0.19). Tests: `sampling_inverts_the_pdf`, `uplift_round_trips` (within 3e-3, from the interpolated table products), `hero_keeps_the_first_wavelength`, `cauchy_dispersion`, `own_spectra_match_their_colours`, `spectral_matches_rgb_on_the_cornell_box`.
- **Spectral thin film, gratings, measured spectra and blackbodies, measured (2026-11-10):** nothing in the model was missing after the spectral core (film per wavelength over the uplifted f0, grating hero wavelength at weight 1 before light sampling, `own` spectra via `up_material` / `up_emission` / `up_emitted`). Spectral optical-discs first failed at GPU/CPU 1.0389 (z 5.99): not the renderer but the comparison. The GPU's capture clamps each pixel's mean at 0 (post.hlsl `beauty` and `integrate`), and hero-only grating rainbows leave many pixel means negative in a channel (2170 CPU values), so the clamp adds light. `tools/oracle.py` now clamps the CPU's pixel means the same way before testing (a no-op for RGB, whose means are never negative). That also explains blackbody-forge's open 0.36% gap above. The GPU's clamp is a display choice and stays; an unclamped spectral EXR would need a raw accumulation readback. Oracle `--set spectral=true`, 320 x 240, GPU 1024 / CPU 512 spp: thin-film 1.0001 (z 0.57), light-temperature-row 1.0003 (z 1.35), measured-cornell 0.9997 (z -1.30), blackbody-forge 1.0000 (z -0.08), preset 0 0.9998 (z -0.95); optical-discs at 2048 / 1024 1.0072 (z 1.14, worst block 1.0046). No GPU change.
- **LPE layers and the other AOVs (2026-11-10):** `--reference N --capture x.layers.exr` writes the GPU capture's layers in its order (`Progressive::layers`, `reference/aov.rs`): beauty, diffuse and specular albedo (per-sample means of the first hit, RGB before uplift, as the GPU without RR), the pixel-centre guide ray's normal, roughness, depth (view axis for perspective and tilt-shift, else distance) and object id, motion 0 (a still), direct (light samples at the first vertex, emission and background at bounce ≤ 1, scaled by the firefly clamp with the sample, `to_rgb` in spectral) and specular (the whole sample when the first bounce was delta or a GGX or coat lobe), then one layer per light path expression (`render::lpe_layer_name`, moved from capture.rs). `view = 12` makes a plain capture the expression `lpe_slot` instead of the beauty, as post.hlsl does. The LPE DFA is `lpe::table`'s own, labels by `lpe::classes`; events are taken independently: `eval_bsdf_parts` returns the diffuse part (the base's diffuse under the sheen and coat), a light sample splits into D and the material's G/S by it, a continued path draws one label from a forked stream (`0x6c706500 ^ bounce`) so the beauty keeps its bits (test), V at medium scatters, S/T at dielectrics and splitter reflections, L carries the light's class (`sample_light` now returns the light's material, None = B), suns on a miss their own. Recording only when asked (`keep_aovs`). Oracle `--set view=12`, partition `C [LB]` / `C D .* [LB]` / `C [GS] .* [LB]` / `C [TV] .* [LB]` (320 x 240, 1024/512 spp): preset 0 0.9998 (z -0.64), 0.9999 (-0.59), 1.0010 (0.93), 0 on both; nighthawks 1.0000 (-0.14), 1.0127 (1.91), 0.9960 (-0.60), 0.9992 (-0.46); `C D'left wall' .* L` preset 0 1.0004 (0.64); `C .* V .* [LB]` cornell-fog 1.0009 (0.70). Layers vs the GPU's (veach-mis, nighthawks, 1024/512): beauty, direct, specular, albedos within 0.25% and |z| < 2; normal, roughness, depth, object id equal at every pixel to 1e-3. Found: the Vulkan capture writes no LPE layers (`vulkan/gpu.rs` read_layers stops at `aov_layers`; D3D12's adds them), open. Not done: the software view (`Live`) records no AOVs, so view 12 and the other debug views still show GPU textures there (the debug-views item). Tests: `aovs_leave_the_beauty_alone`, `light_path_expressions_partition_the_beauty`, `one_bounce_is_all_direct`, `aov::tests::*`.
- **BDPT (2026-11-10):** `Settings::integrator` 1 renders (`reference/integrators/bdpt.rs`), the GPU's model built from the path tracer's own surface code, now shared in `integrators/shading.rs` (`sample_lobes`, `dielectric`, `spectral_material`, `hit_tangent`). Each sample traces a camera subpath and a light subpath (up to min(bounces, 24) bounces, no roulette; free-flight scatter vertices in object media and the fog; false hits passed), and joins them in every way (s, t) under the balance heuristic (`mis_weight`, PBRT-v3 ratios with remap0 and the delta rules). Light subpaths start on table triangles (face normal, uniform area) and spheres (uniform on the sphere), chosen by power, cosine or spot-cone emission and either side of a two-sided panel. The sky, the environment and the suns start none: they keep hits and NEE with their MIS (environment share 0.5 beside the table, procedural emitters by hits only). Clear glass blocks every shadow ray and connection (`Scene::glass_blocks`); portals, the shadow catcher, ground projection, ReSTIR, `mis_mode` and the firefly clamp stay the path tracer's, as on the GPU. Film indices are a single dielectric against air (GPU `vertex_material`); a grating's RGB wavelength is fixed by a hash of the point. Spectral samples carry the hero wavelength from the start. Light tracing (t = 1, pinhole perspective or tilt-shift without aperture, else the camera is a delta vertex) splats one filter-picked pixel per axis (`pick_filtered`, `camera::filter_density`); each row keeps its splats and `Progressive::pass` adds them in row order after the pass, so passes and threads give the same bits. **GPU bug found and fixed:** the GPU took V_DELTA (what a vertex sampled) as "cannot connect", so on a partly transmissive surface the strategies ending there counted only when it had taken the other lobes, while the MIS assumed them always; a transmission-0.5 sphere was 2.8% dark overall and 21% in its block. integrators.hlsl now tests `connectible` (medium, or a material with a non-delta part), as the CPU does. Oracle (`--integrator 1 --cpu-integrator 1`, bounces 16, 1024/256 spp unless noted, 320x240): preset 0 1.0002 z 0.74, veach-ajar 1.0033 z 1.54, white-furnace 1.0001 z 0.39, mirror-room 0.9998 z -0.24, cornell-caustic 0.9977 z -1.00, cornell-fog 0.9984 z -1.32, optics-bench 0.9936 z -1.01, scattering-materials 1.0005 z 0.49, diamond-fire 0.9864 z -0.14, measured-cornell 0.9996 z -0.19, hdri-noon-spheres 0.9954 z -0.71, desert-monolith 0.9999 z -1.27, sunset-tank 1.0107 z 0.09 (4096/1024), underwater 1.0355 z 0.73 (4096/1024, noise); with the scene filter (`--keep-filter`) Gaussian and Blackman-Harris width 2 on cornell-caustic and preset 0 all ok; after the GPU fix the transmission-0.5 test scene 1.0004 z 0.85. CPU BDPT against the GPU path tracer (bounces 24): preset 0, veach-ajar 1.0043 z 1.66, white-furnace, mirror-room, cornell-caustic all ok. Rough glass stays non-reciprocal (map item 7): at roughness 0.3 the test scene's BDPT is 0.26% off the path tracer. The preview blocks of the software view still path-trace. Tests: `mis_weights_of_one_path_sum_to_one`, `delta_vertices_drop_their_strategies`, `a_splat_picks_pixels_by_the_filter`, `bdpt_passes_match_one_render`, `bdpt_agrees_with_the_path_tracer`, `bdpt_sees_glass_as_solid`. `tools/oracle.py` takes `--cpu-integrator 0|1|same` and `--keep-filter`.
- **BDPT re-measured on every built-in scene (2026-11-10;** `--integrator 1 --cpu-integrator same`, 1024/256 spp, 320x240, box filter, after the GPU `connectible` fix): 84 of 97 ok. The rest, each checked against the path tracers: castle, festival-banners, swinging-lantern and wrecking-ball are animated and pass at `--time 0`; cornell-water-tank, stained-glass-hall and glass-stack fail the same way with both sides path tracing (`--integrator 0`, same filter), so they are in the shared model, not BDPT; little-planet's pond (a clear water cylinder) differs between GPU and CPU in path tracing as much as in BDPT (a CPU firefly hid it from the PT oracle); sunset-tank and aquarium are within noise at their spp (sunset-tank needs 4096/1024, above). **glass-of-water is not a CPU BDPT bias:** at 4096/2048 spp GPU/CPU is 0.9971 (z -11.1) for BDPT and 0.9973 (z -6.5) for path tracing, worst blocks the same ((2,3), (2,4), (6,4): the ice at the water line and the tumbler's base, GPU darker), while BDPT / PT is 1.0040 on the CPU and 1.0038 on the GPU with the same blocks (rows 6-7: caustics through the smooth glass from the invisible lights, which light tracing reaches and path tracing can't). So the two BDPTs agree with each other as closely as the two path tracers do; the GPU/CPU gap is in the nested-dielectric model both integrators share (audit item "Nested dielectrics"). One GPU-only gap: shape-material-matrix block (7,7) (floor in the bottom-right corner, lit by the emissive row) is 1.9% darker in GPU BDPT than in GPU path tracing (z -17.9), while CPU BDPT, CPU PT and GPU PT agree there; not fixed.
- **Photon mapping (2026-11-10):** `Settings::integrator` 2 renders (`reference/integrators/photon.rs`), the GPU's progressive estimator on the BDPT walk code (`walk_step`, `sample_vertex`, `surface_vertex`, `escape`, `segment_tr`). Frames are the GPU's: sample k belongs to frame k / `spp`, each frame has its own map of `photons` photons and the radius r0 = `photon_radius` × the bounding sphere's radius (objects, every non-floor triangle, the fog box) shrinks as r²·Π(i + 2/3)/(i + 1) (`radius2`, the same f32 product as passes.rs), so the bias matches the GPU's at equal spp; `Progressive::pass` runs frame by frame and keeps the last frame's map, so any split into passes gives the same bits (`photon_passes_match_one_render`). Photons draw from streams keyed by index and frame, start with the GPU's shares (sky disk, table emitters by power with spot cones, suns from the disk, procedural emitters by Cauchy-Crofton lines), deposit from their second interaction on where `gather_surface` holds and at medium scatters (a separate grid, divided by the local extinction, which `Step::Scatter` now carries), Russian roulette from the third, truncated at the GPU's photons × min(bounces, 10). The grid is exact (cell → run of photons in emission order), the GPU's a hash with systematic sampling of long chains: both unbiased. The gather takes NEE over the table and the HDRI (`bdpt_env_share`, the path tracer's table pdf rescaled to that share) and one BSDF sample of the non-dielectric lobes, balance-weighted; glass blocks shadow rays (`glass_blocks` for any integrator but 0). One deliberate difference: a procedural emitter's photons take `up_emitted` (its own spectrum in spectral mode) where the GPU's `sdf_photon` takes `up`; they differ only for a blackbody or measured-spectrum SDF emitter in spectral mode. Oracle (`--integrator 2 --cpu-integrator 2`, which now passes the GPU's `--spp 4` to the CPU; 320x240, equal spp so equal frames), GPU/CPU (z): preset 0 0.9999 (-0.43), cornell-caustic 0.9951 (-1.75), white-furnace 1.0006 (1.14), veach-mis 1.0089 (2.04), cornell-fog 0.9987 (-0.63), dark-side 0.9482 (-0.76, spectral fog, noisy), sun-greenhouse 1.0001 (0.41) at 256 spp; sdf-gallery 0.9994 (-1.54) and optics-bench 1.0431 (1.38) at 1024. Two block fails, neither the CPU's: optics-bench's worst block moves between runs (1.17 at (7, 5) with 256 spp, 1.15 at (5, 0) with 1024) and its mean falls toward 1 with frames: one map per frame correlates neighbouring pixels, so the oracle's per-pixel standard error understates photon mapping's noise in caustic scenes. sdf-gallery's block (5, 5) (0.976, z -7.8 at 1024) is the GPU: inside the green glass cone (x 216-240, y 150-162) the GPU's photon mapping is 0.075 where its own path tracer is 0.099, the CPU path tracer 0.098 and the CPU's photon mapping 0.096. Not traced on the GPU yet.
- **Photon mapping in glass-of-water (2026-11-10):** GPU/CPU 0.9847 (z -31.8), worst block (6, 4) 0.65, at 256 spp, the same at photon radius 0.03 and 0.003 (so not the radius schedule or the GPU's hash chains), and gone with the main light off. The cause is the scene's contact, not either estimator: the tumbler's base lies in the floor's plane (y = 0, `grounded`), so a photon leaving the base downward meets the base and the floor at the same t and the tie decides whether it lands (0.0003 past the base it is already under the floor). Each side breaks the tie its own way and both lose light there: with the glass and water lifted 4 mm (a copy of the scene) block (6, 4) is 0.304 on both sides (contact: GPU 0.133, CPU 0.205), and the scene passes at 0.9973 (z -5.9; its block ratios scatter 1.2%, so the per-pixel z overstates it about 5×; worst 0.959). Neither side is right at the contact; lifting the glass a millimetre in the scene would fix both and brighten the GPU's image under the base, which is the owner's call. optics-bench (256 spp) has grounded lenses too, but lifting them changes nothing: its block ratios scatter 9% (robust), the mean of the 64 block ratios is 0.991 (z -0.7 against that spread) and the worst block (5, 6) 0.70 is about 3σ of it; its one large outlier, block (5, 7) at 14×, is a single GPU pixel (161, 282) of 130, one camera sample that reached the 0.08-wide beam light through the lenses. Consistent with noise, not proven: the GPU's frames and the CPU's streams are seeded by frame and pixel, so repeated runs are not independent.
- **Not done:** the refused features above (each needs its CPU twin); a self-test that compares a low-sample GPU and CPU render statistically (the roadmap's oracle idea; the comparison is a manual tool today); using it as the reference for `motion_error.py` / `upscale_quality.py`; in the viewport: the convergence graph does not use it as its reference.
- **Tests:** `the_albedo_table_parses_and_a_white_furnace_conserves`, `the_bvh_finds_the_nearest_triangle`, `the_cornell_box_renders`, `passes_match_one_render`, `every_missing_feature_is_refused`, `a_meshs_cutout_renders`, `prepare_applies_the_time_the_flashlight_and_the_beam`, `at_time_runs_the_simulation_and_freezes_it`, `the_sums_keep_small_samples_beside_a_large_one`, `the_side_is_the_faces_not_the_interpolated_normals`, `normals_interpolate_in_object_space`, `a_grazing_shadow_ray_stops_short_of_its_own_emitter`, `the_sampling_strategies_agree`, `at_time_turns_the_camera_and_freezes_it`, `restir_renders_with_the_gpus_share_of_samples`, `camera_rays_follow_each_projection`, `henyey_greenstein_is_normalised_and_sampled`.

- **Spot cosine, hit or sampled (2026-11-09).** A bounce that hits an emitter takes the spot (and IES) cosine against the emitter's face normal, signed (`Surface::emission_cos`), as light sampling does, so a two-sided spot seen from behind is outside its cone on both strategies and MIS weighs the same radiance. The GPU's NEE (`spot(lm,dot(ln,-ld))`) and light tracing (`light_dir_pdf`) agree; its BSDF hit (`spot(m,-dot(direction,h.n))`, pathtrace.hlsl) uses the face-forwarded normal and so mirrors the cone onto the back: a GPU bug, left as is. Oracle gem-display: GPU/CPU 1.0127, z 0.94.
- **Thumbnails, scripts and the convergence graph on the CPU (2026-11-10):** `scenes::render_thumbnails(entries, cpu)`: in the software view (or `--make-thumbnails --cpu`) each child runs `--reference 64 --reference-lenient` at 320 x 200, one at a time (each takes every core), so a machine without ray tracing gets thumbnails; they are keyed by content hash and stay valid later, without RR's denoising. `--reference-lenient` renders what the CPU knows of a refused scene, as the view does, prints `REFERENCE_MISSING …`, and drops glare from a PNG/JPEG (the display transform has none). Measured: all 174 scenes rendered, none failed. In the software view a script's `samples()` is the CPU's and `capture()` saves the CPU's finished pass (`Live::latest`: EXR the plain mean, PNG/JPEG through the CPU display transform; layers and HDR images refused); `--frames … --capture x.exr` in the view now writes the CPU mean too, where it wrote the GPU's one pass (a bug). A scripted `--cpu` run to 16 spp gave an EXR bit-identical to `--reference 16` (preset 0, 320 x 240). Convergence window: in the view "Use current image" takes the CPU's mean and the graph measures the CPU's render (its samples); with Look → Compare's CPU reference running, "Use the CPU render" makes it the reference and restarts only the GPU (`Actions::keep_cpu`; a plain reset restarts both). No image change: oracle preset 0 0.9999 (z -0.41). Tests: `cpu_thumbnails_render_without_a_device`, `the_view_follows_the_camera_and_the_scene` (latest).
- **GPUs without ray tracing, Vulkan (2026-11-10):** acceleration structures, ray query, deferred host operations, shaderInt64 and bufferDeviceAddress are no longer required: `vulkan::suitable` grades a device instead, `grade` ranks any tracing device above any display-only one (then discrete, integrated, CPU types such as lavapipe), and `CAUSTICA_NO_RT=1` forces display-only. Such a device enables none of them, has no TLAS binding (100) in set 0, makes buffers without device addresses, creates only the display, filter and glare pipelines (their SPIR-V declares no ray query or Int64, checked with spirv-dis), builds no BLAS/TLAS, traces nothing (`n` = 0, no path records) and starts no upscaler. `GpuState::ray_tracing` false turns the software view on with a toast and greys out the GPU choice. Picking, the 3D cursor, Ctrl+click focus and the light meter read `gizmo::cpu_probe` there instead of the GPU's object-id pass: `pick_object_hit` gives the object and its view depth, the meter reads the software view's linear colour and the object's diffuse albedo; the room's walls and floor aren't objects, so a click on them is a miss (test `the_cpu_probe_selects_what_the_gpu_probe_would`). The fallback message says when `CAUSTICA_NO_RT=1` forced the path instead of blaming the card. A headless check needs `--frames`: `CAUSTICA_NO_RT=1 caustica --headless --frames 1 --capture x.png`. Not tried on a real non-RT GPU or lavapipe (only the NVIDIA ICD here). The D3D12 twin is not done (DXR 1.1 is still required; this Linux machine can't compile the Windows target). Measured: `CAUSTICA_NO_RT=1 --cpu --headless --samples 16 --capture x.exr` (preset 0, 320 x 240, --debug: no validation messages) is bit-identical to `--reference 16`; the tracing path is unchanged, oracle preset 0 0.9999 (z -0.41). Test: `devices_that_trace_rank_first`.
- **Linux app id (2026-11-09):** `platform::with_app_id` names both windows (D3D12 and Vulkan) "caustica" as the Wayland app id and the X11 WM_CLASS, so desktop rules can match them; a no-op off Linux. Window plumbing only: no image change, so no oracle run.
- **Battle mode** (2026-11-10): `progressive::quadrants` splits the image at the integer halves of the render size into path tracing, path tracing (the GPU's ReSTIR quadrant: it only moves noise), BDPT and photon mapping, as `passes::battle_frame`; a pass renders them one after another, `Scene::set_quadrant` switching the integrator, its rules (spectral under LPE, clear glass blocking) and its region. As on the GPU, light tracing projects and filter-picks inside the region only and its film area is the region's share of the image's (`film`), and the photon quadrant traces that share of `photons` (at least 1024). The time balance is not copied: every quadrant takes the same samples, and its photon boost (×1–4) is left out, which changes the photon quadrant's noise but not its mean: the probabilistic estimate's expectation at a frame depends on the radius, not the photon count, and the GPU's photon buffer holds the full `photons` × min(bounces, 10) deposits in battle mode too, which the CPU's cut now matches (`photon_map`). Preset 3 at 320×240, 512 spp both sides, default pixel filter, bottom-right quadrant mean luminance: GPU battle 0.14913 vs GPU full-frame photon mapping 0.14915, CPU battle 0.14524 vs CPU full-frame 0.14521, so battle mode adds no gap on either side. Its oracle failure (1.0063, z 20.5, worst block 1.0779 at (5, 4)) is preset 3's photon mapping with the path tracer's pixel filter: full-frame `--integrator 2 --cpu-integrator same --keep-filter` fails the same way (1.0067, z 23.8, worst block 1.0783 at (5, 4)), and the path tracer already differs 1.26% in that quadrant; open, not a battle-mode item. The path-traced quadrants are bit for bit the plain render's. Oracle, preset 0, 320×240, 1024 spp both sides, `--set battle=true`: GPU/CPU 1.0003, z 1.66, worst block 0.9964 (z −2.68); per quadrant 1.0004 (z 1.52), 1.0001 (0.40), BDPT 1.0008 (1.58), photon mapping 1.0001 (0.34).

## Grounded dielectric gap (2026-11-10)

Closes "Coplanar dielectric contacts" (section "CPU reference renderer"): where a dielectric face lies exactly on another face, both hits come at equal t and DXR's traversal and the CPU BVH break the tie differently, so neither renderer is wrong and the oracle fails. The owner chose a geometry offset that both renderers read (option B) over tie-break code in traversal.

- **Floor and support contacts, automatic:** `geometry::ground_lift` adds `DIELECTRIC_GAP` (4 mm, in a 2-unit room) to the pivot of a grounded object whose material transmits (`geometry::transmissive`: `transmission > 0`). It is applied in one place, `grounded_y`, which `object_frame` and `object_geometry` share; everything else (instance transforms, `object_corners`, the CPU reference via `geometry::geometry` and `object_transform` for SDFs, physics, gizmo, picking, snapping, lighting bounds) reads those, so nothing applies it twice. An elevated grounded glass (on a plinth) is lifted the same, which also separates it from its support. No per-object toggle.
- **Parts of one file rise together:** an imported multi-part file is grounded as a whole (`mesh::ground` measures the file), so lifting only its glass parts would split a bottle from its cap. `ground_lift` therefore lifts a grounded part when it is transmissive or when any part of its file imported as transmissive (`mesh::file_has_glass`, from the file's own part materials). Limit: a user edit that makes one part of an all-opaque file transmissive lifts that part alone. Test `the_parts_of_a_file_with_glass_rise_together`.
- **Physics:** a transmissive body's collider gets a rapier contact skin of `DIELECTRIC_GAP`, so a simulated glass settles the same 4 mm off the floor and off an opaque body as its grounded pose put it: no drop or jitter when the simulation starts. Rapier sums the two colliders' skins, so glass on glass would rest 8 mm apart; `GlassContacts` (a physics hook, on glass colliders only, marked by `user_data` 1) adds one gap back to such contacts' distance, giving 4 mm. `same_shape` includes the transmission bit, so crossing 0 rebuilds the body. Tests `a_grounded_glass_keeps_its_gap_when_simulated`, `glass_rests_one_gap_off_glass_and_off_opaque` (floor-glass, opaque-glass, glass-opaque and glass-glass stacks settle within 1 mm of the gap), `changing_transmission_rebuilds_the_body`.
- **Opaque on glass floats too, by design:** an opaque object resting on a grounded glass sits on top of the lifted glass, so it floats 4 mm as well (statically and simulated); nothing lifts it back.
- **Object-object contacts: no general rule.** Shrinking or separating nested dielectrics automatically would insert a thin film of the outer medium (an air gap between glass and water gives total internal reflection that the scene never had), and scenes rely on deliberate overlaps (priority CSG). So the built-in scenes were fixed in `tools/build_scenes.py` (`GAP`, same 4 mm): glass-stack's slabs and cone stack with a gap and start off the floor; stained-glass-hall's panes, which overlap in x and shared their front and back faces, step 6 mm in z per column; layered-cocktail's and aquarium's floor-standing vessels (built with `float_at`, not grounded) start at -1 + GAP, and liquids or air sharing a side wall where they overlap (Orange juice and Headspace, Tumbler headspace, the aquarium's air box) are narrowed by GAP. Hand-placed user objects touching exactly still tie; the rule for scenes is to overlap dielectrics (priority decides) or leave a gap, never to make faces coincide.
- **Oracle** (320 x 240, 2048 spp each side, `--time 0.7 --rgb`; before = the 2026-11-06 sweep, same settings):

| scene | before GPU/CPU (z, worst block) | after GPU/CPU (z, worst block) |
|---|---|---|
| cornell-water-tank | 1.0411 (z 88) fail | 1.0005 (z 1.38, block 1.0309 z 2.05) |
| stained-glass-hall | 1.0849 (z 37, block 1.4163) fail | 0.9999 (z -0.88, block 0.9988 z -2.47) |
| glass-stack | 0.9955 (z -17, block 0.9451) fail | 0.9998 (z -0.95, block 0.9990 z -2.05) |
| glass-of-water | 0.9973 (z -6) fail | 1.0000 (z 0.10, block 0.9946 z -3.10) |
| aquarium | 1.0058 (z 6) fail | 1.0000 (z -0.01, block 0.9868 z -2.34) |
| layered-cocktail | 1.0002 (block 0.9673, z -6) fail | 0.9998 (z -0.69, block 0.9814 z -3.64) |
| shape-material-matrix | pass, block 0.9835 (z -4.4) | 1.0002 (z 1.46, block 1.0012 z 3.03) |
| sdf-gallery (glass cone) | block 1.026 (z 9.3), 1024/512 spp | 0.9995 (z -3.03, block 0.9936 z -5.89) ok |

  The gap was not swept below 4 mm (1 cm was the earlier measurement); any non-zero gap removes the exact tie, so a smaller one should also pass but is untested. At normal view distance 4 mm under a 1.4-unit glass is not visible (crop in the lane's scratch folder).

## Closing the medium term, wave 1 (2026-11-03)

Owner: finish every open item before the future goals, batched (roadmap "Closing the medium term").

- **Importers.** PLY (ASCII and both binary orders; normals, UVs, vertex colours averaged into the material colour since vertex colours don't exist) and STL (ASCII and binary, welded by exact position, normals smoothed across edges under 30° so scans are smooth and CAD edges stay sharp) in `src/meshfmt.rs`. OBJ: `Ke` → emission (colour = Ke / its peak), `d` / `Tr` < 0.99 → transmission with `Ni` as IOR, a bright `Ks` over a dark `Kd` → metal. glTF `KHR_materials_volume`: white light turns `attenuationColor` after `attenuationDistance` file units; the material colour (absorption per scene unit) becomes colour · c^(1 / d_scene) with d_scene = d · normalize scale · 0.7. `KHR_lights_punctual` → sphere (point), spot (cone angles to spread and blend) and sun lights, placed with the imported object's frame (`mesh::import_scene`); photometric units at 1 lux = 1/50 scene irradiance, file units taken as metres; lights come in hidden from the camera. *Z-up* beside *+ Mesh* tilts imports −90° about x.
- **Laser colours.** The beam carries red, green and blue at 650 / 550 / 450 nm. Transmissive objects multiply it by their colour on entry (channels under 0.05 drop out); glass with an Abbe number splits a multi-channel beam into one beam per channel, each refracted by the shader's Cauchy fit (`laser::cauchy`). `Puzzle::colors`: a target counts once the channels above 0.5 in its colour arrive and no others (so white on a red target fails). Levels 4 (*Filters*: move red and blue glass into the arms of a splitter) and 5 (*Rainbow*: one prism turn sends red, green and blue each onto its own target); the level test checks each starts unsolved and its solution solves it.
- **Casts shadows** (per light, in `geometry_key` since it picks the instance mask, `AreaLight::shadows`, material flag 8192): settles "hidden lights occlude" by choice. A visible unshadowed light is an opaque instance with `MASK_NO_SHADOW` (8), which `occluded()` and shadow `intersect()` leave out; a hidden and unshadowed light keeps mask 0xff and the per-candidate test (masks can't express two exclusions). Bounce rays still hit the panel.
- **Keyboard focus** (2026-11-06): egui's `wants_keyboard_input` means any focus, and egui hands focus to the first widget on Tab, which killed the shortcuts until Esc. Now `keep_tab_for_app` keeps Tab from egui unless a text field has the keyboard (never during a transform), and `settle_focus` after each frame takes the text field's IME output as the mark of text entry (a DragValue being typed in too) and drops any other focus once it has lasted a frame (the DragValue's field appears the frame after its click). The cost: no Tab navigation across buttons, and a text field scrolled out of view lets go. `App::keys_free` guards the shortcuts; a key release in walk mode always counts. *Scenes* gives its search box the keyboard on opening (`scenes_were_open`, so any way of opening it counts); Up / Down step through the visible cards in reading order, not by grid row (also Left / Right when the box isn't focused), Enter loads, Esc clears the search and then closes, Ctrl+P toggles it.
- **Scene tab layout** (2026-11-06): outliner, *+ Add* (Object, Light, Z-up, Mesh file…; a wide row used to widen the panel, which never shrinks by itself), the selection's inspector, then Physics, Audio and Laser as one-line headers with their box in the title (`feature`: opens when the feature turns on, by the box or a scene load, then stays as left). A viewport pick scrolls the inspector into view once (`scroll_to_inspector`); outliner clicks don't. Each tab has its own scroll offset, and the outliner is one list (`outliner_rows`: fixed rows, lights, objects; 260 px, searched as a whole), so the wheel gets through one list on its way to the panel. The object inspector runs name, Material, Shape and placement, then Mass, Physics and Cloth only with Simulate on (a cloth keeps its row, or it could not be turned off); Locked is under More, Portal in its own header, held open while it has a partner. `Selection::Flashlight` is not transformable, and its `anim::entity` arm is empty: the `_` arm there means the camera.
- **Flashlight** (`Settings::flashlight`, `flashlight_config`; an outliner row beside Main light whose box or T toggles it and whose inspector, `Selection::Flashlight`, shows Intensity, Color and Beam half angle with the rest under More): a hidden spot whose panel occludes nothing, added to each frame's copy and to video exports' per-moment settings (it follows timeline camera keys, not the turntable's orbit). Reworked 2026-11-06: it was a 40° cone 8 cm below the eye, whose shadows hid behind what cast them. Now it is held 0.3 right and 0.4 down, and the beam crosses the view axis 8 units ahead, all adjustable. The beam goes through the IES path: `ies::profile("beam:half,corona,spill")` computes a reflector's profile (Gaussian hot spot, half at 5°; a 3% ring; a corona 2.5 × as wide; flat spill cut by the bezel at 25° or more), or the owner's IES file replaces it. The spill must stay well under 1%: a near surface gets it by inverse square and floods, as the first attempt (2% spill, 17% corona, 42° cut) showed. It ignores audio (`audio_band` 5). Being a light, it is in `geometry_key`, so a moving camera rebuilds the static BLAS each frame while it is on (small: room, floor and lights).
- **Animation.** Booleans, integers and enum names are keyable (`anim::Leaf`); they hold until the next key (strings ride in `Key::text`), free text (names, paths, references such as `portal` and `target`) never is. Auto-key (timeline checkbox) keys finished edits once no drag or transform runs (what physics, walking, the remote and the gamepad change is absorbed as the new reference each frame, `Timeline::absorb`, so only UI edits are keyed), with the state when it was turned on as the reference before the first key. *Keys* menu: later / earlier by a frame or a second, twice as slow or fast from the playhead (`Animation::retime`, the length grows to fit), and copy to the other selected objects or lights (`copy_keys`).
- **Audio.** Per-light *Follows* (automatic, bass, mid, treble, beats, nothing); a beat detector (bass 1.5× its 0.7 s running mean and above 30% of the band peak, at least 0.25 s apart; envelope half-life 80 ms); *Colour shift* moves each light's colour toward its band's (bass red, mid green, treble blue, beats white) at full level.
- **Scenes.** `vermeer-room` (afternoon sun through a west window, haze; the pane is left out because glass blocks NEE shadow rays when glass shadows are on), `weather-project` (a monofrequency disc centred on a mirrored ceiling, so the mirror completes the half sun, in haze), `jack-o-lantern` (a carved mesh from `tools/make_pumpkin.py`, lit by a 1900 K candle inside: a ribbed shell with thickness whose grid triangles are clipped against a signed distance of the face and the zigzag lid, with walls along every cut, so the holes are real and the shell is closed. The first version carved a scattering sphere with priority-2 air shapes; the holes barely showed (owner report 2026-11-03), since the dense skin medium hid them and the air shapes only swap media, so the mesh replaced it).
- **Small items.** The object list draws only the rows in view (`ScrollArea::show_rows`). Closed without change: grounding already caches its lowest point per mesh and transform; the Menger generator's merged runs stay (one instance per cube would only add objects); framing uses the rotated local box's corners, which is tight enough.
- **Moved to wave 2:** IES profiles (shader work with the other light items).

## Closing the medium term, wave 2 (2026-11-03 – 2026-11-04)

Rendering items of the closing batch (roadmap "Closing the medium term").

- **IES profiles** (`src/ies.rs`, `Light::ies`): an LM-63 file's candela table becomes the relative intensity over 0–90° from the light's normal (averaged over the horizontal angles), 60 samples peaking at 1, packed three 8-bit values per float into fields a light never reads (layers, sheen, base_scale, surface_scale, medium.xyz, phase.x; flag bit 14). `spot()` returns `ies_profile` for them, so every emission site (hit, NEE, ReSTIR, BDPT, photons) shapes the light alike. Four profiles in `scenes/ies/`, scene `ies-wall`.
- **Spectral leftovers.** Blackbody lights (`kelvin`) and measured spectra (`Material::spectrum`, the Cornell box's walls and light; `src/spectrum.rs`, `measured-cornell`) are a material's own spectrum (phase.z: > 0 measured, < 0 −kelvin), evaluated per wavelength in the spectral tracers (`own_spectrum`, `up_material`, `up_emitted`) and integrated to the same RGB on the CPU for the RGB tracers, so the two agree. Per-channel extinction in media and fog (`Material::extinction`, `Fog::extinction`, an 8-bit tint in one float): free flight picks a channel uniformly and weights by the one-sample MIS T·σ / mean(T·σ) (`flight`, Kutz et al. 2017), for a Rayleigh-like haze or opal glass. Spectral BDPT (`bdpt_spectral`) and the photon gather's camera side (`photon_gather_spectral`) take one wavelength per sample from the start (`begin_hero` / `hero_rgb`). The spectral BDPT twin once hung the RGB BDPT that ran after it; the statics the RGB variants never write (`path_lambdas`, `hero_only`, `proceed_steps`, `rng_state` and the other sampler state) now have initializers (a static without one starts with whatever the registers held), and with that and the 2026-11-04 driver the pair runs clean (self-test frames 294–295: spectral, then RGB BDPT). Measured on `measured-cornell` (2048 frames): spectral BDPT / spectral PT = 1.009, 1.007, 1.011, the same gap as RGB BDPT / RGB PT (1.009 per channel), which predates this (roadmap).
- **Camera medium (underwater):** `geometry::camera_medium` finds the transmissive object the eye is inside (built-in shapes: odd crossings of a ray; the highest priority wins), and `environment.w` starts camera paths with that medium on the stack (trace, BDPT's camera subpath, the photon gather). Scenes `underwater` (rebuilt), `sunset-tank`.
- **Equiangular sampling** (Kulla and Fajardo 2012), opt in (`Fog::equiangular`, Stage → *Equiangular*; flag bit 6 of `scene_info.w`). On each fog segment the path tracer picks a light as `sample_light` does, then a distance with density ∝ 1/(h² + (t − Δ)²) about the light point (`equiangular`), restricted to the part of the ray inside the light's emission cone (`cone_interval`: a spot's outer cone, an IES or one-sided light's half space), and lights that point. It is MIS'd against the free-flight scatter's light sample, with weights pd/(pd + pe) there and pe/(pd + pe) here, both inside the phase/light balance (`flight_pdf` is the free-flight density, chromatic included). Its random numbers come from a side stream (`side_random`, keyed by pixel, sample and path state), not `random()`, so the Sobol dimensions stay bit-identical: with the option off a fog scene renders a bit-identical image at the old speed. Measured (640×360, 256 frames against 8192; trace ms at 720p): stage-beams RMSE 0.0143 → 0.0121 for 11.2 → 17.7 ms; cornell-fog 0.0129 → 0.0113, 7.8 → 11.4 ms; a 5 cm bulb in thin fog 0.0626 → 0.0517, 6.3 → 8.8 ms. So it lowers the noise per sample by 12–17% but costs 1.4–1.6× the trace time, a loss at equal time on these scenes; hence off by default. Tried and dropped: routing the side stream through `random()` (a branch on every call cost 2.5–5% in every scene), applying it on camera segments only (−1 to −5% RMSE for +12–27% time), and replacing the free-flight scatter's NEE at bounce 0 (the same cost; the shadow ray it saves is small). Most of the remaining error in these scenes is multiple scattering, which equiangular sampling does not touch.
- **Texture pipeline.** sRGB maps are mip-filtered in linear (`halve`: a decode table, the average, encoding through a 65536-entry table), so distant textures keep their brightness (black and white average to sRGB 188, not 128). `KHR_texture_transform` is applied to the UVs at import (`uv_mapping`: the base colour's transform, else the metallic-roughness or emissive map's; its `texCoord` override picks the set). glTF BLEND is stochastic transparency: a negative cutoff, −(the factor's alpha), makes `alpha_hole` call a candidate a hole with probability 1 − alpha, from a hash of pixel, sample, path state and candidate (no `random()`, which would shift the Sobol dimensions by the candidate count); BLEND parts get no micromaps.
- **Emissive maps** (flag 64): glTF `emissiveTexture` and MTL `map_Ke`; the surface emits factor × texel, independent of the base colour (a glTF emissive colour unlike the base colour, which used to be replaced by the base colour, gets a 1×1 white map). `attach_maps` stores the slot + 1 and the mean emitted colour over its peak in `secondary` (only patterns and spots read it) and 1 / the map's mean in `medium.xyz` (only media and IES lights), and now runs before `emitter_table`, which takes the mean colour (`lighting::emitted`). With any emissive map the emitter buffer gets one UV record per emitter after the SDF photon records, so `emitted_color` finds the texel at any point of an emitter triangle from its barycentrics: camera hits, NEE, ReSTIR, BDPT and photons all see the same texture, which keeps MIS unbiased (Lantern, NEE-only / BSDF-only = 1.0008, 1.0004, 1.0001). The selection power stays the triangle's area times the mean colour.
- **Alpha test at the ray-cone LOD: closed, not done.** A path tracer averaging many samples gets the exact coverage from the level-0 alpha; a blurred mip compared against a cutoff thins or thickens distant foliage (a quality cut).
- **Procedural surfaces:** exact capped cylinder and cone (`Sdf::Cylinder`, `Sdf::Cone`: analytic roots of the side quadric inside y ∈ [−1, 1] and the cap discs, in the mesh shapes' proportions), and `Object::detail` per object in the instance's high 24 bits (thousandths; 0 = the default): the torus's tube radius (ring radius 1 − it), the Mandelbulb's power (8 keeps Quilez's trigonometry-free form, other powers the spherical formula), the Menger sponge's levels. Grounding and colliders still use the kind's proxy mesh, so a thin torus floats by the difference.
- **Light sampling of procedural emitters** (trace only; `sdf_nee_on`: off in BSDF-only MIS mode and the lean path). The SDF photon records (`sdf_emitter_records`: each emissive procedural object's world bounding sphere of its unit cube and power CDF) double as light records: `sample_light` picks one by power, a direction uniformly in the cone its bounding sphere subtends (the whole sphere of directions from inside it), and keeps the sample when the first surface that way is that object (`intersect`, its material). With triangle emitters too, the two share the non-sky half evenly (`sdf_share`), so the triangle, sun and equiangular pdfs carry (1 − sdf_share); a BSDF hit on the object is weighted against `sdf_record_prob × sdf_dir_pdf` (ReSTIR vertices, which sample triangles only, keep weight 1). The shadow ray stops `nee_limit` short of the hit, as for triangles: a flat 0.6 mm cut it lost 7% of the light, since from the offset origin a grazing ray to the object's lower rim meets it sooner. Measured on a floor, an emissive SDF sphere and three more SDFs (480×360, against 262144 BSDF-only samples): NEE / BSDF-only on a glossy floor 1.0001, and RMSE 0.0019 at 256 frames (4.8 ms a frame) against BSDF-only's 0.0246 at 256 and 0.0078 at 2048 (2.4 ms), roughly 8× less error at equal time. No gain where the objects sit inside glass (`lava-lamp`: NEE through glass is blocked, as for triangles).
- **Sun split out of an HDRI** (opt in: `Settings::hdri_sun`, Environment → *Split sun*). `environment::split_sun` flood-fills the region above 1/50 of the peak around the brightest texel (at most 0.02 sr, and only when it holds at least 5% of the map's power), fills it with its border ring's mean colour, and keeps the excess as an analytic disc (`env_sun`: direction, 1 − the cosine of the half angle (a few-texel sun's cosine rounds to 1 in f32), radiance) of the same power and solid angle. `sample_env` picks the disc with its power share (`env_sun_color.w`) and samples it uniformly, else the map; `env_pdf` and `environment_radiance` add the disc, so every MIS weight sees the mixture. The polyhaven suns come out 0.33–0.73° in half angle holding 18–74% of the power. Measured on the four spheres (480×360, 256 frames against 16384 or more white-noise samples per mode; relative error percentiles 50 / 90 / 99): the 1k `meadow_2` map 0.0119 / 0.065 / 0.29 → 0.0029 / 0.016 / 0.31 (its 5-texel sun gets bilinear-blurred, which the texel CDF samples poorly); the 8k `kloofendal` map 0.0025 / 0.0128 / 0.083 → 0.0027 / 0.0142 / 0.107 (the map's CDF already finds a 338-texel sun). Means agree within 0.12%. Hence opt in: a large win for low-resolution maps, mixed for high-resolution ones. BDPT and photon emission go through the same `sample_env` / `env_pdf` / `environment_radiance`, so they see the disc too (not measured there).
- **Shadow catcher and ground projection** (`Settings::shadow_catcher`, `Settings::hdri_ground`; `env_ground`). Ground projection: on a path with only delta bounces since the camera (`camera_chain`), a background below the horizon is the map looked up from the capture point (0, floor + height, 0) toward where the ray meets the floor's plane (`background_dir`), so the HDRI's ground lies at a finite distance (the nearest ground stretches where the map's nadir is coarse; a pure-sky map shows radial streaks). Shadow catcher: a camera-chain hit on the floor (material 3) returns the background behind it (`ground_dir` of the hit point when projection is on, else the ray's direction; the background colour when the HDRI is hidden from the camera) times the share of four `sample_light` samples' power (luminance × cosine / pdf, every light kind) whose shadow rays get through, and ends the path; any other ray that hits the floor sees the projected ground unshadowed and ends there. The ratio of sums per sample is consistent, not unbiased (four samples); objects' caustics, reflections and colour bleeding on the floor are not caught, and BDPT and photon mapping ignore both settings. Scene `hdri-shadow-catcher` (noon grass, height 1.7, sun split); self-test frames 297–299.
- **Photon gather walks past near-mirror coats** (`gather_surface`): a camera path in photon mapping used to stop at every non-metal surface and estimate the light it reflects from the photons around it. Through a near-specular lobe that estimate's radius bias is large: `kusama-infinity-room`'s floor (roughness 0.02, reflectivity 1, so F0 = 1) made photon mapping 8% darker than path tracing at the default radius, 20% at a third of it and 3% at three times it, while with walls and floor diffuse the two agreed (0.995). Surfaces with roughness < 0.2 whose F0 luminance exceeds 0.5 (`f0_of`, a coat reflecting most light at normal incidence) are now passed like near-mirror metals, so their reflection is traced to the next diffuse surface: photon / path tracing = 1.0002 at both radii (240×135, 1600 spp). This closes the Kusama gap (roadmap Known issues); it wasn't the gather radius. A partial integrator sweep (55 of 86 built-in scenes; a full run was cut short by low memory) found no scene where photon mapping alone disagrees.
- **Heterogeneous fog** (`Fog::noise`, `Fog::noise_size`; `fog_noise`). The fog's extinction is σ × k(p), k = max(0, 1 + n (2 fBm − 1)) with four octaves of value noise in [0, 1] (`fog_density`), so the majorant is σ (1 + n). Free flight is spectral tracking (Kutz et al. 2017: a real collision with probability ⟨w σ⟩ / (⟨w σ⟩ + ⟨w σ_n⟩) over the path's running weight, which is plain delta tracking for grey fog; `fog_track`, returning the local σ for the volume photon gather), shadow rays ratio tracking (`fog_tr`, in `medium_transmittance` and `segment_tr`), in the path tracer, BDPT and photon mapping alike. The first tentative collision takes `random()`, the rest the side stream (white noise in BDPT and the photon passes), and with noise 0 every site takes the old closed form: `cornell-fog` renders bit-identical. Equiangular sampling is off while the noise is on (its MIS needs the analytic free-flight density). Checked on `cornell-fog` with noise 2 at size 0.3, grey and with extinction (0.4, 0.7, 1): NEE-only, BSDF-only, BDPT and photon mapping all within 0.6% of MIS per channel. Scene `stage-beams` (it absorbed `stage-smoke` in the 2026-11-05 scene overhaul); self-test frames 300–302.
- **Review fixes (2026-11-04):** `geometry_key` includes `detail` (a detail change now rebuilds the SDF instance); a change of SDF kind resets `detail` to 0 (a torus's tube radius is no Mandelbulb power); a black extinction colour is clamped in the editor and UI (it made every channel's σ zero); the ReSTIR hit weight leaves out `sdf_share` (ReSTIR samples triangles only).

## Closing the medium term, wave 3 (2026-11-04)

Engine and tool items of the closing batch (roadmap "Closing the medium term").

- **Drag cost.** Measured at 1440×900 by dragging one object (`rebuild_scene` timed per frame): chess (`beautiful-game`, 32 objects, 2 M triangles) 19.8 → 8.1 ms a drag frame against 7.4 ms static; `forest-1` (1800 objects) 63.5 → 15.9 ms against 8.4 ms. Most of it was on the CPU: `transport_key` turned every object and light into a JSON tree (14 ms on the forest); it now serializes the rest of the settings as before but hashes the objects and lights from bytes written straight from serde (names and light outlines cleared, as before). `geometry_key` serializes a tuple directly instead of a `json!` tree (3 ms). `mesh::load` looked each file up through `resolve`, which checks every scene root on disk, for every object several times per rebuild (3 ms on the chess set); a second map by the stored path (`LOADED`, successes only, so a file that appears later is still found) skips that.
- **TLAS refit.** The TLAS is built with `ALLOW_UPDATE` and kept with its scratch (sized for build and update) and instance buffers (`TlasCache`). When the new instance descriptions differ only in their transforms (bytes 48–64 of each, id, mask, flags and BLAS address, match), it is updated in place (`PERFORM_UPDATE`), with a full build every 64 refits so the tree doesn't degrade as objects move far. One frame in flight makes rewriting the instance buffer safe. The image is identical with and without refits; it saves 0.7 ms a drag frame on the forest (the rest of the drag frame is the frame itself and the CPU side).
- **BLAS compaction.** Mesh and static BLASes are built with `ALLOW_COMPACTION` and a postbuild compacted-size query into a small buffer per BLAS (`build_blas`'s `compact`; the procedural-AABB BLAS is left alone). `compact_blases`, before the instances are written, reads the sizes back (one extra submit, only on rebuilds that built a BLAS), copies each into a buffer of its compacted size (`COPY_MODE_COMPACT`) and swaps it into the mesh and static-group tables; the sources live until the TLAS submit. Chess 105 → 36.6 MB of BLAS, `forest-1` 3.3 → 1.6 MB, `jack-o-lantern` 8.6 → 3.6 MB. Renders are bit-identical with and without it.
- **Incremental vertex upload** (`upload_mesh_vertices`). The object-space vertex buffer used to be rebuilt and uploaded whole whenever the set of meshes changed (adding or hiding one object re-sent every mesh: 19 MB for the chess set). Each mesh now keeps its range (`vertex_ranges`, by mesh key) while it stays in the buffer, hidden or removed ones included, so showing one again uploads nothing; new meshes are appended, and when they don't fit the buffer grows by a quarter (or to what is needed) with the old ranges copied on the GPU. The buffer is repacked from scratch when the holes would outweigh the meshes in use. BLASes own copies of their triangles, so only the shading lookups (`vertex_base`) follow the ranges. Scene loads render bit-identically.
- **Cloth: two-way coupling and self-collision** (`Cloth::mass`, `Cloth::self_collision`, both in the editor's Cloth row). A particle pushed out of a collider whose body is dynamic gives that body the opposite momentum, particle mass × push / step, at the contact point (`ClothSim::pushes`, applied after every cloth stepped, so the parallel stepping keeps its result). Resting, that hands the body the cloth's weight; moving, a heavy cloth shoves a ball. Pushes never reset an awake body's sleep timer, and wake a sleeping one only when, summed over the step, they exceed twice the pushing particles' weight plus 5 cm/s of the body's momentum, and a cloth at rest (counting toward sleep) passes nothing on, so a cloth resting on a box keeps neither awake. The pushes are applied before the rigid step, so its solver takes them in. A cloth much heavier than its body (20 kg on 0.34 kg) still jitters on rapier's soft contacts; up to about ten times the body's mass it settles. Self-collision (on by default) runs once per step after the constraint passes: particles closer than 0.75 grid spacings move apart, found through a hash grid of that cell size; grid neighbours up to two steps apart are left to the constraints. 0.75 of the spacing keeps a particle from slipping through a quad's middle (no point of a quad is farther than 0.71 spacings from a corner), though a fast fold can still tunnel between steps. sponza-cloth's 18 cloths: 2.8 → 3.5 ms per simulated 1/60 s in parallel. Test `a_falling_cloth_pushes_a_ball_and_folds_dont_pass_through`.
- **Camera spline paths** (`Animation::camera_path`, timeline *Camera path*). Keyed per field, an orbit camera's yaw, pitch, distance and target each interpolate alone, so a move between two keys swings the eye around the target instead of travelling straight. With the option on, `Animation::apply` places the camera at `camera_on_path`: the eye and the target, computed at each of the camera's key times (the union of its orbit fields' keys), are joined by centripetal Catmull-Rom splines (Barry–Goldman, α = 0.5: no cusps or loops between close keys; the end segments extended by reflecting the neighbour), and converted back to the orbit fields (`set_orbit`: yaw kept within half a turn of the per-field value, pitch and distance inside `validate()`). A segment keyed Ease starts and stops at rest, Hold holds; Smooth and Linear run at the spline's own pace, so the camera doesn't stop at each key. The path is drawn in the viewport while the timeline isn't playing (`camera_path_points`, 24 samples per segment). Test `a_camera_path_dollies_straight` (a dolly between two keys stays on the line, where the per-field move bows out to the orbit's radius).
- **Curve editor** (timeline *Curves*, `curve_editor`): the selection's float tracks (up to 24; switches, counts and choices have no curve) as curves over the animation's length, each scaled to its own range, with the keys as dots and the playhead. Dragging a key moves it in time (frames unless Shift; never past its neighbours) and value, as one undo step; the value scale stays the one the drag began with. Hovering a key names its path, value and time. Not done: tangent handles (the interpolations have none to edit), box selection, and dragging several keys together.
- **Script console** (`console.rs`, Menu → *Script console…*). *Run* runs a Rhai script from a text box with the `--script` API on its own thread (`Script::run`; `Script::start` reads a file into it), its `print` lines and its end (*Finished* or the error) in a log of the last 2000 lines; *Stop* sets a flag the engine checks on every operation (`on_progress`) and drops a pending `frames(n)` reply, so a script stops inside a loop or a wait. The run is one undo step. **Generators from scripts:** `add(json)` appends an object from its scene-file JSON (the result must pass `validate()`, else the object is dropped and the call errors) and `clear()` removes every object (with their tracks and the puzzle's references to them), so a loop of `add` calls builds a scene, from the console or a `--script` file.
- **Per-frame scripts** (`Animation::script`, the console's *Per frame* tab). `Animation::apply` runs it on the settings' JSON after the tracks and the camera path (`script::per_frame`), with `t`, `frame` and `fps` in scope and `set(key, value)` (number, integer, boolean, or text parsed as JSON else taken as a string) and `get(key)` on any setting by its `/` path; the result goes through `validate()`; if the script fails or sets a value of the wrong type or out of range, the frame keeps the tracks' result and the error shows in the tab. The engine and the compiled script are cached per thread and recompiled when the text changes; at most 2 million operations a frame, so a runaway loop ends with an error (`script::FRAME_ERROR`, shown in the tab) instead of hanging the app. A scene with only a script counts as animated (`Animation::is_empty`), so playback and timeline exports run it. Test `a_per_frame_script_drives_settings`.
- **Multi-layer EXR** (`ImageFormat::Layers`, *EXR with AOVs* in Save the current image and the high-resolution still; `--capture name.layers.exr` and script captures). One EXR part per layer, 32-bit float, ZIP16 (`video::write_layers`, the `exr` crate without its rayon feature; the image crate writes one layer only): `beauty` (linear RGBA, output size) and, at render size, `diffuse_albedo`, `specular_albedo`, `normal` (XYZ), `roughness`, `depth` (linear view z), `motion` (pixels, previous − current), `direct`, `specular`, `object_id` (first-hit material id + 1) and `lpe1`… for each light path expression in use (`Gpu::read_layers`, through a reader for any float texture at its own size, `read_texture`). Parts may differ in size (render vs output size with an upscaler); every part is named, as multi-part files require. Test `layered_exr_round_trips`.
- **Lens prescriptions.** Rows carry the glass's Abbe number V_d (`Row`'s fifth value; 0 = unknown, then `abbe` guesses it from n_d as before, so the double Gauss, wide-angle and fisheye render unchanged), and `indices` fits the Cauchy dispersion through n_d and V_d. The singlet is N-BK7 (64.17, where the guess gave 60.4), so its colour fringes are about 6% narrower than before. New: *Telephoto 200 mm*, designed here (not a commercial prescription): a positive cemented achromat of 100 mm and a negative one of −60 mm, 70 mm apart, each colour-corrected by splitting its thin-lens power by the Abbe numbers (N-BK7 1.5168 / 64.17 and F2 1.62004 / 36.37), stop between the groups; traced 209 mm at f/5.6 in 130 mm of length (telephoto ratio 0.62). Its small rear group vignettes the corners at full aperture. Not done: a Zeiss Planar prescription (the double Gauss here is the same type; no Planar data at hand to cite), and real glasses for the double Gauss, wide-angle and fisheye rows (their sources give n_d only).
- **Export file name templates** (Export → *File name*, `Prefs::name_template`, saved with the preferences): quick saves and high-resolution stills (which add `-high-res`) are named by `video::expand_name`: `{scene}`, `{date}` and `{time}` (local, `GetLocalTime`), `{spp}`, `{w}` and `{h}`; characters Windows forbids become `_`, a device name (CON, NUL, COM1, …) gets a leading `_`, names stop at 120 characters, and an empty name falls back to `caustica`. The self-test and headless runs keep `caustica` (`UiState::fixed_names`), since their checks look for those files. Video exports keep their own editable path. Test `name_templates_expand_and_stay_valid`.
- **The denoiser status line** (status bar, Advanced on): `Gpu::denoiser_activity` says what the denoiser did on the last frame, from the flags that already pick its passes: the native filter reprojecting its history (camera moving), integrating over its history, integrating from scratch after an edit or with temporal history off, or only filtering the held result (no new samples); RR accumulating or reset by an edit or camera cut.
- **Silhouette outlines** (selection and hover). The viewport outlines objects by their visible pixels instead of their projected box: `gizmo::draw_silhouette` adds an egui mesh, a quad over the object's projected box (the whole viewport while a corner is behind the camera, which used to make the box vanish), whose vertex uv marks it (uv.x = −1 − width in pixels, uv.y = the object's id, index + 6). gui.hlsl's `ps_main` sees uv.x < 0 and lights a pixel by how many of 16 samples on two rings (half and full width) land on that id in the object-id texture (u19, scaled from output to render size), when the pixel itself is not on it; so the outline sits just outside what is visible and an occluder cuts it. It is drawn with the UI (the UAV table is bound for the GUI draws too), so captures without the UI never show it, and it needs no new pipeline or constants. Widths: 2 px for the active item, 1.5 for the rest of the selection, 1 for the hover. A hidden object (no pixels) keeps the box; an object wholly behind the camera draws nothing. The ids are compared with a ±0.5 tolerance (interpolated uv). Not done: a selected object that is fully occluded or out of frame shows no outline (the box always showed); the ids are the last traced frame's, so the outline trails a drag by a frame; outlines for lights (they keep their rectangles); objects behind glass or in mirrors aren't outlined there (first hit only); coplanar faces that z-fight show up as speckle in the outline, as they are in the id buffer; no outline during a video export (the ids are at the export's size).
- **LPE labels** ("light off the red wall"; `lpe.rs`). A quoted name right after an event or a set limits it to the surfaces of that name, and a bare name is any event there: `C D'red wall' L`, `C .* L'key'`, `C 'vase' .* L`. Names are the objects' and lights' (lower case, trimmed) plus `left wall`, `right wall`, `back wall`, `floor`, `main light` and `sun` (`lpe::material_names`, by material id). The alphabet grows from 7 events to 8 label classes × 8 (class 0 is every surface whose name no expression uses; up to 7 names over the four slots, shared, `compile_labelled`), so a symbol is class × 8 + event, sets are 64-bit masks, and an unlabelled event matches every class. The table keeps its 32 states per slot, now 64 symbols wide (32 KB), followed by each material id's class (`lpe::classes`, 8192 entries); it is rebuilt when the expressions change or, with a label in use, when a name changes (which also resets the accumulation, since names stay out of the transport key). In the tracer, `lpe_label` is the class of the current hit (set where its material is read) and `lpe_light_label` that of the light the next L event comes from: set by `sample_light`, ReSTIR's `restir_nee`, the equiangular fog sample, an emitter hit and a sun hit.
