V and B events carry class 0 (a label on them never matches). Spaces before a label are ignored like everywhere else (`D 'x'` is `D'x'`). The names count over all four slots: `validate()` and the Look tab compile each expression with the other slots' names (`lpe::check`), so an eighth name is an error instead of a silently black slot. Checked on the Cornell box: `C D'floor' L` equals `C D L` on the floor's pixels and is zero elsewhere (99.7% of pixels; the rest are mixed edge pixels), likewise the left wall. Test `labels_split_the_alphabet`. Not done: building the name list allocates a string per object and light each frame while a label is in use (fine for hundreds, unmeasured at 4096); labels on BDPT and photon mapping (the LPE variant is the path tracer's); several names per surface (an object has one label: its name); labels on the sky or the HDRI; more than 7 names.
- **Material ball thumbnails** (`balls.rs`). The material editor (objects, walls, floor) shows the material on a sphere, 48 points square, and the *Apply a material…* list shows each preset's ball. A ball is a 128 × 128, 40-frame render of a built-in stage (a grounded sphere on a checker floor, sky, a key and a rim light; `SCENE`, never a file in the scene packs), cached as a PNG named by an FNV hash of the material's JSON in `dirs::cache()/materials`. Balls render in the background in a headless copy of the app, up to 8 per process through the `--script` API (`set("objects/0/material", …)`, `frames`, `capture` for each; about 7 s for two in a debug build), so the app's own renderer and settings are untouched. The edited material's ball is asked for only once the material has stayed the same for 0.4 s (no render per slider step), and the previous ball stays up meanwhile; before any ball exists a flat disc of the base colour stands in. Headless runs and the self-test never start a render (`balls::ENABLED`). Test `the_ball_stage_is_a_valid_scene`. File names carry a version (`VERSION`): a batch first deletes balls of other versions and anything an interrupted batch left; a batch that runs over 2 minutes is killed. A failed ball shows the disc. Not done: the cache has no size bound (a PNG is about 20 kB per material ever shown); a material whose texture path contains a backtick or `$` gets no ball (Rhai's backtick strings); a failed ball isn't retried until restart; light thumbnails.

## Startup time (2026-11-04)

New builds sat on a white window for seconds (warm driver shader cache: 5.9 s to the first headless frame; the first launch after a shader change 20–67 s, the driver compiling every pipeline). stderr now prints `STARTUP phase ms` (since `main`: window, device, pipelines, gpu, first-frame). Measured warm, release, 320 × 240 headless: device 0.36 s, the ~22 compute pipelines and 7 SER state objects 3.65 s one after another, NGX init 1.45 s, the rest (FSR, XeSS, resize, scene) 0.2 s.

- The compute pipelines (`create_compute_pipelines`) and the SER state objects (`make_ser`) are created on one thread each (`in_parallel`; the device is free-threaded, `Threaded` wraps the COM pointers): 3.65 → 0.7 s.
- NGX init runs on its own thread from right after the root signature, holding a device reference, and is joined where it used to be called.
- `present_blank` clears the first back buffer to dark grey and presents it right after the swap chain exists (0.36 s), so the window is never white for long; a failure there is only logged.
- Result: first frame at 2.1–2.3 s (headless and windowed), the window dark from 0.36 s.

## Oracle and the pixel-filter finding (2026-11-04)

`tools/oracle.py [--scenes a,b] [--integrator 0|1|2] [--rgb] [--set k=v]` renders each scene on the GPU (raw, `--no-rr --scale 1 firefly=0`, 1024 spp) and with the CPU reference (path tracing unless `--cpu-integrator` says otherwise, 512 spp) at 320 × 240 and tests d = GPU − CPU luminance: the mean's z = mean(d) / (std(d)/√pixels) (structure cancels, the rest is the two renders' noise), failing beyond |z| > 4 and 0.2%, or a block of an 8 × 8 grid beyond |z| > 5 and 2%. Refused scenes print SKIP. Defaults: the eight scenes measured when the reference was written; all pass for path tracing.

Finding: BDPT and photon mapping always use a box pixel filter, the path tracer (and the CPU reference) the scene's filter, Blackman-Harris 1.5 px by default. Filter importance sampling with weight 1 per sample keeps each pixel's weights normalized, but the shifted filters don't sum to 1 over the pixel grid, so the image mean of a sharp, bright, small feature (a light seen directly, a sub-pixel sun) depends on where its edges fall: Cornell box BDPT / PT +0.65% at light size 0.75, −0.55% at 1.5, +0.3% at 0.25, identical for photon mapping. With a 1-px box on both sides they agree (0.9993–0.9999), as do measured-cornell and sky-garden-dusk (BDPT/PT 0.9956, BSDF-only/MIS 1.002, 240 × 135, 2048 spp, 32 bounces), which closes the "+0.9% BDPT" and "dusk 10–12% dark" items. `integrator_sweep.py` dropped its brightest-0.5% mask for matched box filters. Separately, BSDF-only sampling is 0.4% darker than light sampling at the default 6 bounces (light sampling at the last vertex adds one more path length) and equal at 32.

## Vulkan backend: design (2026-11-04)

Goal: the whole renderer on Vulkan (`ash`), selectable on Windows (`--vulkan`) and the only backend on Linux. D3D12 stays the reference: its code is not rewritten behind an abstraction, and the self-test must stay bit-identical.

- **Shape:** `gpu::GpuState` holds the backend-neutral public state the app reads (sizes, sample count, HDR state and levels, status strings, timings, counts, scopes, probe, path and battle results, reference flag); `Gpu` (D3D12) and `vk::Gpu` (Vulkan) each embed one and `Deref` to it. `Renderer` is an enum of the two that `Deref`s to `GpuState` and forwards the ~30 methods the app calls (`render`, `rebuild_scene`, `update_materials`, `capture`, `read_*`, `set_environment`, `set_lut`, `resize*`, `set_hdr`, `reset`, `capabilities`, ...). On Linux only the Vulkan arm exists (`cfg(windows)` on the D3D12 module, the NGX / FSR / XeSS D3D12 bridges, `display.rs`).
- **Shared CPU work:** what both backends compute (the `Constants` block, the instance table and its BLAS grouping, material and emitter packing, which passes run this frame) moves out of `gpu/` into backend-neutral functions as the Vulkan side needs each one, so the two can't drift.
- **Bindings** (SPIR-V from the same HLSL, `dxc -spirv`, see `tools/spirv_check.py`): set 0 = the root signature's space 0 with register classes shifted apart (u0–u31 at bindings 0–31, t at +100, b at +200, s at +300: `-fvk-t-shift 100 0 -fvk-b-shift 200 0 -fvk-s-shift 300 0`); root SRVs / UAVs become storage-buffer descriptors, the TLAS an acceleration-structure descriptor; set 1 = the bindless `maps[]` (descriptor indexing, partially bound, 4096); set 2 = space 2's raw UAVs; the egui texture is set 0 binding 102 in the graphics pipeline. One descriptor set per frame is rewritten when a resource changes (one frame in flight, as on D3D12). Constants: a uniform buffer at the same offsets (`CB_STRIDE` slots), so `Constants` stays one struct.
- **Ray tracing:** `VK_KHR_acceleration_structure` + `VK_KHR_ray_query` (+ buffer device address, deferred host operations); BLAS per mesh and the static groups, TLAS with the same instance masks and custom indices; compaction as on D3D12; opacity micromaps through `VK_EXT_opacity_micromap` later (off until then: alpha falls back to the any-hit loop the shaders already have). No SER (D3D12 measured it slower).
- **Phases** (each ends runnable and verified; the D3D12 self-test unchanged after each):
  1. `GpuState` on D3D12 (done 2026-11-04: 35 public fields, `Gpu` derefs to it; self-test unchanged). The `Renderer` enum comes with phase 2, when there is a second arm.
  2. Done 2026-11-04. `src/vulkan.rs`: `Device` (instance, optional validation layer + debug messenger feeding `vulkan::messages()`, the best device with Vulkan 1.3, acceleration structures, ray query, buffer device address, descriptor indexing, synchronization2), `Swapchain` (one release semaphore per image, rebuilt on resize / out-of-date), `Frame` (one command buffer, fence and acquire semaphore: one frame in flight). `caustica --vulkan [--headless] [--frames N] [--debug] [--no-vsync]` is a standalone smoke run: headless clears an offscreen image N times and checks a readback (`VULKAN_OK …`); windowed clears and presents. Passes on the RTX 4070 Ti SUPER (headless and 300 presented frames; Windows has no validation layer installed) and on lavapipe with validation (headless, and windowed under Xvfb).
  2b. Done 2026-11-04 (the Linux build, moved up from phase 6 so the VM tests every later phase). `windows` is a `cfg(windows)` dependency; `src/platform.rs` holds the shims (`HWND` stand-in, `no_window` for `CREATE_NO_WINDOW`, `local_time`, the taskbar-icon attribute); `display.rs` keeps DXGI / WIC behind `cfg(windows)` (Linux: SDR, no JPEG XR); `dirs.rs` uses XDG config / cache folders; build.rs returns before DXIL, the C++ bridges, the DLL copies and `rc.exe` off Windows. The backend-neutral types (`GpuState`, `Probe`, `AovLayer`, `Capabilities`, `BattleQuadrant`, half floats, `SCOPES`, `BATTLE_NAMES`) moved to `src/render.rs`; `gpu.rs` re-exports them. On Linux `crate::gpu` is `vulkan::gpu`, a `Gpu` with the D3D12 API that clears and presents (captures and readbacks report "not on Vulkan yet"), so the whole app runs: `xvfb-run caustica --frames 30` renders on lavapipe, 136 tests pass. Dead-code lints are allowed off Windows until phase 5. The `Renderer` enum (both arms on Windows) waits for phase 3, when the Vulkan arm draws something worth selecting.
  3. Done 2026-11-04. build.rs compiles every non-SER entry to SPIR-V too (`-fvk-use-dx-layout`, t / b / s shifted to 100 / 200 / 300, spaces = sets; the DXC release from `tools/setup.ps1` / `tools/setup.sh`, optional on Windows, `cfg(spirv)`); `common.hlsli` marks u10 / u26 format-unknown and u30 rgba16f for SPIR-V (DXIL ignores `[[vk::image_format]]`). `tools/spirv_bindings.py` lists the shaders' bindings: their union is conflict-free, so one pipeline layout (`vulkan/layout.rs`, all bindings partially bound, b0 a dynamic uniform buffer) serves every pass as the root signature does. Shared CPU work moved out of `gpu/` and is used by both backends: `render::plan` (static groups, visible meshes, the instance list with masks / opacity / records, the light tables), `render::constants` (the `Constants` fill), `render::texture_layout`; the D3D12 refactor is pixel-identical (4 scenes, max abs difference 0). `vulkan/res.rs`: buffers with device addresses, images in GENERAL, one-shot submits, BLAS / TLAS builds. `vulkan/gpu.rs`: textures, scene upload (rebuilt whole each time: no BLAS reuse, refit or compaction yet), `trace` / `trace_spectral` + `display`, blit to the swap chain, EXR / PNG captures. `src/renderer.rs` (Windows): `Renderer` enum, D3D12 or `--vulkan`; the smoke run moved to `--vulkan-smoke`. Measured against D3D12 (320×240, 64 spp raw): preset 0, shape-material-matrix, veach-mis, birthday-cake within noise on lavapipe (luminance ratio 0.9995–1.0009, |z| ≤ 1.3) and on the RTX (veach-mis 1.0003, z 0.05).
  4. Done 2026-11-05. The native denoiser (`filter` passes 0–6 and the guide copies, the same history logic as D3D12), `read_texture` / `read_floats` / `read_layers` and `.layers.exr` captures (the layer list is shared, `render::aov_layers`), the egui pass (`vulkan/gui.rs`: its own set layout with u19 / t2 / b0 / s0 and one descriptor set per egui texture, since one set can't change texture between draws; dynamic rendering onto the swap chain image after the blit, negative viewport height for D3D clip space), UI captures (`keep_ui_frame` copies the finished swap image), the probe and scopes (read back after the fence, as D3D12), timestamps (13 queries; battle mode's per-quadrant pairs). Render waits on the frame fence at its end, as D3D12's `submit`, and `collect` reads the results.
  5. Done 2026-11-05. BDPT, photon mapping, ReSTIR (presample / initial / spatial, history), glare (the FFT grid), the lens system, LPE (table and AOVs), texture maps (set 1 `maps[]`, mips, `render::attach_maps` shared with D3D12), the LEAN and maps trace variants, environment and its CDF, the 3D LUT, battle mode, HDR swap chains (scRGB `R16G16B16A16_SFLOAT` / `EXTENDED_SRGB_LINEAR`, HDR10 `A2B10G10R10` / `HDR10_ST2084` through `VK_EXT_swapchain_colorspace`, falling back as D3D12's `set_hdr`). Space-2 buffers (path records, LPE AOVs, reservoirs, photons, glare grid) are set 2. **NVIDIA's SPIR-V compiler** expanded the big `static const` tables (the layer albedo fits, the spectral bases) at every use: 40 s and 10 GB per tracer, BDPT never finished. Under `__spirv__` the generated headers declare them as `StructuredBuffer`s t10–t17 instead, filled from the header text by `render::hlsl_table` (the generators `tools/fit_layers.py` / `tools/spectral_tables.py` write both forms); cold compiles are 4–9 s per tracer, under 1 GB. D3D12 vs Vulkan at 160×120, 24 frames (luminance ratio): raw 0.9999, denoised 0.9998, BDPT 1.0000, photons 1.0000, ReSTIR 0.9978, glare 0.9999, textured 1.0002, HDRI 0.9995, battle 1.0002, spectral 0.9999.
  6. Done 2026-11-05 on Windows. `bridge/vk.cpp`: NGX Vulkan (Ray Reconstruction `DLSSD`, DLSS SR) and XeSS Vulkan (`libxess.dll`'s `xessVK*`), the same C ABI shape as the D3D12 bridges; images go as (image, view, format, size) in GENERAL. The instance and device extensions they ask for (`NVSDK_NGX_VULKAN_RequiredExtensions`, `xessVKGetRequired*Extensions`) are enabled where offered, with 16-bit / 8-bit storage and float16 / int8 / int16 where the device has them. `vulkan/gpu/upscale.rs` ports `prepare_upscaler` / `run_upscaler` / the RR path, the jitter and the SR accumulation rule; NGX starts beside the pipeline compile. Against D3D12 (320×240 at 50%, 30 frames, mean absolute difference): RR 0.0006, DLSS SR 0.0015, XeSS 0.0013 (each differs from bicubic by 0.008, as on D3D12). Also fixed: a camera move no longer zeroes the motion vectors (only a history reset does, as D3D12's `rr_reset`). Phase 3 leftovers done: static groups rebuilt only when they change, mesh BLASes kept by key and compacted (one compacted-size query per rebuild, then `COPY_MODE_COMPACT`), the procedural BLAS built once, the TLAS refit when only transforms change (`res::build_tlas`, a full build every 64); chess / sdf-gallery / forest-1 / physics-1 match D3D12 (0.9997–1.0006), and a 120-frame drag on forest-1 refits and matches (0.9999). ffmpeg next to the exe uses the platform's exe suffix, else PATH. `--vulkan --self-test` passes (all 520 frames and every scene file, 48 s; the egui pass needs `fragmentStoresAndAtomics` for the object-id write, now required). A `linux` CI job (clippy, tests, release build; DXC from `tools/setup.sh`) is in `ci.yml`, idle while CI is paused. Not done: FSR on Vulkan (the vendored FidelityFX SDK ships only the DX12 loader), the upscalers on Linux (`dlopen` of the Linux NGX / XeSS libraries, not vendored), shader hot reload on Vulkan (`reload_shaders` is a no-op).
- **Test bed:** a Debian 13 VM (`ssh caustica-vm`, no GPU) runs Mesa's lavapipe (CPU Vulkan with ray queries) for the Linux build, headless renders and validation layers; the RTX machine runs Vulkan on Windows for speed and NGX.

## Shader compile progress (2026-11-05)

On a cold driver cache (a new driver, new shaders) the 22 compute pipelines take seconds to compile even in parallel, with the window blank. Both backends now show a game-style "Compiling shaders 14/22" screen meanwhile: `render::progress` lays out text in a 5×7 pixel font above a bar (as rects, display-encoded colours, every rect inside the frame) and `watch` polls the workers' atomic counter on the calling thread, drawing when the count changes once 250 ms have passed, so a warm cache (well under that) shows nothing. Each backend draws it with clears only, since no pipeline exists yet: Vulkan clears rects inside a dynamic rendering pass on the swap image (`vulkan/progress.rs`) and also puts the count in the window title; D3D12 calls `ClearRenderTargetView` with rects (`present_progress`), at most 16 per call, because one call with the whole text (~170 rects) fails fast inside the runtime (0xc0000409). The window doesn't pump messages during the compile (as before).

## Local releases and the AppImage (2026-11-05)

CI costs money, so releases are built on the owner's machine (owner, 2026-11-05). `tools/release.ps1 -Version X.Y.Z [-AppImage] [-Upload]` does what the CI release job did: a release build into `target/dist` (never the launcher's `target/release`) with `CAUSTICA_VERSION` set, staging and the installer through `tools/make_installer.ps1` (Inno Setup 7 found through the registry; the 6.3+ script compiles unchanged under 7), a zip of the staged folder, and with `-Upload` `gh release create` with the commit list since the previous tag. `-AppImage` copies the tagged commit to the VM (`git archive | ssh tar`), runs `tools/appimage.sh` there and copies the result back.

`tools/appimage.sh` (Linux): the release exe, `scenes/` (the tracked files: the asset packs would make it ~870 MB) and the README in `usr/bin/` of an AppDir with a `.desktop` file, the icon (`assets/caustica.png`) and an AppRun, packed by appimagetool 1.9.1 (pinned by SHA-256 into `vendor/`, run extract-and-run so the build needs no FUSE; it fetches its own runtime). The read-only mount is the app's installed mode (`dirs.rs`): settings, user scenes and captures go to `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/caustica`, caches to `$XDG_CACHE_HOME/caustica`. The Vulkan loader and the driver come from the system, ffmpeg from PATH. 15 MB; on the VM (lavapipe) it renders headless and loads bundled scenes by name. It needs the build machine's glibc or newer (Debian 13's 2.41 from the VM): building on an older base would widen that.

## Outline jitter, the Renderer menu and DLSS on Linux (2026-11-05)

- **Outline jitter (owner report):** with RR or a temporal upscaler on, the guide ray of `write_guides` (pathtrace.hlsl) follows the frame's Halton jitter, and it also wrote `object_id`. Edge pixels then switched object every frame, and the selection outline (gui.hlsl), hover and picking flickered while samples accumulated, on both backends. The ids are now written only on a frame that starts the accumulation (`sampling.x == 0`) or carries no jitter, so they hold the first frame's hits until the view changes. Measured at 320×240 with RR, frames 8 → 9: 1230 changed id pixels before, 0 after. The guides RR reads (albedo, normals, depth, motion) still follow the jitter, as NGX expects.
- **Menu → Renderer (Windows):** `Prefs::vulkan` picks the backend for windowed runs from the next start (the renderer can't be swapped under a live scene). `--vulkan` / `--d3d12` override it for one run; hidden and self-test runs read only the flags, so they stay reproducible. A Vulkan picked in the menu that fails to start falls back to D3D12 and keeps the error in `App::vulkan_failed` for the menu. *Restart now* spawns `current_exe` with the same arguments minus `--vulkan` / `--d3d12` and exits the event loop.
- **Linux DLSS:** `bridge/vk.cpp` builds on Linux too: `#ifdef _WIN32` around the module loading (`dlopen` / `dlsym` there), the exe folder from `/proc/self/exe` (UTF-8 decoded by hand into the `wchar_t` path NGX takes on both platforms). build.rs (`linux_upscalers`) compiles it when `tools/setup.sh` has fetched the DLSS SDK (headers, `lib/Linux_x86_64/libnvsdk_ngx.a`, the `rel/` RR and SR runtimes; not `dlssg`), the XeSS headers and the Vulkan headers, links the static NGX with `stdc++` and `dl`, copies the runtimes beside the binary and sets `cfg(upscalers)`, which gates the FFI in `vulkan/gpu/upscale.rs` (always set on Windows; without the SDK a Linux build has no upscalers and says so in the reasons). `tools/appimage.sh` bundles the runtimes in `usr/bin/` (they are where NGX looks first); `tools/release.ps1` keeps the VM's `vendor/` between releases so the SDK isn't fetched again. XeSS: Intel's SDK (3.0.2, every release zip checked) has Windows DLLs only, so on Linux it reports "Intel ships XeSS for Windows only"; the bridge would `dlopen` a `libxess.so` beside the binary if one appears. Tested: clippy and a build on the VM, NGX linked and declining cleanly on lavapipe; untested on an NVIDIA GPU under Linux (roadmap d).
- **Owner fixes, same day:** Vulkan's texture-map sampler gets 16x anisotropy (it was plain linear, so the anisotropy setting did nothing; `Device::anisotropy` tracks the `samplerAnisotropy` feature). Vulkan presents on frames that trace nothing: pausing, and convergence, had frozen the UI, because `present` was gated on `draw`. The hover outline is gone, along with the per-frame probe readback that fed it: outlines show only for the selection (owner: in Sponza an object is under the pointer everywhere). Not yet run (owner: no checks for this round).

## Linux on a real desktop (2026-11-06)

First run outside the VM: RTX 4070 Ti SUPER, NVIDIA 615.78, KDE Plasma 6.8 on Wayland, one HDR monitor.
- **Exit crashes:** every run segfaulted after `RENDER_OK`. `Gpu` and `egui_winit::State` lived in `App`, past `run_app`, which closes the Wayland connection; NVIDIA's `vkDestroySwapchainKHR` and the smithay-clipboard worker then used dead proxies. `App::exiting` now drops the GPU, the window and the input state, in that order.
- **Hidden runs:** Wayland can't hide a window, so `--headless` and the self-test really show one. They now ignore OS pointer and key events: the real pointer over a UI panel suppressed the light-meter probe, and the self-test failed at frame 209.
- **Self-test:** passes (477 frames). JPEG XR goes through WIC, so `ImageFormat::ALL` and the self-test's frames 228 / 229 skip it off Windows.
- **DLSS:** RR and SR run on the bundled NGX runtimes. At 1280×720, 50%, 40 frames of preset 0, reconstruct takes about 1 ms; the mean absolute difference from bicubic is 0.0054 (RR) and 0.0068 (SR), and a sheet of veach-mis looks right. Means are 2% (RR) and 3% (SR) under native, and not a Linux fault as far as can be told without a D3D12 run here: off a 4-pixel rim around the light (the HDR edge `upscale_quality.py` already singles out) RR is 0.995 of native, and SR rises from 0.982 at 40 frames to 0.996 at 400.
- **`display::Monitor`** (`src/display/linux.rs`): `wp_color_manager_v1` on a connection of its own, instead of the roadmap's `kwinoutputconfig.json`, which keeps stale entries per connector and has no EDID peak unless overridden. The protocol is live, per output and not KDE-only. The window's output comes from winit's monitor name, matched to `wl_output.name`, and rechecked with the description's `image_description_changed` every 30 frames. HDR is PQ, or wider primaries than sRGB, or target peak above reference: KWin describes an HDR output as gamma 2.2 in BT.2020, so the transfer function alone doesn't tell. No protocol or X11: SDR as before. Known limits: an SDR panel with wide primaries also reads as HDR (colours stay right, the compositor converts), because at no headroom only the primaries tell; a failed query stays SDR until the window changes output, since retrying would open a connection every 30 frames where the protocol is absent. The query gives up after 1 s rather than hang the UI thread. A hidden run asks for no output, because Wayland can't hide a window and it would pick up the desktop's HDR (the self-test failed on that).
- **The surface is relative:** the compositor shows a signal of 203 nits at its reference white, not at 203 nits. `GpuState::hdr_signal` scales the encoded levels by 203 / `hdr_reference`; `hdr_levels` stays in real nits for the UI, and D3D12 (reference 0) is unchanged. So an HDR PNG or HDR10 video saved here has paper white at 203 nits, and the video's light level is tagged with the signal peak. Measured by KWin window screenshots (`spectacle -b -n -a`) at reference 510: UI panel 10,12,15 in SDR, scRGB and HDR10 alike, image centre within 3 levels, and paper white set to 255 dims p99 from 205 to 153, which is 0.52 in linear light.
- **`VK_EXT_hdr_metadata`:** enabled where offered; `Swapchain::set_hdr_metadata` sends the signal peak when it changes, so the compositor needn't assume 10000 nits. This also applies to Vulkan on Windows, not yet run there. Runs without errors; its effect wasn't isolated, since nothing here runs without it.
- **Headroom:** with the desktop's brightness at 50% the reference is 258 under a 510 peak (the SDR brightness setting still reads 510, so read the protocol, not the setting). Auto mode matches SDR on the UI panel and the image centre. With paper white 64, the brightest viewport pixel measures 80, 129 and 258 nits for peaks of 80, 128 and 258, in HDR10 and scRGB. Screenshots clip at the reference, so 258–510 nits is unmeasured.
- **Live brightness:** moving the slider with the app open is followed through `image_description_changed`, reference 510 down to 5 and back, no restart. That range showed two faults, now fixed: the default paper white had Windows' floor of 40 nits (8× the desktop at reference 5), and the signal peak passed PQ's 10000 nits (`hdr_signal` caps it).
- **Validation:** the layer found NGX's `VK_EXT_buffer_device_address` enabled beside core `bufferDeviceAddress` (VUID-VkDeviceCreateInfo-pNext-04748), now dropped from what NGX asks for; DLSS still runs. NGX's private `vkCreateCuModuleNVX` chain is filtered out of the messages. The self-test passes under the current layer (1.4.363, Arch's `vulkan-validation-layers`): exit 0, no messages, with handle wrapping on and off. Steam's old layer (header 284) crashes at random and on NGX; don't use it.
- **CI steps by hand:** clippy, tests, debug and release builds and both self-tests pass.
- **Live HDR toggle:** turning HDR off and on in System Settings, twice, is followed without a restart (off reads `hdr: false`, peak and white 200; on reads back 510 and 232) and no errors.
- **Hot reload:** a good edit reloads in 13 s; a broken one reports `gui.hlsl:26:1` and keeps the old pipelines; the revert reloads.
- **Not checked:** the Windows build (these edits are small and cfg-gated, but only Linux was compiled).

## Self-update (2026-11-06)

`src/update.rs`. At startup a background thread reads `releases/latest`; a newer version opens the Update window (version, size, notes, Update, Skip this version, Later, and the *Check for updates at startup* preference, also in the Menu). Download and install are separate clicks, because a release is about 700 MB (the setup and the AppImage bundle the assets): the download never ends in a surprise restart.
- **When it looks:** only an interactive, `Installed`-mode, non-`-dev` run with the preference on: never headless, scripted, frame-limited, the self-test or a build from source. Any failure offers nothing, and nothing waits for the thread.
- **The repository must be public.** The check and the downloads send no token; on a private repo both get a 404 and the app stays silent. `REPO` is the one constant to change. It went public on 2026-11-06 by the owner's decision, after an audit; `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md` ships from the next release, and the licence questions stay open in the roadmap.
- **Verification:** `SHA256SUMS` (`sha256sum` format; `tools/release.ps1` writes it with LF, no BOM) is fetched first. No file, no entry or a wrong hash refuses the update and deletes the download. This catches corruption, not a compromised account, since the sums come from the same place. Releases before this feature have no sums, and an old version has no updater: the first release with one is installed by hand.
- **Windows:** only a copy with `unins000.exe` beside it (else the release page). Downloads to the cache folder, starts the setup with `/SILENT /SUPPRESSMSGBOXES /NORESTART /relaunch=1` (`/SILENT` since 2026-11-11, owner: the setup's progress window shows; it asks nothing, and `skipifsilent` applies to both modes), exits through `shut_down`. `caustica.iss` has a second `[Run]` entry for `/relaunch=1`, because the wizard's is `skipifsilent`. Inno's `UsePreviousPrivileges` default should keep an all-users install all-users. The Update window isn't drawn during an export (only the export window is), so nothing can start an install mid-export.
- **Linux:** `$APPIMAGE` is the running file. Its folder is tested for writing before any download; if it isn't writable, or the app isn't an AppImage, the window offers the release page. Downloads beside it (same filesystem), marks it executable, renames it over the old file, starts it with the same arguments minus the update switches and `APPIMAGE`, `APPDIR`, `ARGV0`, in `OWD`. The file keeps its old, version-numbered name. Update files are per process, so two running copies can't mix their downloads; ones untouched for an hour go at the next start.
- **After an update:** `CAUSTICA_UPDATED_FROM` makes the new process print `UPDATED from X to Y` and show a toast, and it is cleared so a later Menu restart doesn't repeat it.
- **Testing without a release:** `--update-from <file|URL>` offers it as the newest version, with `SHA256SUMS` beside it. `--update-yes` clicks through (prints `UPDATE_START`, `UPDATE_READY`, `UPDATE_FAILED`, `UPDATE_PAGE`, exit 3 or 4 on the last two). `--update-cancel-at <bytes>` cancels the download (`UPDATE_CANCELLED`).
- **Windows under Wine (2026-11-06):** a real Windows release build works from this Linux box under Wine 11: `cargo.exe`, MSVC, the SDK's dxc and rc from the C: drive, `vendor/` from the Windows checkout, Inno Setup's ISCC for the setup. Wine has no DXR, so `--vulkan` (or `"vulkan": true` in the prefs) is what runs there. Checked under it: silent per-user update including the relaunch; a plain `/VERYSILENT` install doesn't start the app; a zip copy gets the release page; a bad checksum is refused; an all-users install stays one copy in Program Files and updates, but the relaunch didn't show under Wine.
- **First real run (2026-11-06):** v0.11.0 updated itself to v0.11.1 from GitHub with no debug source, on Linux (AppImage, 87 s, hash matches the release, relaunched) and under Wine (setup, reinstalled and relaunched). Both were built on the owner's Linux box: the AppImage against its newer glibc, the Windows build under Wine. Starting `caustica.exe` while the setup runs can hold the file and leave the old version in place: seen once in a test that launched it repeatedly. D3D12 can't be tested under Proton here: v0.10.1 aborts the same way after creating its device.
- **Older files:** `update::tests::old_scene_folder_loads` (ignored; `CAUSTICA_OLD_SCENES`) parsed and validated every scene file of v0.3.2, v0.6.0, v0.10.0 and v0.10.1: 771 files, 0 failures. A trimmed one stays as a fixture.

## Scene overhaul (2026-11-05)

Every scene file was reviewed with a feature-coverage audit (which `Settings` fields each scene sets) and contact sheets of the thumbnails; the gaps were features no scene showed, scenes too dark to read, near-duplicates, and bland stages. `tools/build_scenes.py` holds the changes in its "scene overhaul" section; `--overwrite=a,b` now rewrites only the named files, so hand-edited ones (measured-cornell, newtons-cradle, underwater) stay as they are.
- **Removed (redundant):**
  - pool-table: pool-break-1984 is the same table, keyframed.
  - veach-ajar-bdpt: veach-ajar now opens in BDPT.
  - cornell-caustic-photons: cornell-caustic's description points at the integrator.
  - lens-fairy-lights: replaced by street-bokeh.
  - stage-smoke: merged into stage-beams.
  - duck-and-lantern: texture-duck-on-wood shows the duck.
  - 56 generated seeds: each family keeps its 1 to 3 most distinct seeds (45 files, physics all 5).
- **New, one per feature no scene used:**
  - street-bokeh: lens system 1 at f/1.4, dispersion, glare.
  - tilt-shift-village: projection 4.
  - little-planet: projection 3.
  - panorama-gallery: projection 2.
  - isometric-workshop: projection 1, a sphere light.
  - sun-greenhouse: sun light.
  - bulb-workshop: sphere lights at blackbody temperatures.
  - flashlight-crypt: flashlight.
  - lighthouse-fog: noise fog with extinction, a spot turned by a light rotation track.
  - blackbody-forge: `Material::temperature`, spectral.
  - diamond-fire: dispersion 55 at IOR 2.42, spectral.
  - neon-diner: halation, grain, vignette, lift/gain and a LUT.
  - festival-banners: cloth with an edge pin, wind and gusts.
  - swinging-lantern: an 8-link ball-joint chain.
  - Environment scenes for unused HDRIs: fireside glassware, loft ceramics, chrome in the city, glass at sunrise.
  - materials: loft corner.
- **Reworked:**
  - abacus: desk, DOF, six blades, cat eye.
  - bowling-lane: kickbacks, neon, a keyframed strike with a 180° shutter.
  - desert-monolith: dunes under the physical sky.
  - disco-floor: dark glass floor, four spots in equiangular haze.
  - jewellery-box: velvet sheen, a lacquered box, DOF.
  - zen-garden: physical sky, stone lantern, veranda.
  - mirror-maze and glossy-floor-tiles: brighter, coloured orbs.
  - scattering-materials: backlit, opal glass with extinction.
  - night-skyline: beacons, moon, glare.
  - torii-corridor: a camera-path walk.
  - orrery: a Rhai script orbiting the planets at Kepler's rate.
  - bunny-museum: IES disk spots.
  - sdf-gallery: the exact cylinder and cone.
  - stage-beams: noise smoke.
  - veach-ajar: BDPT.
  - By script: dark-side (denser haze, a card for the rainbow), optics-bench (haze shows the beam), birthday-cake (gingham, gift, champagne, DOF), wrecking-ball (physical sky, concrete, spun steel), sponza-cloth (its own view in late sun), and the laser puzzles (a locked housing behind each beam origin, appended so target indices hold).
- **`lut_path`** resolves relative to the scene folders first (`mesh::resolve`, then the working directory), and *Browse…* stores it relative when inside one, so shipped scenes can use `luts/…`. `tools/make_luts.py` writes `scenes/luts/warm-film.cube` (33³, in the gamma-2.2 display space where post.hlsl applies LUTs).
- Equiangular fog sampling is off while fog noise is on, so the noisy scenes (stage-beams, lighthouse-fog) don't set it.

## Tails batch: correctness and Now c (2026-11-05)

Changes made without a build; all are listed as [done 2026-11-05, untested] in the roadmap.
- **One pixel filter for every integrator.** BDPT and photon mapping now jitter camera rays by `filter_offset` (filter importance sampling, as the path tracer does). BDPT's light-tracing strategy (t = 1) used to splat into the one pixel holding the film point, which is a 1-px box. It now filters it as the camera rays are (`splat_filtered`): per axis, one pixel of the support is picked with probability w / W (w = `filter_density` of the offset from the pixel centre: the BH window over 0.35875 · width, a Gaussian normalized over ±3σ, or a box) and gets the sample × Wx · Wy, so pixel p's expected share is exactly its filter weight. The pick hashes the film point and the frame, so the random stream is unchanged. A first version splatted into every pixel of the support (up to 5 × 5, three compare-exchange loops each); on self-test frame 295 (RGB BDPT on mirror + glass with dispersion 20 and four LPE slots) that contention ran past the TDR limit and removed the device. One splat per sample costs what the old 1-px splat did. The MIS weights don't depend on the pixel, so they still sum to 1 per path; each strategy estimates the same filtered pixel. A 1-px box is the old splat exactly. Splats stay inside their dispatch region (battle quadrants). The oracle and the sweep keep their matched 1-px box.
- **Hit side from the face normal.** `intersect` decided `front` (entering vs leaving a dielectric) from the interpolated normal, and `Hit::ng` was that normal. Now the triangle's face normal is computed from its positions, turned toward the shading normal, and decides the side; `ng` is that face normal (ray offsets and the texture footprint). SDF hits keep the gradient.
- **Sun weight.** A sun's power in the light CDF was its irradiance times a nominal 50 units². It is now irradiance times the scene's bounds (floor or Cornell box, and the visible objects) projected along the sun direction (`lighting::sun_area`). That is the flux the scene receives, so a sun beside small bright lights in a large scene gets its share.
- **Photon chains.** A cell's chain is newest first, and deposit order correlates with power: a photon's later, weaker bounces deposit later. So the first 4096 of a longer chain were biased toward weak photons. Past 4096 the gather now counts the chain (up to 2^20) and walks it again, taking every k-th photon from a random start and scaling by k (`restart_chain`, systematic sampling, unbiased).
- **Mesh UVs for texture sets** (`Material::texture_uv`, "Mesh UVs"): `base_scale.w` < 0 takes the mesh's UV branch with |w| repeats per UV unit (the UVs, the footprint scale and the tangents are scaled together). Shapes without UVs read UV 0.
- **HDR10 typed store**: `set_hdr` checks D3D12_FORMAT_SUPPORT2_UAV_TYPED_STORE for R10G10B10A2 before HDR10 and falls back to scRGB.
- **Physics and editing:**
  - G / R / S drop a pending mouse grab.
  - If the simulation starts while a transform runs, its undo step is the transform's original, so the first transform after enabling physics takes one undo.
  - `script::set_paths` runs the per-frame script once at t = 0 with `set` recording paths (cached by source and object count). Objects whose pose it sets become kinematic, as keyed ones are.
- **Startup**: Gpu::new no longer joins NGX's thread when it is still running. The first frames use the native denoiser with the status "DLSS starting", and `poll_ngx` (each frame, and on drop) takes NGX up. Headless runs, the self-test and `--require-rr` call `wait_ngx` first, so their output doesn't change.
- **Caches**: mesh `LOADED` hits re-resolve their path. A failed `rebuild_scene` clears `compact_queue` and `tlas_cache`.
- **Vulkan**:
  - Hot reload compiles SPIR-V with the SPIR-V DXC (`CAUSTICA_DXC_SPIRV` from build.rs, the same flags, SER libraries skipped).
  - `reload_shaders` builds all 22 compute pipelines before swapping any.
  - `progress::watch` calls `PeekMessageW(PM_NOREMOVE)` every 16 ms during a cold compile, so Windows doesn't mark the window as not responding. That peek dispatches only sent messages, and winit buffers what arrives during its callback.

- **HDR retry (found by the self-test, 2026-11-05).** With Windows HDR on, the RTX's Vulkan surface offered only SRGB_NONLINEAR formats (no scRGB, no HDR10), so `set_hdr` fell back to SDR, `App::redraw` saw HDR still wanted and asked again the next frame, and each try resized the output: accumulation reset every frame (`--vulkan` headless runs reported spp samples after any number of frames; self-test frame 2 failed). D3D12 had the same hole when DXGI refuses the colour space. Both backends now set `GpuState::hdr_refused`; the app doesn't ask again until the display changes, and shows the note. The self-test prints a note and skips the forced-HDR checks and the HDR PNG / JPEG XR exports (frames 187–188, 227–229) when the swapchain offers no HDR format. Integrator agreement after the filter work (1024 spp, 240×135, mean ratio per channel to path tracing): with the scenes' filters, BDPT 0.998–1.001 and photon mapping 0.995–1.001 on cornell-caustic, veach-ajar and measured-cornell; with a 1-px box, BDPT 0.9998–1.0016, photon 0.995–1.002; white furnace (box) BDPT 0.9996, photon 0.9953.
- **Hand checks after v0.10.0 (2026-11-05).** Shader hot reload polled only in `redraw`, which an idle loop (paused, converged) doesn't run, so an edit waited for the next render; `about_to_wait` now redraws once when `HotReload::changed`. The reload compiled the entries one after another on the main thread (84 s on Vulkan); it now runs one DXC per entry at once (24 s). Scenes: an emitter enclosing a light shadows it (shadow rays hit the enclosing sphere, visible or not): lighthouse-fog's spot sat inside the lamp ball and the lamp-glow sphere light, so its beam never lit the fog. The lamp and the glow now sit below the spot, and the scene turns glass shadows off so the beam leaves the lantern room. tools/mesh_parts.json held "Â·" for "·" (a `--mesh-info` capture through PowerShell 5.1's `>`), which six mesh and environment scenes carried into their object names; repaired.

## Undo and the last session (2026-11-06)

- **Panel edits** (`UiState::watch_edits`, called from `App::track_edits` after the UI): on a frame after a pointer release or key event, with no button down and no focused widget, `settings` is compared with `edit_base`; a difference pushes `edit_base` as one step. One drag or typed value is one step. The full `PartialEq` only runs on such frames. The check latches: a value still in a focused field is picked up by the first settled frame.
- **Free camera:** yaw, pitch, distance and target never make a step and stay where they are on undo and redo, so undoing a material does not swing the view. `UndoStep::camera` is the exception: `checkpoint_scene` (scene switch, load, generator, reset, AI, restore: Ctrl+Z returns the old scene as it was framed) and Camera inspector edits (`camera_edit`). `fov` is an ordinary edit. Look values restore: nothing display-only is carried over.
- **Merging:** an action's own `checkpoint` and the watcher's next look are the same state, so `push_undo` dedupes ignoring the camera. `finish_transform` keeps its own step: the watcher waits while a transform runs.
- **Not edits:** keys applied for the playhead or a load (`keys_applied`, main.rs; the clean base moves with them while the scene is clean, and an edit waiting in a field is flushed first). Keys applied because the keys themselves changed are that edit's result and stay a step and unsaved. While playback, a physics recording, a script or a walk runs (`foreign`) the base follows and user edits make no step; what is waiting in a field when one starts is flushed first. A running simulation is not foreign: the poses of the objects it moves are taken into both bases when they are compared (`sim_objects`, `follow_poses`), so panel edits stay undoable in a physics scene and its poses alone are not unsaved work. An edit to a moving object's pose makes no step.
- **Where it runs:** `edits_live`, set only in interactive runs. Headless runs, the self-test, `--frames` and `--script` runs have the old checkpoint-only undo and never write the session. Undo and redo keep the camera rule in every run.
- **`App::switch_scene`:** the scene picker, ◀ ▶ / Page keys on presets, Randomize, Today, gamepad triggers and the phone remote: checkpoint, new scene, Stage selected, toast. The script paths call `Settings::preset` directly.
- **Unsaved mark:** `scene_dirty`, against `clean_base` from the last load or save (`mark_clean`), ignoring the camera, `keep_output`'s viewer choices, view, compare and turntable. A * follows the name in the scene picker and the title bar. AI scenes stay unsaved on purpose. Undoing across a load compares with the loaded scene, so the old scene shows a * even if it had no edits. Playback and simulation don't change the mark.
- **last-session.json** (`prefs::write_session`, beside the preferences; temp file then rename): written at most once a minute while dirty and on `shut_down`; removed on a clean close or a save. The start offers it with a prompt for 30 s and Menu → Restore last session for the whole run, never a modal. Restoring pushes an undo step, restores `loaded_file` so Ctrl+S targets the same file, and stays marked unsaved. It restores everything, with the render settings, since those start at their defaults. An unanswered offer keeps its file through a clean close; new unsaved work overwrites it.
- **Export:** a close during an export only arms: the export window says a second close within 6 s cancels it and deletes the file.
- **Prefs** also keep the tab, gizmo tool, Local axes, pivot, snap target and panel width.
- **File names:** the default template is `{scene}-{date}-{time}`. `name_template_migrated` replaces a saved "caustica" once, so typing that name later sticks. Headless runs and the self-test keep `caustica` (`fixed_names`).
- **Tests:** unit tests in `ui.rs` (`one_drag_is_one_undo_step…`, `a_scene_step_brings_its_own_camera_back`, `closing_writes_unsaved_work…`) and `prefs.rs`. Input through egui itself was not driven: hidden runs ignore OS input.
- **Display transform and exports with no device (2026-11-09):** `reference/display.rs` is post.hlsl's beauty view on the CPU (halation, vignette, exposure, the eight tone maps, lift / gamma / gain and the trilinear .cube LUT, the output gamma, grain), so `--reference … --capture out.png|out.jpg` writes what the screen shows (EXR stays linear; layers, HDR PNG and JPEG XR are refused). Glare's FFT is not ported: a look with glare is refused, not written without it. Direct / indirect sliders are ignored, as in the GPU's software view. `video::render_cpu` runs turntable, timeline (motion blur as up to 32 moments with sample indices running on) and high-res still exports through the same writer: `--reference SPP --turntable out.mp4 [--turntable-format …] [--turntable-timeline]` on the CLI, and in the app's software view `CpuExport` (a thread, its own progress window, Live paused). HDR10 is refused (no HDR map on the CPU). The CLI turntable takes the scene's pitch and distance; the GPU's CLI turntable takes `Settings::default()`'s. Measured, preset 0 320 x 240, 256 spp, PNG GPU vs CPU channel means within 0.02/255 (median |d| 0) for the plain look and for AgX, ACES and PBR Neutral with vignette, halation, lift, gain and grade gamma; oracle preset 0 0.9999 (z -0.41) unchanged. Tests: `tone_maps_at_known_points`, `plain_display_is_clamped_linear`, `look_vignette_halation_and_grain`, `lut_identity_and_inverse`, `cpu_turntable_frames_match_the_still`, `image_formats_by_file_name`.

## Spot factor sign (BSDF hits)
A spot's (or IES) emission factor is signed against the emitter's own unflipped face normal everywhere: light sampling, light tracing and emission-hit shading alike (cone out of the front face; a two-sided spot seen from behind has factor 0). BSDF-hit emission and BDPT `vertex_emission` used the viewer-facing `h.n` / `x.n`, which showed the cone from the back; they now use `h.front ? h.ng : -h.ng` (`x.n` sign-flipped by `V_FRONT`). Matches the CPU reference's `emission_cos`. In practice every light-built spot is one-sided (`lighting.rs` sets `optical.w=1` for non-sphere lights, and spot/IES exist only on flat lights), so shipped scenes are unchanged; this only matters for a future two-sided spot emitter.

## Night sky data (2026-11-10)
The inputs of the night-sky redesign (`docs/` plan N1); the shaders and `src/astro.rs` read them in later steps. `tools/prep_sky_data.py` turns the raw downloads in `scenes/sky/raw/` (gitignored) into the shipped files in `scenes/sky/`:
- **`stars.bin`** (1.0 MB, 83,479 stars): HYG v4.2 (David Nash, CC BY-SA 4.0) stars with V ≤ 9.0, Sol excluded, sorted by V ascending. Little-endian: `"CSTR"`, u32 version (1), u32 count, then per star 12 bytes: `ra_rad` f32, `dec_rad` f32 (J2000), V f16, B−V f16. A missing B−V is 0.65. Counts per magnitude: V<−1 1, <0 3, <1 12, <2 34, <3 124, <4 346, <5 1095, <6 3404, <7 10439, <8 25690, <9 42331 (the f16 round-off of V is below 0.001 mag).
- **`milkyway.exr`** (5.7 MB): NASA SVS Deep Star Maps 2020 (svs.gsfc.nasa.gov/4851), 8192 × 4096 half RGB, area-downsampled to 2048 × 1024, half float, ZIP. The low 5 half mantissa bits are rounded off (0.6% mean error, invisible) so the map's sensor noise compresses: 9.2 MB became 5.7 MB. The mean value per solid angle is unchanged (0.006758). Pixel values are the SVS display-referred 0..1 range, not radiance: the bake picks the scale.
- **Orientation** (verified by locating Omega Cen, 47 Tuc, NGC 2808 and NGC 362 in the map to 0.03°): equirectangular in celestial coordinates; row y (0 at the top) has Dec = 90° − (y + 0.5)/H · 180°; column x (0 at the left) has RA = (0.5 − (x + 0.5)/W) · 360°, so RA 0 is the image centre and **RA increases to the left**. The galactic centre (RA 17h45m, Dec −29°) is at 75% of the width.
- **No stars to remove:** stacking the 8k map at the positions of 600 stars with V ≤ 6 gives a point-source excess of 0.0102 against 0.0084 at random positions (no signal; Sirius and Vega are not in the map), so the SVS Milky Way layer is star-free and no inpainting happened. Only the globular clusters and the Magellanic Clouds show as small bright spots (not HYG stars). The script re-checks this and would inpaint V ≤ 9 stars with a local median if a map held them.
- **Regenerate:** put `milkyway_2020_8k.exr` (SVS 4851, "Milky Way", 8k EXR) and `hygdata_v42.csv.gz` (astronexus.com/hyg) in `scenes/sky/raw/`, then `python3 tools/prep_sky_data.py` (numpy only; it reads the EXR with `tools/exr.py` and writes its own). The installer and zip take `scenes/sky` through `git ls-files scenes`, the AppImage through `cp -r scenes`; `raw/` is untracked, so no release carries it.
- Licences: THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md. `stars.bin` is CC BY-SA 4.0.

## Night sky: astronomy (N2)

`src/astro.rs` turns a date, time and place into the rotation from the celestial frame (J2000 RA/Dec, which the star catalogue `scenes/sky/stars.bin` uses) to the sky's local frame (east +x, up +y, north −z, the frame of `sun_local()`; the `north` turn is applied by the shader as for the sun). The chain: Julian date (Meeus), Greenwich mean sidereal time (IAU 1982), precession J2000 to date (IAU 1976), then hour angle and latitude. No nutation, aberration or refraction: errors are arc minutes. `PhysicalSky::local_from_celestial()` is the entry point; `PhysicalSky::julian_date()` takes the instant from `year`, `day`, `solar_time()` minus the longitude's hours and the equation of time (with `clock` that is `time − utc_offset`). `PhysicalSky::year` (default 2026, 1900..2100, serde default so old scenes load unchanged) is in `map_key`, and the Year slider sits under Date.
- `parse_stars` / `load_stars` read `stars.bin` ("CSTR", version 1, count, then RA f32, Dec f32, V f16, B−V f16 per star) and fail with a clear error on a missing file, bad magic, wrong version or a length mismatch.
- Superseded by N2b below: `sun_local()` is now the exact chain.
- Tests: Polaris within 1° of the latitude at every hour, Sirius transit altitude 90° − |lat − dec| (within 0.1°), JD and GMST against Meeus' worked examples, precession rates, round trip, frame handedness.


## Night sky: sun and moon on the exact astronomy (N2b, 2026-11-10)

- `sun_local()` and `moon_local()` rotate the sun's and the moon's J2000 vectors with `local_from_celestial()`, the matrix the stars use, so sun, moon and stars agree. The sun is Meeus ch. 25 (low accuracy, 0.01°; 0.006° off the 25.a example) of the date, taken back to J2000 by the inverse precession (`astro::sun_j2000`). The moon is Meeus ch. 47 cut to 35 longitude and 14 latitude terms plus the additive ones (`astro::moon_ecliptic`; 0.0004° and 0.003° off example 47.a, 0.006° in RA/Dec); geocentric, so no parallax (up to 0.95°, small against a 0.5° disc only at the horizon) and no nutation. The `north` turn is applied by `sun_direction()` exactly as before.
- `moon_age()` (same signature) is the ecliptic elongation of the moon from the sun over 360°, so the phase matches the position: full moon 2026-01-03 10:03 UT gives age 0.5000 and the sun-moon angle 175.7° (short of 180° by the moon's latitude), the first quarter 89.99°, the new moon 359.98°. The bake's lit fraction (1 − cos 2π·age)/2 is unchanged.
- `equation_of_time()` is now the sun's mean longitude minus its right ascension (`astro::equation_of_time`; +16.45 min on 3 Nov, −14.20 min on 11 Feb), evaluated at the mean-time instant (it moves under 30 s a day). Without `clock`, `time` is still apparent solar time: UT = time − longitude/15 − EoT, so the hour angle is exactly time − 12 (test `solar_time_puts_the_sun_on_the_meridian_at_noon`), and the declination now follows the real date and UT, so at a longitude far from 0 the sun differs from before by up to 0.2° of declination. `map_key` now carries the Julian date and the longitude (the sun depends on both).
- `SCENE_CD_M2` (32,000 cd/m² for radiance 1.0) is in `sky.rs` for the night-sky passes; unused until N3.
- Old scenes: the sun moves by 0.00 to 0.69° over the 10 built-ins with a physical sky (sunlit-room-day-cycle 0.69, vermeer-room 0.59, wrecking-ball 0.48, zen-garden 0.22, tilt-shift-village 0.10, desert-monolith 0.08, little-planet 0.03, festival-banners, sky-garden-dusk and underwater 0.00: day 172 sits on Cooper's solstice). Headless 480x270, 24 frames, no RR, before vs after: desert-monolith mean 8-bit diff 0.30 (max 16), sky-garden-dusk 0.03 (max 12). The moon is a different thing: it used to trail the sun by `age` hours of hour angle at the sun's declination, now it is where it is, so night scenes with the moon shift their moonlight direction by tens of degrees.

## Night sky: Milky Way, airglow, light pollution (N4, 2026-11-10)

`sky::bake()` adds three night terms above the horizon (`night_glow`) before the ground is lit, so they light the ground too. They replace the old fixed night floor (2e-7..6e-7, about 10x a real dark sky); only a 1e-12 floor stays so the sampling tables never see a black map. All three scale with `intensity` like the rest of the map. Units: `mag_arcsec2_to_radiance(μ) = 1.08e5 · 10^(−0.4 μ) / SCENE_CD_M2`. New `PhysicalSky` fields (serde default 1, `validate` 0..10, in `map_key`, Stage tab rows under Stars): `milky_way`, `airglow`, `light_pollution`; they reach `transport_key` through `physical_sky`.
- **Milky Way:** `scenes/sky/milkyway.exr` is loaded once (`image`, as `environment::load`; `OnceLock`; a missing file logs and leaves the band out) and converted to radiance at load; each bake texel's direction goes to J2000 through `local_from_celestial()ᵀ`, sampled bilinearly. Calibration: the map is display-referred with a contrast stretch. Map luminance: 2° box on the large Sagittarius star cloud (RA 270.5°, Dec −28.5°) 0.198, Cygnus (300°, +35°) 0.025, north galactic pole 0.00098, median per solid angle 0.0032. One linear scale would put the pole 5.8 mag below the Sgr cloud (the real sky: about 3), so radiance ∝ v^0.5 on luminance (chromaticity kept), with the Sgr cloud at 20.2 mag/arcsec² V. Measured in the test: Sgr 20.15, Cygnus 21.61, galactic pole 23.01; map median 22.4. Constants `MW_REF_VALUE`, `MW_REF_MAG`, `MW_POWER`.
- **Airglow:** 22.0 mag/arcsec² V at the zenith times the van Rhijn factor 1/√(1 − (R/(R+90 km))² sin² z) (6x at the horizon), times the transmittance along the view, so it peaks some degrees up and fades at the horizon. Tint (0.92, 1.03, 0.92), luminance 1 (mild OI 557.7 green; looks come later).
- **Light pollution:** scattered inside the air, so it follows 1 − T(view) (luminance) normalised to the zenith: it rises about 5x to the horizon by itself. 22.6 mag/arcsec² at the zenith at strength 1, which with the airglow makes the dark rural 21.5 (test: 21.59). Warm tint (1.25, 0.98, 0.68).
- The Milky Way and airglow are multiplied by `Atmosphere::transmittance` (the table `march_transmittance` fills) toward the view.
- Day: the noon zenith sky is over 1e6 times the night terms (test); day scenes do not change. The old random baked stars were removed in N3 (catalogue stars as point lights).
- Cost: `night_glow` takes 2.7 ms for 512x128 texels in a debug test (the whole debug-size bake 850 ms), plus a one-time EXR load.
- CPU reference: `reference/environment.rs` takes the map from `bake_cached`, so it sees the same terms. `tools/oracle.py` on a moonless night scene (160x90, 256/64 spp, intensity 1000): GPU/CPU 1.0002, z 0.01, ok.
- Placement (test `milky_way_is_calibrated_and_where_the_galactic_centre_is`): latitude −30°, day 200, 23 h solar time: the galactic centre (J2000 266.40°, −28.94°) from `astro` is at alt 74.7°, az 269.6°; the bake's brightest band texel is at alt 77.7°, az 271.1° (3° off, in the Sgr cloud).
- **fp16:** at intensity 1 the night sky is about 1e-8 scene units, below half-float range (the raw EXR capture reads 0). Test renders used `intensity = 1000`, scaled in Python; it needs N6b pre-exposure to show at intensity 1 (exposure stops at 10). (E5 pre-exposure landed 2026-11-10 for RR and the upscalers only; the EXR capture is not pre-exposed.) **Fixed in E8 for the sky map itself:** `environment::half_scale` stores a night map scaled up (engine-notes "Built-in scenes and auto exposure"); the oracle now agrees at intensity 1.

## Night sky: stars as point lights (N3, 2026-11-10)

The catalogue's stars (`scenes/sky/stars.bin`, 83,479 to V 9, `astro::load_stars`) are point lights, not map texels; the old random texel stars are gone from `sky::bake`. Owner: starlight lights surfaces too. Code: `src/stars.rs` (table, grid, per-frame `Frame`), `pathtrace.hlsl` (`star_*`, `sample_light`, the miss branch), CPU twin `reference/environment.rs` (`Env::stars`, `keep`, `stars_seen`) and `reference/lights.rs` (`sample_sky`, `nee_density`).
- **Frames.** The table stays in J2000. Each frame `stars::Frame` rotates it by `Mat3::from_rotation_y(−north) · PhysicalSky::local_from_celestial()` (world from celestial); its rows go in `Constants` `star_x/y/z` (appended with `star_tau`; `cbuffer Frame` in step) with, in w, the irradiance scale π·intensity·`stars`, the stars' share of sky light samples and the camera cone's chord; `star_tau` holds the zenith optical depth (−ln of `march_transmittance` straight up) and w = 1 while stars are on.
- **Radiometry.** Irradiance per star relative to the sun's: 10^(−0.4 (V + 26.74)); colour a blackbody at Ballesteros's (2012) temperature for B−V, divided per channel by the sun's (B−V 0.65, so the sun's colour is white, as the exo-atmospheric sun light is) and set to luminance 1. Times π·intensity·`stars` (the sun light of intensity I gives irradiance π I). Dimmed by the air: exp(−τ X(y)) with Kasten and Young's (1989) air mass, 0 at and below the horizon (horizon cull). Sirius (V −1.46): 7.6e-11 of the sun.
- **Light sampling (NEE).** A separate power table (luminance CDF, f64 sums, last entry 1) picked by binary search. The sky's NEE pick is subdivided: the number u < p_env that chose the sky is reused as u/p_env, and below the share it picks a star by u/(p_env·share): no new Sobol dimension. With no stars (any non-sky scene, an HDRI, `stars` 0, a day sky) the share is 0, `env_keep()` is exactly 1 and the random numbers drawn are the same, so Sobol stays bit-identical. The map keeps 1 − share of the sky samples: its pdf in light sampling and in the BSDF hit's MIS weight is p_env·(1 − share)·env_pdf. A star sample is a delta direction: it returns a negative pdf, which `nee_density` (both renderers) reads as "no MIS, divide by the pick probability p_env·share·p_i". Below the horizon it returns no sample (half the picks: the table is by catalogue power, not culled per frame).
- **Share** (`stars::share`, at bake; `EnvMap::stars` / `star_share`): star irradiance above the horizon (luminance, through the air) over that plus the map's own (1/inv_norm), clamped to 0.05..0.95; 0 when under 1e-6 (a day sky: stars then take no samples, a bias of under 1e-6 of the sky light). Defaults, changeable: `SHARE_RANGE`, `SHARE_FLOOR`.
- **Camera rays and pure-delta chains** (the path has only delta bounces since the camera: glass, water, false hits, portals; `camera_chain`) test a cone on a miss: the stars within chord c of the direction, their irradiance over the cone's solid angle π c². c = tan(fov/2) / output height (half a pixel; clamped to 2e-6..0.9 of the grid margin), so a star is a point at any resolution and zoom; the size is now a setting (N5, below). A hidden sky (`visible` off) hides them from the camera. Non-delta vertices never see stars on a BSDF ray, so the cone and NEE cover disjoint paths: no MIS weight, no double count.
- **Grid** (built once at load with the table): 180 × 360 cells in declination and RA; each star is listed in every cell its 0.3° cone touches (rows by declination ± 0.3°, columns by asin(sin m / cos δ) + 1%, all columns near a pole), so one cell holds every star a cone up to 0.9 · 0.3° can find.
- **GPU data.** t18 `star_data` (u32 words, `Table::gpu_words`): header (count, rows, columns, offsets word, entries word), 8 words per star (direction, CDF, RGB, probability), offsets, entries; uploaded once with the first starry sky (`set_environment`, both backends). D3D12: root parameter 18 (root SRV; the lens buffer stands in until then). Vulkan: set 0 binding 118 (`SET0_BUFFERS`; the placeholder until then). docs/claude/data-layouts.md.
- **Other integrators.** `star_nee` is set only by `trace` (the path tracer, every variant). ReSTIR DI's sky branch goes through `sample_light`, so it gets stars. Equiangular fog sampling skips the sky as before (a star sample is not anchored, so its MIS weight against equiangular is 1). The shadow catcher's four light samples include stars (|pl|). BDPT and photon mapping leave stars out entirely (no NEE, no cone; their sky pdfs unchanged), on both renderers: a documented gap.
- **BSDF-only MIS (`mis_mode` 2):** stars are delta lights and a BSDF ray cannot hit one, so with light sampling off only camera rays and pure-delta chains see them (the cone); diffuse surfaces get no starlight, as with any point light in that mode. Both renderers agree (the oracle cannot catch it). Changeable: a stars-only NEE in this mode would need its own pick.
- **Not delta here:** metal at roughness 0 is GGX at α 0.001, so stars in a metal mirror come from NEE alone and converge slowly (roadmap).
- **Checks.** Unit tests (`stars.rs`): catalogue and CDF (Sirius' irradiance and probability), picks, grid against brute force (400 probes, three chords, poles), the cone integrates to the star's irradiance, colours, share at night and none by day. `tools/oracle.py` (320 × 240, 1024 GPU / 512 CPU spp): `starlight-field` GPU/CPU 1.0000 (z −0.06); stars off 1.0000 (z 0.07); the stars' own increment on sky and floor agrees to 0.01%, and the brightest star pixel is 2.4100e-4 vs 2.4099e-4. Sirius in a water pane (scratch scene, camera aimed at its reflection, 160 × 120, 16384 / 8192 spp): 0.9998 (z −0.44), the reflection's energy 1.26e-3 vs 1.31e-3 (CPU noise about 5%). Self-test frame 127 asserts the stars are loaded.
- **Scene:** `scenes/builtin/starlight-field.json`: a white floor, no lights, moonless 45° N night at the default sun intensity, auto exposure (E8, "Built-in scenes and auto exposure"; it was intensity 1000 and exposure 10 until then).

## Night sky: tweaks (N5, 2026-11-10)

All the night sky's controls sit in one "Night sky" group of Stage → Physical sky (all tooltipped): Star brightness, Limiting magnitude, Star size, Milky Way, Airglow, Light pollution, Moon. They are `PhysicalSky` fields, so they are scene fields (serde default), validated in `PhysicalSky::validate`, in the bake key (`map_key`) where the baked map depends on them, and in `transport_key` (the whole `physical_sky` object is). Planets: not now (roadmap).
- **Star brightness** is the existing `stars` (0..1000, default 1): the irradiance scale π · intensity · `stars`. Only the label changed.
- **Limiting magnitude** (`limiting_magnitude`, default 6.5, the naked eye in a dark sky, about 9,000 stars; range −2..16; the camera preset later uses 9, the catalogue's end). The table is now sorted brightest first (`Table::new`), so the limit keeps a prefix: `Table::kept(limit)` is its length and `Table::mass(count)` its CDF mass. Light sampling picks among the prefix (`u · mass` into the same CDF, probability `prob / mass`: `Table::pick(u, count)`, `star_pick`), the cone test ignores stars with an index at or past the count (`Table::within`, `star_seen`), and the share (`stars::share`) sums the prefix only. `Frame` carries `count` and `mass`; the shader reads them from a new `Constants` row `star_limit` (x count as a float, y mass; `cbuffer Frame` in step), the table in t18 is unchanged. No star kept (limit below −1.4) turns stars off entirely (`map.stars` false at the bake). The limit is in `map_key` (it changes the share). Dropping the fainter stars drops their light: the 8,920 stars to 6.5 hold 63.6% of the catalogue's total starlight (measured from `stars.bin`), so the default night is 36% less star light than N3's all-stars one (the Milky Way map is star-free, so this light is simply absent; raise the limit to 9 for the camera look).
- **Star size** (`star_size`, default 1, 0.5..32 pixels across). Done on the render side rather than the display pass: the camera cone's radius is `size · tan(fov/2) / height` (`stars::chord`, 1 = the N3 half-pixel radius), and the cone divides the star's irradiance by its solid angle, so a larger star is a dimmer, wider disc of the same total energy: a point spread with no extra pass, on both backends and the CPU twin (`reference/scene.rs`). It is therefore in the transport key (it changes the accumulated pixels; excluding it would blend two sizes in one accumulation), unlike the spec's display-only suggestion. It does not change lighting: NEE treats stars as points. Clamped to 2e-6..0.9 of the grid margin (about 0.27°), which at 4k and a 60° view is about 20 px.
- **Defaults (changeable):** limiting magnitude 6.5, star size 1 px, brightness 1; the ranges above.
- **Checks.** Unit tests: `a_limiting_magnitude_keeps_a_bright_prefix` (sorted, 7,000-13,000 kept at 6.5 (8,920), picks inside the prefix, probabilities sum to 1 over it, the cone sees only the prefix), `star_size_scales_the_cone`; the existing star tests pass with the new signatures.

## Walk avatar (2026-11-10)

The owner's "proper fps view, I wanna see my legs and my reflection": walk mode draws a blocky body under the camera (our own design: cube head, box torso, box arms with hands, two-segment legs; ten boxes).

- **Where it lives.** `src/avatar.rs`. `avatar::add(&mut frame, &stance)` appends the boxes to each frame's copy of the settings (`App::redraw`, after `add_flashlight` and the laser's rods), like the flashlight and the beam. Nothing is in the scene file, undo, auto-key, picking, the outliner or physics (`Body::None`, `locked`), the indices of the scene's own objects are untouched, and exports (`self.settings`) never draw it. `Walk::stance(&settings)` hands over the walker's state; `avatar::standing(&settings)` is the same pose for a walker at rest at the camera (the CPU reference and `--walk`).
- **Two general object flags.** `Object.camera_hidden` (inspector: "Visible to camera", inverted) sets bit 1 of `PackedMaterial.lobes.w` in `lighting::materials`, the bit a light's "Visible to camera" already uses; `Object.casts_shadows` (inspector "Casts shadows", default true) sets bit 13 (8192), the light's "no shadows" bit. Both are scene fields with serde defaults (skipped when default, so old files load and nothing new is written). `render/plan.rs::instance_mask` turns them into `MASK_HIDDEN` / `MASK_NO_SHADOW` (an object under both rules keeps mask 0xff and the shader's per-candidate tests, `pathtrace.hlsl::intersect`); `main.rs::geometry_key` carries both, since they pick the mask (toggling one rebuilds the instances). The CPU reference's `Scene::accept` already tests both bits for any material. Reflections and refractions still see such an object. The avatar's head is the only user: hidden from camera rays and no shadow.
- **Why the head casts no shadow.** The camera sits inside the head, which rests on the torso: a head that shades is a head that blacks out the chest the camera looks down on (found in the first render: a black band across the bottom of the view). Cost: no head shadow on the floor; the torso, arms and legs shade normally.
- **Pose = pure function of the walker.** `Stance { feet, eye_height, yaw, pitch, phase, gait, scale, crouch }`. `phase` is the head bob's stride phase (π per step, advanced by the distance walked on the ground, never the clock), `gait` eases to 1 while striding on the ground and to 0 standing, airborne and flying (`Walk::update`; independent of the B head-bob option). A walker at rest has gait 0, so the same boxes every frame and the accumulation is not restarted (`a_standing_body_ignores_the_stride_phase`). Body units are a 28th of the eye height (Steve-like 8-wide head, 8 × 12 × 4 torso, 12-long legs and arms), scaled with the body scale (mouse wheel).
- **Motion.** Feet follow a path (reach ±4 units, 2.5 lift on the forward swing) through two-bone IK of 2 × 6 units, so knees bend by themselves; a crouch lowers the hips to 6 units, bends the knees and leans the torso (the eye stays where the camera is). Arms swing against the legs of their side (±0.6 rad) plus a share of the lean. The body yaws with the view, the head nods with the pitch (±70°; hidden, so only reflections show it); strafing and walking backwards just slide the same stride. At the stride's extreme the straight 12-unit legs fall about 0.6 units (1.4 cm) short of the floor: kept, the alternative being a bent standing leg.
- **Layout against the camera.** The torso hangs 2.5 units behind the eye (the head stays on it) and the legs 2 units in front of the torso's middle, so that looking down past ~55° shows the legs and the feet beyond the chest edge (first try, legs under the chest: a teal slab, no legs). At pitch 0 no corner of a camera-visible box is inside a 45° cone round the view (a 72° view is 46° wide at its edge), for every stride phase and crouch (`nothing_visible_is_in_front_of_the_eye`); at 75° down legs and hands are in the 40° cone (`looking_down_sees_legs_and_hands`). Hands and arms show from ~65° down, at the edges.
- **Flashlight.** Its light hangs 0.3 beside and 0.4 below the eye in the camera frame (`Flashlight::right/down`): horizontally outside the body (arms reach 8 units = 0.18) at any pitch. The head casts no shadow anyway; a config that moved the light into the torso, an arm or a leg would end every shadow ray in that part, so `avatar::objects` leaves out any part that holds the light (margin 0.02) and the beam survives (`the_flashlight_is_never_inside_the_body_it_lights_from`).
- **Defaults (changeable).** `walk_avatar` (Settings; Scene → Camera → "Walk avatar") on by default; shown while walking and flying; purely visual; plain diffuse materials (teal shirt and sleeves, dark trousers, skin-tone head and hands; roughness 0.8). A viewer choice: `keep_output` and `preset()` carry it. `--set walk_avatar=false` for the A/B.
- **Debug switch.** `--walk` starts in walk mode at the given camera (headless included); with `--reference` the CPU draws `avatar::standing`. `tools/oracle.py --walk` passes it to both sides. The walker stands on the floor (Cornell floor y = −1), so a camera 0.62 above it (y = −0.38) does not move. Headless runs have no keys: standing pose only; the CPU `--reference --walk` stands at the camera (`avatar::standing`) while a live walker's feet are on the floor under the eye, so the two bodies differ when the camera is not at the walker's eye height or has been moved off its feet (documented, not corrected: the oracle puts the camera 0.62 above the floor); the striding pose is covered by unit tests.
- **Cost (Vulkan, RTX 4070 Ti SUPER, release, mirror-room 800 × 600, `--walk --orbit-test`, 400 frames, avatar on vs off).** The camera moves each frame, so the pose changes and `rebuild_scene` runs each frame, as in a physics scene. First measurement: 15.2 ms per frame, of which `update_materials` 12.4 ms: four buffers allocated and freed per update (`d.upload`). `Gpu::update_materials` (Vulkan) now compares the four tables with the last uploaded and leaves identical ones alone (a transform-only change: physics, animation, the avatar): `rebuild_scene` 3.8 ms mean (TLAS refit 2.4 ms with the 64-refit full build as the maximum, `device_wait_idle` 0.6), a saving that physics and animation share. The ten boxes add 0.8 ms of trace time (12.85 → 13.63 ms, +6%, in a scene where every reflection can hit them). The BLAS of the boxes is the shared `shape:Box` one and the TLAS refits (instance masks and flags stay put, `tails` equal), so only the toggle or a flashlight part dropping out costs a full TLAS build. D3D12 has the same structure (`gpu/scene.rs`: TLAS refit already, `update_materials` uploads four buffers per call) but could not be measured here (Linux): the same skip would go in `Gpu::update_materials` there if a Windows measurement shows the uploads matter.
- **Checks.** 13 unit tests in `avatar.rs` (pose determinism, standing ignores the phase, only the head hidden, legs within the body width, feet on the floor, nothing in front of the eye, legs and hands in view looking down, crouch, box → object corners, validation at body scales 0.2–5, mask bits through `lighting::materials` and `instance_mask`, serde defaults, the flashlight); self-test frame 249 asserts ten boxes and one hidden head in the walk frame; self-test 482 frames pass. Oracle, mirror-room 320 × 240, 1024 GPU spp against 512 CPU spp, camera 0.62 above the floor facing the mirror (the whole avatar in the reflection, head included): GPU/CPU 1.0010 (z 0.82, worst block z 2.85); looking down 70° (chest and legs seen directly): 1.0013 (z 1.46, worst block 2.85). The head being hidden shows in both: a camera ray that hit it would fill the frame with skin colour.

- **Review fixes (2026-11-10).** (1) Picking: the probe's ids count the avatar's boxes, so `main.rs` builds the pick list with the same `avatar::add` as the frame (boxes at index >= the scene's own count are never selectable); test `probe_ids_map_the_same_with_the_avatar_in_the_list`. (2) Bit 13 is now tested for procedural/SDF candidates too (`intersect` shadow rays and `occluded`); before, "Casts shadows" off on an SDF object still shadowed on the GPU. (3) `transport_key` leaves the avatar's boxes (name prefix `avatar: `, `avatar::is_part`) out of the objects hash and carries their pose as an `avatar` field the caller strips like the camera fields: a pose change is `reset(false)` (the history survives), not `reset(true)`; the TLAS update comes from `geometry_key`/`material_key`, which still see the boxes. There are no per-instance motion vectors (`write_guides` reprojects the hit point with `previous_vp`, a static-world camera reprojection), so a swinging leg is reprojected as if static; RR handles it as disocclusion-like noise on the legs only. (4) `avatar::add` logs once when the body is dropped for lack of object slots.

- **Merge review (2026-11-10).** BDPT light tracing (t = 1) now skips a surface vertex hidden from the camera (`lobes.w` bit 2, `Object.camera_hidden`), on the GPU (`integrators.hlsl`) and the CPU twin (`bdpt.rs`). Not changed: the GPU `vertex_emission` spot cosine uses the shading normal (`BVertex` has no `ng`), the CPU the face normal; they differ only for a smooth-shaded spot emitter at grazing angles, and every light-built spot is flat.

## Denoiser enum (2026-11-10)

`Settings.denoiser: Denoiser {Off, Native, Rr}` (scene.rs) replaces the bools `rr` and `denoise`. Default `Rr`, as `rr: true, denoise: true` was.
- **Meaning.** `Rr` asks for DLSS Ray Reconstruction; `Native` is the SVGF-style filter (post.hlsl); `Off` shows raw samples. `Settings::rr()` is "RR is the choice" (the renderer's `rr_active` says whether it runs); `Settings::native_filter()` is "the filter runs when RR does not" (`Native`, and `Rr`'s fallback). Whether RR runs is still `rr_active` (frame.rs `rr_active`, Vulkan `decide_rr`).
- **Default taken, changeable:** RR no longer has a separate raw fallback. The old `rr: true, denoise: false` fell back to raw samples when RR could not run (a real lens, a failed start, a GPU without NGX); now `Rr` falls back to the native filter. If a "RR else raw" mode matters, add a variant instead of a bool.
- **Back-compat.** Files from before this change carry `rr` and `denoise`. `Settings` reads them into private `legacy_rr` / `legacy_denoise` (never written) and `validate()` folds them (`migrate_legacy`, `Denoiser::from_legacy`): RR wins, else `denoise` picks Native or Off; a missing key keeps its old default (true). Every load path calls `validate`, and scene files nest `settings`, which is why the fold sits there and not in a pre-pass on the JSON. Tests: `old_rr_and_denoise_keys_load_as_the_same_choice`, `a_scene_file_with_the_old_keys_migrates_inside_its_wrapper`, `the_old_keys_are_never_written_and_the_choice_round_trips`.
- **Flags and keys.** `--rr` picks Rr; `--no-rr` takes Rr to Native and leaves Native and Off; `--raw` picks Off; the last given wins (`--no-rr --raw` and `--raw --no-rr` both give raw samples, which every oracle and reference tool uses). `--set rr=…` / `--set denoise=…` and the script `set` take the same bools (`Denoiser::set_legacy`). The phone remote's key is now `denoiser`; the D key cycles Rr, Native, Off.
- **`transport_key`.** `denoiser` is replaced by a derived `rr` bool: Native and Off share the accumulation and do not restart it (as `denoise` did), Rr and non-Rr do, and `reconfigure_upscalers` still fires on the `rr` change.
- **Capabilities.** `Capabilities::denoiser(d) -> Option<&Backend>` (`None` = always available) beside `upscaler(u)`; the Render tab's Denoiser combo greys unavailable entries with the reason, the Quality bundles use `rr` as before. A future OIDN or NRD adds a variant, `ALL`, `label`, a `Capabilities` field and a pipeline arm in frame.rs and the Vulkan `render`.
- **UI.** Render tab → Reconstruction starts with a Denoiser combo (battle mode greys it: raw samples); the RR status line and model row show for Rr; Denoise strength shows for Native and Rr (greyed while RR runs, `denoiser_idle`). Status bar text and the self-test (`selftest.rs` sets `denoiser`) follow.

## UI batch B: Stage blocks, greyed rows, material groups (2026-11-10)

From `docs/ui-review.md`. The Stage inspector is three `section` + `grid` blocks (Room, Sky and light, Fog) whose grids share `ui::LABEL_WIDTH` as `min_col_width`; the material grids use it too. A control that something else overrides stays visible and greyed through `ui::row_if(label, tip, idle, add)`: the stored value is kept and the row tooltip starts with the reason. The Stage rules live in `stage::overrides` (pure, unit-tested): a measured room or a wall texture grey the wall colours, the physical sky or a loaded HDRI greys Sky light, a sky the camera sees greys Background, and an HDRI that failed to load overrides nothing. The advanced material rows are collapsible groups (`editor::group`: Surface, Coatings, Brushed and grating, Volume, Pattern, Other) with the same grid width; a group starts open when something in it is in use (`default_open`, so its state is kept per header afterwards, not re-derived when another object is selected). Changeable defaults: label width 128 px; the Colour row greys when a Measured spectrum is set; a sphere light has no Rotation row; Hide panel and the first-run strings are as batch A left them. Not checked by eye at 1280 or 1920 wide.

## UI batch C: Look bundles, Compare, save paths, reconstruction rows (2026-11-10)
