From `docs/ui-review.md` (details and the defaults taken are in its "ui-batch-C" update). `look_tab::LOOKS` is a table of five bundles (Neutral, Warm film, Cool, Punchy, Soft); applying one writes `tonemap`, `halation`, `grain`, `grain_size`, `vignette`, `lift`, `grade_gamma` and `gain` only, and `Look::of` derives the selection from the current values (never stored, so no `Settings` field, `validate` or `transport_key` change). Edit the table to change a look; the unit test checks every bundle validates, is recognised, and leaves other fields alone. Expert Look rows (grain size, glare size and threshold, LUT, swapchain, peak and paper white) hide until Show advanced or until they hold a non-default value. The Export tab no longer has a "Scene file" section (the Menu keeps the settings entries, now with tooltips). In the Render tab the Reconstruction rows are ordered Denoiser, RR status and model, native denoiser rows, Quality mode, Render scale, Upscaler, DLSS preset.

## Command palette and keys (ui-batch-D, 2026-11-10)

- **Palette:** Ctrl+K, `src/palette.rs` (`ENTRIES`, `matches`, `State`) and `App::palette_window` / `run_palette` in `ui/windows.rs`. Entries are `Act::Go(tab, focus, section)` for settings, `Act::Window(Win)` and `Act::Do(Do)`. A new Menu window or hard-to-find setting wants a row there; `palette::tests` checks labels are unique and the O / Alt+D keys it shows. It opens the tab and selects `Focus` (Stage, Camera, MainLight, Flashlight, the selected or first Object / AreaLight) but does not scroll to the row. The Ctrl+K key arm is in the viewport key match (not `global_key`: Ctrl crouches while walking).
- **Review fixes (2026-11-10):** the Ctrl+K press that opens the palette is still in egui's input on the first frame, so `palette_window` ignores its own Ctrl+K toggle then (`opened`). Finished mesh imports wait while a video or timeline export runs (`land_mesh_imports` is not called), and loading or opening another scene drops imports still parsing. Both are defaults, changeable.
- **Keys:** auto orbit is O and the denoiser cycle is Alt+D (`App::toggle_orbit`, `cycle_denoiser`); bare A and D only toast the new key. A viewport click keeps the Look and Render tabs (`pick_keeps_tab`).

## Headless runs skip acquire/present (2026-11-10)

- `GpuState::headless` (set from `App::hidden`): Vulkan `present()` does not acquire or present; the blit, the GUI pass and the `keep_ui_frame` copy go to `Gpu::offscreen`, an image in the swap chain's format and size (COLOR_ATTACHMENT, TRANSFER_SRC, TRANSFER_DST; remade when either changes, freed after the previous frame's fence wait) and it is left in its last layout instead of PRESENT_SRC. Captures read the output textures or `ui_frame`, so they are the same bytes as through the swap chain. Why: on Wayland the compositor throttles presents of a hidden window to the monitor rate, flooring `frame_ms_mean` at 6.94 ms (144 Hz) even with `--no-vsync`; the lost time was in acquire/present, not the fence.
- D3D12 (`src/gpu/frame.rs`): `Present` is skipped when `headless`; the backbuffer never leaves PRESENT, so each frame draws into the same buffer. `tools/check_windows.sh` type-checks it on Linux; running it still needs Windows (`--headless` capture identical, `--capture-ui`, self-test with no debug-layer messages). No offscreen target there, so no frame-time gain is measured.
- Changeable default: headless is the hidden-window switch; windowed runs and the progress screen are unchanged.

## OIDN for stills (O2, 2026-11-10)

Intel Open Image Denoise 2.5.1 (CPU device, "RT" filter, quality high) cleans finished stills. `src/oidn.rs`; the libraries come from `vendor/oidn` (docs/claude/setup.md; licences in THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md).
- **Loading.** `libloading` at run time (already in the tree through `ash`, now a direct dependency): a missing library only turns the feature off, with the reason in the log (`OIDN unavailable: ...`) and greyed in the Render tab (`oidn::status()`). The libraries load by path, in the order oneTBB, core, CPU device, front, from the exe's folder (then one folder up for test binaries, then `vendor/oidn`): the Linux front library's RPATH is `$ORIGIN/../lib` and the core looks for the device by name, so each is opened by path first and the loader then matches by soname. `build.rs` (`oidn_runtime`) copies them beside the exe like the DLSS runtimes: Windows the four DLLs (`OpenImageDenoise.dll`, `_core.dll`, `_device_cpu.dll`, `tbb12.dll`, no tbbbind), Linux the four versioned `.so` files (no symlinks). The installer and zip take every `.dll` next to the exe, so they carry them with no change; `tools/appimage.sh` copies `libOpenImageDenoise*.so.*` and `libtbb.so.*` and the three licence texts.
- **Setting.** `Settings.still_denoise: bool` (default off, so no default image changes), Render tab → Reconstruction → *Still denoiser (OIDN)*, `--oidn`, `--set still_denoise=true`. Display-only: it is in `transport_key`'s exclusions, `keep_output` and `preset()` keep it (a viewer choice). No validate range (a bool). It is a separate option, not a `Denoiser` variant: the enum chooses what cleans the live image each frame (RR, native, none), and OIDN is a post-process on a finished one, which composes with any of them (the natural pairing is `--raw`).
- **Where it applies.** PNG and JPEG only; EXR (and layers, HDR PNG, JXR) stay the raw samples (default, changeable: an EXR export could take it as an option).
  - `--capture x.png|jpg`, script `capture` (`video::denoise_capture`): the backend wrote the file, so it is read back, filtered and written again (a JPEG is encoded again at `JPEG_QUALITY`). Backend independent: no D3D12 code changed.
  - Save image (`video::save_still`): the 8-bit readback is filtered before it is encoded.
  - The CPU renderer: `--reference N x.png` (`reference::run`) filters the linear mean in HDR mode and then applies the display transform; the software view (`Live::set_oidn`) publishes the filtered image once the render has stopped (cap reached or paused), keeps the plain mean for EXR saves (`raw`), and `latest()` and script captures follow it.
  - Not done (open): the GPU's live converged view. It would need the filtered image uploaded and shown through the software view's reference-texture path, which touches both backends' display passes.
- **Colour and guides.** The GPU path filters the display-referred 8-bit image (`srgb=true`, `hdr=false`), so the result is exactly the tone-mapped picture without its noise (tone mapping, glare, grain and the LUT stay as they were); the CPU path filters linear HDR before its display transform. Guides: albedo and normal from the AOV layers (`Gpu::read_layers`, one extra readback of ten textures) on the GPU, from a pixel-centre ray (`reference/oidn.rs`) on the CPU. The albedo is the diffuse plus the specular albedo, clamped to 1, so metals (diffuse albedo 0) keep their colour. The guides come from one ray through the pixel centre, so they are noise free and `cleanAux` is set (default, changeable). A guide of another size (a render scale below 100%) is dropped and the colour alone is filtered. Input NaN and negatives are zeroed before the filter.
- **Cost.** One device per call (the device is cheap next to the filter), all cores.
- **Tests.** `oidn::tests` (a noisy synthetic image: RMSE halves at least, with and without guides, HDR and sRGB; bad size refused; guides from layers), skipped with a message when the library is absent.
- **Review fixes (2026-11-10).** The viewer remembers the settings and whether the last image was final (`shown = (want, last)`): a pause with OIDN on filters the image even though running frames were published with the same settings. The guide of the thin lens (aperture > 0, perspective or tilt-shift) goes through the pinhole, so the albedo and normal agree at defocused edges; a lens system's guide still takes one fixed-seed lens sample per pixel (changeable: a centre ray of the exit pupil).
- **Measured (2026-11-10, RTX 4070 Ti SUPER, Vulkan, 24 CPU threads).** RMSE x 1000 of the display-referred PNG (0..1 sRGB, what `--capture` writes) at 960x540 against a 4096 spp raw capture of the same scene; blurred 5x5 in brackets. Raw = `--raw`, Native = `--no-rr`, OIDN = `--raw --oidn`; N frames at 1 spp. Harness: `/home/kaan/caustica-tmp/oidn/measure.py` (not in the repo: it is a 60-line loop over the switches above).

| scene | 16 spp raw | native | OIDN | 64 spp raw | native | OIDN |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| cornell-bunny | 26.8 (6.1) | 10.9 (4.6) | 7.5 (3.2) | 15.2 (3.1) | 7.8 (3.1) | 5.4 (1.9) |
| glass-of-water | 75.2 (34.8) | 36.1 (25.7) | 27.7 (14.9) | 52.9 (20.5) | 26.4 (15.0) | 22.2 (9.4) |
| nighthawks | 68.9 (27.3) | 30.5 (27.0) | 24.9 (21.8) | 53.9 (14.7) | 18.4 (13.3) | 15.9 (11.7) |
| texture-duck-on-wood | 86.9 (32.5) | 13.8 (8.3) | 11.4 (6.7) | 37.8 (9.6) | 10.8 (5.5) | 9.0 (4.0) |

  - OIDN is the lowest in every cell, by 14 to 31% against Native on the plain error and more on the blurred one (the blotches the native filter leaves are low frequency). Read the PNGs: on glass-of-water at 16 spp OIDN is smooth where Native is blotchy, and loses the bubbles and the caustic's fine edge that 16 samples never held (the reference shows them); the stripes, the straw and the wood grain keep their edges (the albedo guide). The duck's wood texture stays sharp.
  - Denoise time (the filter alone, quality high): 960x540 107 to 114 ms, 1920x1080 396 to 408 ms (four runs, cornell-bunny and nighthawks), 480x270 about 80 ms; on 24 threads, so a 4 to 8 core machine takes several times longer. The extra readback of the layers for the guides is not in these.
  - CPU renderer, cornell-bunny 480x270, 16 spp against its own 1024 spp: raw 33.0, the CPU view's SVGF-style filter 16.7, OIDN 12.5.
  - Run and read: the software view (`--cpu --headless --samples 16 --oidn`: the converged image filtered, 80 to 100 ms at 480x270) and an EXR capture (no OIDN line, the file stays raw). A JPEG file is covered by the unit test (`denoise_file`); `--capture x.jpg` itself fails on Vulkan before OIDN runs (`Gpu::capture` saves Rgba8, which the JPEG encoder refuses), a pre-existing bug left alone here, so only Save image reaches the JPEG path.
  - Not run: `tools/oracle.py`, `tools/rr_stability.py` and `--self-test`. Nothing in the GPU's rendering, the shaders or the D3D12 code changed, and with the setting off (the default) no pixel path changes; the guides on the CPU come from a new function that nothing else calls.
- **Needs a Windows check** (it cannot be built here): `build.rs` copying the four DLLs into `target\...\` (and that the installer's `*.dll` glob picks them up), `OpenImageDenoise.dll` loading by path next to the exe with `tbb12.dll` preloaded, `video::save_still` / `denoise_capture` compiling against the `Renderer` wrapper (they use only `read_layers`, `width`, `height`, which both backends have), the Render tab row, and one `--capture x.png --oidn` on D3D12.

## egui 0.33 (step 1 of docs/egui-upgrade-plan.md, 2026-11-10)
- egui, egui-winit, epaint, emath, ecolor bumped 0.31.1 → 0.33.3 together (`cargo update -p egui -p egui-winit`).
- The font atlas is a colour image: the `ImageData::Font` arms are gone from the Vulkan (`vulkan/gui.rs`) and D3D12 (`gpu/frame.rs`, edited by reading) uploaders. Glyph edges no longer go through the old gamma coverage curve.
- Menus close on any click inside by default now. `ui::sticky_menu` keeps the old close-on-click-outside behaviour for the top-bar Menu (its Renderer, Text-to-scene model and About submenus inherit it: `ui.menu_button` inside a menu is a `SubMenuButton`, and `SubMenu` takes the parent's `MenuConfig` when it has none, egui menu.rs:445), LPE Presets and Levels; plain items still close them with `ui.close()` (was `close_menu`). Default choice, changeable per menu.
- `SelectableLabel` → `Button::selectable`; `screen_rect` → `content_rect`; `ColorImage` literals → `ColorImage::new`; the glyph self-test uses `fonts_mut`. `ui.disable()` now also dims (export dialog rows): accepted.
- Visible in SDR captures (1280/1920/2560, render/look/scene tabs): slider handles are rounded rectangles instead of circles, panel text is a little narrower and crisper, combo arrows point down, text weight about the same, top bar rows unchanged.
- Owner checks on Windows: D3D12 compile and UI, and text/translucent-panel brightness in scRGB HDR and HDR10/PQ (not testable headless). No capture or self-test frame opens the Menu submenus; click a slider in Menu → Renderer to confirm it stays open.

## egui 0.34 port

`Context::run_ui` with `Panel::*::show_inside` (see `docs/egui-upgrade-plan.md` step 2). `ui::central_rect(ctx)` replaces `Context::available_rect` for clamping popups and toasts: it is the root `Ui`'s free rect after the panels, stored each frame in egui temp data (default: `content_rect`). Changeable default: `cluster()` widths in `chrome.rs` stay estimates; measure them if the new Atoms API is adopted in the redesign.

## egui 0.37 port (step 3 of docs/egui-upgrade-plan.md, 2026-11-10)

egui, egui-winit and the epaint family 0.34.3 -> 0.37.0.
- **Features:** `egui = { features = ["default_fonts", "monochrome_emoji_fonts"] }`. `default_fonts` is no longer default (no fonts = every glyph a box); the emoji fonts moved behind `monochrome_emoji_fonts`, and without them the arrows, play, pause and record glyphs (U+25B6, 25C0, 23F8, 23FA) were missing. Same bundled fonts as before, so the licence list is unchanged.
- **Arrows:** the proportional family never had U+2192 (only Hack has it), so every "->" arrow in tooltips, hints and the Stage menu text was a box already; `ui::theme` now appends Hack to the Proportional fallback chain. Changeable.
- **Glyph test** (`tests::ui_strings_have_glyphs`): `fonts_mut(|f| f.has_glyph)` still exists and still checks real coverage. It now calls `ui::theme`, asserts that 'A' is covered and an unassigned code point is not (so it can fail), lists every missing character at once, and covers `browser.rs`, `console.rs` and all `ui/*.rs` (they were not in the list before; that is how the arrow boxes were found).
- **TexturesDelta:** `set` is now `HashMap<TextureId, SmallVec<[ImageDelta; 1]>>` (several deltas per id, in order). Both uploaders loop over the deltas of each id (`vulkan/gui.rs`, `gpu/frame.rs`). The delta also asserts on drop (debug only) unless empty: the frame loop moves it into `ui::Textures`, which clears it when the frame ends, and headless/test passes use `ui::discard(output)`.
- **Ids:** `Id::new` -> `Id::unique` (22 sites). A panel's id is now its salt under the parent Ui's scope id, so the side panel's resize-drag check uses `root.scope_id().with("side panel").with("__resize")`.
- **Smaller:** `Panel::show_inside` -> `show`, `CentralPanel::show_inside` -> `show`, `Context::style_mut` -> `global_style_mut`, `Slider::suffix` takes a String. `Modifiers` left `RawInput` already in the 0.36 move; the headless builders only set `Event::Key` / pointer modifiers, which remain.
- Checked: 366 tests, clippy -D warnings, 483-frame self-test, captures at 1280/1920/2560 (render, look, scene tabs; read: text and arrows drawn, layout and top-bar wraps as in 0.34).
- **Windows check (D3D12 not compiled here):** `gpu/frame.rs::update_gui_textures` is now a nested loop (per id, per delta; same body); confirm it builds, that the atlas growing recreates the texture, and the HDR/PQ text look.

## Specular bounce budget (B1, 2026-11-10)

`Settings::specular_bounces` (Render tab, "Specular bounces", 0-64) gives specular vertices their own budget, as games do. A vertex is specular when the path tracer takes a smooth dielectric reflection or transmission (`delta`, the vertex MIS already treats as specular), a beam-splitter mirror reflection, or a GGX lobe (base specular or coat) of a material with `near_delta`: roughness at most 0.1, the coat's too when it has one, isotropic. Volume scattering and every other lobe are diffuse/glossy.

- **Mechanism:** the loop bound is `sampling.z + spec_used` (`bounce_split.x` in `cbuffer Frame`, a new `uint4` before the exposure meter's `meter`/`meter_ctrl`); each specular vertex while `spec_used < specular_bounces` raises `spec_used` by one. So at most `bounces` non-specular vertices and at most `bounces + specular_bounces` vertices in all. Once the specular budget is spent, further specular vertices count against `bounces` (a soft fallback, not a hard stop). `bounce` itself still counts every vertex, so Russian roulette from the fourth vertex, the Sobol dimensions and every `bounce==0` test are unchanged. The CPU reference (`reference/integrators/path.rs`, `near_delta` there) does the same.
- **Default 0** is the legacy budget (every vertex counts against `bounces`), bit-identical, so default images and every oracle and self-test baseline stand. Owner's choice to revisit: a default of 8 would light glass-of-water and the window views at 3 bounces, at the cost of changing baselines.
- **Changeable choices:** the near-delta threshold (0.1 roughness), counting beam-splitter reflections as specular, the soft fallback after the budget, the 64 cap.
- **Not covered:** BDPT, photon mapping (and photon walks) and ReSTIR GI keep `bounces` alone. The bounce heat map's scale is `min(bounces + specular_bounces, 8)`; the per-pixel count (`bounces_used`) counts every vertex.
- **Measured 2026-11-10 (Vulkan, 480×270, raw, no RR):** RMSE against a 600-frame (2400 spp) render at the scene's own settings, 64 frames each at bounces 3: glass-of-water 0.1138 (49.6% of the reference mean; mean 0.177 vs 0.230) with specular 0, 0.0483 (21.0%; mean 0.219) with specular 8; nighthawks 0.0278 (115%; mean 0.018 vs 0.024) vs 0.0099 (40.8%; mean 0.023). Trace time at bounces 3: glass-of-water 0.46 → 1.02 ms, nighthawks 0.30 → 0.46 ms. Oracle (bounces 3, specular 8): glass-of-water GPU/CPU 1.0001 (z 0.24), nighthawks 1.0007 (z 0.47), both ok.

## Realtime preset (B2, 2026-11-10)

`ui::Bundle::Realtime` ("Realtime 67%", first entry of the top bar's Quality menu) is the one bundle that also writes the sampling: spp 1, bounces 3, `specular_bounces` 8 (B1), ReSTIR DI on, render scale 0.67, denoiser RR, or the native filter when RR cannot run (`Bundle::apply(.., rr_ok)`; the combo passes `Capabilities::rr.available`). `Bundle::of` recognises it by those fields (it shares its scale with "Good 67%", so it is tested first), accepting RR or Native. Realtime also forces `integrator` to Path (`specular_bounces` has no effect in BDPT or photon scenes) and, without RR, sets the upscaler like "Native denoiser 67%". Leaving Realtime for another bundle resets `specular_bounces` to 0 (its marker) so the menu names the bundle chosen; the other sampling fields are kept (changeable default). No new `Settings` field, no shader or `Constants` change; default bundles and defaults are unchanged. D3D12 is untouched (the bundle lives in `ui.rs`).

- **Measured 2026-11-10 (Vulkan, RTX 4070 Ti SUPER, release, 2560x1368 headless, 240 frames, `--no-vsync`, `gpu_ms_mean`)** against a 600-frame render at the scene's own settings (4 spp, bounces 10 or 24, RR), RMSE x1000 of compressed luminance l/(1+l) (`tools/` style, as `motion_error.py`), the same after a 5x5 blur, and mean luminance bias:

| scene | own gpu ms (trace + RR) | Realtime gpu ms (trace + RR) | own RMSE / blurred / bias | Realtime RMSE / blurred / bias |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| glass-of-water | 30.4 (23.7 + 6.4) | 11.4 (7.8 + 3.4) | 2.94 / 1.54 / +0.01% | 19.87 / 18.15 / -5.64% |
| nighthawks | 15.2 (8.5 + 6.3) | 6.8 (3.4 + 3.1) | 1.24 / 0.58 / -0.06% | 4.36 / 3.32 / -3.08% |
| colonnade-night | 16.4 (9.6 + 6.4) | 6.6 (3.3 + 3.0) | 1.22 / 0.49 / +0.06% | 3.30 / 1.63 / -0.86% |
| cornell-bunny | 12.2 (5.8 + 6.1) | 4.9 (1.7 + 2.9) | 1.00 / 0.57 / -0.04% | 6.32 / 6.03 / -3.37% |
| stylised-forest | 13.5 (6.8 + 6.4) | 5.7 (2.4 + 3.1) | 1.19 / 0.60 / +0.00% | 3.64 / 3.29 / -0.78% |

  Realtime costs 2.2-2.7x less GPU time (11.4 ms worst, glass-of-water; about 6 ms elsewhere), RR itself 3.0-3.4 ms against 6.1-6.4 at scale 1. The error is the 3-bounce truncation, not noise: the bias is negative everywhere (energy beyond bounce 3 is lost; -5.6% in the glass scene, where the diffuse bounce count matters most) and the blurred RMSE is close to the plain one. Side by side the images (own, Realtime, reference) are close; cornell-bunny and nighthawks are hard to tell apart, the glass is a little darker and smoother.
- **Moving camera (glass-of-water, 1280x720, `--orbit-test`, 124 frames, ends 14.75 degrees from the start; reference 600 frames still at the final yaw; RMSE x1000 / blurred / bias):** own settings still 4.64 / 2.44 / -0.02%, orbit 11.49 / 6.97 / -0.74%; Realtime still 20.43 / 17.50 / -5.71%, orbit 24.63 / 20.63 / -6.15%. Motion adds +4.2 to RMSE for Realtime and +6.9 for the scene's own settings, so RR does not ghost more at 1 spp and scale 0.67; the orbit frame shows a little more smearing of the ice cubes and bubbles inside the glass (middle panel of `glass-of-water.side.png` in the scratch run), which the numbers do not isolate from the bounce bias. One scene only: `tools/motion_error.py` itself is native-filter-only (800x600 Cornell, `--no-rr`), so the orbit was run through `--orbit-test` directly.
- **Oracle (raw, 320x240, 1024 vs 512 spp, `--set bounces=3 --set specular_bounces=8 --set restir=true`):** glass-of-water GPU/CPU 0.9998 (z -0.43), cornell-bunny 0.9994 (z -1.70), nighthawks 1.0027 (z 1.74), stylised-forest 1.0000 (z 1.45), all ok. The preset adds no rendering code, only settings the oracle already covers.
- **Changeable defaults:** every number above (spp 1, bounces 3, specular 8, scale 0.67), the bundle name, RR to Native fallback instead of hiding the entry, ReSTIR DI on. Raising bounces to 4-5 would cut the energy loss for a few tenths of a millisecond of trace time; not measured.

## Light sampling and BSDF sampling agree (accuracy audit B7, 2026-11-10)

Audit row B7 ("NEE-only is 0.2-0.6% darker than MIS, unexplained") came from a 2026-11-01 note in the ReSTIR section. Re-measured on this tree (Linux, Vulkan, 320 × 240, `--raw --no-rr`, the scenes' own filter and bounces, `mis_mode` 0 / 1 / 2 on the GPU, 262144 spp per mode unless noted; ratios of luminance means, z of the per-pixel difference):

- **The 0.57% on night-skyline was fixed on 2026-11-02** ("Grazing shadow rays": the fixed 0.6 mm stop let grazing samples hit their own emitter; that section already said so). The ReSTIR note was never updated.
- **What night-skyline shows now is noise.** NEE-only / MIS: 0.9990 at 4096 spp, 1.0053 (z 1.1) at 65536, 0.9988 (z −1.7) at 1M; BSDF-only / MIS 1.00005 and 0.99996. Light sampling alone has a heavy tail there (the glossy floor's reflections of the towers, and floor and tower points next to the emissive boxes, where 1/r² of area sampling has no bound), so a single render reads low more often than high. Split by light kind on a diffuse floor (sky kept): towers only 1.00005 (z 1.0), beacons only 0.99992 (z −0.2, 1M), moon only 1.00000 (z 0.5). Not the HDRI pdf (audit E2) either.
- **light-temperature-row had a real mismatch, now fixed.** NEE / MIS 1.00004 (z 11.5) and BSDF / MIS 0.99930 (z −11.0); the same at 32 bounces (so not the bounce-limit convention), gone with the pawns removed (1.00000 and 1.00007, z 1.4), and with only the pawns 1.00004 / 0.99916 (z −13.7). The difference sits in bands at the pawns' collar and base, where the smooth-shaded lathe mesh's interpolated normal and its faces disagree most. A sampled direction can lie over the shading normal and under the face. The shadow ray always left from over the face (`h.p + ng·0.0003`), so the face blocks it or, on a convex mesh, a shallow ray clears the next facet; the BSDF's continuation left from the side the direction is on (`−ng`), so it went into the pawn and was lost. The two strategies integrated different visibility, and MIS mixed them.
- **Fix (both renderers):** a non-delta lobe's continuation ray now leaves from `+ng` like the shadow ray (`origin = h.p + ng·(!delta || dot(d, ng) > 0 ? 0.0003 : −0.0003)`; `integrators/path.rs` the same). A direction under the face then meets the face (or clears a convex edge) exactly as the shadow ray does, and bounces again from there. Delta events (glass reflection and refraction) keep the old side rule. After: light-temperature-row NEE / MIS 1.00000 (z −0.5), BSDF / MIS 1.00004 (z 0.8); tea-set (smooth meshes) 0.99990 (z −1.5) / 1.00006 (z 1.5). The default (MIS) render of light-temperature-row gets 0.10% brighter (z 41), 0.3% in the pawn rows: light that used to be lost inside the pawns. Scenes without smooth or normal-mapped meshes do not change (night-skyline's MIS render at 65536 spp is bit-identical).
- **Oracle after the fix** (1024 / 512 spp): preset 0 0.9999, veach-mis 1.0013, mirror-room 1.0010, white-furnace 1.0000, nighthawks 1.0005, gem-display 1.0127 (z 0.9, noise), aquarium 1.0003, shape-material-matrix 0.9997, light-temperature-row 1.0002, night-skyline 1.0001, tea-set 1.0003, all ok; with `mis_mode=1`: light-temperature-row 1.0003, night-skyline 0.9982 (z −0.07), tea-set 1.0002, ok.
- **Test:** `both_strategies_see_the_face_under_a_tilted_normal` (a diffuse floor with its normals leaned 45° toward a panel under it, another panel over it; light sampling only against BSDF sampling only from one camera ray): 0.019 against 0.610 (z −97) with the old rule, within z 4 now.
- **Convention, changeable:** the visibility both strategies now share is the face's, not the smooth surface's. Alternatives: pbrt's rule (a direction on the other side of the face than the view gets f = 0 for a reflection lobe, darker terminators) or a shadow-terminator offset (Hanika 2021, audit C2). BDPT and photon mapping walks (`integrators.hlsl`, `bdpt.rs`, `photon.rs`) keep the old side rule (not measured here: a walk through such a band may still lose what enters the mesh; roadmap).

## CPU truth switch (accuracy plan step 2, 2026-11-10)

The CPU reference copies the GPU's shortcuts on purpose, so `tools/oracle.py` cannot see them (audit `docs/claude/accuracy-audit.md`). `scene::Truth` (a `Settings` field `truth`, read only by `src/reference/`, the GPU ignores it) turns each copied shortcut into the exact computation, one bool per audit row. All off is today's model, so the regression oracle is unchanged (8 default scenes ok, 0.9997-1.0127, no dropped samples).

- **Switches:** `--truth roulette,firefly` / `--truth all` (with `--reference` or `--cpu`; also `--set 'truth={"roulette":true}'` and the scene file), Render tab → Advanced → CPU truth (software view; a change restarts the render), `tools/oracle.py --cpu-truth rows`, `--cpu-set key=json`, `--gpu-set key=json` for one side only. The reference prints `REFERENCE_TRUTH rows` and `REFERENCE_TRUTH_PENDING rows`, and `dropped=` (NaN/Inf samples, B4) on `REFERENCE_OK`.
- **Rows with exact code:** `roulette` (B1: no roulette from bounce 3, in the path tracer and the volume scatter), `firefly` (B3: the clamp off whatever `firefly` says), `floors` (B4: pdf floor 1e-7 and density floor 1e-8 down to f32::MIN_POSITIVE), `collisions` (D1: fog tracking runs to the end of the ray, 2^28 collisions), `fog_cutoff` (D2: no ratio-tracking early out), `absorption_floor` (D6: no 0.001 colour floor), `sdf_march` (C3: 4096 steps, 1e-6; `SdfObject::steps`/`eps`).
- **`glass_btdf` (A1, exact 2026-11-10):** `bsdf::rough_dielectric` / `sample_rough_dielectric`, Walter et al. 2007 GGX BTDF with Heitz visible-normal sampling, reflect or refract about the facet by its exact Fresnel, weight G1(l) (× eta² on refraction). The vertex is non-delta (light sampling + MIS over the summed lobe densities) unless dispersive, filmed, or index-matched; those are sampled exactly but stay delta. Light through the glass lobe needs a straight, glass-free shadow ray. Single scattering: no multiple-scattering compensation (furnace sphere 0.986/0.775/0.546/0.295 at alpha 0.04/0.25/0.5/1), changeable. BDPT and photon mapping keep the lerp. Path tracer only; the flag stays off by default (truth mode), changeable to the CPU default later per the plan.
- **Hooks (flag accepted, exact version is the next step):** `internal_reflection` (A2), `multiscatter` (A3), `fresnel` (A4) (`bsdf.rs` `eval_bsdf_parts`), `offsets` (C1), `shadow_terminator` (C2) (`scene.rs`, the 0.0003 offset sites). With one on, the render is the GPU's model for that row and says so.
- **Sync notes:** `truth` is in `transport_key` (the CPU view restarts on it; the GPU restarts too, harmlessly) and is kept across `preset()` and `keep_output` as a viewer choice; the flags are plain bools, so `validate()` has nothing to check.
- **Measurements** (matrix in the audit): the Russian roulette and the 1024-collision, 1e-4 fog caps, the pdf floors and the absorption floor leave the mean unchanged (all within noise; roulette only costs variance, 2-20% in RMSE at equal spp); the SDF marching caps make `sdf-gallery` 0.55% brighter (1.0055, z 16.5; 12% in the worst block); GPU `firefly=10` loses 7-41% of three caustic scenes; `glass_shadow=false` is 3-10% brighter; ReSTIR agrees to 0.25%; at 6 bounces the GPU is 0.5% dark on the Cornell box and 8-46% on mirror and glass scenes.
- **Not done, changeable:** the exact versions of A2-A4, C1, C2; GPU-side counts of dropped samples; CPU time per sample for the roulette comparison; the GPU `bounces` cap was 32 (`validate`) and is 64 since the Accurate bundle. Open: `lighthouse-fog` fails the block test with the truth switch off at 4096/2048 (worst block 1.0581, z 7.3).


## Exact rough glass on the GPU (accuracy A1, 2026-11-10)

- `Settings::exact_glass` (default **off**; changeable, the Accurate bundle is meant to turn it on) selects shader variants built with `EXACT_BTDF`: `trace_exact` (map code, never LEAN), `trace_spectral_exact`, their SER twins (`Gpu::ser` indices 7, 8) and `bdpt_exact` / `bdpt_spectral_exact`. LPE keeps `trace_lpe` (lerp); photon mapping keeps the lerp. Without the setting nothing changes: the default variants' SPIR-V is byte-identical to the code without the exact blocks (checked by stripping them and recompiling trace, trace_maps, trace_lean, trace_spectral, bdpt).
- The path tracer ports the CPU truth `glass_btdf`: visible-normal sampling (Heitz 2018), facet Fresnel choice, weight G1(l) (times eta² on refraction), a film colouring per channel. Without dispersion or a film and across a real index step the vertex is non-delta: NEE evaluates the glass lobe on either side (transmission shadow rays start at -ng and take the far side's media), its shadow ray stops at any glass (light must arrive straight), and both strategies use the summed lobe densities. Otherwise the vertex stays delta with exact sampling.
- BDPT: `sample_vertex` samples the same BTDF and keeps the vertex `V_DELTA` (light subpaths without eta²), so `vertex_f` needs no change; connections through rough glass are a possible later improvement.
- The CPU reference follows `exact_glass` like truth `glass_btdf`.
- Measured (oracle 320x240, GPU 1024 / CPU 512 spp, `--gpu-set exact_glass=true --cpu-truth glass_btdf`): ice-cubes 0.9988 (z -0.9), frosted copy of ice-cubes (rough 0.5, IOR 1.5) 0.9997; BDPT 1.0004 / 0.9992; spectral 0.9992 / 0.9997; all ok. The lerp against the same truth on the frosted copy: 1.0503, worst block 1.463 (FAIL). Cost at 1280x720: 14.4-14.9 to 18.3-18.5 ms trace.

## Accurate quality bundle (accuracy plan, 2026-11-10)

- **What it is:** the Quality menu's `Accurate` entry (`ui::Bundle::Accurate`, listed before Reference; "Custom quality" once any of its fields is changed by hand, and then it reads as Reference if only the extra ones moved). It writes the output fields of Reference (denoiser Off, `scale` 1, no native AA, so no upscaler runs) and the quality ones: `bounces` 64 (`ui::ACCURATE_BOUNCES`), `exact_glass` (A1), `gentle_roulette` (B1), `lod0_textures` (F1), `glass_shadow` on, `firefly` 0, `restir` off, `sobol` on. The pixel filter, `spectral` and the integrator stay as the user set them. Default bundles and defaults are unchanged: the two new Settings fields default off and the shader reads them from spare constant slots (nothing is recompiled or a new variant).
- **Leaving Accurate (review fix, 2026-11-10):** `Bundle::apply` for any other bundle now calls `Settings::clear_accurate_quality()` when the settings are Accurate (the fields go back to `Settings::default()`), so Best/Fast/Native no longer keep the slow exact tracer, mip 0 textures or 64 bounces, and Reference is reachable after Accurate. Test: `quality_bundles_round_trip`. Scene loads and preset switches still keep Accurate (`keep_output`), by design: its values override a scene file's while the bundle is chosen. `tools/oracle.py` prints a note when `exact_glass` meets a non-path CPU integrator (CPU BDPT and photon mapping keep the lerp model; the default CPU judge is the path tracer). LPE renders keep the lerp tracer on the GPU while the CPU would apply `exact_glass`; the oracle does not compare LPE, so left as is. The combined glass+opaque density was checked: `eval_bsdf` already scales its pdf by `1 - lobes.z`, so no inconsistency.
- **Gentle roulette** (`Settings::gentle_roulette`): the path tracer's roulette starts at bounce 8 instead of 3 and the survival floor is 0.2 instead of 0.05 (`fog_noise.z`; `roulette_start()` / `roulette_floor()` in pathtrace.hlsl, both sites: the medium scatter and the surface). Variance only. The CPU reference follows it (`path.rs`); BDPT and photon mapping keep their own roulette. Test `gentle_roulette_keeps_the_mean` (z within 4 on 40,000 paths at 12 bounces).
- **Textures at full detail** (`Settings::lod0_textures`, `env_ground.w`): `sample_map_lod` zeroes both footprint axes, so every lookup is mip 0, which is the only level the CPU reads. Measured with the oracle (320 x 240, GPU 1024 / CPU 512 spp): `texture-showroom` 1.0062 (z 15.4, FAIL, worst block 1.032) and `texture-rock-garden` 1.0027 (z 11.9, FAIL, worst block 1.091) with the cones, 0.9997 (z -2.0) and 0.9995 (z -3.4), worst blocks 0.998, both ok, with the flag. The roadmap's mip-prefilter residual is gone.
- **64 bounces and the TDR question** (1440 x 900, 220 frames, `--no-vsync`, spp 4, exact glass + gentle roulette + lod 0, trace ms per frame, 32 then 64): `glass-stack` 17.04 / 17.09, `mirror-maze` 18.51 / 18.53, `kusama-infinity-room` 36.55 / 36.58, `infinite-corridor` 35.61 / 35.57. Roulette ends the paths that would use the extra bounces, so 64 costs nothing measurable and a dispatch stays far under the 2 s TDR limit; the only scene with a visible tail is `kusama-infinity-room` (mean 0.3772 at 32, 0.3828 at 64, +1.5%). So the bundle takes 64 (`validate` and the slider now allow 1-64; BDPT stops at 24, photon mapping at its own walk cap). Changeable: `ACCURATE_BOUNCES`.
- **GPU Accurate vs CPU, same settings** (oracle 320 x 240, `--set exact_glass=true --set gentle_roulette=true --set lod0_textures=true --set bounces=64`): preset 0 0.9998, `white-furnace` 1.0001, `kusama-infinity-room` 1.0093 (z 1.5; GPU at 6 bounces against the CPU at 32 was 0.5406), `infinite-corridor` 0.9998, `mirror-maze` 0.9984, `glass-stack` 0.9999, `ice-cubes` 0.9999, `texture-showroom` 0.9997; all ok, worst blocks within 5%. With 32 bounces also `stained-glass-hall` 0.9998, `texture-rock-garden` 0.9995, `gem-display` 0.9879 (z -1.0).
- **Default against Accurate, the convergence graph's metric** (`/home/kaan/caustica-tmp/accurate/conv.py`: 480 x 270, 1 spp per frame, `--raw --no-rr`, RMSE x 1000 of l/(1+l) against Accurate at 4096 spp with white-noise sampling (`sobol=false`, independent of the runs); each GPU frame time is indicative only, other jobs shared the machine):

| scene | spp | default | Accurate |
|---|---|---|---|
| ice-cubes | 16 / 64 / 256 / 1024 | 56.2 / 45.0 / 31.5 / 23.4 | 51.7 / 35.7 / 21.0 / 11.9 |
| texture-showroom | 16 / 64 / 256 / 1024 | 10.8 / 8.4 / 7.8 / 7.6 | 9.7 / 4.6 / 2.4 / 1.4 |

  The default's error flattens at its bias (rough glass lerp: the mean sits 2.4% low on ice-cubes; mip blur on the showroom's textures) while Accurate keeps falling about as 1/sqrt(n). Accurate costs more per sample (exact glass about +25% trace time, measured earlier; the rest is variance-neutral).
- **Open, changeable:** the Tier 1 media and SDF rows (D1, D2, C3: uncapped loops, finer marching) are CPU truth rows only and not in the bundle yet; Tier 2 rows wait on the truth matrix. Accurate survives scene loads and presets (`keep_output`, see "Leaving Accurate" above). Spectral Accurate is not measured (the CPU spectral side was measured only for `exact_glass`).

## Exposure settings and photometric units (E1-E2, 2026-11-10)
Plan: `docs/plan-big-features.md`, "Auto exposure on by default". This step lands the constants and the Settings; the meter (E3), Eyes look (E4) and pre-exposure (E5) come after, so for now `auto_exposure` and `eyes` are stored, shown and validated but do not change the image (the display still multiplies by `exposure`).
- **`src/photometry.rs`:** `SCENE_CD_M2 = 32000` is the one constant (sky.rs uses it; `common.hlsli` has `static const float SCENE_CD_M2`, and a test reads the HLSL text to keep them equal). Also Rec. 709 `luminance`, `scene_to_cd` / `cd_to_scene`, `ev_to_scale` / `scale_to_ev`, and the thresholds `SCOTOPIC_BELOW = 3e-7`, `MESOPIC_BELOW = 1e-4` (scene units), `MIDDLE_GREY = 0.18`. Changeable: the calibration moves only the absolute thresholds.
- **Settings:** `auto_exposure` (bool), `ev_bias` (stops, -16..16, keyable like any float: `animation.key("ev_bias", ..)`; `exposure` keys keep working in manual), `exposure_speed` (1/s, 0.05..50, default 2, the time constant is 1/speed), `meter_mode` (`centre_weighted` default, `spot`, `histogram`), `eyes` (bool), `eyes_strength` (0..1). The meter's histogram percentiles and the centre weighting are fixed constants in E3 (add fields only if wanted).
- **Manual range:** `exposure` is valid from 0 to 2^30 (was 0..10); the Look tab slider runs -8..+30 EV (`EXPOSURE_EV_MIN/MAX`), the remote slider the same, the D-pad multiplies by 1.25 up to 2^30. In auto the D-pad steps `ev_bias` by 0.25 EV; the Look tab shows bias, meter and adaptation instead of the manual slider (E6 finishes remote and false colour).
- **Migration (owner decision: realistic exposure on by default):** `Settings::default()`, new scenes and `preset()` are auto with the Eyes on. `auto_exposure` and `eyes` carry `#[serde(default = "no")]`, so a scene or session file without the key loads manual with the Eyes off and its authored `exposure` untouched. Written files always carry the keys. Preset 6 (the warm Cornell) sets `ev_bias = log2(1.15)` beside its exposure. Tests: `old_scene_json_loads_manual_with_the_same_exposure`, `new_defaults_are_auto_with_the_eyes_and_round_trip`.
- **Display-only:** all six fields are in `transport_key`'s exclusions (changing them never restarts the accumulation). They are scene-authored, so `keep_output` does not copy them (it would undo the migration); `preset()` resets them with the rest of the scene.


## Exposure meter and adaptation (E3, 2026-11-10)
Plan: `docs/plan-big-features.md` E3. With `auto_exposure` on, the display exposure is `2^(metered + ev_bias)`; off, the manual `exposure` (also before the first reading). Every number below is a default, changeable (the owner said not to ask): they are constants in `reference/display.rs` mirrored in `post.hlsl`.
- **GPU meter pass:** post.hlsl's `display` entry dispatched once more just before the display (`meter_ctrl.x` bit 1, constant slot `METER_SLOT` 23), so no new pipeline, root signature or `hotreload::ENTRIES` change. One thread per 4 × 4 output cell reads the display input (`display_input`: the software view's image or the beauty, i.e. the reconstruction or the native/accumulated image, before the lens and film look), takes Rec. 709 luminance and bins its log2 into 128 half-stop bins from -40 to +24 (2^-40 is the black cut: unlit pixels are skipped, an unlit background is no light rather than a dim one), weighted, through a groupshared histogram into u28 row 260 (counts, plus the in-bin position in 1/64 steps, so a reading is good to about 0.01 EV). The last group to finish (a counter at row 261 column 0; all histogram traffic is atomic) takes the totals, clears them and reduces in one thread into the state words (row 261 from column 8: magic, base, current, target, scene log2). The display reads `current` (`exposure_now`, `metered_exposure`). No CPU readback in the loop; the state is read back each frame into `GpuState::metered` (Look tab readout, `check_hdr_output`). Cost: one extra dispatch over 1/16 of the output pixels.
- **Reading:** weights 1-8: centre-weighted `1 + round(7·(1 − r²))` with r over the frame's ellipse, spot 8 inside r < 0.2 and 0 outside, histogram 8 everywhere. Centre-weighted and spot take the weighted mean of log2 luminance between the darkest 1% and the brightest 3% (visible lamps, the sun); histogram mode takes the median. Target EV = log2(0.18) − mean, clamped to the manual range (-8..+30).
- **Adaptation in log2 EV on simulated time:** `current = base + (target − base)(1 − e^(−dt/τ))`. τ is 0.2 s toward a brighter scene (the exposure falls), 3 s toward a darker one, 10 s when the metered luminance is scotopic (below `SCOTOPIC_BELOW`), all scaled by 2 / `exposure_speed` (the default speed 2 gives these). dt: the app's frame step live (the same dt that drives rotation and the timeline), 1/fps in exports; wall time never enters an export.
- **When it reads (`MeterPlan`, `MeterStep`):** headless runs, `--capture` / `--frames` and still exports settle on every frame (jump to the reading). Live it holds (keeps adapting toward the last reading, no new one) while the image has under 4 samples, unless that lasts 4 frames (a moving camera), then adapts; the frame the accumulation reaches its cap settles once and later frames freeze (no meter dispatch); changing `auto_exposure` or the meter mode wakes it. The first reading always settles.
- **Video determinism:** `base` is where a frame's step starts. A video export settles on frame 0; each later frame's first pass commits (`base = current`) and every pass of the frame recomputes `current` from `base` with dt = 1/fps on the latest image, so a frame's exposure depends only on its final image and the frame index, not the pass count or timing (`Job::meter_step`). Two exports give identical exposure per frame (test `adaptation_is_eye_like_and_a_video_is_deterministic`; a seconds-per-frame limit makes the image itself timing-dependent).
- **CPU twin** (`reference/display.rs`: `meter_histogram`, `meter_reading`, `Adaptation`, `MeterPlan`, `still_exposure`): same grid, weights, bins and reduction. `reference::display` (CPU stills, `--reference out.png`) settles on the image; the CPU video export (`video::render_cpu`) settles on frame 0 and adapts at 1/fps after. The CPU software view on screen goes through the GPU display pass, so the GPU meter reads the CPU image there.
- **Same exposure everywhere:** the SDR tone map, the HDR output (`hdr_map`, both branches) and the CPU display use the one exposure. False colour (view 11) shows stops relative to the metered exposure (without the bias) in auto, so mid-grey green means "where the meter put it".
- **Not in this step:** pre-exposure for RR / SR / XeSS (E5), the Eyes look (E4), the remote's bias control (E6), pinning `auto_exposure=false` in the self-test and PNG comparisons (E7). Each of these was done in its own later step (E4, E5, E6, E7: see their sections below). The oracle compares linear EXRs, which the exposure does not touch.

## The Eyes look (E4, 2026-11-10)

Scotopic vision for the display, off for old files and on in new scenes (`eyes`, `eyes_strength`, display-only, outside `transport_key`). GPU in `post.hlsl` (`eyes_mix`, `eyes`, applied to scene light right before the exposure in `tonemap` and both `hdr_map` paths); CPU twin `reference/display.rs` (`eyes_mix`, `eyes_look`, before the exposure in `display_exposed`). The strength travels in `Constants.meter.w` (0 when off), so no new cbuffer field.

- Adapted luminance: middle grey over the metered exposure without the bias (`metered_exposure()` on the GPU, `Adaptation::metered` / `still_exposures` on the CPU; the manual exposure when auto is off or before a reading), times `SCENE_CD_M2`. So the eye follows the meter's adaptation time constants, and the bias stays a look that doesn't change how dark the eye thinks it is.
- Mix: 0 at or above 3 cd/m², smoothstep in log10 luminance to 1 at 0.01 cd/m², times `eyes_strength`. At 0 the shader returns the input untouched, so a daylight image is bit-identical with the Eyes on or off (test `eyes_leave_daylight_bit_identical_and_desaturate_the_night`).
- Rod vision: scotopic luminance from linear Rec. 709 with weights (0.04, 0.53, 0.43) (a V'(λ) approximation: rods peak near 507 nm, red barely counts; they sum to 1 so white keeps its level), shown in a Purkinje tint (0.80, 1.00, 1.30) normalised to luminance 1. `c + (tint·rod − c)·mix`.
- Defaults chosen without the owner (asked not to ask), all changeable: the 3 / 0.01 cd/m² bounds, the rod weights, the tint, the smoothstep, using the metered rather than the displayed exposure. Not modelled: loss of acuity (scotopic blur) and rod noise.
- Open: GPU vs CPU agreement under `tools/oracle.py` on a night scene; the D3D12 side is not compiled on Linux (Vulkan uses the same HLSL).

## Pre-exposure for RR and the upscalers (E5, 2026-11-10)

Night radiance (1e-9..1e-5 scene units) underflows the fp16 inside DLSS RR, DLSS SR, FSR and XeSS (our textures are RGBA32F; the libraries are not). Each frame takes a pre-exposure P from the previous frame's displayed exposure (`GpuState::metered` read back with one frame in flight, `Adaptation::exposure`, so the manual exposure when auto is off), and:

- `render::pre_exposure`: P is a power of two, rounded from log2 of the exposure, kept while the exposure stays within `PRE_EXPOSURE_HYSTERESIS` = 1 stop of it, clamped to 2^±40. Powers of two make the scale and its inverse exact in fp32, so the input textures come back bit-identical.
- Scale passes: the `display` entry with `meter_ctrl.x` bit 3 (`scale_constants`: texture in `y`, factor bits in `w`; rgb only, so accumulation's M2 alpha is untouched), constant slots 24–26 (`PRE_EXPOSURE_SLOT`, `CB_SLOTS` 27). Before the reconstruction the colour input (texture 0 for RR, 8 or 1 for the upscalers) is multiplied by P; after it the input is divided back and texture 14 is divided by P. Every later reader (display, meter, scopes, light meter / probe, captures, EXR via `linear_output`, oracle readbacks) therefore sees scene units, and nothing runs at P = 1. Both backends (`Gpu::pre_expose` in `gpu/frame.rs` and `vulkan/gpu/upscale.rs`).
- Bridges: `InPreExposure = P` for DLSSD (ngx.cpp, vk.cpp) and DLSS SR, `preExposure = P` for FSR, so their histories rescale when P steps. XeSS has no pre-exposure input (its `exposureScale` multiplies the colour), so it stays 1 and only the data is scaled: a P step can show for a few frames in XeSS history (open). `InExposureScale` stays 1 and the SR auto-exposure flags stay.
- The NGX jitter rule is unchanged. `CAUSTICA_PRE_EXPOSURE=0` forces P = 1 for before/after measurements.
- Defaults chosen without the owner (asked not to ask), all changeable: power-of-two rounding, 1-stop hysteresis, the ±40 stop clamp, the previous frame's displayed (biased) rather than metered exposure, keeping XeSS at exposureScale 1.
- Measured (Vulkan, RTX, 640x480, `CAUSTICA_PRE_EXPOSURE=0` as before):
  - `tools/rr_stability.py` default (Cornell, manual exposure, P = 1): edge temporal std 0.00700 and mean frame difference 0.000158 before and after, identical (nothing runs at P = 1). PASS.
  - Same with `auto_exposure` on: edge std 0.00855 to 0.00846, frame difference 0.000475 to 0.000184. PASS both.
  - starlight-field, auto exposure, RR at scale 0.667, 96 frames, last 32 scored against a 200-star reference (the brightest 200 star pixels): 0 stars vanished (ratio < 0.25) and 0 flicker (temporal CV > 0.5) both ways; median brightness ratio 0.826 to 0.889, median temporal CV 0.136 to 0.059, mean frame difference 0.00059 to 0.00029. Stars survive RR; pre-exposure halves their flicker. Open: the D3D12 side is written but not compiled on Linux; FSR is D3D12-only so untested here; XeSS on a P step not measured.


## Tests pin the display (E7, 2026-11-10)
Plan E7. New scenes, presets and `Settings::default()` have `auto_exposure` and `eyes` on, so any image comparison that is not a linear EXR would depend on the meter. Pinned now:
- Tools: `oracle.py`, `motion_error.py`, `upscale_quality.py`, `native_aa.py`, `integrator_sweep.py` add `--set auto_exposure=false --set eyes=false` (after the scene and preset arguments, which replace the settings); `rr_stability.py` writes `auto_exposure`, `eyes`, `exposure: 1` into its scene. `scene_sheet.py` is a look contact sheet, not a comparison, and keeps each scene's own look. `tools/verify.ps1`'s PNG captures (`validation-ui.png`, `rr-quality.png`, `raw.png`) are smoke outputs for the eye: the run only needs `RENDER_OK` and nothing diffs them against a golden, so they are not pinned (its `--self-test` pins itself). `tools/perf_baseline.ps1` writes timings to CSV and compares no image. If a PNG golden is ever added it must pass `--set auto_exposure=false --set eyes=false` or compare linear EXR. All of them read linear EXRs except the sheet, so this was belt and braces for the oracle.
- CPU tests: every display test builds on `plain()`, which pins `exposure = 1`, `auto_exposure = false` and `eyes = false` (so neither the meter nor the Eyes look touches it); the meter and Eyes tests turn them on explicitly (E3, E4 tests).
- Self-test: `self_test_script` ends by forcing both off on every frame except 240 and 245, because a preset or scene file load resets them to on. Frame 240 (auto exposure on preset 0) asserts the GPU meter's EV puts the CPU-measured metered log2 luminance at middle grey within 0.15 stop. Frame 245 lights preset 0 with `light_intensity = 12e-7` (the default light intensity of 12 times 1e-7, so the box is 1e-7 as bright; the background is set to 3e-10..6e-10 to match, and the reconstruction is Off for this frame because RR at that radiance loses the walls' colour (measured: no red-dominant pixels with RR, 11283 of 120000 without), and the check is of the display, not the denoiser) and asserts the meter adapted to full rod vision (`eyes_mix` > 0.99), the linear output has red pixels and the 8-bit output has no more than 1% of lit pixels redder than blue. The self-test runs with `frames` set, so the meter settles on every frame and needs no wait.
- Measured (Vulkan, Linux, RTX): `--self-test` 483 frames, RENDER_OK, frames 240 and 245 pass; `tools/oracle.py` default list (8 scenes) all ok, GPU/CPU 0.9998..1.0127, |z| <= 1.24; `cargo test` 378 passed, clippy -D warnings clean. The D3D12 side of the new checks is not compiled on Linux (they use `Renderer` methods `read_floats`/`read_rgba8` and the shared `metered` field, which both backends provide).
- Defaults chosen without the owner, all changeable: the 0.15 stop tolerance, the 1e-7 dimming (`12e-7` in the code), the 1% bound.

## Exposure UI and the night camera (E6 + N7 preset B, 2026-11-10)
Look tab (look_tab.rs): *Auto exposure* (checkbox), then in auto *Exposure bias* (EV, -16..16), *Meter* (mode, plus the metered EV now and where it is heading), *Adaptation* (speed, log slider); in manual the old EV slider (-8..+30). *Eyes* and *Eyes strength* follow. Tooltips state the effect first. Status bar, Advanced only: the displayed EV and, in auto, the adapted luminance in cd/m2 (`chrome.rs exposure_readout`: `Adaptation::scene_log2` through `photometry::scene_to_cd`; the readback is the frame's, so it lags the display by a frame). D-pad up/down act on `ev_bias` in auto and on `exposure` in manual (windows.rs). The phone remote gained `auto_exposure` and `ev_bias` (KEYS is 17 long, the page a checkbox and a bias slider); its Exposure slider stays the manual one. Remote keys go through `Settings::validate` like `--set`.

Night camera presets (stage.rs, Stage tab, Physical sky, Night sky group, row "Camera"): two buttons that set only these fields.
- **Naked eye** (A, the default look): limiting magnitude 6.5, `airglow_warmth` 0, Eyes on, auto exposure on, `ev_bias` 0.
- **Night camera** (B): limiting magnitude 9, `airglow_warmth` 1, Eyes off, auto exposure on, `ev_bias` = `NIGHT_CAMERA_EV_BIAS` = -1.5. The bias is a chosen default for the "20 s, f/2.8, ISO 1600" feel: such a frame is recorded a little darker than the meter's mid-grey choice, so the sky stays a night. Changeable: edit the constant, or move the slider after the click.
New `PhysicalSky::airglow_warmth` (0..1, serde default 0, `validate`, in `map_key`, Stage row "Airglow tint"): the airglow's tint is `lerp(AIRGLOW_TINT, AIRGLOW_WARM_TINT, w)`, warm = (1.08, 1.02, 0.70), luminance about 1 like the neutral one, so total airglow brightness does not change. It reaches the GPU and the CPU reference through the same env bake, so the oracle sees no new path. Display-only reach: it changes the baked sky, so it is part of `transport_key` via `physical_sky`. The Eyes look is a display field and is not.

## Exposure review fixes (E7b, 2026-11-10)

From an independent plan review. Defaults below are the owner's to change.
- **Film and lens thresholds in exposed units.** Halation (threshold 1, "white") and glare (`glare_threshold`) compare against the exposed value: post.hlsl `exposed_threshold(t) = t / exposure_now()` and the CPU twin `reference::display::exposed_threshold(t, exposure)`. With auto exposure on, a night scene's highlights now bloom and a daylight sky no longer all does. Done in the shader rather than passed from the CPU, because the GPU meter's exposure lives on the GPU (the CPU's readback is a frame late); it is display-only, so no transport reset. At exposure 1 (the manual default) the threshold is bit-identical to before; a saved scene with a manual exposure other than 1 and halation or glare on now blooms a little differently (the threshold moved with its exposure). The other film fields (grain, vignette, lift/gamma/gain, LUT) work on exposed or tone-mapped values already. Test: `halation_threshold_is_in_exposed_units`. Pass order: the meter runs before the glare passes (both backends), so the glare threshold sees the exposure the display uses this frame, not the previous one (a first frame, or an adapting video frame); the meter's own input reads the previous frame's glare, and its halation uses the pre-update exposure on the very first frame only (changeable). The headless `--turntable` loop sets the meter step per pass like the interactive export.
- **Firefly clamp stays in scene units.** Its floor (the mean is never taken below 0.05) is absolute scene luminance (pathtrace.hlsl, `progressive.rs::firefly_scale`), so a very dark scene is clamped harder than a bright one. Transport is unchanged; the Render tab tooltip says so; the exposure-relative floor is a roadmap item.
- **Auto to manual keeps the image.** `ExposureSeen::seed` (the frame loop, before the frame's copy): when `auto_exposure` went off since the last frame with the exposure, bias and scene name unchanged, `exposure = 2^(metered EV + ev_bias)` from the last valid readback (`manual_from_auto`). That covers the checkbox, AE-L, the remote and the console; a scene load, preset or undo that sets its own exposure (or another scene) keeps theirs. Heuristic: an undo that only turns auto off is seeded too. Test: `switching_auto_off_keeps_the_exposure`.
- **Meter hold during light edits.** `MeterHold`: a change of `light_key` (emission, albedo and secondary colour of objects, walls and floor, area light colour/intensity/enabled, the main light's intensity/temperature/tint/on, sky, wall colours, background, HDRI strength, physical sky intensity and on) while egui has the pointer held (a slider drag, a colour picker) freezes the meter (`MeterOp::Freeze`) for `METER_HOLD_SECONDS` = 1 s after the last change; the status bar shows *Exposure held*. Animation, physics, the remote and scripts change values without the pointer and still adapt. Stills and headless runs never hold. The CPU view is displayed through the same display pass and meter step, so the rule is the same there. Test: `a_light_edit_holds_the_meter_for_a_second`.
- **RENDER_OK carries the meter:** `meter_ev=` (settled metered EV, no bias; `none` in manual or before a reading) and `exposure=` (final linear exposure, bias included) at the end of the line (`render_ok_meter`, test `the_render_ok_line_carries_the_meter`). E8 reads it.
- **AE-L:** a Look tab button next to the Auto exposure checkbox and the palette entry *Lock the exposure (AE-L)*: turn auto off; the seed above copies the current exposure into the manual one.


## Built-in scenes and auto exposure (E8, 2026-11-10)

Plan E8, revised after a plan review. The first formula in the plan (`ev_bias = log2(old exposure)`) was wrong: under auto the shown exposure is the metered one times 2^ev_bias, so it double-counts the meter. The start value is **`ev_bias = log2(old exposure / settled metered exposure)`**, which reproduces the old image whatever the meter decides.
- **What changed in the files:** every built-in except white-furnace, layered-furnace and measured-cornell (the reference scenes stay manual and linear on purpose) now carries `auto_exposure: true`, `ev_bias` and `eyes: true` (94 files; `exposure` stays as the manual fallback, so switching auto off gives the pre-E8 look). Written by `tools/build_scenes.py` from `tools/scene_exposure.json` (slug to stops): scripted scenes get the keys when regenerated, and `retune_exposure` patches every file in `scenes/builtin` by a JSON round trip (all three layouts in the folder: indent 1, indent 2, with or without the final newline), so the diff is only those keys. Run `python tools/build_scenes.py` (no `--overwrite`: four scenes edited by hand after the script wrote them, flashlight-crypt, measured-cornell, newtons-cradle and underwater, are kept as they are and only get the keys). Edit the table and rerun to change a bias.
- **Procedure:** (a) one headless sweep, release build, 640 x 360, 120 frames (RR on, as shipped), `--set auto_exposure=true --set ev_bias=0 --set eyes=false`, reading `meter_ev` from the RENDER_OK line; bias = log2(old exposure) - meter_ev, rounded to 0.01. (b) every scene again from its file (auto, Eyes on, 60 frames) against the same file with `--set auto_exposure=false --set eyes=false` (the old look); mean display luminance (sRGB decoded, Rec. 709) compared. No scene differs by more than 1/3 EV (largest veach-mis -0.24, pipe-organ -0.14, dark-side +0.06; the other 90 within 0.05), so nothing was hand-tuned for that. The 26 scenes on the plan's retest list were looked at side by side (old | new, 640 x 360) and judged by eye: bright emitters do not crush the rest (street-bokeh, disco-floor, stage-beams, jack-o-lantern, candle-shelf), black backgrounds are not lifted into noise (fairy-lights, bunny-museum, gem-display), night scenes stay as dark as authored.
- **Meter noise:** the meter reads a 4 spp image through RR; a scene lit by a few tiny very bright lights moves by 0.2-0.3 EV between runs (veach-mis 14.53 then 14.21 at 60 frames). The Eyes look makes the darkest corners a little darker and bluer than before. Both are expected.
- **Owner default, changeable:** hand-lit night scenes (nighthawks, jack-o-lantern, lighthouse-fog, candle-shelf, colonnade-night, night-skyline, fairy-lights, flashlight-crypt) keep their authored light levels, so they are photopic and the Eyes look hardly touches them; the large negative biases (-3 to -6 EV, dark-side -5.8, veach-mis -13) are the "this scene is meant to be dark" of the old exposure written as a bias. Dimming those lights to real levels (so they go scotopic and the meter does the work) is a per-scene edit of the light intensities; if wanted, set the scene's bias to 0 afterwards.
- **Day cycle:** a plain auto exposure holds the room at one brightness all day (the meter brightens dusk by 28 EV), so the cycle stopped reading as an evening. `sunlit-room-day-cycle` keys `ev_bias` along the same 12 s as the sun: -2.05, -2.6, -3.6, -4.6, -6.0 at 0, 3, 6, 9, 12 s (linear), chosen by eye. Mean display luminance at nine times between 0 and 12 s falls monotonically (0.069, 0.049, 0.040, 0.016, 0.0096, 0.0059, 0.0034, 0.0016, 0.0007). The old fade fell about 0, -0.85, -2.8, -4.8 EV at 0, 3, 6, 9 s; the keys are gentler. The scene file has no `exposure` track (the plan expected one: it was a constant 2.0).
- **starlight-field and clear-night:** starlight-field goes auto at the default sun intensity 1 (it was intensity 1000 and exposure 10, still dark), manual fallback exposure 10000, `ev_bias` -2. The new built-in **clear-night** (scripted in `build_scenes.py`): physical sky at latitude 33 S, day 172, solar time 23.7, `moon` off, the galactic centre about 80 degrees up, a camera looking up from the ground (pitch -32, fov 80) over dark ground and four rocks; auto, `ev_bias` -2. At bias 0 the meter makes the whole frame mid grey (the sky is most of the image and is as dark as the ground), which is a long exposure, not the naked eye; -2 leaves the Milky Way and stars readable on a dim sky. Changeable.
- **A GPU fix this needed (sky map in half floats):** at intensity 1 a moonless sky is about 1e-8 scene units, under half-float's smallest value, so the zenith stored as black and the band edges as jagged steps (the N4 note's "fp16" item; E5 pre-exposure only covers the denoiser inputs). `environment::half_scale(peak)`, used by both backends where the map is uploaded: a map that peaks below 0.01 is divided by a power of two that puts its peak in 1024..2048 (the shader already multiplies the strength and the pdf normalisation by `env_scale`); every other map keeps the factor of 1 or the old over-range division, so their stored bits do not change. Test `half_scale_lifts_a_night_map_and_leaves_the_rest`. Oracle (`tools/oracle.py --scenes starlight-field,clear-night --floor 1e-13`, 320 x 240, 1024 / 512 spp, intensity 1, no workaround): starlight-field GPU/CPU 1.0000, z 0.58; clear-night 1.0000, z -0.30; worst blocks 1.0015 and 1.0002. Before the fix the same scene rendered the black zenith above. The D3D12 side is the same one-line change, not compiled on Linux.
- **Thumbnails:** `scenes::RENDER_VERSION` 2 to 3 (the display and the files changed), so every thumbnail made before has a stale name; `remove_stale` deletes them and `--make-thumbnails` re-renders all. Checked with a scratch folder holding a stale-hash dark-side thumbnail and one for a scene that does not exist: both removed, every scene rendered (counts under "Measured" below).
- **Measured (Vulkan, Linux, RTX):** `--self-test` 484 frames (308 scripted, 176 scene files), RENDER_OK, exit 0; `cargo test` 383 passed, 6 ignored; clippy `-D warnings` clean; `--make-thumbnails --thumbnails-out` rendered 176 scene thumbnails and 12 material balls with no failure (98 of them built-in, clear-night and starlight-field not black), and the two planted stale files were removed.
- **Not done / open:** the table's biases come from one Linux (Vulkan, RTX) sweep at 640 x 360; the D3D12 thumbnails of a release come from the Windows build. Dimming the night scenes' lights to real levels (above). The meter's reading of tiny-light scenes is noisy by 0.3 EV, so a re-run of the sweep can move a bias by that much.
