## Merge of the six lanes and its fixes (2026-11-10)

Headless, OIDN, egui 0.37 and the Windows check tools went in first, then bounces, accuracy and exposure by a three-way git merge (local branch `merge-int`; each lane's state on `merge-in-*`). What only the combination needed:
- **Specular budget and glass:** a glass vertex (GPU lobes 3 and 4, the CPU's dielectric branch) counts against `specular_bounces` only at roughness <= 0.1, like glossy, whichever glass model traced it. Before, the lerp model counted rough glass at any roughness and the exact BTDF (`exact_glass`, non-delta with glass NEE) never did. Changes nothing at the default budget 0.
- **Spectral BDPT and photon mapping were 3x** with a dispersive lens (street-bokeh: 2.990x and 2.985x the path tracer): the camera took the hero wavelength's 3 and `hero_rgb` took it again. Now the camera only selects the channel when the hero is already on (`camera_ray`: `hero_only ? (1,0,0) : (3,0,0)`); `photon_gather` starts its hero before the camera ray, as `bdpt` did, and the CPU reference marks the hero taken before its camera ray for BDPT and photon (`progressive.rs`), so both sides take the 3 once, in the output. The path tracer is unchanged.
- **Auto exposure after a resize:** the meter texture is recreated empty, and the low-sample Hold had nothing to hold, so the display showed the manual exposure for about three frames. A Hold with no state now reads (`main.rs`, the meter op).
- **CPU OIDN stills meter the unfiltered mean** (`reference::run`, `video::write_cpu_image_exposed`), as the GPU meters before `denoise_capture` filters the file.
- **Merge build fixes:** egui 0.37's `egui_is_using_pointer` (the meter hold while dragging), the D3D12 `GpuState` initializer's exposure fields, and Windows-only dead code put under `cfg(test)`. The D3D12 side still needs a Windows run (docs/claude/windows-handoff.md).


## Hidden runs on X11 under Wayland (2026-11-11)
- **Problem:** Wayland has no hidden window. winit's `with_visible(false)` is ignored there, so every hidden run (the AppImage's thumbnail step, one `--headless` child per scene; headless renders; turntables) put an entry on the taskbar for its whole life.
- **Fix:** `platform::event_loop` builds the event loop with X11 (`EventLoopBuilderExtX11::with_x11`) when the run is hidden and `DISPLAY` names a local server whose socket (`/tmp/.X11-unix/XN`) accepts a connection, which is XWayland on a Wayland desktop. An X11 window that is never mapped is not a managed client, so no taskbar entry. winit allows one event loop per process, so there is no fallback after a failed X11 connection. The connect test catches a stale socket but not a refused authorisation (a run that inherited `DISPLAY` without `XAUTHORITY`, as from a systemd unit or ssh); such a run fails at startup, and unsetting `DISPLAY` puts it back on Wayland.
- **Stay on Wayland:** runs that draw the interface (`show_ui`: the self-test, `--capture-ui`), since X11 reports another UI scale; scripts and `hdr_mode = 2`, which switch the swapchain to HDR, which XWayland doesn't offer.
- **Effect on images:** Wayland sends one more resize at startup, which restarts the accumulation once more, so a hidden run now starts at Sobol block 2 (`restarts`) instead of 3. Captures keep the same mean (194.765 vs 194.782 on hdri-noon-spheres, 4096 spp) and differ only in noise; each backend is still deterministic run to run. Hidden-run captures and thumbnails made before this differ from new ones by that noise.
- Checked: the hidden run connects only to `X11-unix/X0` and is absent from `_NET_CLIENT_LIST`; with `DISPLAY` unset it connects to `wayland-1` as before.


## UI layout B: tool rail and inspector (2026-11-12)
Spec `docs/ui-redesign-b.md`; mockups are the "Option B in detail" boards of the design canvas linked there (read as files: `project/B-*.dc.html`). It is built; the open tails are in the roadmap ("UI and editor").

- **Frame order** (`App::draw_ui`): top bar, status bar, tool rail (left), right column, then the timeline, so the timeline runs under the viewport only and the column keeps its height.
- **Top bar** (`chrome.rs` `top_bar`): one row that never wraps. Menu, scene combo with ‹ ›, pinned windows, the playback block (pause, restart, spp) centred in the spare room, pinned commands and live controls, `+`, then Quality, View and GPU / CPU laid out from the right edge. `top_middle` measures the pins: the rightmost ones lose their label first (only pins that have an icon), then move into a `»` menu.
- **Pins** (`prefs::Pin`, `Prefs::top_pins`): `Do`, `Window` or `Widget`, stored as JSON values and read one by one (`Prefs::pins`), so a name from another version is skipped and kept in the file, not a reason to lose the prefs. Defaults: Scenes, Assets, Compare, Save image. Pin or unpin by right-click on a Menu item, a Ctrl+K entry or a pinned button (which also has Move left / right), or from `+`. Pins run through `App::run_palette` (`run_pin`); `pin_active` gives toggles and window openers their pressed look. Live controls (`palette::Widget`: exposure in EV, denoiser, sample cap) are switched on under Menu > Settings > Top bar or `+`. Window pins sit left of the playback block and the rest right of it; Move left / right works inside a group.
- **Menu:** File, Edit, Window, Settings, Renderer (Windows), Help. The Keys lines are gone (F1 has them). Reset everything asks first (`reset_dialog`, an `egui::Modal`) and leaves the pins. Help > Check for updates clears the skipped version and checks now. The footer names the running API.
- **Tool rail** (`tool_rail`): Select, Move, Rotate, Scale; Walk (right-click: Walk avatar), Frame, Add (the outliner's Add menu plus Assets…); the timeline toggle at the bottom, with a dot when a closed timeline holds keys.
- **Viewport strip** (`transform_strip`): Space, Pivot and Snap float at the viewport's top left whenever something is selected or a transform runs. The software view's message moved to the bottom left.
- **Right column** (`side_panel`): the outliner over a 36-point vertical tab strip and the open tab's properties. The outliner's share is a draggable split (`Prefs::outliner_split`, 4 rows to 45 %). Above Render, Look and Export, and in a column shorter than 520 points, it folds to its header line ("Tall block selected"), which opens it. Width 360 to 560 points (`prefs::PANEL_WIDTH`).
- **Outliner** (`scene_tab.rs` `outliner`, `outliner_rows`): a tree. Stage with Camera, Sky, Main light and Flashlight under it, then the Area lights and Objects groups, which close. The filter looks inside closed groups and drops empty headers. Right-aligned dim text gives the state (sky kind, off, light kind, hidden). The Sky row selects the Stage and opens Lights.
- **Tabs** (`ui::Tab`): Render, Look, Lights, Object, Material, Export. `Tab::Scene` is gone; old prefs files read it as Object (`serde(alias)`), and `--ui-tab scene` still works.
  - Lights: Sky and sun, Sky map (HDRI), Background and Fog on top whatever is selected, the selected light under them, Audio last.
  - Object: the selected object (Duplicate, Delete, Frame, name, shape and placement, portal); the Room with the Stage selected; Camera and Lens otherwise. Physics world is always at the bottom.
  - Material: the selected object's material, or Walls and Floor with the Stage selected.
  - `Tab::after_pick` is the pick rule: a light opens Lights, anything else Object (Material stays for an object or the Stage), and Render, Look and Export stay put unless the pick is a double-click or a palette entry. Outliner clicks follow the same rule.
- **Sections** (`ui::fold`): collapsible, with a value preview at the right of the header so a closed section still tells the state; open or closed is remembered per section. Render: Reconstruction, Sampling, Upscaling and filter, CPU renderer, Advanced. Look: the preset combo on the title, then Exposure, Tone, Grade, Film, Compare, Tools, Light path expressions, Display. Export: Save the current image, High-resolution still, Video.
- **Single homes:** the View combo and the GPU / CPU switch are in the top bar only (the Look row, the Render tab's switch and the Menu checkbox are gone); "Check for updates at startup" is in Menu > Settings only.
- **Windows that were Scene-tab sections:** Describe a scene (AI) and Laser puzzle (Menu > Window, `Win::Describe`, `Win::Puzzle`). The puzzle window opens by itself when a scene turns the puzzle on.
- **Export progress** (`video_ui.rs`): while `App::export` or `cpu_export` runs, `draw_ui` draws the top bar, the status bar, the rail and the column only, all disabled except the card (`export_card`) on top of the Export tab, which the column shows whatever tab was open (`self.ui.tab` is not changed, so the tab comes back after). The card: title and per cent, the preview (GPU jobs), frame and spp, elapsed and time left, the bar, a frame strip (one cell per frame, three spans when cells would be under 4 points), format and size, Cancel and "Open folder when done" (`Prefs::open_folder_done`; never in hidden or self-test runs). The top bar's middle becomes a chip (`export_chip`), the status bar says "Exporting frame N / M" and the viewport carries "Locked while exporting". `App::export_info` feeds all three. Every ended export (saved, cancelled, failed) goes to `Prefs::recent_exports` (8, newest first; `UiState::export_ended`), listed under Export > Recent exports; a click shows the file in its folder. The chip is not a button: the Export tab is already open. No Pause (owner cut). `--ui-export` starts a 100-frame PNG export on the first frame, for a capture of the card.
- **Timeline** (`timeline_ui.rs`): under the viewport only, opaque. The toolbar is one row at every width: under 1240 points of strip the length, frame rate, shutter and Clear move into a More menu (placed after Keys, so it is never clipped), and under 900 the frame number goes and Loop, Camera path and Curves join them (`timeline_toggles`, `timeline_setting`). Under the scrub strip there is one row per thing that holds keys (`track_owners`: objects and lights by index, the main light and the camera by their field lists, the rest as an "Other values" row that selects nothing), the selection first, six rows before it scrolls. A row's name (a 150-point column, `TRACK_NAMES`; the strip starts where the lanes do) selects it by the pick rule; its lane shows its keys and scrubs like the strip. The outliner marks what holds keys with a diamond (`tree_row_keyed`, `has_keys`). The gizmo layer is clipped to the viewport (`overlay.rs`), since the image and so the outlines run on under the panels.
- **Scenes and Assets browsers** (`windows.rs` `scenes_window`, `browser.rs` `assets_window`): both fill the viewport (a frameless window fixed to `central_rect`, so the bars, the rail and the column stay usable and the image keeps rendering behind), and the one opened last closes the other. Three columns: a 200-point sidebar, the cards, a 300-point details pane with the actions. Scenes sidebar: Library (All scenes, Favorites, Recent, with counts), Tags (the `TOP_TAGS` chips as rows with counts, More tags as before; the list and the tag still combine), Create (Generate… and Save current scene… replace the cards with their form, `UiState::scene_page`). Assets sidebar: Source, then the source's kinds; the details pane stacks one button per use ("Use as sky map", "Apply to the floor", …). Close buttons and Esc return to the image. Not built from the board: collections and tag editing (owner cut).
- **Scenes browser, list tools:** the Library has All scenes, Favorites, Recent and My scenes with counts; My scenes is `own_scene`: the `user` pack, plus any pack under the per-user scenes folder when installed. Over the cards sit a Sort combo (`prefs::SceneSort`: Pack with pack headings, Name, Recently used, Favorites first, Date modified from the file's mtime cached in `UiState::scene_dates`) and S / M / L card sizes (`CARD_WIDTHS` 104 / 160 / 232 points, `Prefs::scene_cards`). Both are kept in prefs.json and read leniently; the sorts are stable, so ties keep the pack order.
- **Assets browser, local view and downloads:** "On this computer > Downloaded" lists every catalogue asset found in the scenes folders, across sources, from a background scan of the cached catalogues (no network); the scan reruns after a download, a catalogue fetch or Rescan. Sky maps get an All / Outdoor / Indoor / Studio segment from Poly Haven's `categories` (kept in front of `Asset::tags`), offered only where the catalogue has them. Running downloads (parallel, one thread each) are a strip at the foot of the details pane with name, bar, size and Cancel (`download_strip`, three rows then "+ N more"); failures stay there until Dismiss or the next download, and a cancel is a toast. The details pane has its own Cancel for the selected asset, since the strip shows three rows. `assets::install` fetches the file `installed` looks for last, so a set broken off part-way is not taken for an installed one.
- **Key marks** (`timeline_ui.rs`: `KeyMarks`, `key_scope`, `key_mark`, `key_slot`, `App::key_marks_begin` / `key_marks_end`): while the Lights, Object or Material tab is drawn, every `ui::row` keeps a 10-point slot before its controls (`row_controls`), and a row named by `key_mark("field")` under the current `key_scope` prefix shows a diamond there: an outline on hover when the value has no keys, an accent outline when it has keys elsewhere, filled when one is at the playhead. A click sets a key from the current value (`anim::key_path`, then `Timeline::settle`) or removes the one at the playhead (`Animation::remove_keys`), with an undo checkpoint and a toast, whether or not the timeline is open. A value the scene JSON leaves out at its default can't be keyed until it is changed. The dot after Position is the revert dot, not a key mark.
- **Pin icons:** every pinnable command and window has a line icon (`icons.rs`, mapped in `chrome.rs` `pin_short`); only live controls have none. A pin's right-click menu has "Icon only", stored in the pin's own `top_pins` entry as `{"pin": …, "icon_only": true}` (bare pins otherwise, so old files and unknown entries read as before); it survives reordering and is dropped on unpin and by "Reset top bar". In `top_middle` such pins start compact, then the rightmost labels go, then pins move to `»`.
- **Header previews:** Grade reads "Neutral" or the wheels off neutral plus the LUT; Film reads "Off", the active effects, or "4 effects"; Tools reads "off", the tools that are on, or "5 on". `fold` right-aligns the preview in 12-point monospace and cuts what does not fit at the left, so each builder keeps to the room beside its title: values drop to names (`brief` in look_tab.rs), the Upscaling header uses short upscaler names (`upscaler_short`), the Reconstruction header drops "(100%)" or shows the percentage alone (`reconstruction_preview`), and a long HDRI name ends in "…".
- **Viewport during an export:** a GPU export's output has the export's size, which the window can't show as it is (D3D12 copies it to the back buffer only at equal size and otherwise clears, which was the black viewport; Vulkan would blit it stretched under the panels). So `export_lock_note` paints the job's preview over the central rect, fitted: the view read just before `Job::start` until the first frame is done, then the last finished frame (at most 960 px on the long side, one upload per redraw, the same texture as the card, `take_export_preview`; freed when the job ends). A CPU export leaves the viewport alone.
- **Prefs from a newer version:** `Prefs::tab` reads leniently (`lenient_tab`), like the pins. Builds before 2026-11-12 do not: a prefs file written by this layout names tabs they don't know (Object, Lights, Material), fails to parse there and is reset to defaults.
- **Icons** (`ui/icons.rs`): the mockups' SVG paths, parsed once into polylines and stroked by the painter. No icon font, so the glyph test has nothing to miss.
- **Changeable defaults (the spec's section 9, answered without the review's text, which was not recorded):** Display stays in Look; Lens stays with the Camera in Object; Battle mode stays in Render > Sampling; Look > Tools stays a section; spp shows in the top bar and the status bar; no gamepad block in the F1 sheet yet. The strip turns horizontal only under 350 points of column width (the spec says 400, but its default width of 370 is drawn with a vertical strip).
